English · [Français](manuel.md)

# Cyber Pinball: technical manual

Complete reference of the prototype, in English (the French version, [manuel.md](manuel.md), is the reference): how each part works, the files it writes, commands, unattended tests and known limitations. Project overview: [README.md](../README.md) (in French: [README.fr.md](../README.fr.md)).

The design notes cited here (`docs/recherche/…`) are internal working documents: they are not published with the code.

Open-source frontend for virtual pinball cabinets. The idea: you describe the cabinet once, and Cyber Pinball writes and maintains the configuration of every component (VPX, then B2S, PinUP, DMD, DOF). No more editing an `.ini` by hand.

**Status: prototype of milestones 1 and 2.**
- **Screens**: EDID detection, a setup wizard driven by the cabinet buttons (roles, flipper edge, playfield diagonal), generation of `VPinballX.ini` with automatic table framing, corrected automatically when Windows renumbers or moves the screens.
- **Cabinet configurations**: several screen sets for one installation (two machines, a desktop PC and a cabinet), each with its own roles, sound, buttons and `VPinballX.ini`; the one that is plugged in is recognized automatically at startup, before each game and by the `sync` command (see [Cabinet configurations](#cabinet-configurations)).
- **Sound**: a wizard step that plays a sound on each audio output and has you pick the backglass output and the playfield output, then tests the speakers of a 5.1 or 7.1 sound card; `SoundDeviceBG`, `SoundDevice` and `Sound3D` are rewritten at every startup with the current names of the outputs (see [Sound](#sound)).
- **Table menu**: one window per cabinet screen, VPX launched with `-Play`, a loading screen (on a cabinet, the loading media alone, full screen; on a desktop, with the game controls), return to the menu with the foreground taken back, statistics in each table's `.info` file, VPX errors reported in plain language. With the playfield screen lying flat and rotated while Windows stays in landscape, the menu, the details screen, table management and the settings rotate for a player standing at the flipper edge (`playfield.flipperEdge` in the cabinet model, mapped to the screen's current orientation), in portrait at 90° and 270°; the backglass, DMD and topper do not rotate.
- **Full-screen windows**: each window (menu, details screen, settings, wizard) covers its whole screen, Windows taskbar included, with no border line: Electron full screen (`coverDisplay`, `app/src/cover-display.ts`), without forcing the foreground. VPX comes to the front during a game, and the Windows key or Alt+Tab bring back the desktop and the taskbar. System menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Bureau Windows" (Windows desktop), or Ctrl+Shift+D from the menu, frees every screen of the PC, however many there are (desktop mode, see the screen guard); Ctrl+Shift+D again, the Cyber Pinball button in the taskbar or Alt+Tab reopens the menu on all its screens.
- **No screen ever shows the Windows desktop** (screen guard, `app/src/screen-guard.ts`), from startup to the end of a game: one black cover per screen, opened before any other window and never in the foreground; the menu windows stay behind VPX during loading and play; the menu ↔ wizard restart hands over without a gap; the taskbars are hidden during a game. **Never a light screen** either: without a Windows system backdrop, a window stays transparent until its first frame, and only goes full screen once shown. A screen that stays uncovered for more than one second is reported in the console (see [Launching a game](#launching-a-game)).

## Getting started

Requirements: Windows 10 or 11, Node.js 22 or later.

The simplest way: double-click `lanceur.cmd`. It offers a menu (play, installation, opening VPX, detection, wizard, synchronization, endurance, tests, off-screen demos, data folder) and compiles whatever is needed before each action. `.\lanceur.cmd sync` runs an action directly. The scenarios to test by hand are in [docs/manual-tests.md](manual-tests.md).

**As an end user**: the "Fabriquer et déployer" (Build and deploy) action (or `npm run deployer`) produces `CyberPinball.exe` and copies it to `<racine>\app\`, with a `Cyber Pinball.lnk` shortcut at the root. The executable finds its root by itself (the folder that contains `app\`); if VPX is missing, it offers to install it from the screen.

**To distribute**: the "Fabriquer l'installeur" (Build the installer) action (or `npm run installeur`) produces `release\CyberPinball-Setup.exe` (light version) and `release\CyberPinball-Setup-complet.exe` (with VPX). See [Windows installer](#windows-installer).

The same commands with npm:

```bash
npm install
npm test          # builds and runs the core tests
npm run probe     # lists the detected screens and audio outputs, their fingerprint and the name VPX expects
npm start         # table menu (screen wizard on first startup)
npm run assistant # full wizard (-- --boutons, --lanceur, --tilt, --ecrans, --playfield, --disposition, --son: a single step)
npm run fabriquer # executable in release\win-unpacked\ (npm run deployer: also copies it to <racine>\app\)
npm run installeur # same, then release\CyberPinball-Setup.exe and -complet.exe (-- --leger: without the full one)
npm run sync      # what the application does at every startup: fixes VPinballX.ini if the screens have moved
npm run vpx-schema # regenerates the VPX settings schema from its source code (-- --check: check only)
```

The keys are those of the cabinet buttons, recorded at the first step of the wizard ("Boutons" (Buttons), see below). Until that step has been done, they are the ones cabinet encoders send by default:

| Button | Default key | Setup wizard | Table menu |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Left / right flipper | Left / right Shift | Change screen; sound: backglass / playfield | Choose a table |
| Start | 1 | Confirm; sound: both | Play |
| Exit | Esc | Skip a role, start over; sound: no role; buttons: skip an optional button | Menu: back, VPX settings, buttons, quit |
| (keyboard) | arrows, Enter, Esc | Like the flippers, Start, Exit | Like the flippers, Start, Exit |
| (keyboard) | Tab | Button: refused (reserved) | System menu, from anywhere in the menu; then Tab / Shift+Tab to move through it |
| (mouse) | ⚙ top right | | System menu |
| (mouse) | "‹ Retour…" (Back) above the title | | Like Exit, in every submenu, on the game page and in the VPX settings |
| (keyboard) | H | | Show the controls |
| (keyboard) | R | | VPX settings |
| (keyboard) | Backspace | Previous step | |
| (keyboard) | Ctrl+Q | Quit | Quit |
| (keyboard) | Ctrl+Shift+D | | "Bureau Windows" (Windows desktop): frees every screen of the PC; again, even from another application: back to the menu |
| (keyboard) | Ctrl+Shift+K | | Stop a stuck game |

The keyboard keys only work if no cabinet button already sends them (an arrow recorded as the right flipper no longer moves back); the same goes for H and R.

**Shortcuts in the system menu**: an entry that has a shortcut shows it to the right of its label, as circled keys like the help bar ("Réglages de VPX" (VPX settings): R, "Commandes de la borne" (Cabinet controls): H, "Bureau Windows" (Windows desktop): Ctrl Shift D), and the shortcut also triggers it from that menu. The keys shown are read where the shortcut is defined: this cabinet's interface keys (`interfaceKeys`, a key taken by a button disappears) and the combinations in `SHORTCUTS` (`packages/core/src/shortcuts.ts`), which also give the global shortcut and the controls reminder. A joystick button is not shown (its name does not fit in a key). Art direction model: `LigneMenu`, `.cp-shortcut`.

**Back at the top of the screen**: every menu screen (system menu, game page, doctor, adding, catalog, media, configurations…) has a "‹ Retour…" (Back) button above its title that does what Exit does; with the mouse, no need to scroll to the bottom of the list to find "Retour" (Back). Its label is that of the screen's action that does the same thing ("Retour à la fiche" (Back to game page), "Annuler" (Cancel)…), otherwise "Retour" (Back); it is not shown when that action is the screen's only one (a message and its "Continuer" (Continue)). Every screen described by `Built` (`app/src/library.ts`) gets it from its mandatory `back` field: a new screen cannot forget it (`backLabel`, the renderer's `back` action, `.lib-back` in `app/renderer/library.*`). The on-screen keyboard covers it without removing it, so the page does not jump. The VPX settings have their own under the brand: "‹ Retour aux sections" (Back to sections) in a section of the "Avancé" (Advanced) tab, "‹ Fermer" (Close) elsewhere.

During a game, the keys are those written in VPX's `[Input]` section (shown again on the loading screen on a desktop, and in the reminder, H key): by default Shift for the flippers, Enter for the plunger, 5 for a credit, 1 for Start, Esc to quit (VPX 10.8.1 quits as soon as it is pressed), P to pause (pressing it again resumes the game), and both MagnaSave buttons together (without them, Credit and Start) for VPX's menu, where the screens are placed ("Adjusting the display during a game").

## Installation and file layout

All of Cyber Pinball lives in a **root chosen at installation** (the launcher's "Installer" (Install) action), e.g. `D:\CyberPinball`. The layout is fixed: an update moves nothing.

```
D:\CyberPinball\
  app\                         Cyber Pinball, replaced entirely at each update
  emulators\vpx\10.8.1-5436\   VPX as published, one version per folder
  emulators\vpx\current\       junction to the active version
  tables\  media\              tables and media, all emulator families
  pinmame\roms\                ROM library: each ROM stored once, linked into each table
  config\VPinballX.ini         passed to VPX with -Ini, independent of its version; VPinballX-<id>.ini: other configurations
  data\                        cabinet model (cabinet.json, all its configurations), backups
  downloads\                   verified archives, to reinstall without a network; VPS database
  import\                      tables dropped from a USB stick, offered for adding
```

- Third-party components (VPX today, other emulators later) are described in [packages/installer/components.json](../packages/installer/components.json): version, official URL, SHA-256, and distribution mode (`automatic` if the license allows shipping them, `manual` otherwise). They are not in the git repository.
- An archive whose hash does not match the manifest is deleted, never installed. Running the installation again downloads nothing a second time.
- The root is refused in Program Files, in Windows and on a network share. The choice is stored in `%APPDATA%\CyberPinball\root.json`.
- Before a root is chosen, everything is written to a sandbox (`%APPDATA%\CyberPinball\sandbox\`). It is taken over automatically during installation.
- Before each write of `VPinballX.ini`, the previous version is backed up to `data\backups\` (10 versions kept). Only the keys owned by Cyber Pinball are modified.
- An owned key changed by hand (or through VPX's in-game menu) is never silently overwritten: `data\vpx-owned.json` keeps the values last written to each `VPinballX.ini` (one set of values per cabinet configuration), and at startup the menu asks whether to keep the new value or restore Cyber Pinball's (`npm run sync -- --reecrire` on the command line). Keeping a screen size imports it back into the cabinet model; keeping any other key leaves it to the user (`vpxManual` in `cabinet.json`, editable in the VPX settings) until the next run of the screen wizard, which takes back control of all keys. A number rewritten by VPX with different decimals does not count as a change.
- Variables for testing: `CYBERPINBALL_ROOT` (another root), `CYBERPINBALL_HOME` (isolated sandbox), `CYBERPINBALL_VPX_INI` (another file).

On the command line: `node packages/installer/dist/cli.js setup <racine>`, `install [id]`, `status`.

### Windows installer

`npm run installeur` builds the executable, then two French-language NSIS installers in `release\`:

| File | Size | VPX |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `CyberPinball-Setup.exe` (light) | 111 MiB | downloaded on first launch (173 MB), verified by SHA-256 |
| `CyberPinball-Setup-complet.exe` | 276 MiB | official archive included, placed in `downloads\`: installed on first launch, no connection needed |

What the installer does:
- **Root folder** of your choice. Suggested: the one from the previous installation, otherwise the root stored in `root.json`, otherwise `D:\CyberPinball` if `D:` is a local disk, otherwise `C:\CyberPinball`. Same refusals as `packages/installer`: Program Files, Windows, network folder (mapped network drives included), plus a missing drive or an incomplete path. An `app\` folder that does not contain Cyber Pinball is refused too.
- The application goes into `<racine>\app\` (replaced entirely at each update), the root layout is created and the root is stored in `%APPDATA%\CyberPinball\root.json`.
- Data from before a root was chosen (`%APPDATA%\CyberPinball`: cabinet model, backups, `sandbox\VPinballX.ini`) is taken over as `setup` does it, never overwriting the root's own data.
- Shortcuts: `Cyber Pinball.lnk` at the root and in the Start menu; on the desktop (checked by default); optionally at Windows startup, for a dedicated cabinet (the session's Startup folder).
- Per-user installation, without administrator rights, with an entry in "Programmes et fonctionnalités" (Programs and Features) (`HKCU`).
- If Cyber Pinball is open, the installer and the uninstaller ask you to close it.
- Command line: `/S` silent, `/DEMARRAGE` launch at Windows startup, `/SANSBUREAU` no desktop shortcut, `/D=D:\Borne` root (last, without quotes). Exit code 2 if the root is refused.

**Uninstalling removes the application only**: the `app\` folder, the shortcuts and the "Programmes et fonctionnalités" entry. Tables, media, configuration, data (cabinet model, backups), emulators, downloads, `import\` and `root.json` stay: a reinstallation into the same root finds them as they were. To erase everything, delete the root by hand. The uninstaller (`app\Désinstaller Cyber Pinball.exe`, `/S` for silent mode) refuses to run outside an `app\` folder that contains `CyberPinball.exe` and the `installation.ini` log.

**VPX license and the full version.** Most of VPX's files are under GPLv3+; the rest keeps a license derived from MAME's (`docs\license.txt` in the archive), which allows redistribution on three conditions: do not sell it or use it in a commercial product or activity, provide the sources of any modified version, reproduce the license. The full version embeds the unchanged official archive (hash checked against the manifest at build time) and, next to it, `<archive>.licence.txt`: the full license text read from the archive itself, the archive's address and the commit of its sources. It can therefore be redistributed free of charge, but **must never be sold, nor shipped with a cabinet that is sold or operated commercially**. The build stops if the license found in the archive changes. The archive is taken from `CYBERPINBALL_VPX_ARCHIVE` if that variable is set, otherwise from `cache\downloads\` (downloaded if needed).

The NSIS script is [scripts/installeur/cyberpinball.nsi](../scripts/installeur/cyberpinball.nsi), compiled by [scripts/installeur.mjs](../scripts/installeur.mjs) with the NSIS 3 that electron-builder downloads (hash verified). The icon is drawn in [app/renderer/icone.svg](../app/renderer/icone.svg), which also serves as the logo of the drawn topper; `node scripts/icone.mjs` renders it off-screen at each size of the `.ico` and derives the welcome-page banner from it (into `app\ressources\`). In the executable, koffi is reduced to the JavaScript loaded at run time and the `win32-x64` binary.

### Code signing

Nothing is signed without a certificate: the build carries on without asking anything. With a code signing certificate (Authenticode), `CyberPinball.exe`, the uninstaller and both installers are signed and timestamped by `signtool` (the uninstaller is written and signed before being embedded). The certificate is designated through environment variables, never in the repository ([scripts/signature.mjs](../scripts/signature.mjs)):

| Variable | Role |
| --- | --- |
| `CYBERPINBALL_SIGN_SHA1` | thumbprint of a certificate in the Windows store: USB token or HSM, mandatory for certificates issued since June 2023 |
| `CYBERPINBALL_SIGN_PFX`, `CYBERPINBALL_SIGN_PASSWORD` | `.pfx` file and its password (older or test certificate) |
| `CYBERPINBALL_SIGN_ARGS` | `signtool` arguments that replace the previous ones, e.g. `/dlib … /dmdf …` for a cloud signing service (Azure Trusted Signing) |
| `CYBERPINBALL_SIGN_TIMESTAMP` | RFC 3161 timestamp server (default: `http://timestamp.digicert.com`) |
| `SIGNTOOL_PATH` | `signtool.exe` to use (default: the most recent one from the Windows Kits, otherwise the one supplied by electron-builder) |

Procedure, once the certificate has been obtained in the name of Cyber Pinball's publisher:

```powershell
certutil -user -store My                    # certificate thumbprint ("Cert Hash(sha1)", « Hachage cert. (sha1) » on French Windows)
$env:CYBERPINBALL_SIGN_SHA1 = '<empreinte>'
npm run installeur
Get-AuthenticodeSignature release\CyberPinball-Setup.exe, release\win-unpacked\CyberPinball.exe   # Status: Valid
```

An OV certificate gives a valid signature, but SmartScreen keeps warning until the publisher's reputation is established; an EV certificate or Azure Trusted Signing establishes it right away.

### Updating from the cabinet

An installed cabinet updates itself from the system menu, entry **"Mise à jour de Cyber Pinball" (Cyber Pinball update)** (details: installed version, and available version once checked). Nothing is installed on its own: a quiet check runs right at start-up, then every 6 hours while Cyber Pinball keeps running (every 5 minutes while it fails, for instance when the network is not ready yet as the cabinet boots), without downloading anything or showing anything if it fails; an error from a manual check does not stop it; an available version puts a **red dot** on the menu's gear (⚙) and on the "Mise à jour de Cyber Pinball" (Cyber Pinball update) entry of the system menu (`attention` on system menu entries, carried by the gear as soon as one entry has it). Once opened, the entry shows straight away the release notes of every version missed since the cabinet's own (the feed's `history`), and "Mettre à jour maintenant" (Update now), selected by default: installing takes one button press. A result older than 10 minutes is checked again. Off-screen, tests only check their own test site (`CYBERPINBALL_MAJ_URL`), never GitHub. Code: [packages/core/src/update.ts](../packages/core/src/update.ts) (feed, versions, download), [app/src/updates-menu.ts](../app/src/updates-menu.ts) (screen).

- **Where**: the releases of the public repository [github.com/gocode/cyberpinball](https://github.com/gocode/cyberpinball). The cabinet reads `https://github.com/gocode/cyberpinball/releases/latest/download/cyberpinball-maj.json` (a GitHub redirect, free of its API's rate limit; pre-releases are not listed there). The repository is written in a single place (`UPDATE_REPOSITORY`), equal to `repository` in `package.json` (checked by the tests); the installer derives from it the website shown in "Programmes et fonctionnalités" (Programs and Features).
- **Signed feed**: `cyberpinball-maj.json` contains the manifest (version, date, release notes in French and, when `CHANGELOG.en.md` has the version's section, in English (`notesByLanguage.en`): the cabinet shows those in its language, otherwise the French ones; light installer: release address, size, SHA-256) and its Ed25519 signature. The cabinet only reads a feed signed by the publishing key, whose public half ships with the application ([update-key.ts](../packages/core/src/update-key.ts)): a compromised GitHub account is not enough to get a program installed on the cabinets. The downloaded installer (`<racine>\downloads\cyberpinball\CyberPinball-Setup-<version>.exe`, previous ones are deleted) is verified (size and SHA-256 from the signed feed) before it is launched; a bad file is deleted.
- **Screens**: checking; "à jour" (up to date: latest published version and its date); "Cyber Pinball x.y.z est disponible" (Cyber Pinball x.y.z is available), with release notes, size and verification, plus "Mettre à jour maintenant" and "Plus tard" (Later); download (MiB received, percentage, "Annuler" (Cancel)); "Installation"; "Mise à jour impossible" (Update failed: no network, no published version, signature or hash refused) with "Réessayer" (Retry). When run from the sources (`npm start`), the check works but installation is not offered.
- **Installation**: the usual installer, in `/MAJ /D=<racine>` mode (plus `/S` off-screen). Only its progress page is shown; it waits for Cyber Pinball to close (up to 60 s), keeps the shortcuts chosen at the previous installation (desktop, Windows startup, read from `app\installation.ini`; for a cabinet deployed with `npm run deployer`, which has none, the ones that exist), is started from the download folder (a working directory inside `app\`, the one the cabinet shortcut uses, would prevent renaming it), renames the old `app\` to `app.precedente` for the duration of the install (retried for 10 s; if it is still held, nothing changes and Cyber Pinball restarts, no click needed) (put back if it fails, deleted if it succeeds), then restarts Cyber Pinball with its previous arguments (`CYBERPINBALL_MAJ_ARGS`). Tables, media, configuration and data are not touched, as with any reinstallation.
- **Light update**: each release also attaches `CyberPinball-application.zip` (1.5 MiB), the contents of `app\resources\app` alone: the JavaScript, the interface, koffi. A cabinet whose **base** matches the package's takes it instead of the installer. The base (`cyberpinball.socle` in the application's `package.json`, for instance `electron-44.5.1-842034390a0b`) sums up everything around `resources\app` that only the installer changes: Electron and electron-builder versions, the executable's configuration and icon ([scripts/fabriquer.mjs](../scripts/fabriquer.mjs)). The package is verified like the installer (size and SHA-256 from the signed feed), unpacked in `downloads\cyberpinball\application-<version>\` and refused if it does not hold the announced version and base; Cyber Pinball then starts the new version's `maj-legere.js` through its own executable in Node mode (`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`), and closes. The script ([app/src/maj-legere.ts](../app/src/maj-legere.ts)) waits for it to close, renames `resources\app` to `resources\app.precedente` (retried for 10 s), puts the new one in its place (the old one restored if that fails, deleted otherwise; at the next startup if a Chromium process still closing holds one of its files), writes the version in "Programmes et fonctionnalités" (Programs and Features) if the installation is the one registered there, then restarts Cyber Pinball with its previous arguments; log in `downloads\cyberpinball\maj-legere.txt`. A few seconds, with no installer and no window. A version from before light updates (no base), or with another base, goes through the installer.
- **Quick release** (`publier.mjs --rapide`): only the application is built (no electron-builder, in seconds) and published with its feed; the installers are not rebuilt. The feed points to those of the last full release (`installer.version`): a cabinet with another base first installs that version, then is offered the light update on restart ("Installeur de la x.y.z, puis la mise à jour légère vers …" (Installer for x.y.z, then the light update to …)). A quick release is refused if the built base no longer matches the last published feed (Electron updated, icon changed…): a full release is needed then. A feed with no light package, or an installer that brings nothing newer than the installed version, offers nothing impossible: the screen says so, with no "Mettre à jour maintenant" (Update now) button.
- **Pre-releases**: the "Préversions : non reçues / reçues" (Pre-releases: not received / received) entry of the update screens sets the cabinet (kept in `<données>\preversions.json`). A cabinet that receives them looks for the feed of the highest-version release, pre-releases included, in the GitHub API's list of releases (no token, 60 requests per hour: plenty); an unreadable list falls back to the final releases' feed. The feed is still checked by its signature. Other cabinets never see a pre-release (`0.1.3-essai.1`, published as a "pre-release"), and the final version (`0.1.3`) goes ahead of its pre-releases.
- **At the next startup**: `data\mise-a-jour.json`, written before the installer or the light update is launched, tells whether the expected version is running: a banner "Cyber Pinball x.y.z est installé (avant : …)" (Cyber Pinball x.y.z is installed (before: …)), or "La mise à jour vers x.y.z n'a pas abouti : la version … est restée en place" (The update to x.y.z did not complete: version … stayed in place). A version newer than the previous one counts as installed (a quick release's installer sets up an older version than the feed's).

#### Cheat sheet: publishing a release

Everything is done from the launcher: double-click `lanceur.cmd` (folder `D:\wamp\www\cyberpinball`), section **"Fabrication et publication sur GitHub"** (Building and publishing on GitHub), or run `.\lanceur.cmd <action>` directly.

| When | Launcher action | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Once, then when the token expires | `jeton` | Saves the GitHub token (masked input) in `%USERPROFILE%\.cyberpinball\jeton-github.txt`, outside the repository |
| Before each release | `version` | Moves to the next version (patch, `mineure` for minor or `majeure` for major), opens both CHANGELOGs in Notepad, checks the release notes (as many in English as in French), then commits "Version x.y.z" |
| Before publishing, if needed | `verifier` | `npm test` (tests, publication check, translations) and the version's release notes |
| To go live | `publier` | Both installers, the signed feed, the code snapshot on GitHub, the release, then the repository description (15 to 20 min). Cabinets see the version as soon as it ends |
| To go faster | `publier-leger` | Same with the light installer only, the one cabinets download (twice as fast) |
| For an everyday improvement | `publier-rapide` | Without rebuilding the installers (1 to 2 min): the application alone and the feed; cabinets with the same base update within seconds, the others through the installers of the last full release. Refused if Electron or the executable changed |
| To try it on your own cabinet | `preversion` | Moves to the next test pre-release (`0.1.3-essai.1`, then `-essai.2`…) and commits it; `publier-rapide` puts it online, only for cabinets set to "Préversions : reçues" (Pre-releases: received). `version` then gives the final version (`0.1.3`) |
| To review before everyone else | `publier-brouillon` | Draft release: invisible to cabinets until "Publish release" on GitHub |
| Code only, no version | `envoyer` | Code snapshot on `main`, without a release: cabinets see nothing |
| Repository description | `decrire` | Repository description and topics, taken from `package.json` |
| Before a delicate release | `essai-maj` | Full off-screen test: installs one version, publishes the next on a local site, updates and checks (about 15 min) |
| After changing the light update | `essai-maj-legere` | Same with a quick release: the cabinet gets the light package, with no installer (about 10 min) |
| On this cabinet, without publishing | `deployer` | Builds the application and copies it into `<racine>\app` (fast, no installer) |

Each publication asks for confirmation ("o") before sending anything, and stops without publishing if the release notes are missing, if a change is not committed in git, or if the publication check finds a secret. The signing key (`%USERPROFILE%\.cyberpinball\cle-publication-maj.pem`) and the token stay outside the repository, as do the secret files that `.gitignore` keeps out.

#### Publishing a release

```powershell
# 1. Version in package.json, release notes in CHANGELOG.md ("## 0.2.0" section, one "-" line per item), commit, push to GitHub.
# 2. Once and for all: a fine-grained GitHub token, limited to the gocode/cyberpinball repository, with
#    "Contents" and "Administration" read and write, saved on its own in this file (outside the repository):
#    %USERPROFILE%\.cyberpinball\jeton-github.txt   (or the CYBERPINBALL_GITHUB_TOKEN variable)
npm run publier                      # -- --brouillon: release left as a draft, invisible to cabinets
npm run publier -- --rapide          # without rebuilding the installers: light package and feed only
```

[scripts/publier.mjs](../scripts/publier.mjs) runs the publication check, builds both installers, writes and signs the feed, reads it back with the shipped key, creates the `v<version>` release on the pushed commit (refused if there are uncommitted changes, if the commit is not on GitHub or if the release already exists), attaches `CyberPinball-Setup.exe`, `CyberPinball-Setup-complet.exe`, `CyberPinball-application.zip` and `cyberpinball-maj.json` (with `--rapide`: the light package and the feed only, after reading the last published feed to reuse its installers and compare the base), publishes it, then reads the feed again the way a cabinet does. A version with a prerelease tag (`0.2.0-essai.1`) goes out as a "pre-release": only cabinets that accept pre-releases see it; with no section of its own in the CHANGELOG, it takes the release notes of its final version (`## 0.2.0`), otherwise "Préversion d'essai de la 0.2.0" (Test pre-release of 0.2.0). Finally, it sets the repository description and topics, taken from `description` and `keywords` in `package.json`; `npm run decrire` sets them on their own (`-- --essai`: checks without sending anything). For this, the token also needs the "Administration: read and write" permission.

**Publishing key**: created once by `node scripts/cle-publication.mjs` (never replaced afterwards; `--verifier` checks that it matches the shipped key). The private key is never in the repository: `%USERPROFILE%\.cyberpinball\cle-publication-maj.pem`, or `CYBERPINBALL_MAJ_CLE_PRIVEE`. **Back it up off the machine**: if it were lost, installed cabinets would refuse every later update and would have to be reinstalled by hand.

**Publication check** ([scripts/audit-publication.mjs](../scripts/audit-publication.mjs), run by `npm test` and by publishing): since the repository is public, it rejects, in tracked files, private keys, tokens (GitHub, OpenAI, AWS, Slack, Google), passwords written in plain text, Windows profile paths (`C:\Users\<nom>`), e-mail addresses not on its allow list (commit co-author, font authors, examples) and the names from a private list kept outside the repository (`%USERPROFILE%\.cyberpinball\audit-noms.txt`, one per line, or `CYBERPINBALL_AUDIT_NOMS`). `--commits <a..b>` also checks the messages and authors of those commits.

**Unattended test**: `.\docs\demo-mise-a-jour.ps1` (hidden desktop, simulated screens) builds the installer for the repository's version, with another base (`CYBERPINBALL_SOCLE_ESSAI`, as after an Electron change: the light package does not suit it), publishes the next version on a local site (`CYBERPINBALL_VERSION_ESSAI`, `publier --local`, feed signed with the real key), installs the old version in a sandbox with launch at startup and no desktop shortcut, makes it reject a forged feed, then runs the real update: the old version downloads, launches the installer and closes, the installer replaces it and starts the new version, which announces the update and reports itself up to date. It checks the installed version, the kept shortcuts, that `app.precedente` is deleted, that the follow-up record is consumed, the site's requests, the layout and the off-screen watch. With `-Legere` (action `essai-maj-legere`), the old version is fully published on the local site, the next one as a quick release reusing its installers: the cabinet downloads the light package (never an installer), closes, `maj-legere.js` replaces `resources\app` and restarts it; it also checks the application's version and the one left in `installation.ini`, that the package was downloaded once, that `resources\app.precedente` and the unpacked package are deleted, and the light update's log. `CYBERPINBALL_MAJ_URL` overrides the feed address; `CYBERPINBALL_MAJ_CLE` (PEM file) overrides the public key, only in a sandbox (`CYBERPINBALL_HOME`). Also only in a sandbox, `CYBERPINBALL_MAJ_INSTALLEE=1` makes its root count as an installation: the screen offers "Update now" even when running from source (project page screenshots).

## VPS database

When tables are added, each one is identified in the [VPS database](https://github.com/VirtualPinballSpreadsheet/vps-db) (Virtual Pinball Spreadsheet, file `db/vpsdb.json`, about 2,600 games), which is then used to name the folder "Title (Manufacturer Year)" and fill in the `.info`: `Title`, `Manufacturer`, `Year`, `VPSId` (the game identifier, shared by every version in the folder), `Authors` (those of the identified table version) and `IPDBId`. Fields already present in an existing `.info`, or in one shipped with the archive, are kept.

- **License**: the `vps-db` repository declares no license (checked on October 20, 2026: no LICENSE file, `license: null` in the GitHub API, nothing on the website). Without a license, redistribution is not allowed, so Cyber Pinball does not ship the database. It is downloaded the first time a table is added, from its official address (`https://raw.githubusercontent.com/VirtualPinballSpreadsheet/vps-db/main/db/vpsdb.json`), and kept in `<racine>\downloads\vpsdb.json`, like the components. The tests use a hand-copied extract of a few games.
- **Updates**: at most every 12 hours, in the background, when "Ajouter une table" (Add a table) is opened; `lastUpdated.json` (a few bytes) tells whether the database has changed. Without a network, the cache is used as is; with neither cache nor network, names are derived from the files, as before. An invalid response never replaces the cache.
- **Matching** ([packages/core/src/vps.ts](../packages/core/src/vps.ts)): based on the names of the `.vpx` files, of the `.directb2s` in the same package, of the archive and of the folders, and on the ROM read from the script. The VPUniverse numeric prefix is ignored; titles are compared regardless of case, accents and punctuation, with or without the article ("Addams Family" for "The Addams Family"), by main title ("Terminator 2" for "Terminator 2 - Judgment Day"), and "Dr." for "Doctor"; manufacturer and year break ties between games with the same name; authors ("VPW", "g5k", "Bigus") and version number identify the table version. The result is a list of games, each with a score between 0 and 1.
- **Confidence**: at 0.85 or more, with a lead of 0.08 over the next one, the game is picked without asking. Otherwise, the "Quel jeu est-ce ?" (Which game is it?) screen offers up to five games, plus "Garder" (Keep) for the name derived from the files (game not in the database); a game not in the database with no candidate gets the file name, without asking. From the plan, "Choisir le jeu" (Choose the game) or "Changer le jeu" (Change the game) reopens the screen for each table. Nothing moves until "Installer" (Install).
- Measured on the whole database (a made-up name "Name (Manufacturer Year).vpx" for each of the 2,366 games that have a VPX table): 99.7% identified first, no wrong certainty; the others are duplicates within the database.

## Adding a table: the funnel, the catalog, "Récupérer", waiting list

System menu > "Tables" > **"Ajouter une table" (Add a table)** is the only entry for adding a table, whatever the method: you go in, then pick the method. The online catalog (**"Trouver une table en ligne" (Find a table online)**) shows every game in the VPS database that can be played in VPX (2,370 on October 22, 2026, 960 of them with no ROM to find), its versions, and what it takes to play it on this cabinet. Design, rights and site terms: `docs/recherche/acquisition.md`; funnel and the original "one-click" add: `docs/recherche/entonnoir-ajout.md`.

- **The funnel** (`buildAddSource`, `app/src/library.ts`), with the cabinet buttons as with the mouse, in this order: **"Prêt à installer" (Ready to install)** (games whose received files are waiting to be installed: "Installer : <jeu>" (Install: <game>) opens the plan); **"Importer le contenu du dossier d'import" (Import the import folder's content)** (`<racine>\import`, filled from a USB stick, each item listed); **"Trouver une table en ligne" (Find a table online)** (the catalog); **"Reprendre un fichier téléchargé" (Reuse a downloaded file)** (Cyber Pinball's downloads, without downloading anything again), **"Choisir des fichiers…" (Choose files…)** and **"Choisir un dossier…" (Choose a folder…)** (Cyber Pinball's file picker, see [Downloads and file picker](#downloads-and-file-picker); or drag and drop onto the window, from anywhere); **"Récupérés et attendus" (Received and expected)** (the waiting list); **"Mises à jour des tables" (Table updates)**; **"Comptes des sites" (Site accounts)**. Before October 25, 2026, the catalog, the waiting list, updates and accounts were four more entries in the system menu, next to "Ajouter une table", which only offered files: the menu entry now carries a red dot when received files are waiting to be installed. The catalog screens come back to the funnel. A test (`packages/core/src/test/menu-paths.test.ts`) refuses any interface or documentation text that still cites these entries at the top level of the system menu.
- **Quick install** ("Installation rapide", at the top of a game's page, and on a version's "Ce qu'il faut" (What it takes)): the recommended version, in one go. The essentials to fetch and the recommended backglass (`quickNeeds`, `packages/core/src/acquisition.ts`) enter the waiting list and are fetched one after the other, the essentials (the table, the ROM) first: each page opens by itself in the "Récupérer" window as soon as the previous file is received (or downloads without a window with direct download); once everything is there, the window closes and the plan opens, the game already chosen. The backglass never holds the plan back: as soon as the essentials are received, "Installer sans attendre" (Install without waiting) closes the window and opens the plan, the backglass staying awaited in the list. Window closed along the way: "Continuer l'installation rapide" (Continue the quick install) reopens the next page; "Arrêter" (Stop) keeps what was received. A version with an essential that has no live link says so and is not started.
- **VPinMediaDB media when adding**: the plan of a new table recognized in the VPS database (catalog, import folder, picked or dropped files) has a "Médias de VPinMediaDB, après l'installation" (VPinMediaDB media, after the install) section: what VPinMediaDB has for this game (wheel, playfield, backglass, full DMD, flyer, music, the variant "Chercher les médias" picks), the size, and the license warning. Kept by default; "Médias de VPinMediaDB : oui / non" (VPinMediaDB media: yes / no) leaves them out. After "Installer" (Install), only the missing ones are downloaded (none is replaced), source noted in the `.info`, and the table check counts them ("Ajouté avec la table", Added with the table). This is the original "one-click" add, a table and its media from what you have, found again. Index unavailable: the section says so, the table installs alone.

- **What Cyber Pinball does not do**: download on the user's behalf. Nearly all VPS links lead to forum pages (vpuniverse.com, vpforums.org…) that require an account and whose terms forbid bots and rehosting; no manufacturer allows its ROMs to be distributed for emulation. The warning is shown on first use (accepted once, with the date stored in `<données>\acquisition.json`).
- **Catalog**: twelve games per page, with their VPS thumbnail, their type, their versions and their ROM; "Chercher un titre…" (Search a title…) matches title, initials and ROM name, like the media search; "Filtres et tri" (Filters and sorting) opens the filters screen, one line per filter, Start for the next value, with, next to it, each value of the chosen filter and its number of games, the other filters kept:
  - **Jeux** (Games): all, no ROM to find, solid-state, electromechanical, original creations;
  - **Fichiers** (Files): what the game offers for download, with a live link: backglass (`.directb2s`), ROM, PuP-Pack (one included with a table counts), remastered sounds, DMD colors, music, topper, point of view, a VR version, or media on VPinMediaDB (from its index, downloaded the first time this filter is used);
  - **Site**: where the table, or the files of the "Fichiers" filter, is downloaded: vpuniverse.com, vpforums.org, pinballnirvana.com, or no account (Google Drive, MEGA, Dropbox, GitHub…; a YouTube video or an IPDB page does not count);
  - **Sur la borne** (On the cabinet): not installed yet (default), installed or not, already installed;
  - **Tri** (Sort): by title or newest first (during a search, the order is the search's).

  "Effacer les filtres" (Clear the filters) goes back to the defaults; "Voir les <n> jeux" (See the <n> games) (or Exit) goes back to the catalog. Every game comes from the VPS database. Code: `catalog()`, `supportFiles()`, `linkOnSite()` in `packages/core/src/acquisition.ts`.
- **A game's details screen**: its VPX versions, the **recommended** one first (not a MOD when possible, VPW preferred, the most recently updated, with a working link; the reasons are spelled out), then the others, with their particulars (MOD, VR, nFozzy, Fleep, what they include), the date and the site.
- **Choosing a file: the file gallery** (`app/src/file-gallery.ts`, core `packages/core/src/sitefiles.ts`): every list of files to choose from is a gallery, never a list of names. This covers a game's versions; "<part> : au choix" (<part>: pick one) on "Ce qu'il faut" (What it takes) (backglass, ROM, PuP-Pack, colours, sounds, music, topper, point of view); the same from the parts of an installed game; and "Voir les <n> au choix" (See the <n> to choose from) on "Ce qu'il manque pour jouer" (What is missing to play), offered as soon as the VPS database knows several files for a step. Each tile, three per row, shows:
  - the **preview** in the shape of what it shows (upright table, 4:3 backglass, 4:1 DMD colours, `FILE_PREVIEW_ASPECT`): the VPS database thumbnail, otherwise the screenshot the author uploaded to their site;
  - the name (authors and version, or the ROM name), the state (Installed, Recommended, Received, Awaited) and at most two particulars (2Screens, FullDMD, MOD…);
  - the **popularity** ("1 324 téléchargements · 5/5", 1,324 downloads · 5/5), then the date and the site.

  Under the gallery, the detail of the chosen tile: authors and version, rating and number of reviews, views, size, account required, category on the site, particulars and comment, and for a version, why it is recommended. "Trier : …" (Sort: …) goes from the screen's order ("écrans de la borne", cabinet screens; "conseillée d'abord", recommended first; or the date) to popularity (most downloaded first, unknown ones last), then to the date; the chosen order is kept from one screen to the next during the visit. The date is the VPS database's when it gives one: the order does not move when the file pages arrive.
- **Site file pages** (`app/src/site-files.ts`): the VPS database gives neither downloads nor rating, and no thumbnail for ROMs, PuP-Packs, colours and sounds. When a gallery opens, Cyber Pinball reads each file's page, as a visitor, with the cookies of the "Récupérer" (Get) session, without showing it or running its scripts, four at a time; each page read fills its tile:
  - **VPUniverse**: its schema.org description (downloads, views, rating out of 5 and number of reviews, author, version, date, size, category, screenshots);
  - **VPForums**: the "File Information" block, the stars and votes under the title, the author's screenshot (never another file's thumbnail).

  Pages are kept for a day in `<downloads>\sites\fiches.json` (a page that says nothing or a network failure is read again an hour later), screenshots in `sites\apercus`. A page that has gone (404) carries "Retiré du site" (Removed from the site); another site (Dropbox, MEGA, Google Drive) "ne dit ni téléchargements ni note" (gives neither downloads nor rating); a page received but unreadable is reported in the log ("Fiche de vpuniverse illisible"). On 27 October 2026, The Matrix's "Fronton (.directb2s) : au choix" was only a list of names, with neither preview nor downloads. `sitefiles.test.ts` reads both witness pages (`packages/core/test-pages/vpuniverse-fiche.html`, `vpforums-fiche.html`) and rejects any list of VPS files to choose from built outside the gallery. Mock-up: `.\docs\recherche\maquettes\fichiers-galerie.ps1`.
- **What it takes**, worked out without downloading anything, from the cabinet's screens and the ROMs already there: the table; the backglass (`.directb2s`), unless it comes with the table or the cabinet has no backglass screen, with the ones that suit its screens first; the ROM, unless the game needs none or it comes with the table ("déjà sur la borne" (already on the cabinet) if the library or a table has it); then, optional, PuP-Pack, remastered sounds, DMD colors (if there is a display), music, topper (if there is a topper screen), point of view. Each line gives its site and whether an account is needed.
- **"Récupérer" (Fetch) window** (Start on a line): the file's page opens under a Cyber Pinball banner that reminds you what to download (game, version, authors, ROM), with "Précédent" (Back), "Retour à la fiche" (Back to the page), "Ouvrir dans mon navigateur" (Open in my browser) and "Fermer (Échap)" (Close (Esc)). You sign in and download yourself, with keyboard and mouse; Cyber Pinball clicks nothing, fills in nothing and sees no password (separate session, `persist:acquisition`, like a browser). Each downloaded file is stored in `<racine>\downloads\acquisition` and matched to what was expected through the page it came from. Close, Esc or Alt+F4 does not lose the page: the window's history is kept (`navigationHistory`), and reopening it for the same starting page ("Chercher sur le web…" (Search the web...) for the same media, for instance) picks up the page where you were, "Précédent" (Back) included ("Repris là où vous en étiez", Back where you left off). Esc, the key you press out of habit to close an enlarged image or after a screenshot, used to close the window and start again from the starting page. A video of the page in full screen keeps Esc for itself (it leaves full screen). Minimized (Windows+D, Windows+M), the window comes back when reopened. The cabinet's Exit (key or joystick button) closes it too, even while typing in the page. While it is open, no cabinet window comes in front of it: any other Cyber Pinball window that takes the focus (click, joystick button, script reading) hands it straight back; in 0.1.3-essai.2, the first click in the page brought the backglass in front.
  - **Banner sized to the screen**: the banner is written in reference pixels (a 1,080 px short side, `--u` in `app/renderer/acquire.css`), like the menu: it follows the screen it covers, at 1366 × 768 as in 4K, and no text in it goes below 20 px (the game at 30, what is expected at 22, everything else at 20: buttons, host, status, screen line, engines, video page, download lines). Reference heights: 132 (164 with the line about the chosen screen), plus 76 for the search line, 62 for a video page, 48 per download and 12 below; the main process makes the same count, multiplied by the screen's scale, and places the site's page right below (`BAR`, `barHeight`, `app/src/acquire-window.ts`). On a narrow screen (portrait, 4:3, 5:4: less than 1,500 wide for 1,080 high), the four buttons no longer fit beside the rest: they move below it and share the width (196 high, 228 with the screen line; `html.narrow`), and a download's text (size, speed, time left) takes room from the bar; layout reviewed by Codex. Up to 0.1.4-essai.21, the banner was in fixed px: 96 px high and texts of 12 to 16 px whatever the screen size (6 to 8 reference px on a 4K screen at 100 %); the layout guard reported them in `demo-telechargement.ps1`, `demo-recherche-libre.ps1` and `demo-parcours.ps1`. A test now refuses any font size in fixed px in the screens' stylesheets (`plancher-textes.test.ts`).
  - **Screen and pointer**: the page is read and driven with the mouse. Its screen is chosen in **"Écran du navigateur intégré" (Built-in browser screen)** (system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Configurations de la borne" (Cabinet configurations) > the configuration; shortcut in "Comptes des sites" (Site accounts)): **Automatique** (Automatic), or one of the cabinet's roles (Playfield, Backglass, DMD, Topper: only those the configuration has). The choice is stored in the configuration (`browserScreen` in `cabinet.json`, `packages/core/src/browser-screen.ts`) by role: it follows the screen found by its fingerprint, even when Windows renumbers it, and the full wizard keeps it. Each choice has its preview line, highlighted when it is pointed at with the buttons or the mouse: the screen (name, size), landscape or portrait, which role it is shared with (backglass and DMD on the same screen), upright facing the player or, for the playfield lying flat, "the page and the mouse go sideways there"; for Automatic, the screen it gives right now. It applies to every "Récupérer" window (catalog, waiting list, site accounts, site media, web search), from the next opening. The chosen screen is not connected: the window takes the automatic screen, and one more orange line in the banner says so ("DMD is not connected, opened on Backglass (automatic)"); the turned playfield remains possible if chosen, and the same line warns that the page and the mouse go sideways there. **Automatic**, the behaviour from before the setting, is the screen where the mouse moves the same way as the picture (`readingScreen`, `packages/core/src/layout.ts`): the playfield if the menu is not turned there (desktop, cabinet where Windows follows the screen), otherwise the largest of the menu's other screens, the backglass first; on the cabinet, the page landed now on the DMD, now on the backglass, hence the choice. On a cabinet whose playfield lies flat and turned (flippers on the left or right of the Windows picture, which VPX corrects by turning its rendering), the page used to open on Windows' main screen, the playfield: sideways for the player, with a mouse moving at right angles to their hand, which vanished as soon as it crossed onto another screen, where the menu's windows hide the pointer. On opening, the pointer is brought to the middle of the window if it is not there already (`SetCursorPos`, `setCursorPosition` in `packages/win-displays/src/windows.ts`).
  - **Download progress**: while a download of the session is in progress, the banner gives it a line, below its buttons: the file name, a thin bar (signal green on teal), the percentage, then "48.2 MB of 312 MB · 3.1 MB/s · about 1 min", and "Annuler" (Cancel). **Size the site does not announce** (file served compressed or in chunks, common on forums): Cyber Pinball reads it on the page the download starts from, where the site shows it ("Size 212.6 MB" on vpuniverse.com, "File Size 40.81MB" on vpforums.org; on a page with several files, the size written next to the file's name; a "Size", "Taille" or "File Size" label right against the size, otherwise nothing), read at each page loaded in the window and in direct download (`PAGE_SIZE_PROBE`, `expectedSizeFromPage`, `packages/core/src/transfer.ts`). The line then says "48 % · 2,0 Mo sur environ 4 Mo · 884 ko/s · quelques secondes" (48% · 2.0 MB of about 4 MB · 884 KB/s · a few seconds), bar included; 99% at most before the end, and a size exceeded by more than 3% is dropped. Neither the site nor its page gives it: "1,6 Mo reçus, taille non annoncée · 682 ko/s" (1.6 MB received, size not announced · 682 KB/s), no percentage, and the bar stays an empty dotted track (in 0.1.3-essai.5, a sliding segment looked like a half-full bar and said nothing). Menu notices, media proposals and VPinMediaDB downloads put the percentage first ("15 % · 48,2 Mo sur 312 Mo · …"). Paused or interrupted: the line turns orange and says so. The speed is smoothed over a few seconds and shown after one second of measurement; the time left is rounded ("a few seconds", "about 25 s", "about 4 min", "about 1 h 20 min") so it does not jitter (`packages/core/src/transfer.ts`, `formatTransfer`). Every download of the session has its line, those of the visible page as well as those started without a window (direct download, site media), while the window is open; closed and reopened, it finds them again. Beyond three, two lines and a summary of the others ("And 2 more: 8.9 MB · 1.3 MB/s"). The banner grows by one line per download and the site's page moves down by as much. Downloads are sampled four times a second, not on every packet; the menu (catalog and waiting-list notice), media proposals and VPinMediaDB downloads say the same thing, at most twice a second. **Cancel**: with the mouse, or with the cabinet buttons: Start points at the latest download (its line is highlighted, "Start again: cancel · flippers: another one · Exit: keep"), the flippers pick another one, Start again cancels it within six seconds; Exit (or Esc) gives up without closing the window. In the page, Start only counts outside a text field, and Enter, the arrows, Space and Tab stay with the page. The cancelled file is deleted and the banner says so ("Téléchargement annulé : …" (Download cancelled)); at the end, "Reçu : …" (Received) as before. Off-screen demonstration with a slow local server: `docs\demo-telechargement.ps1`.
- **Waiting list** ("Récupérés et attendus" (Received and expected), in the funnel, the catalog and "Ce qu'il faut"): what is being fetched, game by game, "Attendu" (Expected) or "Reçu" (Received). A file downloaded in your own browser is found through "Surveiller Téléchargements : oui" (Watch Downloads: yes) (the Windows Downloads folder, only while something is expected; a file still downloading, `.crdownload` or `.part`, is ignored) or through "Chercher dans Téléchargements" (Search Downloads); it is matched by its name (zip named after the ROM, `.directb2s`, game name) and left where it is.
- **Received file filed by its nature**: before it is filed, each received file is recognised from its extension and, for an archive (zip, 7z, rar), from what it contains, listed without unpacking it (`tar -tf`): a table (a `.vpx`, or an archive containing one), a backglass (`.directb2s`), a ROM (a `.zip` of chip files, with no folder), a PuP-Pack (`.pup` files), remastered sounds (`altsound.csv`), DMD colours (Serum, VNI), music, a point of view, a topper, or **menu media** (images and videos, no `.vpx`). It goes to the **game part** its content says, whatever item was expected: a PuP-Pack downloaded from the table's page, with the "Récupérer" (Get) window open for the table, goes to the game's "PuP-Pack" part (the one that was expected, or a part **added** to the game for it: the VPS file of the same page gives its name, otherwise the file name), and the table stays expected; the message says so. The game must be certain: the window was open for it, or the file comes from the page of one of its items. A **complete archive** (table, backglass, ROM, PuP-Pack together) fills at once every part it brings: those that were expected separately no longer are ("apporte aussi : …", also brings, on the item's line, "Fourni", Included, on "Ce qu'il faut", What it takes). Without a certain game (nothing expected matches it), the file is **kept aside** and the "Fichier reçu à vérifier" (Received file to check) screen asks where to file it. An unreadable archive is taken without checking, and the line says so. Getting an already received item again **replaces** it: the old file is kept aside. On 27 October 2026, The Matrix's VPUniverse page offered the table and its PuP-Pack: the PuP-Pack zip ended up in "Table", "À corriger" (To fix), and blocked installing the table, which had been received.
- **Fixing the waiting list**, with the buttons as with the mouse: each item is a line to choose (Start or click) that opens its screen: state, received file (size, date, where from), recognised content, what it expects. For a received item: "Réaffecter à : …" (Reassign to: …) (the game's item the file fits, if there is one), "Vider l'élément" (Clear the item) (it is expected again, the file is kept aside), "Remplacer par un autre fichier" (Replace with another file) (kept aside, received in `downloads\acquisition`, arrived in Downloads over the last month, fitting ones first; or "Rouvrir la page pour le télécharger de nouveau" (Reopen the page to download it again); or a file dropped on the window), "Réaffecter à un autre élément" (Reassign to another item) (fitting ones first; an item already received keeps its previous file aside), "Vider, fichier à la corbeille" (Clear, file to the trash). For an expected item: "Rouvrir la page" (Reopen the page), "Choisir un fichier déjà téléchargé" (Choose a file already downloaded), "Ne plus l'attendre" (Stop waiting for it). Everywhere, "Retirer tout le jeu de la liste" (Remove the whole game from the queue): keep the received files where they are, or put them in the trash. Assigning by hand a file that does not fit asks for confirmation ("Ce fichier ne convient pas", This file does not fit); assigned anyway, it is noted (`received.forced`): it is never redirected automatically and blocks nothing. "Le garder de côté" (Keep it aside) is noted the same way (`kept`). Nothing is ever deleted: the trash is Cyber Pinball's (`<racine>\trash\… Liste d'attente`). Files without an item are listed apart, "Reçus sans élément" (Received, not assigned) (`strays` in `acquisition.json`).
- **Repairing what is already there**: at startup, when the waiting list opens (and "Ce qu'il faut", What it takes, and before installing), the content of received files not noted yet is read once and noted in `acquisition.json` (`received.content`), then each file is put back in the part it belongs to, without asking (`repairWaitList`): a file filed in a part that is not its own (a PuP-Pack in "Table") moves to its own, and the item is expected again; a file that came from the page of another item of the same part (two versions of a table) goes back to it, and the origin recorded in the installed table's `.info` follows; a file kept aside whose game is certain (it comes from the page of one of its items) becomes a part of that game, **installed** if the table already has it (a PuP-Pack put by hand into `pupvideos`), otherwise received and to install. A message says what was filed. What was chosen by hand (assigned anyway, deliberately kept aside) is left alone. A file that is gone becomes "À corriger" (To fix). No need to edit `acquisition.json` by hand.
- **Installation**: "Installer : <jeu>" (Install: <game>) opens the usual add plan with the received files, the VPS game already chosen; nothing moves until "Installer" (Install), then the doctor checks the table. A file **to fix never blocks** the rest: the plan leaves it aside ("PuP-Pack à corriger à part", PuP-Pack to fix separately), and only a game where everything is to fix opens the item. What is fetched for an installed table ("Ce qu'il manque pour jouer", What's missing to play, "Parties du jeu", Game parts) all goes to it: the plan targets its folder (`PlanOptions.target`). The origin is recorded in the `.info` (`CyberPinball.source`: VPS game and file, version, authors, page, date); the copies in `downloads\acquisition` are deleted, the files in Downloads stay.
- **Table updates** (in the funnel): a table whose VPS file has a newer version, according to its recorded origin or, without one, according to the author and version written in the table; Start opens what is needed for the new version.
- **Site accounts** (in the funnel): vpuniverse.com, vpforums.org, pinballnirvana.com and pinsound-community.org, each "Connecté" (Signed in, with the date until which the sign-in is kept), "connecté pour cette fois seulement" (signed in for this time only) or "Pas connecté" (Not signed in), read from the cookies of the "Récupérer" session, all subdomains included. "Se connecter" (Sign in) opens the site's sign-in page in the "Récupérer" window: you enter your own account there and tick "Remember me"; the sign-in is then kept from one start to the next, as in a browser (about 3 months on vpuniverse.com, one year on pinballnirvana.com). "Se déconnecter" (Sign out) deletes that site's cookies and nothing else. Cyber Pinball neither sees nor keeps any password.
- **Direct download** (a setting in "Comptes des sites" (Site accounts), off by default): when the user is signed in to the site, "Récupérer" loads the page in a hidden view of that session and follows the download link the site offers (page, confirmation page, at most four pages), without showing the page; the file lands in the waiting list as usual. As soon as the site asks for something, the page opens in the "Récupérer" window, with the reason in orange in the banner: not signed in, Cloudflare check or CAPTCHA, several files to choose from, enforced wait (free-account timer), terms to accept, limit reached, proxy refused, file on another site, page not recognized. Cyber Pinball builds no address, accepts no terms, skips no wait and solves no check: one download, one page, at the user's request. Site flows and terms: `docs/recherche/comptes-sites.md` and `docs/recherche/acquisition.md`, § 14.
- **Code**: `packages/core/src/acquisition.ts` (hosts, catalog, recommendation, what it takes, waiting list, matching, origin, updates), tests in `packages/core/src/test/acquisition.test.ts`; screens in `app/src/catalog.ts`, window in `app/src/acquire-window.ts` (banner `app/renderer/acquire.*`), watching in `app/src/downloads-watch.ts`, accounts and download probes in `packages/core/src/sitesessions.ts` (tests `sitesessions.test.ts`). Off-screen demos, on fake pages (no forum page is loaded): `.\docs\demo-catalogue.ps1` (funnel, filters with the buttons, quick install with VPinMediaDB media downloaded from GitHub, add-on fetched in your own browser), `.\docs\demo-comptes.ps1` for accounts and direct download, and `.\docs\demo-ecran-navigateur.ps1` for the built-in browser screen (the cabinet's simulated screens: the window on each chosen role, the DMD unplugged; overview `*-borne.png`). Browser screen: `packages/core/src/browser-screen.ts` (tests `browser-screen.test.ts`), `app/src/browser-screen.ts`, choice in `app/src/configurations-menu.ts`. Manual tests: [docs/manual-tests.md](manual-tests.md), "Manual tests: finding a table".

## Download manager

One place to follow everything that is coming, from a site or a friend, the way a game console does: **Menu système (System menu) > Téléchargements (Downloads)** (at the root, between Médias (Media) and Borne (Cabinet): from the tables menu, Exit, the right flipper three times, then Start), also kept in **Menu système (System menu) > Tables > Téléchargements (Downloads)**. On the tables menu, a **pill** left of the gear sums it up ("2 en cours · 45 %" (2 downloading · 45%), "En attente" (Waiting), "1 bloqué" (1 blocked), "1 prêt" (1 ready), a single text at a time, by priority); a click opens it, Exit brings you back.

- **The list**: "En cours et en attente" (Downloading and waiting), then "Terminés et bloqués" (Finished and blocked), in the order they were requested. Each row gives its source (a friend, a site, VPinMediaDB, the update), its state as a word ("En cours" (Downloading), "En attente de Bruno" (Waiting for Bruno), "Nouvel essai à 22:31" (Next try at 22:31), "En pause" (Paused), "Prêt" (Ready), "Échec" (Failed)), what is received and what is left, its bar (blue downloading, amber waiting, green ready, red blocked; hatched with no known size), and the next step with its sign: **↩** the screen it takes you back to, **↻** when it resumes, **‖** paused, **!** what to do. Rows update in place: a row only changes section, and the list only gains or loses a row, on a gesture (opening, an action), and the cursor follows the download. At the bottom: "Tout mettre en pause" (Pause all) (or "Tout reprendre" (Resume all)), "Retirer les terminés" (Clear finished) (never the blocked ones), "Fichiers téléchargés" (Downloaded files) (the inventory, below) and "Retour" (Back).
- **A download's sheet**: its state, what is received (file n of N, rate, time left), its source, its resume, the screen it takes you back to and where it is stored. Actions depending on the state: **"Revenir à …" (Go back to …)** (finished: the original screen with what was received; in progress: the original screen, the download carries on), "Mettre en pause" (Pause), "Reprendre" (Resume), "Réessayer maintenant" (Try again now) (a site waiting for its next try), "Annuler le téléchargement" (Cancel the download) (after a confirmation, the cursor on "Garder le téléchargement" (Keep the download): what was received is deleted), "Retirer de la liste" (Remove from the list). A friend who is away: "Revenir à …" first, resuming is automatic.
- **Back to the original screen**: each download remembers the screen it was requested from, as data that survives a restart. A table's media received from a friend take you back to that table's "Médias du jeu" (Game media), the files in the draft, ready to save; a friend's table, or parts, to the add plan ("Annuler" (Cancel) goes back to "Parties du jeu" (Game parts) or to the friend's library); a waiting-list file to the waiting list or to its table's game parts; a VPinMediaDB media item to "Médias du jeu"; the update to "Mise à jour de Cyber Pinball" (Cyber Pinball update). If that screen no longer exists (table removed, friend deleted), a message says so, then the fallback: the library of the friend the download came from, otherwise the manager. A download that finishes while its transfer screen is open is handed over at once; otherwise a notification says so on the tables menu ("Téléchargement fini" (Download finished), see "Tables menu notifications"), a banner on a full screen ("Téléchargement terminé : …" (Download finished: …)), and the pill and the system menu entry keep it "ready" (red dot on the gear).
- **Resuming**: each file builds up in a partial file kept from one session to the next, and what resumes starts from the size of the partial on disk, never from a counter: nothing is lost, nothing arrives twice. A **friend** who goes offline puts the download "En attente de …" (Waiting for …); as soon as they are connected again (checked every three seconds), the cabinet asks them for the file from what it already has, they re-read and hash the beginning then send the rest. A **site page** (built-in browser, VPUniverse, VPForums…): Chromium resumes a short cut by itself; a long cut leaves the partial and the download's state (URLs followed, ETag, date), and the manager resumes it at the next try (15 s, 30 s, 1 min, 2 min, 5 min, 10 min, then every 15 min), in the same cookie session, after a restart too. A **direct URL** (VPinMediaDB, update, VPX, yt-dlp) asks for the rest (`Range`, `If-Range` header); if the site sends everything, the partial starts over from zero. What is followed by its screen (a VPinMediaDB plan, the update, VPX) keeps its partial: relaunched from that screen, it resumes there. The queue is written to `<données>\telechargements.json` at every state change (progress at most every two seconds): it survives closing Cyber Pinball and restarting the PC; what was running waits for its source at the next start and resumes by itself. A paused download never resumes by itself.
- **Verification**: a resumed file is verified in full, like a file received in one go. Between friends, the SHA-256 fingerprint sent at the end covers the whole file, and the receiver hashes the beginning it had before adding the rest; a media item also passes its content check. A direct URL: the index MD5 (VPinMediaDB), the manifest SHA-256 (update, VPX, yt-dlp). A wrong fingerprint after a resume (the kept beginning was damaged) restarts the file from zero, once; a wrong file is never handed over. A cabinet from before resuming sends everything: the partial starts over from zero.
- **Invariants** (tests): every download source in the application is listed and goes through the manager, and only `resumable.ts` writes an HTTP response body to disk (`downloadsources.test.ts`); an interrupted download (site cut, friend going offline in the middle of sending a table, simulated restart) resumes with nothing lost and nothing twice, a damaged beginning is caught at the end, a pause is never relaunched, every return screen is valid or has its fallback (`downloads-resume.test.ts`).
- **Code**: `packages/core/src/downloadqueue.ts` (queue, states, return screen and fallback, texts), `packages/core/src/downloadmanager.ts` (scheduling, resuming, writing the queue) and `packages/core/src/resumable.ts` (HTTP resume); sources in `app/src/download-sources.ts` (friend, direct URL) and `app/src/acquire-window.ts` (`siteTransport`); screen in `app/src/download-center.ts`, pill in `app/renderer/frontend.js`; demonstration `docs\demo-gestionnaire-telechargements.ps1` (Bruno goes offline in the middle of sending, then comes back at the next start), mock-up `docs/recherche/maquettes/gestionnaire-telechargements.cjs`.

## Tables menu notifications

On the tables menu, left of the gear, what happens on the network and in downloads shows without covering anything: the **friends online pill** (the friends icon, a green dot, the number; absent when no friend is online; a click opens "Mes amis" (My friends)) left of the downloads pill, and **one notification at a time**, discreet, that fades away on its own. On the desktop it sits on the brand's line, left of the pills, and stops before the playfield and backglass frames; on a cabinet the area is narrow, so it moves below the pills, aligned with them on the right, away from the wheel and the title. It never hides the table's media or the wheel.

- **What is announced**, most urgent first: a friend request received ("Demande d'ami : Zoé" (Friend request: Zoé), amber; a click opens "Demandes d'amis" (Friend requests)), a request accepted ("Zoé a accepté votre demande" (Zoé accepted your request)), a blocked download (the cause first: "Plus de place sur le disque · …" (No space left on the disk · …), red), a finished download ("Téléchargement fini" (Download finished), the table and where it came from, green; a click opens Downloads), a friend coming online ("Bruno vient de se connecter" (Bruno just came online), blue), a friend's record or badge (green), a friend starting a game ("Bruno lance une partie" (Bruno is starting a game), the table, blue). The words always say what happens; the colour only repeats it (Codex review, 29 October 2026: blue for information, green for success).
- **Never a stack**: a single notification on screen, about 6 seconds, then a pause before the next. What arrives together, or while another one is showing, is grouped by kind ("3 amis viennent de se connecter" (3 friends just came online), the names as detail); three kinds or more waiting together, back from a game for instance, make a single summary ("4 nouveautés" (4 new notifications), "1 demande d'ami · 1 téléchargement fini · …").
- **Never at the wrong moment**: nothing during loading or a game, in attract mode, in guest mode (neither pill nor notification), under a full screen (system menu, details page, message), the help, a short notice or the opening hours' shutdown warning; what arrives waits for the tables menu, then expires (10 minutes for a connection, 30 for a finished download, one hour for a friend request) or drops if it no longer applies (the friend left again, the request was answered, the download was opened). The same event is not repeated before its delay (a friend whose connection comes and goes: once every quarter of an hour). At startup, what is already there (friends, requests, finished downloads) is not news, nor are friends connecting in the first ten seconds: it is the cabinet arriving; the pill counts them. A friend just accepted is not announced as "just came online", nor are old records (more than a quarter of an hour) a friend sends again when reconnecting.
- **A single mechanism**: a finished download its screen did not take back is announced by the notification on the tables menu; on a full screen, by the usual short banner, and the notification does not repeat it afterwards.
- **Setting**: **Menu système (System menu) > Médias (Media) > "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract)** > "Notifications du menu" (Menu notifications): "affichées" (shown, the default), "compteur seul" (counter only: the friends pill, no messages) or "coupées" (off: neither pill nor messages; the downloads pill stays). Kept in `<données>\medias.json`, field `notices`.
- **Texts**: a 24 px title and a 20 px detail, each on one line: names and titles shortened at the source (40 and 56 characters at most), then to the width really left (the detail first loses its last segments, then its last words, with "…"; the start, which carries the meaning, stays).
- **Code**: the queue, rules, texts and snapshots in `packages/core/src/notices.ts` (`NOTICE_RULES`, `createNoticeCenter`, `socialNotices`, `downloadNotices`); the wiring in `app/src/menu-notices.ts` (network and download-queue snapshots, clock, redraw only when the notification changes) and `app/src/frontend.ts` (`canShow`); the rendering in `app/renderer/frontend.js` (`cornerBox`, `noticeToast`, `fitNotice`) and `frontend.css` (`.status-corner`, `.friends-pill`, `.notice-toast`). A new source only adds a kind to `NoticeKind`, its rule and texts, and its snapshot.
- **Tests**: `packages/core/src/test/notices.test.ts` (one at a time and on time, grouping, summary, priority, expiry, nothing when the screen does not allow it, delay before repeating, snapshots without the startup state, every kind translated and fitting its line with long names, the `canShow` condition read in `frontend.ts`, banner and notification without repetition, texts at the 20 px floor). Mock-up reviewed by Codex: `docs/recherche/maquettes/notifications-roue.cjs` (`.\docs\recherche\maquettes\notifications-roue.ps1`, images in `tmp\maquette-notifications`). Off-screen demonstration, with simulated friends (never the real network):

```powershell
.\docs\demo-notifications-roue.ps1 [-Langues fr, en] [-Ecrans borne, poste-deux-ecrans]
```

## Downloads and file picker

Everything Cyber Pinball downloads ends up under `<racine>\downloads`, sorted by origin: `acquisition\` (the "Récupérer" (Get) window and direct download, waiting list), `sites\<site>-<entry>\fichiers\` (community site media, recorded in `sites\recus.json`), `sites\web\…` (web search from a media item, `sites\web.json`), `vpinmediadb\` (VPinMediaDB files named after their MD5, deleted after a day), `cyberpinball\` (update installers) and, at the top, component archives (VPX), kept to reinstall offline. Indexes (`vpsdb.json`, `vpinmdb.json`…), records, site thumbnails and downloads in progress are internal: neither listed nor emptied.

- **"Fichiers téléchargés" (Downloaded files) screen** (Menu système (System menu) > Téléchargements (Downloads) > "Fichiers téléchargés"): each file, newest first (then by name or by size), with its kind (table, backglass, ROM, image, video, archive, installer), where it came from (site, "Récupérer", site media, web search, VPinMediaDB, update), its date, its size, and what it is for, with a word and a sign: **✓ Installed with** <table>, **! To install** (received by the waiting list, not installed yet), **○ Kept aside**, **i Received for** <media> (sites, web, VPinMediaDB), **✓** what was done with it when it was reused through the picker (log `downloads\journal.json`), **○ No known use**. A file opens (Start or click): its facts, its address, **what it contains** (archive listed on opening) and **the game** it most likely belongs to (the waiting list game that received it or one of whose items comes from the same page, otherwise the installed table whose title is in its name). Actions: **"Ajouter à <table>"** (Add to <table>: add plan targeting this table, the file stays where it is; a waiting list file "to install" is then marked installed), **"Ajouter à un autre jeu…"** (Add to another game…: any installed table, or any waiting list game, in the part the content says; unreadable content: "Quelle partie ?", Which part?), **"Installer comme nouvelle table"** (Install as a new table: when it contains a `.vpx`, or when we cannot tell) and "Mettre à la corbeille" (Move to the trash). "Afficher Téléchargements de Windows" (Show Windows Downloads) adds the last month's files from that folder, without opening File Explorer. **"Tout vider" (Empty all)** moves the whole area (never Windows Downloads) to the Cyber Pinball trash (`<racine>\trash\<date> Téléchargements`), after a confirmation whose cursor is on "Annuler" (Cancel) and which says what the waiting list loses: an item received but not installed is awaited again, a file kept aside is forgotten; an installed table no longer needs its file.
- **File picker**: wherever a file or folder is chosen on the PC (the "Ajouter une table" funnel, the path's "J'ai déjà le fichier" (I already have the file), the ROM library's "Ajouter" (Add), a media item's "Choisir un fichier…" (Choose a file…), "Chercher ailleurs…" (Look elsewhere…) to replace a waiting-list file, another frontend's folder for the media takeover), Cyber Pinball opens its own screen instead of the Windows dialog box, which opened on the main screen and could not be driven with the buttons. It shows where you are, with the flippers (choose), Start (open a folder, tick a file, or take it when only one is expected), Exit (parent folder, then cancel), the mouse (click, drag a file onto the window) and the keyboard (Tab, Enter, Esc). **Places**: Cyber Pinball's downloads (opened by default when they have what is wanted, with each file's origin and use), Windows Downloads, the import folder, the Desktop, then USB drives and disks (`Get-CimInstance Win32_LogicalDisk`, read again every 15 seconds: a plugged-in drive appears at the next opening). The content is **filtered** on what the screen expects ("Filtre : Fronton (.mp4, .png…)"); other files are counted, and "Afficher tous les fichiers" (Show all files) shows them, marked "Ne convient pas" (Does not fit). Several files can be ticked across folders ("Prendre les 2 fichiers choisis", Take the 2 chosen files); "Prendre ce dossier entier" (Take this whole folder) takes the open folder.
