Distinct states: empty folder, no fitting file, drive removed or access denied ("Réessayer", Try again). A file reused from the downloads area is noted in the log. On a desktop, three columns (places, content, moves); on the cabinet (portrait, rotated playfield), places as chips three across, then the content, then the moves in two columns. Cancelling brings back the screen you came from, cursor included.
- **Invariant**: a test (`packages/core/src/test/downloads.test.ts`) refuses any `showOpenDialog` or `showSaveDialog` in `app/src`.
- **Code**: `packages/core/src/downloads.ts` (inventory, origin, link to the waiting list, to site records and to the VPinMediaDB index, log, trash) and `packages/core/src/filepicker.ts` (places, drives, filters, folder content, going up), tests in `packages/core/src/test/downloads.test.ts`; screens in `app/src/downloads-screen.ts` and `app/src/file-picker.ts` (`lib-picker` and `lib-downloads` layout, `app/renderer/library.css`). Mock-ups: design system, "Téléchargements, borne" and "Sélecteur de fichiers, bureau / borne". Off-screen demonstration: `.\docs\demo-telechargements.ps1` (sandbox, USB drive simulated by `CYBERPINBALL_LECTEURS`, landscape, rotated cabinet and portrait, French and English). Manual tests: [docs/manual-tests.md](manual-tests.md), "Downloads and file picker".

## What's missing to play: guided path

A table that will not start almost always has a cause Cyber Pinball already knows (missing ROM, VPX not installed…), but the doctor often stopped at the finding ("ROM « t2_l8 » absente: put the file in the import folder") without saying where to find the file. The **"Ce qu'il manque pour jouer" (What's missing to play)** screen leads all the way to the game: each step says where it stands and offers the next move, one press of Start away (or one click).

- **What it takes to play**: the `.vpx` file and, if its script asks for one, the ROM, when it is missing or unreadable for PinMAME (not linked, misplaced, scattered, old name without an alias). A ROM found through PinMAME's shared folder, or provided by an alias to a neighbouring revision, is enough. Nothing else holds "Jouer" (Play) back: DMD content, `.directb2s` backglass, PuP-Pack, media, DMD colours, alternative sounds, tidying the table are offered, never required. A single rule, in the core: [packages/core/src/playable.ts](../packages/core/src/playable.ts) (`blocksPlay`, `playBlockers`, `requiredForPlay`). The doctor follows it (only ROM findings have the "error" severity; `playSeverity`, applied by `diagnose`, brings any other finding down to "warning"), and so do the path, the catalog's "Ce qu'il faut" ("indispensable", essential: the table and the ROM), the quick install, the details screen and the "Fichiers de la table" (Table files) inventory (`romBlockers`, which no longer say "ROM missing" when PinMAME finds it through its shared folder). Tests: `playable.test.ts` (every doctor rule, even marked "error") and the invariant checked on every trapped table (`doctor-traps.test.ts`).
- **Where to find it**: at the top of the details screen when the ROM is missing, or when a path is under way ("Reprendre : Étape 3 sur 4", Resume: Step 3 of 4); after a game that failed ("Start : voir ce qu'il manque pour jouer", Start: see what's missing to play); in the diagnosis, as soon as a finding keeps the table from playing; in the waiting list ("Reprendre : ce qu'il manque pour jouer <table>", Resume: what's missing to play <table>). Missing DMD content is offered on the details screen after "Jouer" ("Pour aller plus loin", Going further), never at the top.
- **Steps**: first the path up to "Jouer" (Play), numbered: "Borne et VPX" (Cabinet and VPX), "Table" (the .vpx file, and the ROM its script asks for), "ROM <name>" (if the script uses PinMAME), then "Jouer". At the top, "Étape n sur N" (Step n of N, the path only), a bar with one segment per step and "2 étapes réglées · 1 indispensable à faire · 1 amélioration proposée" (2 steps settled · 1 essential to do · 1 suggested improvement). Then **"Pour aller plus loin" (Going further)**: optional, without a number (a "+" sign) or a place in the count, never essential: "Rangement de la table" (Tidying the table: table outside its folder, script replaced by a `.vbs`, crash report), "Contenu du DMD" (DMD content, if the script loads a FlexDMD or UltraDMD folder), "Fronton" (Backglass, if the cabinet has a backglass screen), "PuP-Pack" (if the script loads one), "Affichage" (Display, if a table-specific setting needs fixing). Each step has its state, a word and a sign: "Réglé" (Settled), "À faire" (To do), "Conseillé" (Advised), "Attendu" (Waiting), "À installer" (To install), "Facultatif" (Optional), "En attente" (Pending), "Non vérifié" (Not checked). The current one is the first essential step not settled, otherwise "Jouer" ("Tout est prêt pour jouer", All set to play). Its moves come first, the advised one lit; then the first move of every other open step, the path then the improvements (a backglass, the DMD content), "Revérifier" (Check again) and "Diagnostic complet" (Full diagnosis).
- **DMD content**: the folder a FlexDMD or UltraDMD script loads (`.ProjectFolder = "./TheGooniesDMD/"`) is optional. Without it, the DMD's place keeps a default DMD: during a game, the ROM's, drawn by VPX's PinMAME and ScoreView plugins (§ "VPX plugins"); in the menu, Cyber Pinball's dot-matrix display (`fallback.js`). VPX 10.8.1's FlexDMD plugin hands `Nothing` back to the script for a missing image (`plugins/flexdmd/actors/Image.cpp`, commit af26b2d93): the DMD the script draws stays empty, and a script that does not expect it stops on "Type mismatch" (VPW's Goonies). The failed game then says so ("Le script s'est arrêté sur le DMD qu'il dessine…", The script stopped on the DMD it draws…), and the step offers "Récupérer la table d'origine" (Get the original table) or "J'ai l'archive de la table" (I have the table's archive); a folder emptied loose at the root of the table is put back in place.
- **Missing ROM**: Cyber Pinball looks in the VPS database (cached, no network) for ROM files of that name, the table's own game first, then the other revisions of the same game (`romSources`). "Récupérer la ROM t2_l8" (Get the t2_l8 ROM) adds it to the waiting list, attached to the table, and opens its page in the "Récupérer" window (or downloads it directly if "Téléchargement direct" (Direct download) is on and the account signed in): you sign in and download yourself there. Cyber Pinball never downloads on the user's behalf from a forum that needs an account (§ "Finding a table" above). While waiting: "Rouvrir la page" (Reopen the page) and "Chercher dans Téléchargements" (Search Downloads). The received file ("Récupérer" window, watched Downloads folder) turns the step into "À installer" (To install): "Installer le fichier reçu" (Install the received file) shows the add plan (ROM library, link in the table), then comes back to the path, which reads the table again. A neighbouring revision already on the cabinet is offered second ("Jouer avec t2_l6, révision voisine", Play with t2_l6, neighbouring revision, with the warning from § "ROM library"), or first if the VPS database gives no link; a neighbouring revision given by the database comes last. Without a link: "Chercher t2_l8 dans le catalogue" (Search the catalog for t2_l8); always: "J'ai déjà le fichier" (I already have the file: drop it on the screen, or pick it on this PC), which also goes through the plan and comes back to the path.
- **Resuming later**: the state is read again at every opening (doctor's findings, waiting list, VPS database). The steps already shown are remembered in `<données>\parcours.json`, with the last position: a step settled since stays in the list, ticked, and "Étape n sur N" keeps its meaning. The path is forgotten when the game is started from it.
- **Code**: plan in [packages/core/src/journey.ts](../packages/core/src/journey.ts) (`planJourney`, `romSources`, `journeyChoices`, remembered paths), tests in `packages/core/src/test/journey.test.ts` (trapped tables analysed by the doctor, from a missing ROM up to "Jouer"); screen in `app/src/journey.ts`, rendering `renderJourney` (`app/renderer/library.js`); catalog entry points (`fetchFor`, `reopen`, `scanDownloads`, `openSearch`) in `app/src/catalog.ts`. Mockups: `EcranParcours` and `EcranParcoursBureau` in the design system. Off-screen demo: `.\docs\demo-parcours.ps1`. Manual tests: [docs/manual-tests.md](manual-tests.md), "What's missing to play".

**What keeps a game from working**, what the doctor said about it, and what the path does:

| Cause | Doctor's finding | Before the path | Step and move in the path |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VPX not installed | none: the script is not read, the ROM not checked | "Aucune table" (No table) message, VPX installation | "Borne et VPX": "Installer Visual Pinball X" (Install Visual Pinball X); the ROM waits ("En attente", Pending) |
| Cabinet not set up | none | wizard on first start | "Borne et VPX": "Configurer la borne" (Set up the cabinet) |
| Table loose in the tables folder | `outsideFolder` | move it | "Rangement de la table" (Tidying the table), going further: the same fix |
| Unreadable script, replaced by a `.vbs`, or adapted for an older version | `scriptUnreadable`, `scriptOverride`, `scriptPatchStale` | `.vbs` to the recycle bin; Cyber Pinball's `.vbs` rewritten from the new script | "ROM" not checked; the `.vbs` under "Rangement de la table" |
| Missing ROM | `romMissing`, without a fix | "put the file in the import folder" | "ROM": its page in the VPS database, "Récupérer" window, awaited then received for the table, plan, back to the path |
| Neighbouring revision on the cabinet | `romAlias` (blocking) | write the alias | offered second, after the exact ROM |
| Only another revision in the VPS database | none | nothing | "Récupérer t2_l6, révision voisine" (Get t2_l6, neighbouring revision), then the alias |
| Old VPinMAME name | `romAliasHistoric` | write the alias, or "bond.zip is needed too" | the fix, or the real ROM to get |
| ROM in the library, not linked | `romNotLinked` | link it | the same fix |
| ROM elsewhere in the table folder | `romMisplaced` | put it away | the same fix |
| Alias no longer needed | `romAliasObsolete` | remove the line | "Conseillé" (Advised) |
| Missing DMD folder | `dmdFolderMissing` (warning) | gather the loose files, otherwise "add it"; it used to be blocking | "Contenu du DMD", going further: the fix, or the original table to get ("J'ai l'archive de la table", I have the table's archive); "Jouer" right away |
| Missing or misnamed backglass | `backglassMissing`, `backglassMisnamed` | information, or rename | "Fronton", going further: rename, or the backglass that suits the screens |
| PuP-Pack not found, one folder too deep, switched off by the script, option | `pupMissing`, `pupNested`, `pupDisabledByScript`, `pupOptionNotApplied`, `pupOptionUnsuited` | rename, move up, switch the pack on in a `.vbs`, apply the option | "PuP-Pack", going further: the fix, or the pack to get |
| Framing or screens forced by the table | `manualView`, `tableWindows` | remove the setting | "Affichage", going further |
| Screens changed, configuration to choose | none (seen at launch) | message before the game | outside the path: the launch opens the configuration choice |
| Another cabinet menu open | none (seen at launch) | "PinUP Popper est ouvert" (PinUP Popper is open) | outside the path |
| Joystick buttons VPX did not see | none (seen after the game) | redo the "Boutons" (Buttons) step | outside the path |

## Game page: its files

A table's game page (Start on a table in the menu) shows its statistics on one line and a **bounded summary** of its files: one line per kind present (table, backglass, ROM, DMD content, PuP videos, DMD colours, alternative sounds, music, own settings, media), three names at most per line ("t2_l3, t2_l4 and 4 more"), a "Absents" (Missing) line for the missing kinds and a "À vérifier" (To check) line (misnamed backglass, script replaced by a `.vbs`, crash report). Upright (cabinet with a turned playfield, or portrait screen), the information keeps its place and the actions go on two columns; the action list scrolls if it overflows. On a cabinet, the action list used to take the whole height: with many files, only the first statistics line was still visible.

**"Fichiers de la table"** (Table files) (game page action, detail "66 files · 940 KB · 3 to check"): the whole table folder, grouped by kind, the table and its backglasses first. Each file is a line to choose there, with its full name (it wraps), its folder inside the table's, its state and its size: "cette table" (this table) or "autre version du dossier" (other version in the folder), backglass "chargé par VPX avec cette table" (loaded by VPX with this table) or "mal nommé : VPX ne le chargera pas" (misnamed: VPX will not load it), ROM "lien vers la bibliothèque de ROM" (link to the ROM library), "copie à part : la bibliothèque a aussi cette ROM" (separate copy: the library also has this ROM) or "propre à la table" (own to the table), "demandée par le script" (asked for by the script), PinMAME save and settings, override script, crash report. Folders filed as one block (a PuP-Pack, alternative sounds, DMD colours, FlexDMD content, menu media) are one line, with their number of files; music is listed file by file. What the script expects and is missing (ROM, DMD folder) appears at the top of its kind, "absente" (missing). With the buttons, the flippers go through the list, which scrolls to keep the chosen file visible; Start opens the file's **detail**: type, full folder, size, date, state, and for a linked ROM the library file it shares; "Bibliothèque de ROM" (ROM library) and "Diagnostiquer et réparer" (Diagnose and fix) lead there when useful. In the common tables folder, only the table's companion files (same name) belong to it. The inventory is read when the game page opens, not on each button press.

Code: [packages/core/src/tablecontents.ts](../packages/core/src/tablecontents.ts) (inventory, tests `tablecontents.test.ts`), screens in [app/src/table-files.ts](../app/src/table-files.ts), summary in `buildSheet` (`app/src/library.ts`), layout `.lib-sheet`, `.lib-files` and `.lib-facts` in `app/renderer/library.css`; lines to choose (`Row.pick`) do not repeat their action in the list. Off-screen demo: `.\docs\demo-fiche-fichiers.ps1` (few and many files, long names, landscape, turned cabinet and portrait, French and English; the report checks that the chosen file is always fully visible).

## Game parts

A game is made of **parts**: the table (`.vpx`), the backglass (`.directb2s`), the ROM, the PuP-Pack, the DMD colours (Serum, VNI), the remastered sounds, the music, the point of view (`.pov`), its own settings (`.ini` named like the table), the topper and the menu media. The same parts are used everywhere: the waiting list (`NeedKind`, `PART_KINDS` in `packages/core/src/acquisition.ts`), the page of an installed table (`GAME_PARTS`, `installedParts` in `packages/core/src/gameparts.ts`) and Downloads. Before 27 October 2026, a file could only go to the item that was expected: a PuP-Pack arriving with the table had nowhere to go, and the page of an installed table offered no way to add one.

- **On reception**: each file goes to the part its content says, added to the game if needed, several parts at once for a complete archive; a file of another nature never blocks installing what is ready (see [Adding a table](#adding-a-table-the-funnel-the-catalog-récupérer-waiting-list)).
- **"Parties du jeu" (Game parts)** (page of a table, after "Médias du jeu", Game media; closed to guests, door `sheet.parts`): one line per part, **Présent** (Present: files and size), **Reçu, à installer** (Received, to install), **Attendu** (Expected: waiting list), **Inutile** (Not needed: ROM of a table that uses none) or missing ("2 sur VPS", 2 on VPS, when the database knows some); then "Chercher sur le web…" (Search the web…) and **"Jeu VPS : …"** (VPS game). The VPS game is the one the table points to (`tableGame`, `packages/core/src/gameparts.ts`): its origin or its `.info`, otherwise the only game its media come from, otherwise the media's deep search (name written in the table, file, folder, ROM, PuP-Pack, author), kept when it is certain (0.8 at least, well ahead of the next one, or confirmed by the media). Not certain: "Jeu VPS : à choisir" (VPS game: to choose) shows the leads and "Chercher un titre…" (Search a title…); the choice is kept in the `.info` (`Info.VPSId`). Without a game, everything still works: the sites and the web search by the table's title. On 27 October 2026, The Goonies table ("VPW 2021" in its `.info`, "Original" at VPS) said "unknown to the VPS database": only the `.info` identifier was read.
- **A part: its proposals, from every source, at once**: opening a part starts the pre-search on all its sources (`partSources`) and each one redraws the screen when it arrives: the VPS database files ("Fichiers de VPS : voir les N au choix", VPS files: see the N to choose from, the file gallery: preview, popularity, sort), the already downloaded files that fit, **VPForums** (signed in: otherwise "Se connecter à VPForums…", Sign in to VPForums…) and **VPUniverse** (the part's categories, `PART_SITE_NODES`: PuP-Packs 120, backglasses 33 and 189, colours 173, 101 and 174, altsound 113, ROM 15, POV 68, tables 82, music 112, topper 160 and 93, media 72). The proposals are tiles of the media gallery (thumbnail, site, popularity, "Autre jeu", Other game, last); one line per source says what it gave ("recherche…", searching…, "3 propositions", "rien pour ce jeu", nothing for this game, "connectez-vous…", sign in…, wait imposed by the site). Start on a proposal: its page opens in "Récupérer" (Get: VPS or site), the received file is filed for this table, then "Installer ce qui est reçu" (Install what was received); an already downloaded file opens the add plan. **"Chercher sur le web…"** (Search the web…) is always there. Then: "Tous les fichiers téléchargés…" (All downloaded files…, those that do not fit included), **"Chercher un fichier…"** (Look for a file…: Cyber Pinball's file picker), "Installer ce qui est reçu" (Install what was received), "Options du PuP-Pack" (PuP-Pack options), "Médias du jeu" (Game media) for the media, **"Réaffecter à une autre table"** (Reassign to another table: its files are moved there, named like it for a backglass, a point of view or own settings; what it had instead goes to the trash) and **"Retirer de la table"** (Remove from the table: confirmation, cursor on "Annuler", Cancel; Cyber Pinball trash, `<root>\trash\<date> Parties du jeu`; a library ROM stays there; removing the PuP-Pack also removes Cyber Pinball's `.vbs` that turned it on). Every addition goes through the add plan, **targeting this table**: a pack whose name does not say the game, or a `.vpx` (another version), goes to it; "Annuler" (Cancel) goes back to the part's screen.
- **Code**: [app/src/game-parts.ts](../app/src/game-parts.ts) (screens), `routeReceived`, `repairWaitList`, `contentParts`, `partFits` in [packages/core/src/acquisition.ts](../packages/core/src/acquisition.ts), `target` of `planImport` in [packages/core/src/tableimport.ts](../packages/core/src/tableimport.ts). Tests: `packages/core/src/test/parties.test.ts` (two files from one page, each nature in its part, complete archive, nothing blocking, the 27/10/2026 cabinet filed by itself, targeted plan, present parts). Off-screen demo: `docs\demo-parties-du-jeu.ps1` (captures in `tmp\demo-parties-du-jeu\captures`).

## Never stuck: the web from every adding screen

Wherever something is added or chosen, a way out to the web: **"Chercher sur le web…"** (Search the web…) opens the "Récupérer" (Get) window on a pre-filled free search (DuckDuckGo; engines to choose in the banner; "vpinball" and the part's word added to the title, `partWebQuery`). What is downloaded there goes to the screen's game, in the part its content says, added if needed, then "Installer ce qui est reçu" (Install what was received); without a game (funnel, catalog, waiting list, Downloads, ROM library), the file opens the add plan of a new table. On 27 October 2026, a missing part without a VPS file only offered Downloads and a file from the PC: one got stuck.

- **Screens** (`ADD_SCREENS`, `packages/core/src/addscreens.ts`): the "Ajouter une table" (Add a table) funnel, the catalog, the page of a VPS database game, "Ce qu'il faut" (What it takes), "… : au choix" (… : choose one), "Récupérés et attendus" (Received and expected), a waiting list item and its replacement, "Parties du jeu" (Game parts), a part and its downloaded files, Downloads and "Ajouter à un jeu" (Add to a game), "Ce qu'il manque pour jouer" (What's missing to play), "Médias du jeu" (Game media), a medium's screen ("Chercher moi-même sur le web…", Search the web myself…), its proposals, VPinMediaDB and the ROM library. The action carries its screen (`Action.web`); in a screen without icons, it has none either.
- **VPUniverse**: its search ignores words of three letters or fewer, and an "and" search then returns nothing ("goonies never say die"): they are removed from the address (`siteSearchText`), for media as for parts. A visitor gets only one search every 90 s or so: the "Please wait 88 seconds…" page is recognised (`reason: 'flood'`), never taken for "no results"; a site's searches run one after another, and the refused one starts again after the announced wait (150 s at most), the site's line saying so.
- **Invariants**: `packages/core/src/test/addscreens.test.ts` refuses an adding or choice screen without "Chercher sur le web…" (registry, and adding screen titles in the code), checks that each part is searched everywhere (VPS, Downloads, VPForums, VPUniverse, the web), that The Goonies is recognised with no identifier in its `.info`, and reads two VPUniverse witness pages (`packages/core/test-pages/vpuniverse-parties.html`, `vpuniverse-attente.html`). Off-screen demo: `docs\demo-parties-du-jeu.ps1` (real sites, waits included).

## ROM library

A ROM is often used by several tables (versions, mods of the same machine). Each ROM is stored **only once**, in `<racine>\pinmame\roms`, and each table that plays it gets a **hard link** to it in its own `pinmame\roms` (where VPX 10.8.1 looks first): the same file on disk, taking no extra space. The folder was not invented: `<racine>\pinmame` is already the shared PinMAME folder that the sync suggests for `[Plugin.PinMAME] PinMAMEPath` as soon as it contains `roms`; a table without a link therefore still finds its ROM there, as a fallback, **provided it has no `pinmame\roms` folder**: as soon as that folder exists, the PinMAME plugin looks only there (`PinMAMEPlugin.cpp`, lines 436-477, commit af26b2d93), and the doctor reports it as blocking when the ROM is missing from it. Code: [packages/core/src/romlibrary.ts](../packages/core/src/romlibrary.ts).

**Per-table ROM aliases** ([packages/core/src/romalias.ts](../packages/core/src/romalias.ts)): the plugin also reads `alias.txt` in the table's PinMAME folder (`<table>\pinmame`), one line "name requested by the script,game loaded", even before the ROM. The doctor uses it in three ways:

- **Neighboring revision**: the requested ROM is missing, but another revision of the same game (`t2_l6` for `t2_l8`) is in the table or the library; on request, it links it and writes the alias. The warning says so: lamps, coils or switches may differ, settings and high scores (NVRAM) are not carried over, and remastered sounds and DMD colors stored under the other name are no longer found. As long as the alias is in use, a reminder stays in the diagnosis.
- **Old VPinMAME names** ("goldfinger" for "bond", 119 names taken from PinMAME's `release/VPMAlias.txt`, in [packages/core/src/vpmalias.ts](../packages/core/src/vpmalias.ts)): VPinMAME translated them on its own, but the VPX 10.8.1 plugin no longer knows them; the doctor writes the line, and says whether the real ROM is missing too.
- **Alias no longer needed**: the requested ROM has arrived, but the alias takes precedence over it; "Revenir à la ROM demandée" (Back to the requested ROM) removes the line (and the file, if no line is left).

- **Same volume required**: a hard link only connects two folders on the same disk (NTFS). A table on a different volume from the root (junction to another disk, FAT32 or exFAT drive) gets a **copy**, and the plan says so.
- **Identity**: two files with the same name are the same ROM if their content is the same: the fingerprint covers the chips in the zip (path, CRC and size of each file), so the same ROM zipped by two tools (different compression, different dates) remains a single version; a file that is not a readable zip is compared byte for byte (SHA-1). Two different contents under the same name: the most recent (file date) is kept, or the one chosen in the plan ("Garder plutôt…" (Keep instead…)); the other goes to the recycle bin. An identical copy replaced by a link is deleted (its content stays in the library, byte for byte: this is checked just before); any other replaced file goes to the recycle bin.
- **Adding**: an imported ROM enters the library, then is linked into **every installed table that plays it**: those whose script requests it without having any copy, those that already have a copy, and the table the import is meant for. A new table whose ROM is already in the library gets it without importing anything more. A ROM that no table is expecting yet is stored anyway, for the table to come. Scripts of tables not yet read are read before the plan (VPX `-ExtractVBS`, about one second each, then cached; if VPX does not return the script, the one written in the `.vpx` is read without it, unless the script is protected).
- **ROM recognised by its content** ([packages/core/src/romzip.ts](../packages/core/src/romzip.ts)): PinMAME (VPinMAME as well as the VPX 10.8.1 plugin) only reads a ROM as `<nom>.zip`, **its chips at the root of the zip**. An archive is a ROM when it contains nothing but chips (no table, image, video, sound or other archive; `.txt` notes are tolerated), all in one folder: at the root, or in a subfolder (`t2_l8.zip › t2_l8 › t2_l8.rom, t2_u14.l3…`, like many downloaded ROMs). It is recognised everywhere: a zip on its own, a zip inside a table's package (zip, 7z, rar), a ROM as 7z or rar, a folder of unpacked chips (`roms\t2_l8\`). Its name comes, in this order, from a ROM requested by a table's script and carried by the archive or a folder (case included: `T2_L8.ZIP` becomes `t2_l8.zip`), from a known ROM name (VPS database, old VPinMAME names), from a chip named like a requested ROM (`t2_l8.rom` in "Terminator 2 ROM.zip"), from the only requested ROM of the same family as the chips (`t2_u14.l3` → `t2_l8`), and finally from the archive name. A ROM whose chips are in a folder is **rebuilt as a flat zip** (chips copied as they are, without recompression, dated like the original archive) before entering the library; the plan says so ("ROM t2_l8 reconnue dans le dossier « t2_l8 »" (ROM t2_l8 recognised in the "t2_l8" folder)), as it does for a rename. Its files are never stored one by one in a table's folder.
- **Tidying up** (system menu, Exit in the menu: "Ranger les ROM dans la bibliothèque" (Move ROMs into the library)): for ROMs from before the library. The plan shows, ROM by ROM, the one that enters the library, the copies replaced by a link, the links added, the versions set aside and the space freed; nothing moves until "Ranger" (Tidy up). The summary gives the space taken by ROMs measured before and after.
- **Overall view** (system menu: "Bibliothèque de ROM" (ROM library)), with the cabinet buttons: each ROM, the tables that use it and its size; the ROMs to tidy up; **unused** ROMs (no table has it or expects it), with the recycle bin offered one by one or for all; ROMs **expected but missing**, with "Ajouter" (Add). Disk space counts each physical file once and states what the links save. Only ROMs in `pinmame\roms` folders are concerned: `.zip` files stored elsewhere (FlexDMD fonts, for example) are not ROMs.
- **Doctor**: "ROM pas liée dans la table" (ROM not linked in the table) (it is in the library, not in `pinmame\roms`: link it) and "Copie de ROM au lieu d'un lien" (ROM copy instead of a link) (replace it with a link; for a different version, use the library one, the other goes to the recycle bin). The table's details screen marks a linked ROM with "(bibliothèque)" ((library)).
- **Scattered ROM, ROM in a subfolder** (repairing what versions up to 0.1.3-essai.1 left behind: they unpacked a ROM zip with a subfolder and stored its chips at the root of the table): "ROM « t2_l8 » en fichiers éparpillés dans la table" (ROM "t2_l8" scattered as loose files in the table) spots the chips of the requested ROM left at the root of the table (same name as the ROM or same family: `t2_l8.rom`, `t2_u14.l3`…; never `.vgm`, `.ini` or `.log`) or in a folder named after it; "Refaire t2_l8.zip dans la bibliothèque et le lier" (Rebuild t2_l8.zip in the library and link it) rebuilds the flat zip, stores it in the library, links it in `pinmame\roms` and moves the chips to the recycle bin. If the ROM is already there (library, link, alias), all that is left is moving these files to the recycle bin. "ROM illisible : ses fichiers sont dans un sous-dossier du zip" (ROM unreadable: its files are in a subfolder of the zip) spots such a zip put by hand in `pinmame\roms`, and "ROM mal rangée" (Misplaced ROM) a subfolder zip elsewhere in the table: both rebuild it flat. The "Ce qu'il manque pour jouer" (What's missing to play) journey offers these fixes on the spot, before any search.

Deleting a table only sends its link to the recycle bin: the ROM stays in the library, and the overall view then offers it as unused if no table plays it anymore.

## PuP-Packs

Cyber Pinball targets **VPX 10.8.1 plugin mode**: the PUP plugin shipped with VPX replaces PinUP Player, so there is nothing to install or register (decision of October 20, 2026; classic mode and DOF will come later). The plugin looks for the pack in `<dossier de la table>\pupvideos\<nom>`, case-insensitively, where `<nom>` is the value the script passes to `PuPlayer.B2SInit` or `Init`, or failing that the ROM being played. The full research, with references to the VPX code, is in `docs/recherche/pup-packs.md`.

**When adding** ([packages/core/src/puppack.ts](../packages/core/src/puppack.ts), [tableimport.ts](../packages/core/src/tableimport.ts)), a pack (a folder containing `screens.pup`, `playlists.pup`, `triggers.pup`, or options that contain them) is:

- **named** the way the script loads it: name passed to `B2SInit` (a literal, or a variable such as `pGameName`, `cPuPPack`), then the ROM, then the name passed to `Init`. A pack already correctly named keeps its name; a pack named otherwise ("Monster Bash PuP-Pack v2") takes the expected name. Without a readable script (VPX missing), it keeps its folder name and the plan points this out;
- **flattened**: `rom\rom\…`, or an archive folder that contains only the pack (a readme next to it does not get in the way);
- **cleaned**: `._*`, `.DS_Store`, `__MACOSX` and 0-byte MP4 files are not installed;
- **replaced entirely** if it already exists: the old folder goes to the recycle bin, never a mix;
- **configured**: the screen layout option is applied by copying its files, **without ever running a `.bat`**.

**Options.** Published packs store their variants in `PuP-Pack_Options\<option>\` and ship one `.bat` per option at the root, for example `Option 2 - FullDMD on Backglass.bat`:

```bat
rem echo off
xcopy /y "PuP-Pack_Options\Option 2 - FullDMD on Backglass\*"
del /Q PuPDesktopPunch.exe
del /Q PuPInit.bat
```

Every `.bat` whose name mentions an option (or that copies files) is **read, not run**. Recognized: `copy` and `xcopy` of a folder, a pattern (`*`, `*.pup`) or a file of the pack to its root or one of its folders (`/s`, `/e` to recurse), `del`/`erase` of files at the root (they go to the recycle bin), and anything with no effect: `rem`, `::`, `echo`, `@echo off`, `pause`, `cls`, `exit`, `title`, `cd`/`pushd` to the `.bat` folder (`%~dp0`, `.`), `>nul`. Anything else makes the analysis fail: absolute path, `..`, variable (`%PUPDIR%`), `start`, `if`, `for`, `call`, `set`, `move`, `ren`, `rd`, chaining (`&`, `|`), redirection to a file, copy with no matching file. Option folders with no readable `.bat` (packs shipped with only `PuP-Pack_Options`, like those applied by hand on Linux) also become options. Classic-mode tool `.bat` files (`PuPInit.bat`, `configdisplays.bat`) are ignored.

**Choosing the option**, automatically, based on the cabinet model: the option name ("Topper", "FullDMD on Backglass", "2 Screen", "Desktop", "16x9", "4x3", "AIO", "Stream"…) and then its `screens.pup` (screen 0 on: topper; screen 5 or 1 inside the backglass through CustomPos: DMD in the backglass) are compared with the assigned roles (backglass, DMD, topper) and with the aspect ratios of the DMD (real 4:1 or FullDMD 16:9) and of the backglass. A desktop or all-in-one cabinet option is never picked on a cabinet. If the choice is not clear-cut, if the cabinet is not configured, or if a `.bat` could not be read, the "Quelle option du PuP-Pack ?" (Which PuP-Pack option?) screen opens before the plan, showing the rejected `.bat` and the reason; from the plan, "Changer l'option du PuP-Pack" (Change the PuP-Pack option) reopens it. The chosen option and the pack's original name are kept in the table's `.info` (`CyberPinball.pup.<nom>`, other fields are preserved): when the pack is updated, the same option is reused without asking.

**DMD frame on the backglass.** Many packs have neither a DMD screen (1) nor a FullDMD screen (5), but a screen placed on the backglass whose name or playlist mentions DMD (*Star Trek LE*: screen 13 "BGdmdText", at 26 %, 79 %, over 48 % × 21 % of the backglass). When it is on, the pack plays its own animations there: the real DMD stays on its own screen (option for a cabinet with a DMD). When it is off, the frame stays empty so the DMD can be placed there (option for a two-screen cabinet). With no screen 1, no screen 5 and no frame, nothing about the DMD is inferred from `screens.pup`: a pack without a DMD screen does not mean "cabinet without a DMD" (rule fixed on 24 October 2026: the option made for a four-screen cabinet ranked below the one with the empty frame). The ROM's DMD cannot show inside a pack's frame: the PUP plugin, which then draws the backglass window, never draws the DMD (`docs/recherche/pup-packs.md`).

**Changing the option after installation**: table details screen > "PuP-Pack", offered as soon as one of the table's packs has options (`tablePupPacks`, `packages/core/src/doctor.ts`). The screen shows the applied option, the one that suits the cabinet, and every option with what it targets (topper, DMD on its own screen or in the backglass, FullDMD, aspect ratios). Start applies the chosen option: its files are copied, the files they replace and those its `.bat` deletes go to the trash (`trash\<date> PuP-Pack <folder>`: the previous state, to go back to it), and no `.bat` is ever run (`pupOptionOps`). If one of the option's files is missing from the pack, nothing is touched and the message names it (`missingSources`). After the copy, the pack is read again: "appliquée" (applied) is only shown if the option is recognised in place, otherwise the message says what is missing (`checkAppliedOption`). The choice is remembered in the `.info` (`option`, and `chosen: "user"`: the doctor no longer questions it) and applies from the next game; "Jouer" (Play) is offered on the same screen to see it. On the details screen, the "PuP-Pack" line turns orange when another option suits the cabinet better. "Pour cette borne" (For this cabinet) names the recommended option and the cabinet as configured (backglass, FullDMD or 4:1 DMD, topper); when two options are equally good, it names both, to be tried. A list taller than the screen shows a rule on the edge behind which entries remain, at the top as at the bottom, and the selected entry is brought whole into view when the selection changes (`markOverflow`, `app/renderer/library.js`, every list of the management screens). **A list's position only follows the user's gestures** (flippers, Tab, click, mouse wheel): a screen redrawn by an update (download progress, "Attente du site · 27 s" (Waiting for the site · 27 s), state of a received file) puts every list back where it was, without bringing the selected item back into view; the proposals can be browsed with the wheel while a card downloads. Only `app/renderer/scroll-keeper.js` scrolls a list (menu and VPX settings): it records every scrolled list before the render and restores it after, and only brings the selection into view if it changed or another screen opens (which starts from the top); a list whose content changes in nature carries its own key (`data-scroll-key`, details of a VPX setting) and starts from the top. What is on screen does not move either: the selected item, if it was in view, stays at the same place in its list when an update inserts something above it (a site's "Non connecté" (Not signed in) banner at the top of the proposals), and if it was whole and grows ("Choisi, à enregistrer" (Chosen, to be saved)), the list moves just enough for it to stay whole. A screen opened directly on an item further down (media received from a friend) keeps it whole as long as no gesture has followed, even when the cards' images load afterwards. These positions are measured in the list's layout (`offsetTop`), never on screen: a cabinet's playfield is rotated and `getBoundingClientRect` measures the other axis there (a selection that overflowed passed for whole, 28 October 2026). October 2026, The Matrix's "Chargement : propositions" (Loading: proposals): every progress tick redrew the list then brought the downloading card back into it. Never a fade: it made the last visible line look disabled; the layout guard reports a fade on a scrolling list and a selection that is not whole in its list, `defilement.test.ts` rejects a fade in the stylesheets. A list on its own (system menu, sections) starts just below the title, at the same height from one screen to the next, centred horizontally only. Off-screen demo: `.\docs\demo-defilement.ps1 [-Langues fr, en] [-Ecrans borne, poste-deux-ecrans]` (system menu sections, then "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) walked down to the bottom and back up, one capture per key, in `tmp\demo-defilement`).

**Recognising the option in place** (`appliedOption`, [puppack.ts](../packages/core/src/puppack.ts)). Everything the option copies must be at the root, identical, and what its `.bat` deletes must be absent; only the preview images placed at the root (`Option 2.JPG`, for PinUP Popper) do not count. The `.pup` files alone are not enough: on *Guardians of the Galaxy* (`gotg_2020`), options 0 "3 Screen Desktop" and 2 "3 Screen FullDMD" have the same `screens.pup`, `playlists.pup` and `triggers.pup`; only `Option Selected.txt` and the desktop tools (`PuPDesktopPunch.exe`, `PuPInit.bat`) tell them apart. Until 26 October 2026, the first option with the same `.pup` files won: option 2, once applied, was always shown as "Option 0" ("however many times I confirm option 2, it stays on option 0"). A complete option comes before an option whose `.pup` files alone are there ("en partie seulement", partly only, with what is missing); between options with identical files, the one remembered in the `.info` wins, and the screen says so ("Mêmes fichiers", same files). The `.bat` is read in order: `del Option*.jpg` then `xcopy … Option 2.JPG` leaves the preview in place (before, it went to the trash right after being copied). An option's folder is the folder of its `.pup` files; an options folder that a `.bat` copies does not become a second option, and two options never share a name: every GOTG option appeared twice, and the tie between twins hid the recommendation ("pas de choix net", no clear choice). A backup folder (`PuP-Pack_Options - Copy`, `_backup`, `….orig`, `….bak`) is never an option (`isBackupName`). "NoDMD" written as one word reads as "No DMD".

**PuP-Pack switched off by the script.** Some scripts only start the pack when a variable allows it, and ship it switched off: *2001 - A Space Odyssey (Original 2025)* writes `usePUP = False` ("enable Pinup Player functions for this table"). However well the pack is placed and its option applied, the script never calls `PuPlayer.B2SInit`: the PUP plugin loads nothing (`vpinball.log` says "Plugin PUP loaded", never "PUP path") and the game only shows the usual backglass and DMD. `pupScriptSwitch` ([puppack.ts](../packages/core/src/puppack.ts)) follows the conditions around `CreateObject("PinUpPlayer.PinDisplay")`, in its Sub and then where that Sub is called, and keeps the variable whose starting value, a literal never switched back on elsewhere, prevents reaching it. It stays silent when the value comes from a table option, a file read or another variable (*Guardians of the Galaxy*: `DMDMode`). To switch the pack on without modifying the table, Cyber Pinball writes `<table>.vbs` next to the `.vpx`: VPX 10.8.1 plays that file instead of the table's script (`PinTable::LoadScriptOverride`; the log says "Loading script: …"). It is the script read from the `.vpx` (`readVpxScript`, [vpxinfo.ts](../packages/core/src/vpxinfo.ts), without VPX), byte for byte, with only the value changed, plus a last line `' CyberPinball-script {…}` that keeps the fingerprint of the original script ([scriptpatch.ts](../packages/core/src/scriptpatch.ts)); script error line numbers do not move. That `.vbs` is not a "replaced script": the details screen says "Script adapté" (Adapted script). When a table is added with its pack, or a pack for a table already installed, the plan writes it automatically; when the table is updated, the plan rewrites it from the new script, or moves it to the recycle bin if the new version starts the pack by itself. A `.vbs` from elsewhere is never replaced: the doctor offers to switch the pack on in it. A protected (encrypted) script cannot be read without VPX: the doctor then says which value to change.

**Relayed switch, and PuP-DMD (27 October 2026).** *The Goonies Never Say Die Pinball (VPW 2021)* ships `Const enablePupPack = False`, then `Dim HasPuP : HasPup = enablePupPack`, and only starts the pack `If HasPup Then`. You could hear the table's music, never the pack. The log said "Plugin PUP loaded", with no "PUP path". `pupScriptSwitch` now follows a variable that only copies another one (two relays at most, never changed in a Sub) back to the literal value: that is the one switched on. The PuP-DMD (the score the script writes on the pack's DMD screen, `LabelInit`) often has its own switch (`Const enablePupDMD = False`), found the same way by `pupDmdSwitch`. Switched off, the pack lays its empty frame (`PuPFrames\default.png`) in the DMD window, on top of the table's DMD, and the script asks PinUP Player to hide that screen (`SendMSG` FN 12), which the PUP plugin does not do. `pupSwitchesToEnable` therefore switches both on when the option in place takes the DMD window (`packDrawsDmd`) of a cabinet that has one, otherwise the pack alone. The `.vbs` keeps them all in its last line (`kind: 'dmd'` for the PuP-DMD). The doctor reads the script again from the `.vpx` rather than trusting the cached reading, which might predate this (readings at version 6: an older reading of the same table is redone without VPX, `cachedScript`). Adding a table or a pack does the same.

**Pack nobody starts (28 October 2026).** *Game of Thrones LE (VPW, Stern 2015)* is an original table with FlexDMD and no ROM. Its script never creates `PinUpPlayer.PinDisplay` ("`Dim PuPlayer ' Not currently used`"); mikeleonheart's pack was made for PinUP Player hooked to B2S Server, which looked for it under the game name given to B2S (`cGameName = "gameofthrones"`). The VPX 10.8.1 PUP plugin only loads a pack by itself for a PinMAME game (`OnControllersChanged` only keeps a `pinmame::…` game, never `b2s::…`): the log said "Plugin PUP loaded", never "PUP path". The doctor said nothing: with no PuP call and no ROM, it expected no name, and none of its rules applied. It was the third pack in a row installed with no video on the backglass. Now `pupLoaderNeeded` ([puppack.ts](../packages/core/src/puppack.ts)) recognises this case; the expected name is the game name for B2S (`b2sName`, read from `.B2SName = "…"` or `cGameName`), failing that the name of the pack's only folder, and the pack is renamed to it (the option kept in the `.info` follows, `renamePupMemory`). Cyber Pinball's `.vbs` then starts the pack the way the table would (`addPupLoader`): `Sub Table1_Init() : CyberPinball_PuPStart` on the Sub's line (the table object's name is read from the `.vpx`, `readVpxTableName`), and, after the last line, a Sub that creates `PinUpPlayer.PinDisplay` and calls `B2SInit "", "<pack>"`, under `On Error Resume Next` then `Err.Clear`. Line numbers do not move; the last line keeps `"loader": {…}`, and the `.vbs` is rebuilt when the table is updated as long as the new script does not start the pack itself. It is done before each game ("PuP-Pack jamais lancé" (PuP-Pack never started), in `PUP_PLAY_REPAIRS`). Safeguard: a pack present that no name points to (several folders, no game name) gives "PuP-Pack jamais chargé" (PuP-Pack never loaded) instead of silence. The test `puplancement.test.ts` simulates the plugin (name given by the played script, or ROM, then `pupvideos\<name>\screens.pup`) on each kind of table: no ROM and no PuP, `B2SInit` under the right or another name, `usePUP = False`, PinMAME game; after the pre-game repairs, each one loads its pack.

**DMD image triggers without PinMAME.** The *Game of Thrones* pack has 185 active triggers, 183 of them `D<n>` with `PupCapture\<n>.bmp`: PinUP Player recognised the table's DMD images. The PUP plugin only receives these images from PinMAME ROMs (`GetIdentifyFrame`); FlexDMD does not provide them (`FlexDMDPlugin.cpp`, "TODO … pup events"). The script, however, can read the FlexDMD frame: `FlexDMD.DmdPixels` returns 128 × 32 bytes at the exact 16 levels of the captures (0, 17 … 255 in `GRAY_4`), as an array VBScript reads dot by dot. So Cyber Pinball's `.vbs` redoes the plugin's matching ([pupcapture.ts](../packages/core/src/pupcapture.ts), research in `docs/recherche/pupcapture-flexdmd.md`): `Sub FrameTimer_Timer() : CyberPinball_PuPCapture` on the line of the Sub of the table's frame timer (the one whose script sets `Interval = -1`), and, after the last line, a block that reads the frame at most every 20 ms of `GameTime`, compares 16 probe dots then the hash of the area of the candidate captures only, and sends `B2SData "D<n>", 1` then `0` to the object that started the pack (`CyberPinball_PuP`, or the script's own), like libpupdmd: the first capture by increasing number, never the same one twice in a row. The block's first line carries its settings (pack, objects, timer, depth, hash of the `PupCapture` files), and so does the `.vbs`'s last line (`"capture": {…}`): when the captures or the script change, the block is rebuilt in place; when the table is updated, it follows the rebuilt `.vbs`. The doctor offers it ("Vidéos du PuP-Pack déclenchées par le DMD : sans effet" (PuP-Pack videos triggered by the DMD: no effect), fix "Reconnaître les images du DMD" (Match the DMD frames)) and the menu does it by itself before the game (`PUP_PLAY_REPAIRS`). Impossible without a `FlexDMD.FlexDMD` object, with a colour DMD, without a frame timer, without the pack's object in a variable, or with a protected script: the finding says so, with no fix. Nothing is done when the script sends `D` triggers itself (`PuPlayer.B2SData "D…"`, `dmdTriggers`). On *Game of Thrones*, 120 of the 183 active captures are, dot for dot, a frame of a FlexDMD asset; the others mix an asset with the script's text, to be checked on the cabinet. Cost measured in `cscript`: 0.28 ms per read of an ordinary frame. Tests: `pupcapture.test.ts` runs the generated VBScript in `cscript` (simulated FlexDMD and PinDisplay) and compares it with libpupdmd's matching; `pupcapture-medecin.test.ts` checks the repair, updated captures and table, a `.vbs` from elsewhere, `usePUP = False`. Script readings at version 7.

**Background already in place (28 October 2026).** The PUP plugin restarts a screen's background video on every `SetBG` trigger, even when it is the one already playing (`PUPMediaManager::Play`); PinUP Player let it play on. On *Game of Thrones*, the DMD's "choose your house" screen alternates with a filler frame: D11 (Stark, `SetBG`) restarted on every return, and the backglass showed almost only the video's black start, frame included, with flashes of Stark. `backgroundTriggers` ([pupcapture.ts](../packages/core/src/pupcapture.ts)) reads from `triggers.pup` the `D<n>` triggers whose active lines all set a file as background; the matching only sends them when they change at least one screen's background (`CyberPinball_PuPCapChanges`). A trigger that does anything else goes out as before. `triggers.pup` is part of the block's fingerprint (`captureSignature`): a `.vbs` already written is rebuilt before the next game. A background changed by a trigger the matching does not send (`E…`, sent by the table) is not tracked.

**Files the plugin reads differently (28 October 2026).** The background fix was not enough: the *Game of Thrones* backglass still flickered. The real cause was in the pack's files. The house sigils (`Choose your House\Stark.mp4`…) are PNG or JPEG images, the backglass background (`Backglass\Backglass.mp4`) a JPEG, all named `.mp4`. PinUP Player showed them as images; the PUP plugin decides by extension (`PUPScreen::Play`: only `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg` and `.bmp` are images) and hands them to FFmpeg, which plays them as one-frame videos, in a loop: an almost black backglass, with flashes of the sigil. The same pack names `9y10perro y montaña.mp4` in a UTF-8 `triggers.pup`; the plugin converts that name with the Windows code page (`std::filesystem::path` from a `std::string`, `PUPTrigger::CreateFromCSV`), does not find the file and drops the trigger ("PlayFile not found" in `vpinball.log`). `packFileFixes` ([pupfiles.ts](../packages/core/src/pupfiles.ts)) reads the nature of each playlist file from its first bytes (`mediaKind`): an image named like a video gets its true extension back, a name with accents (file or playlist folder) becomes ASCII (`asciiName`), never overwriting another file, and every `.pup` in the pack, options and backups included, is rewritten byte for byte except the changed names: the option in place is still recognised. A name the table's script mentions is never changed. This is the finding "Fichiers du PuP-Pack que le plugin PUP lirait mal" (PuP-Pack files the PUP plugin would misread), repaired before each game (`PUP_PLAY_REPAIRS`) and now also as soon as a table or a pack is installed (`install`, [library.ts](../app/src/library.ts), which applies all the `PUP_PLAY_REPAIRS` repairs). What remains missing, a file named by an active trigger or a lit screen and absent from the pack, is reported ("Fichiers du PuP-Pack absents" (PuP-Pack files missing), `missingPackFiles`, no fix): on the cabinet, bietekwiet's FullDMD video for *Game of Thrones*, and one file in the *2001*, *Star Trek* and *The Matrix* packs. The test `pupfichiers.test.ts` simulates the plugin's reading (names read with the Windows code page, case-insensitive) and checks that after the repairs every named file is found and no image carries a video extension.

**Backglass left to the `.directb2s`, and repairs before the game.** An option may place the Backglass screen (2) inside the FullDMD (5): on *2001*, "(3 Screens) B2S Users, …, Pup on Fulldmd" writes `2,Backglass,…,"5,12.7,13.5,74.8,74.6"`. The plugin then draws nothing in the backglass window: a window is only filled if its root (topper: screen 0; backglass: 2, otherwise 6; DMD: 5, otherwise 1) is not a screen placed inside another one with no children, and a visible screen descends from it (`PUPManager::Render`, reproduced by `pupWindows` in [puppack.ts](../packages/core/src/puppack.ts)). These "B2S Users" options rely on the table's `.directb2s`; without it, the backglass stays on a still image (seen on 26 October 2026). The automatic choice therefore reads, in each option's `screens.pup`, which windows it fills: a backglass left empty costs a lot if the table has no `.directb2s`, a little if that is unknown, almost nothing otherwise (the pack on the backglass stays preferred). Whether the topper window is filled wins over the name ("Default Locale Topper Off" puts the topper in the main screen). The doctor then offers the option that puts the pack on the backglass ("Option du PuP-Pack inadaptée à la borne", with "fronton laissé au .directb2s" (backglass left to the .directb2s) and "table sans .directb2s" (table without a .directb2s)). Before each game, the menu itself applies the pack repairs the doctor would offer (`PUP_PLAY_REPAIRS`, `repairPupForPlay`: pack renamed or moved up, option copied or changed, script that switches the pack off, stale Cyber Pinball `.vbs`): without them, a pack stayed off until the doctor was opened. An option chosen on the details screen is kept, the table is never modified, replaced files go to the recycle bin (`trash\… Avant la partie <folder>`), and the menu's log notes each repair ("PuP-Pack (…) : …").

**Playlist order.** A screen whose `screens.pup` gives the playlist without a file plays the first file of the folder. The PUP plugin sorts those files case-sensitively (`PUPPlaylist.cpp`, `std::ranges::sort`), PinUP Player like Windows. Authors often name a transparent image so that it comes first (`aaclear.png`, `blank.png`, `black.png`); for the plugin, `Overlay1 by Dr Nobody TY.png` came first. On *2001*, screen 14 "Callouts" thus showed a whole frame squashed in the middle of the backglass (26 October 2026); *Guardians of the Galaxy* had six folders in that case. `playlistRenames` ([puppack.ts](../packages/core/src/puppack.ts)) finds, folder by folder, the file PinUP Player would play first and renames it in upper case, extension kept: it then comes first in both orders. The plugin finds a file named by a trigger or by `screens.pup` regardless of case: nothing else changes. This is done on import, by the doctor ("Playlist du PuP-Pack lue dans le mauvais ordre", PuP-Pack playlist read in the wrong order) and before each game.

**The doctor** ([doctor.ts](../packages/core/src/doctor.ts)) checks, without launching VPX:

| Finding | When | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PuP-Pack not found | the script loads a pack (or a `pupvideos` folder exists) and there is no `pupvideos\<nom attendu>` | rename the only folder present; otherwise, add the pack |
| PuP-Pack stored one folder too deep | `pupvideos\X\X\screens.pup` | move the pack up |
| Unused PuP-Pack | another folder in `pupvideos` that no version in the folder loads | recycle bin |
| PuP-Pack switched off by the script | an expected pack is in place, and the script VPX plays (the `.vbs` next to the table if there is one) never starts the pack (`usePUP = False`, or `HasPup = enablePupPack` with `enablePupPack = False`), or, the pack's option taking the DMD window, leaves the PuP-DMD off (`enablePupDMD = False`: "PuP-DMD coupée par le script" (PuP-DMD switched off by the script)) | switch each switch on in `<table>.vbs` (or in the `.vbs` already there); protected script: the values to change |
| PuP-Pack never started | a table without ROM whose script never starts PuP, a pack in place under the expected name (game name for B2S, or only folder), and no `.vbs` that starts it | start it in `<table>.vbs`, at the beginning of `<table>_Init` (also done before the game); protected script: nothing to do without VPX |
| Videos triggered by the DMD: no effect | a pack with `PupCapture`, a table without ROM, active `D<n>` triggers with their image, a script that sends no `D`, and no `.vbs` that matches them up to date | match them in `<table>.vbs` on the FlexDMD frame (also done before the game); colour DMD, no frame timer or protected script: none |
| Script adapted for an older version | Cyber Pinball's `.vbs` was made from another script than the `.vpx`'s (table updated by hand) | rewrite it from the new script, or move it to the recycle bin |
| Option not applied, or modified | options exist, and the root has no `.pup` (or its `.pup` files match no option, `appliedOption`) | copy the chosen option again (remembered, otherwise based on the cabinet) and remember it |
| Option differs from the remembered one, or is incomplete | the option remembered in the `.info` is not the one whose files are in place (`.bat` run by hand, interrupted copy), or is only partly there (a file it deletes came back) | chosen on the details screen, or partly in place: apply it again; chosen automatically at installation: remember the option in place (also done before the game) |
| Option unsuited to the cabinet | an option is applied, and another one scores clearly better for the current cabinet (cabinet changed since installation, older automatic choice, backglass left to the `.directb2s` of a table that has none), unless the option was chosen on the details screen | apply the better one and remember it |
| The PuP-Pack would hide the table's DMD | the pack takes the DMD window (screen 5 or 1), the table draws its DMD (ROM, FlexDMD, UltraDMD) without writing the score on the pack, and the table's `.ini` does not set `[ScoreView] Priority.PUP` | write `[ScoreView] Priority.PUP = 0` in the table's `.ini` (also done before the game) |
| PuP-Pack files the PUP plugin would misread | in a playlist, an image named like a video (`Stark.mp4`, a PNG), or a name with accents | give the image its extension back, make the name ASCII, rewrite every `.pup` in the pack (options included); also done on install and before the game; name mentioned by the script: left |
| PuP-Pack files missing | an active trigger or a lit screen names a file missing from its playlist | none: the pack does not ship it (information) |
| Playlist read in the wrong order | in a folder of the pack, the first file for the PUP plugin (case-sensitive sort) is not PinUP Player's (Windows sort): `aaclear.png` behind `Overlay1….png` | rename the intended file in upper case (extension kept); also done on import and before the game |
| Pack screens missing from the cabinet | a visible screen targets the topper, the DMD or the backglass while that role is not assigned | — (information) |
| Playfield videos not displayed | screen 3 (Playfield) is visible: the plugin does not render it | — |
| PuP-Pack driven by the script | `playlistadd` in the script and no `.pup` | — |
| Pack meant for another ROM | `PupCapture` folder and a ROM different from the pack's original name, or a ROM in another language (suffix `f`, `g`, `i`, `s` after the version, assumed) | — |
| VPX log | the table's last game in `vpinball.log` ("Starting script" or "Startup done" from VPX 10.8.1): pack loaded without `screens.pup`, no pack loaded (unless the script switches the pack off: the previous finding says so), `Not implemented` functions. Until 25 October 2026 the rule waited for "Starting Play mode", which VPX only writes when launched from its editor: it never fired | — |

**Unrecognized cases** (the plan then offers the options to pick with the buttons, or the doctor reports it): `.bat` files that ask for a choice (`choice`, `set /p`) or test for files (`if exist`), `.bat` files that run an executable (`PuPDesktopPunch.exe`, installers), options stored outside a folder whose name contains "option" and with no readable `.bat`, packs with no `.pup` at all outside a `pupvideos` folder (old script-driven packs: recognized only if they already arrive inside `pupvideos\`), a pack-specific `PinUpPlayer.ini` (ignored by the plugin), changes made in the PuP-Pack Editor (enabling topper videos, for example) that no longer match any option: reported as information, never overwritten without the button. The VPX FFmpeg DLLs and the `Priority.PUP` keys in `VPinballX.ini` (which put the pack ahead of the directb2s) belong to the VPX sync, not to this module.

Sources for the option rule: the `.bat` example in [dekay's vpinball wiki](https://github-wiki-see.page/m/dekay/vpinball-wiki/wiki/PUP-Packs), [pup-research](https://github.com/francisdb/pup-research) ("copy the .pup files from `PuP-Pack_Options/<option>` to the root"), the [Batocera wiki](https://wiki.batocera.org/systems:vpinball) (Batman 66: `Option 3 - Desktop`), and option names of VPUniverse and vpinball.com packs, from search engine summaries (Tron Legacy, Transformers, Aliens, Pirates of the Caribbean, PinEvent packs: "Option 1 - Backglass and Topper", "2 Screen - 4x3 or 5x4", "Full DMD on Backglass", "Backbox AIO", "PUP Stream"). VPUniverse pages refuse automated reading (403): the rule still needs confirming on real packs (manual tests 44 to 51).

## Structure

| Folder | Role |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/core` | Cabinet model, EDID, fingerprints, screen matching, INI, VPX generator, buttons and keys (`buttons.ts`). No Electron, no Windows. |
| `packages/win-displays` | Windows functions through FFI (koffi): screen detection (DisplayConfig, EDID, scaling, ContainerId), audio outputs (`audio.ts`, MMDevices registry read by `registry.ts`), connected USB devices (`usb.ts`, for real DMDs), windows and foreground (`windows.ts`), screen orientation (`orientation.ts`). |
| `packages/installer` | Manifest of third-party components, choice of the root, verified download, extraction, junction to the active version. |
| `app` | Electron application: entry point (`main.ts`), setup wizard for buttons, screens and sound (`wizard.ts`, test sounds in `renderer/sound.js`), table menu (`frontend.ts`), VPX settings (`settings.ts`), table management (`library.ts`), VPX launch and monitoring (`launcher.ts`), `sync` command. |
| `docs/design-system` | Visual language of the redesign (tokens, `.cp-*` components, icons, screen mockups) and its `index.html` page; regenerated by `node docs/design-system/src/construire.mjs`. Applied to the interface (`app/renderer/theme.css`), to the built-in fallbacks (`fallback.css`) and to the icon (`app/renderer/icone.svg`). |

## VPX settings

From the table menu, R key (or Exit then "Réglages de VPX" (VPX settings)); standalone: `npx electron app --reglages [--table=<morceau du nom>]`, launcher action "reglages".

- The list of settings is not hand-coded: `npm run vpx-schema` reads `src/core/Settings_properties.inl` from the VPX source code at the commit of the installed version (`sourceCommit` in the manifest) and writes `packages/installer/vpx-settings/<version>.json`. It adds the settings that plugins declare when they load, read from the `plugins/` folder of the same commit (`packages/core/src/vpx-plugin-settings.ts`): each plugin's `[Plugin.<id>] Enable` (`plugin.cfg`), the `MSGPI_*_SETTING` macros actually registered (`RegisterSetting`), and `[Backglass|ScoreView|Topper] Priority.<moteur>` for each rendering engine of the secondary windows. For 10.8.1-5436: 1,240 settings, 101 of them from plugins. Each plugin setting records the plugin that declares it: it has no effect when that plugin is off. With `-- --ini <VPinballX.ini>`, it checks that every setting in the schema appears in a file written by that version (except those of plugins VPX did not load); with `-- --check`, it fails if the committed file differs from the source code.
- "Essentiels" (Essentials) tab: a translated selection (`packages/core/src/vpx-essentials.ts`), including the groups "Backglass, PuP et DMD réel" (Backglass, PuP and real DMD) and "Dossiers communs à toutes les tables" (Folders shared by all tables). "Avancé" (Advanced) tab: the whole schema by section, with the original labels and descriptions, plus keys outside the schema (keys, options declared by tables) as read-only; an `[Input] Mapping.*` key is shown with the name of the action and of the key.
- Flipper buttons: move and pick a value; Start: edit, confirm; Exit: cancel, go back; Del: default value; mouse and wheel also work, with "‹ Retour aux sections" (Back to sections) or "‹ Fermer" (Close) under the brand.
- Keys written by Cyber Pinball (windows, plugins on or off, plugin priorities on the assigned windows, `SecurityLevel` and `AskToTurnOn`, which prevent VPX from asking questions during play (see [Launching a game](#launching-a-game)), `NudgeStrength`, which keeps the table still on screen (see [Tilt](#tilt)), detected real DMD, button keys once the "Boutons" (Buttons) step is done, and audio outputs once the "Son" (Sound) step is done) are shown but cannot be changed, along with where their value comes from and how to change it.
- Writing: only the setting's line changes (`IniDocument`), with a timestamped backup (`writeWithBackup`). "Valeur par défaut" (Default value) leaves the key empty, which is the VPX convention.
- Per table (`openSettings({ scope: 'table', table })`): the `<table>.ini` file next to the .vpx, found the way VPX does it (table name, otherwise folder name). Only values that differ from the global setting are kept in it, as VPX does; an empty file is deleted. In a sandbox (`CYBERPINBALL_HOME`), it is written to `<bac à sable>\sandbox\tables\` and never next to the real .vpx.

## System menu: five sections

The system menu (Exit, Tab or ⚙ from the table menu) shows only five sections, then "Retour aux tables" (Back to tables). Each section opens on its entries; Exit (or "Retour", Back) goes up one level, with the selection on the section being left. A screen opened from an entry returns, through its "Retour", to the section it came from, with the selection on that entry; the gear always reopens the sections.

| Section | Entries |
| --- | --- |
| Tables | Add a table, Downloads, ROM library |
| Médias (Media) | Find missing media (on VPinMediaDB); Media, sound and attract mode |
| Amis (Friends) | My friends, Find a player, Friend requests, My badges, Sharing and network (see [Friends: the cabinet network](#friends-the-cabinet-network)) |
| Borne (Cabinet) | Cabinet health, Cabinet setup, VPX settings, Cabinet controls, Windows desktop (Open Windows Explorer on a [dedicated cabinet](#dedicated-cabinet)), Dedicated cabinet, Guest mode |
| Cyber Pinball | Langue / Language, Cyber Pinball update, Migration, Quit Cyber Pinball, Sleep, Restart the cabinet, Shut down the cabinet |

"Migration" (in Cyber Pinball) gathers what is only used once, when coming from another frontend: "Reprendre les médias" (Import media, from PinUP Popper or PinballX) and "Regrouper les ROM" (Gather ROMs) in the library.

An entry's red dot (received files to install, update available, DMD screen to check…) goes up to its section, then to the gear; on opening, the selection goes to the first one that carries one.

The tree (sections, order, name and icon of each entry) is in [packages/core/src/systemmenu.ts](../packages/core/src/systemmenu.ts) (`SYSTEM_ROOT`, `SYSTEM_GROUPS`, `SYSTEM_ITEMS`). The application provides what each entry does through its identifier (`library.provideSystemItem`, `app/src/library.ts` and `frontend.ts`); a new entry is placed by adding its identifier to a section, without touching the others. [systemmenu.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/systemmenu.test.ts) pins the invariants: each entry placed once, one icon per entry within a section (and not the section's own), every entry provided by the application, and **every "System menu > …" path** written in this manual, its translation, the manual tests, the READMEs and the interface texts names entries that exist, in that order. Demonstrations name an entry (`action:…`): from the system menu, the autopilot finds it in its section.

## Cabinet health

System menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Santé de la borne" (Cabinet health) gathers on one page what other screens already know, to see at a glance what prevents playing or deserves a look. Each line has a state — **OK** (tick, green), **À vérifier** (To check: exclamation mark, amber), **Bloquant** (Blocking: cross, red), never colour alone — and opens, with Start or a click, the screen that repairs it; its "Retour" (Back) returns to the page. The summary at the top counts the lines in each state and says how old the check is.

| Check | Where it comes from | States | What the line opens |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Disk space | One drive per root, among those of the tables, the downloads and the data | Blocking under 2 GB free, or a tables drive that no longer responds; To check under 10 GB | Downloads (free up space) |
| Tables | Each table's doctor, on its already-read script (`diagnose`, `playBlockers`) | Blocking: a requested ROM is missing; To check: the table crashed, unreadable script, or script never read; OK: no requested ROM is missing | The table doctor (five lines at most, then "See every table to review"); ROM library (read the scripts) |
| PuP-Packs | The doctor of each table that has a `pupvideos` folder (`pupFacts`, `PUP_HEALTH_FINDINGS`): pack not found or badly placed, switched off by the script, option missing or made for another cabinet, pack that would hide the DMD, VPX log that did not see it loaded | To check: one line per table, with the cause, and "réparé de lui-même avant la prochaine partie" (repaired automatically before the next game) when the menu takes care of it (`PUP_PLAY_REPAIRS`); OK: all ready; nothing without any pack | The table's doctor, where the repair takes one step |
| Media | The "Find missing media" plan on the VPinMediaDB index already kept, offline | To check: VPinMediaDB has media for tables recognised with certainty, or has never been consulted; OK otherwise. Never blocking: media are optional | Missing media |
| Screens | The setup in use against the plugged-in screens (`scoreConfiguration`, `chooseConfiguration`) | Blocking: no setup, none of its screens plugged in, or playfield not found; To check: a screen is missing, another setup would match better, or "VPX confond deux écrans" (VPX mixes up two screens): two roles on two screens with the same name, which VPX 10.8.1 cannot tell apart (`homonymousRoles`, it opens both windows on the first; PuP videos meant for one hide behind the other), "recalé à chaque partie" (realigned at every game, see below), or "pas le playfield" (not the playfield) when the playfield is one of them | Cabinet setup |
| Visual Pinball X | The active version, against the installer manifest's (offline) | Blocking: VPX missing; To check: a newer version is known | Install VPX (the old version stays alongside); VPX settings when all is well |

**Fast to open.** The check runs in the background and yields to the menu between two tables: it never starts VPX, downloads nothing, and takes a few seconds for 200 tables. The page opens at once on the last check, kept from one launch to the next in `<data>\cache\sante.json`, then runs it again ("Vérifié il y a 2 min · nouvelle vérification en cours…", Checked 2 min ago · new check under way…); the last summary stays on screen until the new one is ready. A check also runs 15 seconds after start-up, then every 30 minutes (never during a game), and "Vérifier maintenant" (Check now) starts one.

**No window above VPX.** When a game starts, `closeTopmostWindows` ([frontend.ts](../app/src/frontend.ts)) closes any Cyber Pinball window that is "always on top" (only "Récupérer" is): it would sit in front of the windows where the PUP plugin draws. [pupvisible.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/pupvisible.test.ts) refuses any other always-on-top window.

**VPX windows moved back to their screen.** VPX 10.8.1 recognises a screen by its name only (`Window::GetDisplayConfig`) and takes the first one that bears it. On the "Poste à 3 écrans" development PC, the backglass and the DMD are two PL2294H: VPX opened "Visual Pinball Backglass" and "Visual Pinball Score View" on the same screen (`vpinball.log`: "Window #1" and "Window #2" "on display PL2294H"), where they overlapped, and the PuP videos meant for the DMD were hidden (27 October 2026). No `VPinballX.ini` setting avoids it: in windowed mode, `WndX` and `WndY` are relative to the chosen screen and must fit in it. During the game, the loop that reads the window stack every 2 s (`watchOccupied`, [frontend.ts](../app/src/frontend.ts)) therefore moves each VPX ancillary window back to its role's screen. It recognises the window by its title (`VPX_ANCILLARY_WINDOWS`: Backglass, Score View, Topper) and moves it at the same size, at the same place within the screen, without activating it or changing its rank in the stack (`moveWindow`, `SetWindowPos` with `SWP_NOACTIVATE`, `SWP_NOZORDER` and `SWP_ASYNCWINDOWPOS`). This only happens with a VPX version that finds screens by name only (the profile's `name` format) and for roles whose name is shared by another plugged-in screen (`confusableRoles`: another role's screen, or an unused screen of the same model). A window already on its screen is never touched: what is adjusted in the VPX menu stays. A window that VPX puts elsewhere again more than three times is left where it puts it, and the menu log says so. The playfield window, the rendering one, is never moved: when the playfield is one of the mixed-up roles, cabinet health says so. Pure code: `vpxWindowMoves` ([vpxwindows.ts](../packages/core/src/vpxwindows.ts)), pinned by [vpxplacement.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/vpxplacement.test.ts); the menu log notes each window moved back ("Partie : « Visual Pinball Score View » ramenée sur l'écran du rôle dmd…").

**Badge.** A Blocking line in the last check puts the red dot on "Santé de la borne", on the Borne section and on the gear; the entry's details say how many issues are blocking or to check.

Code: the lines and their states in [packages/core/src/health.ts](../packages/core/src/health.ts), the check, the cache and the page in `app/src/health.ts`, the rendering in `app/renderer/library.js` (`renderHealth`). [health.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/health.test.ts) pins every state of every check and that **every line leads to a real screen**: a system menu entry placed in the tree and provided by the application, a table's doctor, the list of tables to review or the VPX installation. The off-screen demonstration `.\docs\demo-sante.ps1` captures the page on the cabinet (4K playfield lying down) and on a desktop (1920 × 1080, 1366 × 768), in French and English, and goes through each repair and back (captures in `tmp\demo-sante\captures`).

## Shutting down, sleeping, restarting the cabinet

In the system menu's Cyber Pinball section, after "Quitter Cyber Pinball" (Quit Cyber Pinball; the ways out together, from the lightest to the heaviest): "Mettre en veille" (Sleep), "Redémarrer la borne" (Restart the cabinet), "Éteindre la borne" (Shut down the cabinet). Each opens a confirmation screen that says what is going to happen; Start confirms (the action is preselected, in green like any current choice: going through the confirmation is caution enough), Exit or "Annuler" (Cancel) returns to the section, with the selection on the entry. The module is [app/src/power.ts](../app/src/power.ts); the method, the simulation and the scheduled action are in [packages/core/src/power.ts](../packages/core/src/power.ts), the sleep call in [packages/win-displays/src/power.ts](../packages/win-displays/src/power.ts).

- **Shut down, restart**: every screen turns plain black, the playfield only showing "Extinction de la borne…" (Shutting the cabinet down…; no brand, media or gear), then the menu saves its settings (state of the menu media), gives back the taskbars a game may have hidden, then runs `shutdown.exe /s /t 0` (`/r` to restart). Its windows and veils stay in place until Windows closes the application, with its own shutdown screen already shown: no screen shows the desktop in between. Full shutdown: Windows Fast Startup only applies with `/hybrid`, so the cabinet starts cold, its USB devices (Pinscape, encoders) enumerated again. Without `/f` (which `/t 0` does not imply): an open application can save, or hold Windows back; after a minute without a shutdown, the menu says so ("La borne ne s'est pas éteinte", The cabinet did not shut down) and Exit brings it back.
- **Sleep**: the same black screen ("Mise en veille…", Going to sleep…), settings saved, then S3 sleep through `powrprof.dll`'s `SetSuspendState(FALSE, FALSE, FALSE)`, called directly through koffi, on a separate thread. Cyber Pinball stays open: on wake, the table menu comes back as it was. Never `rundll32 powrprof.dll,SetSuspendState 0,1,0`: rundll32 passes its own arguments (window, instance, command line), the first one, "hibernate", receives a non-null window handle, and the PC hibernates whenever hibernation is enabled, which is the default on Windows 11 and on the cabinet (`powercfg /a`: S3 sleep and hibernation). The process's shutdown privilege (`SeShutdownPrivilege`) is enabled before the call. Hybrid sleep, if Windows enabled it, is still sleep (memory is also copied to disk). A PC with Modern Standby only (S0 low power idle, no S3) does not allow requesting it: `IsPwrSuspendAllowed` says so beforehand, the entry shows "Indisponible : ce PC n'a que la veille moderne" (Not available: this PC only has Modern Standby) and its screen ("Veille indisponible depuis Cyber Pinball", Sleep not available from Cyber Pinball) points to the Windows Start menu, without asking Windows anything.
- **Scheduled action**: `createPowerControl` performs an action right away (`run`) or at a given time (`schedule(action, at)`, cancelled by `cancel()`, listed by `scheduled()`). The time is read again at least every minute (sleep suspends timers); an action whose time passed more than five minutes ago (cabinet asleep or off at that time) is missed, not caught up on wake (`onMissed`). A scheduled action can wait for the cabinet to be free (`ready`, read every second) until a deadline (`until`), and announce itself with a warning (`notice`) that a button removes. Every action, asked from the menu or scheduled, then waits for Windows to shut down (`settle`). The menu exposes it through `createPowerMenu(…).control`, for the cabinet schedule's automatic stop (see [Cabinet schedule](#cabinet-schedule)).
- **Tests**: nothing ever shuts down. The real executor is only built when no test variable at all is set (`powerSimulated`): offscreen, autopilot, captures, endurance, sandbox (`CYBERPINBALL_HOME`), unit tests, even with `CYBERPINBALL_A_L_ECRAN=1`, and on request (`CYBERPINBALL_ALIMENTATION_SIMULEE=1`). The action is only logged there ("Alimentation simulée : shutdown.exe /s /t 0"), the black screen stays 2.5 s, then the menu comes back with the notice "Mode essai : extinction simulée, le PC reste allumé" (Test mode: shutdown simulated, the PC stays on; cleared as soon as another confirmation opens). `CYBERPINBALL_ALIMENTATION_SANS_VEILLE=1` simulates, in tests only, a PC without S3 sleep.
