- **Proofs**: [power.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/power.test.ts) pins the invariants (every test simulated, real executor never built in a test, no other path to shutdown in the code: `shutdown.exe`, `SetSuspendState`, `rundll32`, `ExitWindowsEx`, `Stop-Computer`…; methods without `/f` or hibernation; scheduled, missed, cancelled action). `.\docs\demo-alimentation.ps1` captures, offscreen, each confirmation screen, its cancellation, the black screen and the return to the menu, and the screen of a PC without S3 sleep, in French and English, on the cabinet and on a two-screen desktop (captures in `tmp\demo-alimentation`).

## Guest mode

For a party or the kids: System menu > Borne > Mode invité (Cabinet > Guest mode), then "Activer le mode invité" (Turn on guest mode). The table menu opens locked, and stays so until unlocked, even after a restart (`mode-invite.json` in the data folder).

- **What stays open**: choosing a table (flippers, arrow keys, wheel, click), opening its game page, playing, reading the flyer and the rules, the controls reminder (Help button, H, F1), stopping a stuck game (Ctrl+Shift+K). The game page only offers Play, Flyer, Rules and Back to the menu, without the "Files" section.
- **What is closed**: the system menu (Exit, Tab, gear), VPX settings (R, Settings button), Windows desktop (Ctrl+Shift+D), Ctrl+Q, Alt+F4 on a menu window, the game page actions that edit, delete, repair or download (Edit, Game media, Files, PuP-Pack, Adjust the display, Settings for this table, Diagnose, Delete, What is missing to play, Going further), files dragged onto the window, and Start on a menu message that repairs or installs (wizard, VPX, buttons, VPinballX.ini edited by hand, what is missing to play): Exit closes the message without changing anything. A closed door does nothing but show "Locked · guest mode" for three seconds at the top right, next to the padlock that replaces the gear (with the word "Guest"); the menu footer no longer offers "Exit: menu", and the controls reminder only lists what works.
- **Unlocking**: both flippers and both MagnaSaves held together for 3 seconds, from the table menu, a game page or a message. Each hand holds its flipper and the MagnaSave next to it, the very MagnaSave gesture: doable alone, without letting go of the cabinet. Four buttons held at once never happen by chance in a menu driven one button at a time, and the combination is not listened to during a game or its loading (VPX has the buttons then): it never gets in the way of play. The padlock ring fills up; released before the end, it drops back. At the end, guest mode stops (saved), "Guest mode off" replaces the padlock for a few seconds, then the gear comes back. The combination is only written on the entry's screen, never on the menu nor during a game. It follows the buttons recorded in the "Buttons" step; a MagnaSave that was not recorded is held with a keyboard's Left Ctrl or Right Ctrl (the entry's screen says so), and a keyboard's default keys (Shift and Ctrl, left and right) also unlock as long as no other button has taken them. The first button pressed does what it usually does (a flipper moves the selection by one); the next ones, held with it, nothing else.
- **Troubleshooting**: without the buttons (broken MagnaSave), with Cyber Pinball closed, delete `mode-invite.json` from the data folder.

**Entry points.** Every way of acting on the menu has a name in `GUEST_DOORS` ([packages/core/src/guestmode.ts](../packages/core/src/guestmode.ts)), open or closed to guests: keys and buttons (`handleKey`, `app/src/frontend.ts`), clicks (the `action` channel: gear, table wheel), global shortcuts, closing a window, dragged files (`library.handleAction`), Start on each kind of message (`MESSAGE_DOORS`), table management screens (`GUEST_SCREENS`: the game page and its pages only) and game page actions (`SheetAction`: each one declares its door). The compiler requires a door for every kind of message and every game page action; in guest mode, a screen action without a door is hidden, so a new action stays closed until it is declared. The autopilot goes through the same doors. [guestmode.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/guestmode.test.ts) pins the invariants: the list of open doors (opening one means writing it there with its reason), every declared door is used in `app/src`, the game page and the messages declare their doors, and **every input source in `app/src`** (`ipcMain` channel, global shortcut, `before-input-event`, joystick buttons) is counted file by file: a new one makes the test fail until it says which door it goes through. The lock, the hint, the combination (held buttons, timer, release, game in progress) and the saved setting are tested there too. Off-screen demonstration: `.\docs\demo-mode-invite.ps1` (entry, closed doors, reduced game page, flyer, controls reminder, unlocking, then a restart still locked; captures in `tmp\demo-mode-invite`). Mock-up reviewed by Codex: `docs\recherche\maquettes\mode-invite.ps1`.

**Limits.** During a game, VPX keeps its own controls: its in-game menu (both MagnaSaves) and its settings stay reachable, and Exit leaves the game as usual. Guest mode does not lock Windows (Windows key, Alt+Tab, Ctrl+Alt+Del), nor the wizard started separately from the launcher.

## Friends: the cabinet network

System menu > "Amis" (Friends) links cabinets together **with no server**: each finds the others and talks to them directly, over the Internet or on the local network. Sharing is **on by default**; "Partage et réseau" (Sharing and network) turns it off.

| Entry | What it shows |
| --- | --- |
| Mes amis (My friends) | Online, then offline. For each: playing and which table (in real time), in the menu, or seen how long ago; their latest event ("Nouveau meilleur score sur …", New high score on; "Badge reçu : …", Badge earned; "A joué à …", Played). Start opens their page: latest events, their library, "Inviter à une partie en direct" (Invite to a live game; greyed out, "Hors ligne", Offline, when they are not), "Voir sa bibliothèque" (See their library), "Retirer de mes amis" (Remove from my friends), "Bloquer" (Block). At the top, an invitation received or the live game in progress. |
| Trouver un joueur (Find a player) | The directory: "Chercher un nom…" (Search for a name, ignoring accents and case, "#A1B2" for the tag), cabinets "À proximité (réseau local)" (Nearby, local network) and "Vus récemment" (Seen recently). A player: "Envoyer une demande d'ami" (Send a friend request), or cancel, accept, decline, see their page, block. |
| Demandes d'amis (Friend requests) | Received (to answer, red dot up to the gear) and sent ("En attente : remise dès que sa borne sera en ligne", Waiting: delivered as soon as their cabinet is online). |
| Mes badges (My badges) | Earned and to earn: First game, Ten tables, A hundred games, An hour on one table, Ten hours of play, On the high score table, Grand Champion, Five-time champion. |
| Partage et réseau (Sharing and network) | Sharing on or off, "Votre nom : Alexis #8A12" (Your name), local network, library shared or not, Windows Firewall, what others see, blocked players. The selection opens on "Retour" (Back): a stray Start does not turn sharing off. |

**Identity.** Each cabinet has an Ed25519 key, created on first start and kept in `<data>\social.json` (with friends, requests, directory, events and badges; never shared). The name (2 to 24 letters, digits, spaces, `'`, `.`, `_`, `-`) is signed by this key; the `#A1B2` tag comes from the key and tells two players with the same name apart. An unreadable `social.json` is set aside (`social.json.illisible-<date>`), never overwritten.

**Directory with no server.** Every cabinet with sharing on joins the same Hyperswarm topic (`cyberpinball/annuaire/1`, on Holepunch's DHT, bootstrap nodes `node1-3.hyperdht.org`). On each connection, two cabinets exchange their profile and up to 200 profiles they have seen: the directory travels from cabinet to cabinet. A relayed profile only gets in if its signature is valid; the most recent wins; a profile not seen again for 60 days leaves. Two players find each other as well as a thousand: the DHT is shared by every Holepunch user. They only need to be on at the same time.

**Friend request.** It leaves as soon as the other cabinet can be reached, otherwise on its next connection (the requesting cabinet joins it by its key). On each connection, both cabinets restate where they stand (friend: "accept"; pending request: "request"), so an acceptance or removal made while the other was off still gets through. Requested on both sides: friends at once. A decline is remembered for 30 days (the same request gets "no" without coming back on screen). Blocking removes the player from the directory and cuts any connection with their cabinet.

**Presence and events.** Friends receive: online or not, the table being played as soon as VPX shows it (title, manufacturer, year, VPS id, never a path) and the return to the menu; high scores entered at the end of the game (VPX only records the score at the end: the score during a ball cannot be read), games of at least one minute and badges earned. A cabinet that connects receives each friend's last ten events.

**Badges.** Computed from what the tables already record (games and play time in each `.info`, high scores in `VPReg.stg`). On the first computation, those the history deserves are granted without an event: friends only see "Badge reçu" for what happens afterwards ([badges.ts](../packages/core/src/badges.ts)).

**Local network.** A multicast beacon (`239.255.42.99:47999`, TTL 1, every 5 s) announces the cabinet; neighbouring cabinets connect over encrypted TCP (secret-stream, the same Noise as Hyperswarm), even without the Internet. "Nearby" as long as the beacon is less than a minute old.

**Windows Firewall.** Without a rule, Windows would show its "Allow access" prompt on the network's first start, which nobody can answer on a cabinet without a mouse. The installer therefore adds the "Cyber Pinball" rule (inbound, `CyberPinball.exe` only, all profiles), with Windows' permission (UAC), once and only if it is missing ("Partage entre bornes : autoriser dans le pare-feu de Windows" section); never during an update (`/MAJ`), silently (`/S`, unless `/PAREFEU`) or in a sandbox. Uninstalling removes it. When it is missing (run from source, `npm run deployer`, UAC declined), "Partage et réseau" says so and offers "Autoriser dans le pare-feu de Windows" (Allow in Windows Firewall). Without it, friends over the Internet still get through (outbound NAT hole punching); the local network and cabinets calling in, less well. The commands: [packages/core/src/firewall.ts](../packages/core/src/firewall.ts), the elevation: `app/src/firewall-rule.ts`.

**Shared library.** On by default between friends (decision of 29/10/2026): two players who became friends each see the other's library without setting anything. "Partage et réseau" > "Ne plus partager ma bibliothèque" (Stop sharing my library) turns it off, "Partager ma bibliothèque avec mes amis" (Share my library with my friends) turns it back on; this choice of the player is kept (`shareLibraryChosen`), and a `social.json` written before this default, which said "no" without anyone choosing it, is read back as "shared". The screen reminds you to only share what you have the right to share (captures of works, ROMs and PuP-Packs remain their rights holders'). Friends see the list of tables and, for each, its own media (those in `medias\`: logo, backglass, videos, rules, music; neither the cabinet's defaults nor the image taken from the `.directb2s`). A friend's page > "Voir sa bibliothèque" (See their library): "Sur votre borne" (On your cabinet: same game by VPS id, ROM, or title and year; "2 manquants", 2 missing) and "Absentes de votre borne" (Not on your cabinet). One of their tables: its media, "Déjà équipé" (Already has one: your table already has this type of media, not necessarily the same file) or "Manquant" (Missing), then "Récupérer les n médias manquants" (Get the n missing media), "Récupérer les n médias" (Get the n media) or "Inviter … à jouer" (Invite … to play). The transfer shows its progress and can be cancelled (Exit); each file arrives in `<data>\amis\recus\` and only leaves it if it has the announced size and SHA-256 fingerprint and first bytes that say media of its type (the import's checks, § 5.1 of the design). It then enters the table's "Médias du jeu" (Game media) draft, with its origin ("la borne de Bruno #A1B2", Bruno's cabinet), the cursor on the first media item received ("Afficheur large reçu de Bruno : vérifiez-le, puis enregistrez.", Wide display received from Bruno: check it, then save): you see it, then "Enregistrer" (Save) shows the plan before writing. **A friend's whole table.** A table you do not have: "Récupérer toute la table" (Get the whole table); a table you already have: "Choisir les parties de sa table" (Choose parts of their table). The parts screen asks the friend's cabinet for the list of its files, sorted by game part ("Parties du jeu"): table (the `.vpx`, the DMD content and the extra scripts), backglass, ROM, PuP-Pack, DMD colours, sounds, music, point of view, own settings, menu media; each part with its number of files and its weight, and yours opposite when you have it. Start on a part takes it ("La sienne", Theirs) or leaves it ("La vôtre", Yours; "Pas prise", Not taken). By default: all their parts you do not have, except those under rights, unticked with their note (decisions of 29/10/2026): the ROM ("l'œuvre du fabricant : à ne prendre que si vous en avez le droit", the manufacturer's work: only take it if you have the right to) and the PuP-Pack ("vidéos et musiques souvent tirées du jeu : à ne prendre que si vous en avez le droit", videos and music often taken from the game: only take it if you have the right to). Start on one of them opens its legal notice ("La ROM : vos droits", The ROM: your rights; "Le PuP-Pack : vos droits", The PuP-Pack: your rights): what it is and who owns it, where it comes from ("Ce contenu vient directement de la borne de … : Cyber Pinball ne le fournit pas et ne vérifie pas vos droits. Vous en êtes seul responsable.", This content comes straight from …'s cabinet: Cyber Pinball does not provide it and does not check your rights. You alone are responsible for it.), then the pledge to tick, "J'atteste avoir le droit de détenir ce contenu (par exemple la machine d'origine ou une licence) et je m'engage à respecter le droit d'auteur et les conditions d'utilisation de ses ayants droit." (I certify that I have the right to hold this content, for example the original machine or a licence, and I agree to respect the copyright and the terms of use of its rights holders; "J'atteste et je m'engage", I certify and agree, "À cocher", To tick, then "Cochée", Ticked). "Prendre la ROM" (Take the ROM) or "Prendre le PuP-Pack" (Take the PuP-Pack) does nothing until it is ticked (decision of 29/10/2026). The attestation holds for this table while the screen is open; it follows the download (`attested`, `attestedAt` in its queue record), and a file of a rights part that was not attested never enters it (`attestedTableFiles`). At the top, "Récupérer les n parties choisies" (Get the n chosen parts: the weight; refused if there is not enough room, plus 1 GiB, on the data drive). A reminder: a table contains a script that VPX runs, to be taken only from friends you trust. The files arrive in `<data>\amis\tables\<date>\<table folder>\` under their relative path, checked on both sides (never "..", nor a drive, nor an absolute path, nor a reserved Windows name, nor a program: `.exe`, `.dll`, `.bat`, `.ps1`, `.lnk`…), each with its size and its SHA-256 fingerprint, computed while sending (a file of several gigabytes is read only once). Saves, high scores (`nvram`, `VPReg.stg`), PinMAME settings and statistics never leave. Then the folder goes through the add plan, like a downloaded table: a new table, or into yours (the parts taken replace yours, to the trash); nothing is put away before "Installer" (Install). A file missing on arrival: nothing is installed. An invitation to a live game on a table you do not have offers "La récupérer chez …" (Get it from …). "Trouver cette table dans le catalogue" (Find this table in the catalogue) remains there to get it from its site. What travels carries no path of the cabinet: each table, media item and file has an opaque id that only the sharing cabinet links to a file; only friends can ask for them.

**Live game.** Two entries. A friend's page > "Inviter à une partie en direct" (Invite to a live game): choose a table (those their known library also has first). A table's page > "Jouer avec un ami" (Play with a friend), right under "Jouer" (Play): choose the friend (online ones first, those who also have the table at the top, offline friends greyed out below). Both stay visible when you cannot invite, greyed out with their reason: "Hors ligne : invitez-le quand il sera connecté" (Offline: invite them once they are connected), "Aucun ami en ligne pour l'instant" (No friend online right now), "Pas encore d'ami : Menu système > Amis > Trouver un joueur" (No friend yet: System menu > Friends > Find a player); a friend in a game can be invited too ("En partie : il recevra une notification discrète sur sa table", In a game: they will get a discreet notification on their table). A room opens, the invitation leaves; up to four players ("Inviter un autre ami", Invite another friend). On the friend's side, the invitation chime rings and "Invitation de …" (Invitation from) opens by itself over the table menu, the cursor on "Répondre plus tard" (Answer later, with the time left): a button already pressed neither accepts nor declines; otherwise it waits at the top of "Mes amis", two minutes at most. The cabinet checks there that it has the same version as the host, mandatorily (decision of 30/10/2026: a score to compete on, and tournaments later): the same `.vpx` (SHA-256 fingerprint, kept in `<data>\amis\empreintes.json`) and the same script played. The script played: the table's own, or the `.vbs` next to it when it replaces it (VPX then plays the `.vbs`); a modified `.vbs`, or one from another version, must be the same for everyone, the one Cyber Pinball writes itself (PuP repairs, specific to each cabinet) does not count. Not the same version, no "Accepter" (Accept): "Autre version de cette table" (Another version of this table) (or "Même table, autre script", Same table, another script, a `.vbs` next to the table), and "Récupérer sa version chez …" (Fetch …'s version) opens their table in their shared library, the table ticked, then the add plan; all that is left is to ask for a new invitation. On the host's side: "N'a pas la même version : il peut récupérer la vôtre" (Does not have the same version: they can fetch yours) ([meme-version.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/meme-version.test.ts)). The same `.vpx` is recognised under another name: the invitation gives its size, the cabinet computes the fingerprint of its tables of that size. The log says what it found ("la même version", the same version; "une autre version : partie refusée", another version: game refused; "le même .vpx, mais un autre script", the same .vpx but another script; "reconnue par l'empreinte de son .vpx", recognised by its .vpx fingerprint), or "introuvable ici" (not found here) with the game looked for. A table fetched from a friend keeps the name it has on their cabinet: a `.info` with its title, manufacturer, year and VPS game comes with the received files, and the add plan takes it before the game's name on VPS ([meme-table-autre-nom.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/meme-table-autre-nom.test.ts)). The number of balls per game counts too: known on both sides and different (5 against 3), the score goes out of the ranking, "5 billes au lieu de 3" (5 balls instead of 3) in the invitation, the room and the results ("Autre nombre de billes : score hors classement", Another number of balls: score not ranked); unknown on one side, it judges nothing. It is read, never guessed ([ballcount.ts](../packages/core/src/ballcount.ts), [app/src/ball-count.ts](../app/src/ball-count.ts)): for a ROM table, the "Balls Per Game" adjustment in `<table>\pinmame\nvram\<rom>.nv` (otherwise the shared PinMAME folder), according to that ROM's memory map from the [Pinball Memory Maps](https://github.com/tomlogic/pinball-memory-maps) project (ODbL licence), downloaded on demand and kept in `<data>\cache\cartes-memoire`; without a `.nv`, the table has not run yet and the ROM starts from its factory setting (the map's default); a ROM whose map does not describe this adjustment (Stern SAM: Star Trek, The Walking Dead) stays unknown. Without a ROM: "Const BallsPerGame = 3" outside the script's procedures (GOTG, The Goonies, The Matrix), or the service menu of VPW tables (Game of Thrones: "BALL PER GAME", the value chosen in `VPReg.stg`, otherwise the default). A table that creates its settings on the fly (2001) stays unknown ([nombre-de-billes.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/nombre-de-billes.test.ts)). Without the table, only "Refuser" (Decline) remains, with "Trouver cette table dans le catalogue". The host starts the game as soon as a friend has accepted: "Départ commun" (Shared start) opens on every cabinet, a large number from 5 to 1 in the centre and the players below (the cursor on "Retour au menu", Back to menu: a Start pressed to get ready does not leave the game, the start is still on), then the table starts everywhere as if by Start. Each plays their own game on their cabinet: nothing is controlled remotely. Back in the menu, "Résultats" (Results): the players whose score entered the table's high score table, ranked, their score shown large ("Meilleur score connu", Best known score); the others without a rank, "Score non disponible" (Score not available: an unknown score is not ranked); each player's play time; "Autre nombre de billes : score hors classement" (Another number of balls: score not ranked) when that is the case (that score is shown without a rank). The screen fills in as the others come back (fifteen minutes at most after the first). The host can "Rejouer avec les mêmes joueurs" (Play again with the same players). The host runs the game and sends the room to everyone; guests do not need to be friends with each other. **The table's copy**: a live game never touches the player's usual ROM memory, records or settings. VPX plays a copy of the table, made just before launch in `<root>\parties\<game>\` (same drive as the tables) ([tabledouble.ts](../packages/core/src/tabledouble.ts), [app/src/table-double.ts](../app/src/table-double.ts)): linked (hard link, the same file with no extra space) what nothing writes during a game, the `.vpx`, the backglass, the ROMs, images, sounds, videos and fonts; copied everything else, what VPX, PinMAME, PuP or B2S may write (`pinmame\nvram`, `pinmame\cfg`, `user\VPReg.stg`, `.ini`, `.xml`, `.vbs`, PuP scripts); left out `cache\` (VPX rebuilds it) and `medias\`. A table that plays its ROM from the shared PinMAME folder gets its own `pinmame\roms` (links) and a copy of its memory in its copy, otherwise PinMAME would write into the shared folder. The game's records are read in the copy, then it is deleted, never following a link to a folder; a copy left by a crash goes at the next start. No copy, no game, never a fallback to the usual folder: table sharing its folder with others, link or junction in its folder, ROM not found, tables on another drive; the screen says so, "Votre table habituelle reste intacte : rien n'a été lancé" (Your usual table stays untouched: nothing was started). The log compares the usual folder's fingerprint (size and date of each file) before and after: "dossier de la table intact (N fichiers comparés)" (table folder untouched, N files compared), or what changed. The test has a fake VPX write everywhere these programs write, then compares every byte ([double-de-table.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/double-de-table.test.ts)). Measured on 30/10/2026 on the cabinet: Game of Thrones, 1853 links and 19 copies in 3.8 s, none of the 1881 files changed. **The host's settings**: everyone starts from the same machine state, without Cyber Pinball having to read or understand each setting ([hostsettings.ts](../packages/core/src/hostsettings.ts)). As soon as a friend accepts, the host's cabinet sends them, for each ROM of the table, its memory (`pinmame\nvram\<rom>.nv`: balls per game, ball save, tilt warnings, extra balls, high scores) and its DIP switches (`pinmame\cfg\<rom>.cfg`), and the values the table saves (`user\VPReg.stg`: the service menu of VPW tables, their high scores); a file the host does not have goes as "absent". In 96 KiB pieces (a message between cabinets carries 256 KiB at most; a Stern SAM ROM's memory is 128 KiB), each file checked by its SHA-256 fingerprint; the guest tells the host whether it received them whole. At launch, they replace the copies in the guest's copy of the table, and a file absent on the host is removed from it: both cabinets then start from factory settings. The guest's usual folder does not move. In the room: "Prêt, réglages de l'hôte reçus" (Ready, host's settings received); "en route" (on their way) while they travel; "non reçus : score hors classement" (not received: score not ranked) if they arrived damaged, too large (over 1 MiB per file), or if the guest's cabinet predates 30/10/2026; in the results, "Hors classement : réglages de l'hôte non reçus" (Not ranked: host's settings not received). To play with 5 balls, the host sets their own table to 5 before inviting. Settings specific to each cabinet do not travel: PinMAME's `default.cfg`, the table's display and sound options (`.ini`) ([reglages-de-l-hote.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/reglages-de-l-hote.test.ts)). **The group's high score board**: the scores of ranked live games, per exact version of the table (same `.vpx`, same script), kept by each cabinet that played in `<data>\amis\records.json`, twenty places ([groupboard.ts](../packages/core/src/groupboard.ts)). Each ranked player's best score goes in, once per game; out of the ranking (host's settings not received, another number of balls), it does not. The room shows "Record du groupe à battre : …" (Group record to beat) (or "Pas encore de record du groupe sur cette version", No group record on this version yet); the results, "Records du groupe sur cette version" (Group records on this version), the top five. In the game, the table's high score table is the group's: before launch, its records are written into the copy's `VPReg.stg`, in place of the host's, the last place left at zero, so that each player's score always enters it and can be read on return. Only the values are rewritten, in place, within the room already allocated to each: the structure of the file, which VPX reads through Windows, does not change; credits and settings (the host's number of balls) stay. Between friends, the knocker only rings for the group record beaten; these records are not sent to friends like those of a solo game. A ROM table (PinMAME: T2, Star Trek, The Walking Dead) keeps its high score table in the ROM memory: it is read and written in the copy's `<rom>.nv` according to that ROM's map (Pinball Memory Maps, cached in `<data>\cache\cartes-memoire`), mirrored copies and checksums redone (one wrong checksum, and the ROM restores its factory high scores); then everything is read back, and a single wrong checksum cancels the write ([romscores.ts](../packages/core/src/romscores.ts)). Checked on 30/10/2026 on the cabinet's memories: the 134 checksums of T2, 73 of Star Trek and 23 of The Walking Dead, correct according to this computation, stay correct after the high score table, the only thing changed, is written. No map for the ROM, a map without `validation`, a 4-bit memory: nothing is written or read, nothing enters the board ("Aucun score classé pour l'instant", No ranked score yet). Without a `.nv` (the host has none: factory settings), the ROM starts from its factory high scores: a score lower than the last one cannot be read ([tableau-du-groupe.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/tableau-du-groupe.test.ts), [records-rom.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/records-rom.test.ts)). **Invitation during a game**: the cabinet no longer answers "busy", it leaves the choice to the player. The chime rings (the only interface sound allowed during a game, with the coil thump), and a discreet card appears over VPX, in the playfield's top left corner as the player sees it (a recording's indicator holds the right corner): "Bruno vous invite à jouer" (Bruno invites you to play), the table, the time left and "Quittez la partie pour le rejoindre, ou continuez : elle s'efface seule" (Leave the game to join them, or keep playing: it fades away by itself). A window without focus, that clicks go through and that no recording sees ([app/src/game-notice.ts](../app/src/game-notice.ts), [gamenotice.ts](../packages/core/src/gamenotice.ts)): VPX keeps control. After 12 s, it shrinks to a pill "Invitation de Bruno" (Invitation from Bruno) with the time left, until the invitation expires, is cancelled or the game ends. Nothing to do to ignore it; leaving your game brings you back to the menu, where the invitation opens by itself if it is still valid. Only a live game already in progress answers "busy". In the room, the host reads "Invité, en partie : notifié sur sa table, il peut quitter la sienne pour vous rejoindre" (Invited, in a game: notified on their table, they can leave theirs to join you). Seeing the others' score, ball and DMD during the game is the next step (§ 13.3 of the design), to be decided on a mockup.

**What others see.** Everyone: the name and tag. Friends: presence, the table being played, high scores, badges, games, and the library if it is shared (its tables, their parts and their media; a ROM or a PuP-Pack only if the friend ticks them, attesting they have the right to). Connected cabinets see the IP address, as in any peer-to-peer exchange.

**Tests.** Never the real network during a test: `socialNetMode` turns the network off as soon as a test variable is set (autopilot, captures, stress, `CYBERPINBALL_HOME` sandbox, simulated screens), except `CYBERPINBALL_RESEAU=simule`: an in-process HyperDHT test network and an in-memory local network, on 127.0.0.1 only, with a simulated firewall rule. `CYBERPINBALL_RESEAU=non` also turns it off in normal use.

Code: decisions in [packages/core/src/social.ts](../packages/core/src/social.ts) (profile, messages, directory, friendship, events), [friendlibrary.ts](../packages/core/src/friendlibrary.ts) (shared library, file messages) and [livegame.ts](../packages/core/src/livegame.ts) (invitation, room, start, results), transport in `app/src/social-net.ts` (the only module that opens sockets), services in `app/src/friend-content.ts` and `app/src/live-game.ts`, screens in `app/src/friends-menu.ts`, `app/src/friend-library-menu.ts` and `app/src/live-game-menu.ts`. A player's name always follows their most recent signed profile, wherever it comes from (hello, relayed directory, beacon), never an older one (`learnProfiles`, `renamePeer`). Proofs: [social.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/social.test.ts) (names, signatures, trapped messages, directory, friendship rules on both sides, badges, network mode), [social-net.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/social-net.test.ts) (two cabinets on a test network: directory, request, acceptance even with a cabinet off, presence, high score, removal, local network without the Internet, blocking; no socket outside 127.0.0.1; no other module loads Hyperswarm, `net` or `dgram`), [firewall.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/firewall.test.ts) (netsh output in every language, same rule in the installer, never during an update, silently or in a sandbox), [friend-services.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/friend-services.test.ts) (library shared by default and refused once turned off, media received identical to the byte, unknown id refused, whole table part by part with no saves, program or untaken ROM, trapped path refused, live game from invitation to results), [friends-menu.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/friends-menu.test.ts) (each screen, sharing by default, each part of a table leading to its own action and the ROM unticked, countdown cursor, invitation greyed out with its reason and "Jouer avec un ami"), [invitation-en-partie.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/invitation-en-partie.test.ts) (invitation received during a game: kept, never "busy"; card then pill, playfield corner in all four orientations, window without focus or capture, opened on return to the menu). Off-screen demo: `.\docs\demo-amis.ps1` ("amis" route: fictional cabinets, a friend playing who beats their high score, a request, a nearby cabinet, an offline friend, Bruno's library and one media item taken into the draft; "partie" route: Bruno's invitation, the countdown, a simulated game, the results, on a fake VPX installation (`New-FauxVpx` in `docs\hors-ecran.ps1`), never the real emulators; "en-jeu" route: a game started by Start, Bruno's invitation during the game (card, then pill), then the invitation opened on return to the menu, on the same fake installation; captures in `tmp\demo-amis`). Their tables are made up (`DEMO_TABLES` in `app/src/social-demo.ts`, no real game: [demo-tables-inventees.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/demo-tables-inventees.test.ts)). `-Accueil`: both routes, on the `borne` screens, tables and artwork regenerated, then the images of the project page's Friends section, `docs\images\<language>\friends.webp` ("My friends" and Bruno's page), `friends-library.webp` (his library and his table the cabinet does not have) and `live-game.webp` (his invitation and the shared start; for the game, Bruno has the same version of the table), playfield turned upright.

**Limits.** The score during the game is not read (VPX only records it at the end): a live game only compares high scores entered on the table's high score table and play times. One file at a time per friend; an interrupted transfer resumes where it stopped as soon as the friend is back (see "Download manager"). The directory only knows the cabinets seen since sharing started; two players must be on at the same time to find each other, and behind two symmetric NATs (CGNAT, 4G tethering) with no third cabinet to relay, the connection can fail. Adding the firewall rule for real (UAC) has only been tried in principle, never on the development machine's real firewall.

## Setup wizard and configuration menu

The setup wizard (`app/src/wizard.ts`, screens `app/renderer/screen.*`) runs at first start, or through "Assistant complet" (Full setup wizard), and goes through: **buttons** (capturing each button), **analog plunger** (sensor plunger, accelerometer), **tilt** (cabinet tilt bob or tilt bob simulated by VPX), **screens and roles** (playfield, backglass, DMD, topper), **playfield orientation and size** (flipper edge, flipping the screen in Windows if VPX needs it, diagonal), **screen layout** (zones), **sound** (audio outputs, speaker test), then the summary and the writing of `cabinet.json` and `VPinballX.ini`. The list of steps, what each one decides and its keys are in [packages/core/src/wizardsteps.ts](../packages/core/src/wizardsteps.ts) (`WIZARD_PHASES`, `WIZARD_STEPS`), tested in [wizardsteps.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/wizardsteps.test.ts).

**System menu (Exit, Tab or ⚙) > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration)** gathers everything that sets up the cabinet. Each step can be redone there on its own and returns to the table menu, saved or not:

| Entry | Option | What is redone | `VPinballX.ini` keys written |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| "Boutons du jeu" (Game buttons) | `--boutons` | game button capture and test | `[Input]` |
| "Boutons de l'interface" (Interface buttons) | `--interface` | capture and test of the buttons that drive Cyber Pinball | none |
| "Lanceur analogique" (Analog plunger) | `--lanceur` | sensor plunger detection, direction, dead zone, speed, accelerometer, test | sensor `[Input]` keys |
| "Tilt" | `--tilt` | cabinet tilt bob or tilt bob simulated by VPX, contact angle, nudge test | `[Player] SimulatedPlumb`, `PlumbThresholdAngle` |
| "Écrans et rôles" (Screens and roles) | `--ecrans` | the screen of each role; flipper edge and diagonal only if the playfield moves to another screen | VPX windows and framing |
| "Orientation et taille du playfield" (Playfield orientation and size) | `--playfield` | flipper edge, screen flip, diagonal | VPX windows and framing |
| "Disposition des écrans" (Screen layout) | `--disposition` | zone of each role on its screen | VPX windows and framing |
| "Son de la borne" (Cabinet sound) | `--son` | backglass and playfield outputs, speaker test | `SoundDevice`, `SoundDeviceBG`, `Sound3D` |
| "Assistant complet" | | all the steps in a row | all managed keys |
| "Touches par défaut des encodeurs" (Default encoder keys) | | immediate reset of both button sets, no wizard | `[Input]` |
| "Configurations de la borne" (Cabinet configurations) | | choose, rename, trash, new configuration | |

A single step starts from the saved model (roles, screens, flipper edge), changes only its own fields in `cabinet.json` (`stepCabinet`) and writes only its own `VPinballX.ini` keys (the `only` option of the sync), even ones changed by hand; the other keys stay as they are until the next sync from the menu. The summary shows only those keys; Exit there restarts the step, Backspace goes back to the last question. Windows and framing: `[Player] Playfield*`, `[Backglass]`, `[ScoreView]`, `[Topper]`, `BGSet`, `ScreenWidth`, `ScreenHeight`, `CabinetAutofitMode`.

**Leaving without saving, at any step** (layout editor, flip countdown and summary included): the "Quitter sans enregistrer ?" (Leave without saving?) question opens with

- the **"Quitter sans enregistrer"** (Leave without saving) button, always shown at the top right of every screen (also clickable in the layout editor);
- **Ctrl+Q** on a keyboard; Ctrl+Q again answers yes;
- **Esc or Exit held for 2 s** (the button shows "Maintenu : quitter sans enregistrer ? dans 1 s" (Held: leave without saving? in 1 s));
- **during button capture, any key held for 3 s**, which is not captured. At that point the wizard does not yet know which key is Exit: this is the only gesture that works with any encoder. So that it does not get in the way of the capture, a button is captured **on release**: a press on a cabinet button lasts well under a second, the difference is invisible, and a held key is never captured (nothing to undo). Outside button capture, only a held Esc or Exit opens the question: held flippers keep their effect (speeding up a setting in the editor);
- **Exit (or Backspace) on the first question** of a step, when there is nothing before it: the playfield in the full wizard, the flipper edge in orientation alone, the first output in sound alone, the editor in layout alone.

The question says what would be lost ("Boutons : Flipper gauche sur A" (Buttons: Left flipper on A), "DMD : …", "Flippers à gauche de l'image" (Flippers on the left of the image), "Son…" (Sound…), "Écran du playfield : remis dans son orientation d'origine" (Playfield screen: restored to its original orientation), or "Rien n'a été changé" (Nothing was changed)) and where you will end up. **Start (or Enter): yes** · **Exit, Esc or Backspace: no** · the same gesture again (a key held again): yes · with no answer within 20 s, the wizard resumes. During button capture, the Start and Exit already captured also answer. Yes: nothing is written, neither `cabinet.json` nor `VPinballX.ini`; an unconfirmed screen orientation is restored; back to the table menu or, on first start without a model (the menu cannot open), Cyber Pinball closes, as announced on screen ("l'assistant reprendra au prochain démarrage" (the wizard will resume at next start)). A test pins the invariant: the question comes before any step in `handleKey`, the mouse before any other action, every state sent to the screens carries the button, and nothing is written outside the two save functions.

**"Placer librement…"** (Place freely…): at the screens step, after the whole screens and the ready-made spots (bottom of the backglass in 4:1 or 16:9, inset in the playfield), the backglass, DMD and topper offer "Placer librement…". Start opens the layout editor on that role, placed on its saved screen (otherwise a free screen, otherwise the backglass screen), with the cursor on the "Zone" line; "Revenir à l'assistant" (Back to the wizard) keeps the chosen spot and moves on to the next role, Exit returns to the question as it was.

## Interface language

Everything Cyber Pinball displays exists in French and in English: table menu and game pages, system menu, setup wizard, VPX settings, the "Get" window, and the messages and findings of the core (doctor, synchronisation, import, update). Dates and numbers follow the language (`fr-FR` or `en-GB`). Console logs, test reports and files written for other tools stay in French.

- **By default**: the Windows language if it is French, English otherwise (`app.getPreferredSystemLanguages()`).
- **System menu > "Cyber Pinball" > "Language / Langue"**: the entry is named in both languages, so it can be found whatever language is shown, and each language is offered in its own words ("Français", "English"). The choice, made with the cabinet buttons like everything else, is kept in `<data>\language.json` (`{ "language": "en" }`); the menu restarts at once through the screen guard (`relaunchCovered`), as for a change of configuration, and reopens its windows in the new language.
- **`CYBERPINBALL_LANGUE=fr|en`** forces the language (tests, screenshots); a choice made in the menu also replaces it for the restart.

**Method** (`packages/core/src/i18n.ts`): every displayed text goes through `t('texte français', { parameter })`, its French form being the key; the English catalogue, split by part of the interface in `packages/core/src/i18n/en/*.ts`, gives its translation. Parameters go in braces (`t('{role} : aucun écran', { role })`), plurals through `tn(n, '{n} table', '{n} tables')` (0 and 1 are singular in French, only 1 in English), lists through `formatList` ("a, b et c" / "a, b and c"), dates and numbers through `formatDate`, `formatDateTime`, `formatNumber`. The language is set once per process, at start-up (`app/src/language.ts`, imported by `main.ts` before the modules that display anything): no text is translated while a module loads, and the label tables of the core (`ROLES`, `BUTTONS`, `WIZARD_STEPS`…) are getters (`get label()`). Windows receive the language and the catalogue through their preload (`cyberI18n`); `app/renderer/i18n.js`, loaded before the other scripts of each page, gives them the same `t`, `tn` and `uiLocale()`, and translates the fixed texts of the pages, marked `data-i18n` (with a context key, `data-i18n="…"`, when one French text has two meanings).

**Invariant** (`packages/core/src/test/i18n.test.ts`, run by `npm test`): the scan of the interface sources (`i18n-scan.ts`: `app/src`, `app/renderer`, `packages/core/src`, `packages/installer/src`, `packages/win-displays/src`) collects every key passed to `t()`, `tn()` and `data-i18n`, and fails on a key without translation, a key that is not a literal (`${…}` template, variable), different `{parameters}` between a key and its translation, one key translated in two ways, a translation left in French, a key nothing uses any more, and a text that looks French outside `t()` (accents, common words; comments and console logs excepted). A text that must not be translated (file name, search pattern, proper noun) carries `// i18n-ignore` at the end of its line. The test also checks that no text is translated while the core modules load. By hand, on a few files:

```bash
npx tsc -b packages/core && node packages/core/dist/test/i18n-scan.js app/src/wizard.ts app/renderer/screen.js
```

At run time, a text requested without a translation is shown in French and the console reports it once ("Traduction manquante : …").

## Menu icons

Every menu entry carries an icon of its own, and every screen opened from the system menu shows its entry's icon before its title: you can tell at a glance where you are. The icons were drawn for Cyber Pinball (art direction: Codex, briefs and answers in `docs/recherche/icones`), with no icon font and no online resource.

- **A single registry**: [app/renderer/icons.js](../app/renderer/icons.js) draws each icon once (`CYBER_ICONS`, SVG geometry only on a 24 grid) and `cyberIcon(name)` places it in the page. Every window loads it before its scripts; the design system reads it too (`docs/design-system/src/icones.mjs`, "Icônes" sheet). The names known to the code are in [packages/core/src/icons.ts](../packages/core/src/icons.ts) (`ICON_NAMES`, `IconName` type).
- **Declarative**: an entry picks its icon with an `icon` field — required on `SystemItem` (system menu), optional on `Action`, `Row` and `Built` (screen title) in `app/src/library.ts`; `WizardStep.icon` for the steps of "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet setup), `EssentialGroup.icon` for the groups of the VPX settings. A screen opened from the system menu that declares no icon takes its entry's (catalogue, adding a table, ROMs, media), except a table's screens (game page, doctor…), titled with the game's name.
- **Drawing and sizes**: a 2-unit stroke for the whole family (`theme.css`, `.icon`), in the text color. For a 1,080 px short side: 30 px before a menu entry, 24 px from the label and aligned on its first line; 60 px before a title; 38 px for the gear (72 px click area); 22 to 26 px in the tabs and groups of the settings; never under 20 px.
- **States**: the icon follows its label (dark on the green bar, red at rest for a destructive action, dark on the red bar once selected); the "waiting for the user" dot sits at the top right of the icon, as on the gear.
- **No icon** before a value, a choice in a list, a table or a configuration: only before what opens or does something. "Retour" (Back) carries the `back` arrow in menus whose entries have icons.

**Adding an icon**: draw it in the right family of `icons.js` (`path`, `circle`, `rect`… elements and their geometry attributes only, live area 3 to 21), add its name to `ICON_NAMES`, declare it on the entry, rebuild the design system (`node docs/design-system/src/construire.mjs`) and look at it in the sheet, at 22, 30 and 60 px.

**Invariants**: [icons.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/icons.test.ts) (run by `npm test`) checks that the registry and `ICON_NAMES` have exactly the same names, that each drawing is geometry only within the 24 frame, that no two names draw the same icon, that every page drawing an icon loads the registry before its scripts, that no screen draws an SVG itself, and that the entries of "Configuration de la borne" and the settings groups each have their own. On screen, the layout guard (`app/src/layout-check.ts`) reports an unknown icon, one smaller than 20 px, or the same icon for two entries of a menu (`data-icon-menu`). Off-screen demonstration, in French and English, on the cabinet (playfield lying sideways) and on a desktop screen:

```powershell
.\docs\demo-icones.ps1 [-Langues fr, en] [-Ecrans borne, poste-deux-ecrans] [-Etapes menu, reglages]
```

## Cabinet buttons

**Two independent sets of buttons.** The **game** buttons (flippers, Start, Exit, coin, plunger, MagnaSaves, Extra Ball, pause, tilt, nudges, volume) are what VPX receives during a game: `cabinet.json` > `buttons`, written to `[Input] Mapping.*`. The **interface** buttons (Previous, Next, Confirm, Back and menu; optional: Cheat sheet (Aide-mémoire), VPX settings) drive Cyber Pinball: table menu, details screens, VPX settings, wizard, warnings; `cabinet.json` > `navigation`, which VPX does not read. Each set is captured, summarised and tested on its own, through the same screen and functions (`KeySet`: `GAME_KEYS`, `INTERFACE_KEYS`; `recordKey`, `keyProblems`, `keysToTry`, `packages/core/src/buttons.ts`); keyboard key or joystick button, freely from one button to the next, and one physical button can serve both sets (each set only refuses its own duplicates).

- **By default**, the interface is on the same physical buttons as the game flippers, Start and Exit (`defaultNavigation`). A cabinet saved before the interface buttons existed (no `navigation`) therefore keeps its navigation on its game buttons, with nothing to redo (`navigationButtons`, soft migration). Once the interface buttons are saved, capturing the game differently no longer changes them.
- **Keyboard fallback keys**, always active: Tab (menu and configuration), left / up arrows (previous), right / down (next), Enter (confirm), Esc (back). An interface button cannot take another role's fallback key ("Entrée reste la touche de secours « Valider »", Enter stays the "Confirm" fallback key, `refusedNavigationKey`). H and F1 (cheat sheet) and R (VPX settings) stay active as long as no cabinet button, game or interface, sends them (`interfaceKeys`, `withoutButtonKeys`).
- **The whole interface reads its own buttons**: menu (`interfaceKeys`), VPX settings, "other frontend open" warning, wizard (`navigationKeys`), always given the whole cabinet (`ButtonsSource`), never the game buttons alone (invariant test in `buttons.test.ts`). The "During a game" cheat sheet and the in-game display adjustment stay the game's (`gameKeys`, `displayKeys`).
- **Inconsistent capture** of the interface buttons (`navigationProblems`): the interface falls back to the game flippers, Start and Exit, and the menu offers at startup to redo the step ("Boutons de l'interface à refaire", Interface buttons need redoing; Start: `--interface`).

This is the first step of the wizard, since everything else is driven with the buttons: the screen asks "Appuyez sur le bouton" (Press the button) for left flipper, right flipper, Start, Exit, coin, plunger, then the optional buttons (left and right MagnaSave, Extra Ball, Pause, tilt bob, slam tilt, nudges to the left, to the right and forward, volume − and +), which Exit skips. The same question is shown on every screen (upright for the player on the previous playfield), above the list of all buttons: those already captured with their key, the current one, those still to come, so you can decide in advance which cabinet button will play which role. A key already captured for another button is refused, as are the Windows key and keys VPX cannot read; a button counts when it is released, only once, and a key held for 3 s opens "Quitter sans enregistrer ?" (Leave without saving?) instead of being captured.

**Full wizard**: the game buttons first, then the interface buttons, offered on the same buttons as the flippers, Start and Exit just captured: "Valider" (Confirm, Start) keeps them without a test; one button can be redone on its own, or "Tout relever" (Capture all) asks for them one by one (Previous, Next, Confirm, Back and menu, then Cheat sheet and VPX settings, which Back skips). On the interface summary, Previous, Next, Confirm and Back whose key changed must have been pressed before Confirm; on the game summary, the flippers, Start and Exit. The interface keys in effect, the arrows and Enter also choose and run on both summaries, without counting as a test. With no answer on the first step (20 s), both sets keep their keys and the wizard moves on to the screens.

This button-by-button capture is the one for a new cabinet (no keys saved yet); each button still to come shows, dimmed, the key currently in effect, i.e. the encoders' key. On a cabinet that is already configured, and always for the step on its own, the step opens straight on the summary: each button with its current key, nothing to redo. A single button can be changed on its own: click its line, or use the flippers to reach its line (after the actions) then Start; press the new button and you are back at the summary, the other keys kept (Backspace cancels). "Tout relever" (Capture all) starts the full capture over.

A mistake can be fixed without the cabinet buttons, since those are precisely what is being captured: click a line in the list (that button is asked again, then the step resumes at the first unanswered button), Backspace or "◀ Précédent" (◀ Previous) (the previous button; Backspace therefore cannot be captured as a button), "Touches par défaut" (Default keys) and "Tout relever", always shown and clickable (the pointer appears when you move the mouse).

The summary is also a test: the flippers choose the action ("Valider" (Confirm), "Précédent", "Touches par défaut", "Tout relever") or a button to redo, Start runs that choice. A flipper, Start or Exit whose key has just been captured (different from the one in effect) must have been pressed at least once before "Valider" is accepted (check mark in the list, "Essai des touches relevées : Flipper gauche ✓ · …" (Testing captured keys: Left flipper ✓ · …)): a wrong capture therefore cannot be saved without the player noticing. Kept keys and the encoders' default keys are known and need no test: "Touches par défaut" then "Enregistrer" (Save) saves at once. A keyboard's arrows and Enter also choose and run, without counting as a test. "Valider" moves on to the screens (or saves, for the step on its own). In the full wizard, if nobody presses anything for 20 seconds, the current keys are kept (the encoders' keys on a new cabinet): an unattended cabinet thus moves on to the screens as before.

On their own: system menu (Exit) > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Boutons du jeu" (Game buttons; or "Boutons de l'interface", Interface buttons, `npm run assistant -- --interface`, which writes nothing to `VPinballX.ini`: "VPinballX.ini inchangé : VPX ne lit pas les boutons de l'interface."; in that step, "Touches par défaut" gives back the game flippers, Start and Exit in effect), `npm run assistant -- --boutons`, the launcher's `boutons` action, or the "Affecter les boutons de la borne" (Assign the cabinet buttons) action of a key in the VPX settings. The rest of the model is kept; only the `[Input]` keys are taken over.

**Access to configuration without the buttons**: a wrong capture (for example Exit on a button you can no longer find) must never lock you out of the settings. From the table menu and its messages, a discreet gear at the top right (⚙, clickable on top of everything, more visible when you move the mouse) and a keyboard's Tab key open the system menu and its sections (Tables, Médias, Borne, Cyber Pinball): add a table, VPX settings, cabinet configuration (buttons, screens, playfield, layout, sound, full wizard, default encoder keys, configurations), ROM library, quit. In these menus, Tab and Shift+Tab move the cursor whatever buttons have been captured, and every line can be clicked. Tab and Backspace are reserved: the "Boutons" (Buttons) step refuses them, and a capture containing one is treated as inconsistent (default keys).

**Default encoder keys** (left / right Shift, 1, Esc, 5, Enter…), the fallback when a saved capture no longer drives the cabinet: system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" > "Touches par défaut des encodeurs" (Default encoder keys) (with the mouse: click "Exit : menu" (Exit: menu) at the foot of the menu, then the lines), "Remettre les touches par défaut des encodeurs" (Restore the default encoder keys) in the details of a key in the VPX settings ("Avancé" (Advanced) > Input), and, without any button, the launcher's `boutons-defaut` action (`node app/dist/sync-cli.js --boutons-par-defaut`). `cabinet.json` goes back to the default keys, for both sets (the interface on the encoders' flippers, Start and Exit), and `[Input]` is rewritten: VPX actions that had lost their key get it back (`withDefaultButtons`, `app/src/buttons-reset.ts`).

**Inconsistent capture** (required button with no key, the same key for two buttons, a refused key, for example after editing `cabinet.json` by hand): `buttonProblems` detects it, the whole interface falls back to the default keys (`buttonKeys`), the sync stops writing `[Input]` and reports it, and the menu offers at startup to redo the "Boutons" step.

The keys are stored in `cabinet.json` (`buttons` for the game, `navigation` for the interface, `KeyboardEvent.code` codes: `ShiftLeft`, `Digit1`… or joystick tokens): the interface reads `navigation` (or, without it, the flippers, Start and Exit of `buttons`) in the wizard, menu, details screen, table management and VPX settings (`navigationKeys`, `interfaceKeys`, `packages/core/src/buttons.ts`); the in-game key reminder (`gameKeys`) and `[Input]` read `buttons`. A model without `buttons` keeps the default keys and leaves `[Input]` alone.

**What VPX expects** (read from `src/input/InputManager.cpp` and `InputAction.cpp` at commit `af26b2d93` of 10.8.1-5436): VPX no longer stores DirectInput codes (`LFlipKey`, `JoyLFlipKey`… no longer exist). Each action has its own `[Input] Mapping.<action>` key, a list of alternatives separated by ` | `, each one a combination separated by ` & `; a keyboard key is written `Key;<scancode SDL>` (HID usage: A = 4, 1 = 30, Enter = 40, left Shift = 225), a gamepad button `SDLJoy_<GUID SDL>_<n>;<bouton>`. An empty value means VPX's default key. Cyber Pinball translates `KeyboardEvent.code` into an SDL scancode (`SDL_SCANCODES`) and writes:

| Button | `[Input]` keys (VPX default) |
| --- | --- |
| Left / right flipper | `Mapping.LeftFlipper`, `LeftStagedFlipper`, `UILeft` (`Key;225`) / `RightFlipper`, `RightStagedFlipper`, `UIRight` (`Key;229`) |
| Start, Exit | `Mapping.Start` (`Key;30`), `Mapping.ExitGame` (`Key;41`) |
| Coin, plunger | `Mapping.Credit1` (`Key;34`), `Mapping.LaunchBall` (`Key;40`) |
| Left / right MagnaSave | `Mapping.LeftMagna`, `UIUp` (`Key;224`) / `RightMagna`, `UIDown` (`Key;228`) |
| Extra Ball, Pause, tilt bob | `Mapping.ExtraBall` (`Key;5`), `Mapping.Pause` (`Key;19`), `Mapping.Tilt` (`Key;23`) |
| Slam tilt (coin door) | `Mapping.SlamTilt` (`Key;74`) |
| Nudges | `Mapping.LeftNudge` (`Key;29`), `RightNudge` (`Key;56`), `CenterNudge` (`Key;44`) |
| Volume − / + | `Mapping.VolumeDown` (`Key;45`), `Mapping.VolumeUp` (`Key;46`) |

- A skipped optional button keeps VPX's default key.
- **Pause**: VPX's `Pause` action stops the game, and pressing it again resumes it (`SetPlayState(!IsPlaying())`, `InputManager.cpp`). Up to 0.1.3-essai.6, no cabinet button led to it: only the P key of a plugged-in keyboard paused. A capture made before this button does not have it: P stays the pause key, unless another button took it; redo "Boutons du jeu" (Game buttons) to give it to a cabinet button.
- Only single keyboard keys are replaced: a gamepad button or a combination added in VPX's menu (F12, "Input Settings") stays alongside.
- Another VPX action whose default key is that of a button loses it (`Key;0`, no key: an empty value would give it back): otherwise an Extra Ball button on D would also open the debugger. Duplicates that VPX intends are kept (Volume − and Service 7 share `-`). The action gets its key back when the conflict goes away. The summary and the sync's findings name these actions.
- These keys are managed like the others: a key changed by hand in VPX is reported at startup, kept or reset as you choose (`[Input] Mapping.LeftFlipper = Key;4 au lieu de Key;225`). The VPX settings show them in the "Avancé" tab, Input section, with the names of the action and of the key.
- Table scripts do not see the difference: `LeftFlipperKey`, `StartGameKey`… hold the action's identifier, not the key (`ScriptGlobalTable.cpp`). VPX also sends the script the DirectInput code of each key pressed, which an old table can test directly.

**Joystick buttons (Pinscape on a KL25Z, VirtuaPin, encoder in gamepad mode)**: a Pinscape controller sends its buttons as a USB joystick's by default, not as keyboard keys. Cyber Pinball reads them with VPX's own SDL 3 library (`SDL364.dll` next to `VPinballX64.exe`), loaded through koffi and initialised the way VPX initialises it (`SDL_INIT_JOYSTICK | SDL_INIT_GAMEPAD`, no hints; `src/input/SDLInputHandler.h` at commit `af26b2d93`): same GUIDs, same joystick order, same button numbering. VPX names a joystick `SDLJoy_<GUID SDL>_<rank>`, the rank telling apart two joysticks with the same GUID (`joystickDeviceIds`), and a button by its SDL number, from 0. Polled 100 times a second in the main process (`packages/win-displays/src/joystick.ts`, `app/src/joystick-input.ts`), without SDL's event queue or a message pump that would hijack Electron's.

- **The whole interface**: each press reaches the Cyber Pinball window that has focus as a key, whose code (`KeyboardEvent.code`) is VPX's token for the button (`SDLJoy_…_1;3`). The "Boutons" (Buttons) step captures it like a key ("Bouton 4 du joystick", Joystick button 4, numbered from 1 as in Windows' "Game controllers" and the Pinscape configuration tool), refuses it twice, and the menu, the table page, the VPX settings, the "other frontend open" warning and the "Récupérer" (Fetch) window (Exit) follow it. A held button repeats like a key (500 ms, then 30 times a second): a held flipper scrolls the menu. During a game no Cyber Pinball window has focus: presses go nowhere. During capture, a line says what is read: "Joystick lu : mjrnet Pinscape Controller" (Joystick read), "Aucun joystick branché" (No joystick connected), or "Joysticks lus une fois VPX installé" (Joysticks are read once VPX is installed).
- **Keyboard and joystick mixed**: keyboard keys and joystick buttons are captured along the same path (`recordButton`), freely from one button to the next: flippers on the Pinscape, Start and credit on an encoder in keyboard mode, for instance. Windows sends keyboard keys to the foreground window, whereas Cyber Pinball hands joystick buttons to the window Electron considers active; a window can be active without being in the foreground (a relaunched process that Windows refuses the foreground): on the real cabinet (0.1.3-essai.1), joystick buttons were captured, keyboard keys no longer were. Each joystick button press therefore checks that the foreground belongs to Cyber Pinball and takes it back otherwise (`keyboardFollows`, `app/src/foreground.ts`), with a warning in the console ("Clavier : le premier plan de Windows était à …", Keyboard: the Windows foreground was with …) and, in the "Boutons" step, "clavier repris à « window » (process)" (keyboard taken back from …; "clavier retenu par …", keyboard held by …, if Windows refuses) after the joystick line; the wizard takes it when it opens. Focus moving from one wizard screen to another no longer makes it forget a key being held down.
- **Keyboard key releases**: capture counts a button when it is released, and guest mode follows the buttons held down. Yet cancelling a key press in `before-input-event` also makes Chromium drop its release (`RenderWidgetHostImpl`, `suppress_events_until_keydown_`): until 27 October 2026, no keyboard key was captured (the release never arrived, and the key "held" opened "Quitter sans enregistrer ?" (Quit without saving?) after 3 s), whereas joystick buttons, handed over without going through Chromium, were captured; guest mode considered a released key as still held. Windows that read releases (wizard, menu) therefore go through `onKeyInput` (`app/src/key-input.ts`): the handled press is not cancelled there, the page cancels it (`app/src/preload.ts`, in the capture phase, after asking the main process whether it handled it), and the release arrives. [buttons.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/buttons.test.ts) rejects any `before-input-event` handler that reads releases itself; [clavier-reel.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/clavier-reel.test.ts) captures, off screen, every game and interface button with real keys (`sendInputEvent`), as well as with SDL's virtual joystick, and checks what is saved.
- **`[Input]`**: each of the button's actions gets the joystick token first, and keeps VPX's keyboard key alongside (`SDLJoy_…_1;0 | Key;225`), unless another cabinet button took it. A new capture removes the joystick buttons written the previous time and keeps those added in F12 > "Input Settings" (`withButtonMapping`). VPX's in-game menu follows the combination of both MagnaSaves, joystick buttons included (`SDLJoy_…;6 & SDLJoy_…;7`).
- **Check after each game**: VPX writes the joysticks it sees to `vpinball.log` ("Joystick connected: mjrnet Pinscape Controller #1 (… VPX UID: SDLJoy_…_1)"). If VPX saw joysticks but not the one of the captured buttons (`joysticksUnseenByVpx`), Cyber Pinball and VPX do not name it the same way: the menu says so ("Boutons du joystick introuvables dans VPX", Joystick buttons not found by VPX) and Start reopens the "Boutons" step.
- **Outside the capture**: the Pinscape's analog plunger and accelerometer are axes, assigned by the "Lanceur" (Plunger) step (see [Analog plunger and accelerometer](#analog-plunger-and-accelerometer)). Hats and axes assigned to an action in VPX stay alongside the buttons, like a gamepad's.
- **Without VPX installed**, or if the SDL library is missing, joysticks are not read (the step says so): the keyboard, the encoders' default keys and the mouse remain.

### Leaving a long list: the list and the screen actions

A management screen that shows a **content list** (the tables of "Ajouter à un jeu" (Add to a game), the cards of "Médias du jeu" (Game media), the tiles of a file gallery, a friend's library, the files and places of the file picker, the lines of the cabinet health page, the downloads) has two zones: the list, and the screen actions ("Retour" (Back), the other gestures). The core decides by nature, never by length (`screenZones`, [packages/core/src/listzones.ts](../packages/core/src/listzones.ts)): a media card, a line to pick (`Row.pick`) or a health line is in the list; everything else is an action. A list of options (two or three settings to choose from, "Ce que montre cet écran" (What this screen shows)) declares it (`Section.options`) and stays a plain list of actions. A screen without a content list, or with nothing else, does not change.

- **The list is recognisable** by its frame (`.lib-zone`, `app/renderer/library.js`, `zoneFrame`) and its header bar, outside what scrolls: on the left the position of the choice ("17 / 37") or, outside it, the number of items; on the right the marker, a circled key and what it does: "Exit · Sortir de la liste" (Leave the list) while in it, "MagnaSave · Revenir à la liste" (Back to the list) otherwise (a click on it does the same). The zone holding the choice has its frame lit in teal; the other one dims a little. One focus only: green stays on the chosen item.
- **Exit in the list** brings back to the screen actions, on the entry it was entered from, otherwise on the one that does the same as Exit ("Retour", "Annuler" (Cancel)), otherwise on the first one (`leaveList`). A second Exit leaves the screen, as before. The help bar says so: "Exit ou Échap : sortir de la liste".
- **Left or right MagnaSave** (F6 on a keyboard, the Windows key that moves between panes) switches from one zone to the other without an extra step, and finds again the item left in the list (`switchZone`, `ZoneMemory`). The MagnaSave buttons are those of the wizard's "Boutons" (Buttons) step, otherwise the encoders' default keys (left and right Ctrl); a key already taken by an interface button keeps its role (`zoneSwitchKeys`, [buttons.ts](../packages/core/src/buttons.ts)). The switch happens on release, and only if nothing else was pressed meanwhile: the Ctrl of a shortcut (Ctrl+Shift+D) never switches.
- **A flipper press** follows the screen order and goes from one zone to the other at their edge, as before (`zoneStep`): a cabinet without MagnaSave always reaches its actions. **A held flipper** stays in its zone and stops at its end, without wrapping around (`zoneHold`); after one second, it speeds up (`holdMove`): letter by letter on a list sorted by name, section by section (one letter at most every quarter of a second, the initial shown large in the header bar; a table put first, the "probable" one, does not prevent the jump), three at a time otherwise.

Safeguards: [sortie-de-liste.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/sortie-de-liste.test.ts) fixes the invariant on every screen with zones (Exit from any item of the list leads to the actions, never out of the screen; the switch goes and comes back to the position left; a held flipper stays in its zone), checks that the renderer puts each content list in its frame with its marker, and lists every scrolling list in `app/renderer`: a new one fails the test until it is classified (content list, action list, or other with its reason; the VPX settings lists have their own navigation, Exit goes up one level there). In the real rendering, the layout safeguard reports "liste de contenu sans cadre ni repère" (content list without frame or marker). Off-screen demonstration: `.\docs\demo-sortie-de-liste.ps1` ("Ajouter à un jeu", "Médias du jeu", a backglass gallery, a friend's library; cabinet and desktop, French and English; captures in `tmp\demo-sortie-de-liste\captures`).

## Analog plunger and accelerometer

An **analog plunger** (potentiometer or sensor plunger, read by a Pinscape on KL25Z or another controller in joystick mode) is not a button: it is a joystick **axis**, whose value follows the plunger's travel. The wizard's "Lanceur" (Plunger) step assigns and sets it, along with the Pinscape's accelerometer for nudging ([app/src/plunger-step.ts](../app/src/plunger-step.ts), [packages/core/src/plunger.ts](../packages/core/src/plunger.ts)). It follows the interface buttons in the full wizard; on its own: system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Lanceur analogique" (Analog plunger) (`npm run assistant -- --lanceur`, `plunger` action of the launcher). Unattended demo, with a virtual SDL Pinscape: `docs\demo-lanceur.ps1 -Run`.

- **Detection**: "Tirez le lanceur à fond, puis lâchez-le" (Pull the plunger all the way, then let go). Cyber Pinball reads every axis of every joystick through VPX's SDL library (the same numbers as VPX); the axis that moved the most, and came back to rest, becomes the plunger, with its direction (pulling raises or lowers the value). The first readings of a freshly opened joystick don't count, and an axis that jumped without coming back (a gamepad trigger) gives way to the plunger. Meanwhile a gauge shows the axis that is moving.
- **Proposed setting**: a dead zone just above the noise measured at rest; on a six-axis Pinscape ("X/Y/Z + RX/RY/RZ" in its config tool) whose plunger is on Z, the **speed measured by the board** on RZ, more accurate than the one VPX computes; the same Pinscape's **accelerometer** on X and Y.
- **Test**: the gauge shows the position as VPX will compute it (dead zone, direction), from forward to fully pulled, with the furthest pull; each shot (plunger released) shows its strength. Lines: sensor (detect again), direction, dead zone (0 to 30 %, with the flippers), speed, accelerometer; then "Enregistrer" (Save) ("Continuer" (Continue) in the full wizard) or "Pas de lanceur analogique" (No analog plunger). Everything also works with the mouse.
- **Rest far from 0**: VPX has no offset, the plunger would look pulled all the time. The step says so: the rest position is set on the board (Pinscape calibration).
- **Already set**: a cabinet where the step was done opens on its setting; otherwise, a plunger already assigned in VPX (its menu, or its accepted Pinscape layout) is taken as is ("Réglage trouvé dans VPX", Setting found in VPX). In the full wizard, a cabinet with no joystick with axes and no known setting skips the step without writing anything.

Keys written (`vpxAnalogSettings`, format read in `PlungerHandler.cpp`, `PhysicsSensor.cpp`, `NudgeHandler.cpp` and `GamepadNudge.cpp` at commit `af26b2d93`); a sensor value is written `<joystick>;<axis>;<P|V|A>;<dead zone>;<scale>;<limit>`, the axis numbered like VPX (0x200 plus the SDL axis: Z is 514), a negative scale reversing the axis:

| `[Input]` key | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `PlungerSensorCount` | 1, or 0 with no analog plunger |
| `Mapping.Plunger0.Position` | `SDLJoy_…_1;514;P;0.020000;1.000000;1.000000` |
| `Mapping.Plunger0.Velocity` | `SDLJoy_…_1;517;V;0.000000;12.500000;1.000000` (Pinscape speed), otherwise empty |
| `NudgeSensorCount` | 1, or 0 with no accelerometer |
| `Mapping.Nudge0.Type`, `X`, `Y` | 0 (gamepad), `SDLJoy_…_1;512;A;0.000000;2.000000;1.000000`, `SDLJoy_…_1;513;A;…` (the scale is the nudge strength: ×2 proposed for a Pinscape, see [Tilt](#tilt)) |
| `Device.<joystick>.NoAutoLayout` | 1 for each joystick whose buttons or sensors Cyber Pinball writes |

- **VPX's default layout turned off**: VPX recognises a six-axis Pinscape and offers, during a game, to apply its layout (plunger on Z, accelerometer on X and Y). Accepting first erases everything coming from that joystick (`ApplyDefaultDeviceMapping`, `ClearDeviceMappings`), including the buttons mapped by Cyber Pinball. `NoAutoLayout` turns that offer off for the cabinet's joysticks; a Pinscape set to "X/Y/Z" only (three axes) was not recognised by VPX anyway, and its plunger only worked once assigned by hand.
- These keys belong to the "Lanceur" step (`isAnalogKey`) and no longer to the "Boutons" (Buttons) step; `NoAutoLayout` belongs to both. Like the other managed keys, a setting later changed in VPX's menu (the dead zone, for example) is reported, then kept or reset as you choose.
- The "Lanceur" (Launch) button (`Mapping.LaunchBall`) stays: it serves tables with an automatic plunger, and the Pinscape can send it when the plunger is pushed forward ("ZB Launch Ball", set on the board).

## Tilt

What makes the cabinet tilt is decided at the wizard's "Tilt" step ([app/src/tilt-step.ts](../app/src/tilt-step.ts), [packages/core/src/tilt.ts](../packages/core/src/tilt.ts)). It follows the "Lanceur" (Plunger) step in the full wizard, which skips it without writing anything on a cabinet with no accelerometer, no captured tilt bob and no saved tilt (VPX then keeps its simulated tilt bob); on its own: system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Tilt" (`npm run assistant -- --tilt`, `tilt` action of the launcher). Unattended demo, with a virtual SDL Pinscape: `docs\demo-tilt.ps1 -Run`.

**What VPX does with it** (VPX 10.8.1, commit `af26b2d93`):

- the **Tilt** action (`[Input] Mapping.Tilt`, T by default) reaches the table script as the `MechanicalTilt` key, which PinMAME scripts call `keyBangBack` (`scripts/VPMKeys.vbs`): `vpmKeyDown` calls `vpmNudge.DoMechTilt` (`WPC.vbs`, `s11.vbs`, `sega.vbs`…), which pulses the machine's tilt bob switch (`vpmNudge.TiltSwitch`, `core.vbs`). The ROM counts its warnings, then tilts, like the real machine;
- the **simulated tilt bob** (`physics/cabinet/PlumbHandler.cpp`) is a 10 cm pendulum driven by the cabinet's acceleration: the one VPX derives from the accelerometer's nudges (`GamepadNudge.cpp`: nudge intent, then a 113 kg cabinet on its legs) and from the nudge buttons. When it touches its ring, it presses the Tilt action itself. `[Player]` settings (`Settings_properties.inl`): `SimulatedPlumb` (1 by default), `PlumbThresholdAngle` (contact angle in degrees, 0.15 to 4, 1 by default; smaller, easier tilt), `PlumbDamping` (0 to 2, 1). A table's `.ini` can override them for that table (`PhysicsEngine.cpp` reads the table's settings);
- the **SlamTilt** action (`Mapping.SlamTilt`, Home by default) reaches the script as `keySlamDoorHit`: the machine's slam tilt switch (`swSlamTilt`, 21 on a WPC) stays closed while the button is held, and the ROM ends the game for every player.

The Pinscape guide (mjrnet, "Nudge & Tilt") wires a real tilt bob like a button, on a KL25Z input set as a joystick button (or the T key); with it, the simulated tilt bob is redundant: two tilt bobs, a single bump would count twice.

**The four cases, and what Cyber Pinball writes**:

| Cabinet | "Boutons" (Buttons) step | "Tilt" step | `VPinballX.ini` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Wired tilt bob (Pinscape or encoder) | "Balancier de tilt" (Tilt bob) captured (joystick button or T) | "Balancier de la borne" (Cabinet tilt bob) | `[Input] Mapping.Tilt = SDLJoy_…;9 \| Key;23`, `[Player] SimulatedPlumb = 0` |
| Accelerometer only | "Balancier de tilt" skipped (Exit) | "Balancier simulé par VPX" (Tilt bob simulated by VPX), contact angle | `[Player] SimulatedPlumb = 1`, `PlumbThresholdAngle = 0.5` (for example) |
| Coin door slam tilt | "Slam tilt" captured | no effect | `[Input] Mapping.SlamTilt = SDLJoy_…;11 \| Key;74` |
| Neither a tilt bob nor simulation | "Balancier de tilt" skipped | "Pas de tilt" (No tilt) | `[Player] SimulatedPlumb = 0`; a keyboard's T key stays the tilt |

**Capturing the tilt bob** ("Boutons" step): the optional "Balancier de tilt" (Tilt bob) button asks "Faites toucher l'anneau au balancier" (Make the tilt bob touch its ring) and recalls what it is (the pendulum against its ring, wired to a Pinscape or encoder input). It is captured like the others, from the joystick as from the keyboard (`recordButton`). A contact bounces, and a swinging tilt bob touches its ring again: the key just captured no longer counts for the next button for 1 s (`BOUNCE_MS`, `wizard.ts`), except after Exit skips a button (it is pressed several times in a row). "Slam tilt" comes next, optional too.

**The "Tilt" step**:

- **Proposal**: a captured tilt bob wins ("Balancier de la borne", simulated tilt bob off); without one, what VPX does today ("Réglage actuel de VPX : balancier simulé par VPX, contact à 1,00°", Current VPX setting: tilt bob simulated by VPX, contact at 1.00°). A cabinet whose step is done opens on its setting. "Tilt" line: Start (or a click) switches modes: "Balancier de la borne" and "Balancier simulé par VPX" with a captured tilt bob, otherwise "Balancier simulé par VPX" and "Pas de tilt".
- **Contact angle** (simulated tilt bob): Start, then the flippers, in 0.05° steps, from 0.15° to 4°; Start to finish. Smaller, easier tilt: 4° is the hardest.
- **Nudge strength** (with an accelerometer): the scale of the accelerometer's X and Y axes (`[Input] Mapping.Nudge0.X` and `.Y`, `NUDGE_GAIN` in [plunger.ts](../packages/core/src/plunger.ts)), from ×0.50 to ×6, in 0.25 steps; Start, then the flippers. VPX reads the accelerometer like a gamepad stick (`Mapping.Nudge0.Type = 0`, its own default layout for a Pinscape, `SDLInputHandler.h`): a value of 1 is worth g/2 m/s² (`GamepadNudge.cpp`), and any nudge under 1 m/s² is ignored (`NudgeIntentHandler.cpp`). But a Pinscape set to "1G" (its default setting) sends the acceleration in g, full scale at 1 g (`rawToReport`, the firmware's `main.cpp`): at scale 1, VPX ignores everything under 0.2 g and only sees half of the rest. On the real cabinet (October 2026), the ball barely moved and the simulated tilt bob never tilted, even at 4° (the hardest angle). At ×2, 1 g measured is 1 g in VPX: that is the strength proposed for a Pinscape ("Lanceur" step and "Tilt" step).
