Under the ring, the test shows the strongest nudge read in the last 3 seconds, as a percentage of the scale, and the threshold under which VPX ignores it (`nudgeFloor`: 20 % at ×1, 10 % at ×2); the tilt bob simulation follows the chosen strength. A strength set in VPX's menu (an axis scale, a reversed direction) is taken over as is. An accelerometer left at strength ×1 is reported at each synchronization ("Secousses : accéléromètre lu à la force ×1,00…", Nudging: accelerometer read at strength ×1.00…).
- **Test**: the contact ring seen from above. With the simulated tilt bob, the dot follows the tilt bob computed as VPX will compute it, one millisecond at a time, from the accelerometer axes read by VPX's SDL library (same axis shaping, same `Mapping.Nudge0.Strength`, same damping): each contact lights the ring, "TILT" under it, and counts as a Tilt press (the tilt bob bounces on the ring: a hard nudge often makes several, as in VPX); the footer gives the share of the contact reached and the nudge in g. With the cabinet tilt bob, the ring lights at each real contact ("Contacts du balancier : n", Tilt bob contacts: n). Without an accelerometer set ("Lanceur" step), the step says so: only the nudge buttons will move the simulated tilt bob.
- **Keys**: `[Player] SimulatedPlumb` and `PlumbThresholdAngle` (`vpxTiltSettings`, `isTiltKey`), only after the step (smooth migration: without it, `[Player]` stays as it is). Damping is left to VPX (F12 > "Nudge Settings"). Like the other managed keys, an angle changed later in a game is reported, kept or reset as you choose.

**Inconsistent settings reported** (`tiltFindings`, at each synchronization: result of the wizard and its steps, `sync` action): tilt bob captured, "Tilt" step never done and simulated tilt bob on (two tilt bobs); "Balancier de la borne" chosen without a captured button; simulated tilt bob without an accelerometer or nudge button; simulated tilt bob chosen but turned off by hand in `VPinballX.ini`.

**Table still on screen**: `[Player] NudgeStrength = 0`, at each synchronization, whether or not the "Tilt" step is done (`VPX_STILL_TABLE`, [tilt.ts](../packages/core/src/tilt.ts)). This VPX setting ("Visual Nudge Strength", from 0 to 2, 1 by default) only shifts the playfield image by as much as the simulated cabinet moves (`Renderer.cpp`: screen offset, or table translation without the static prerender). The ball, the simulated tilt bob and the script do not depend on it. On a cabinet, the real cabinet is what moves: the table stays still on screen and only the ball reacts to nudges (test on a real cabinet, October 2026). A managed key like the others: set back by hand in VPX, it is reported instead of being overwritten. A table's `.ini` can still override it for that table.

**"Pendant une partie" (During a game) reminder**: a single "Tilt" line (the reminder already fills the screen): the tilt bob's key or button, "secousse trop forte" (nudging too hard) when the simulated tilt bob is chosen, and "slam tilt" with its key if it has one (`gameKeys`).

## Cabinet configurations

The same installation can be used on several machines: three screens besides the main one on one PC, two on another, a cabinet. `data\cabinet.json` holds all the **configurations**; each one is a complete cabinet model (screens by EDID fingerprint, roles, zones, flipper edge, diagonal, sound, buttons, keys left to the user) with its **own `VPinballX.ini`** and its own memory of the keys written. Tables, ROMs, media, per-table settings and statistics stay shared. Code: [packages/core/src/configurations.ts](../packages/core/src/configurations.ts) (computation), [packages/core/src/store.ts](../packages/core/src/store.ts) (files), [app/src/configurations-menu.ts](../app/src/configurations-menu.ts) (screens), tests in [configurations.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/configurations.test.ts).

- **Migration**: a `cabinet.json` from before configurations becomes, the first time it is read, the "Borne" (Cabinet) configuration, taken over as is, which keeps `config\VPinballX.ini` and its memory; the old file is kept as `data\cabinet-v1.json`. Nothing to do.
- **Recognition** (at startup, before each game, `sync`): each configuration gets a score from the connected screens (`scoreConfiguration`): 100 per screen found by its serial number, 60 per screen found by model and port (weak fingerprint), 30 by model and size only (cable moved to another port), −50 per missing screen, −10 per extra connected screen. A configuration is a candidate with at least one screen found, at most one missing (a screen turned off) and no more extra screens than screens found. The only complete candidate is picked automatically; otherwise a choice already made for these same screens; otherwise the best candidate if it is alone with its score. A partly found configuration stays selected with a warning, and the sync warns about the role of the screen that is off (and blocks if it is the playfield), as before. Every choice is explained: "« Second poste » reconnue : 3 écrans sur 3 retrouvés (2 par numéro de série, 1 par modèle et port) — score 260, devant « Borne » (score -110)" ("Second poste" recognized: 3 of 3 screens found (2 by serial number, 1 by model and port), score 260, ahead of "Borne" (score -110)).
- **Ambiguity or no match**: nothing is written. The menu opens on the main screen with "Quelle configuration est branchée ?" (Which configuration is connected?): the known configurations, from closest to farthest with their score, "Nouvelle configuration" (New configuration) (full wizard) and "Plus tard" (Later) (the menu stays usable; a game asks for the configuration again). The choice is remembered for these screens (10 screen sets remembered) and the menu restarts on the right screens. On the command line: exit code 3, and `npm run sync -- --configuration "Nom"` makes the same choice.
- **Configuration change**: a sync finding ("Configuration changée : « Borne » à la place de « Second poste »…" (Configuration changed: "Borne" instead of "Second poste"…)) and a 12 s banner at the top of the menu, with no button to press.
- **System menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Configurations de la borne" (Cabinet configurations)** (its details name the active configuration, and say whether it is forced): the list with each one's score for the connected screens; for each one, "Utiliser avec ces écrans" (Use with these screens), "Toujours utiliser celle-ci" (Always use this one) (forced: recognition is suspended until "Reconnaissance automatique" (Automatic recognition)), "Écran du navigateur intégré" (Built-in browser screen) (where the "Récupérer" window opens, see [Screen and pointer](#adding-a-table-the-funnel-the-catalog-récupérer-waiting-list)), "Renommer" (Rename) (on-screen keyboard, or a real keyboard), "Supprimer" (Delete) (configuration trash, restorable with its file; the last one cannot be deleted); "Nouvelle configuration" restarts the wizard with `--nouvelle-configuration`.
- **New configuration**: the wizard creates the configuration ("Poste à 3 écrans" (3-screen PC), to be renamed), remembered for the connected screens. Its `VPinballX-<id>.ini` file starts as a copy of the active configuration's file: hand-made settings that do not depend on the screens (graphics quality, physics, keys) carry over; the wizard then writes the screens, sound and buttons into it. "Assistant complet" (Full setup wizard) redoes the active configuration, and so does the "Boutons" (Buttons) step on its own.
- **One `VPinballX.ini` per configuration, rather than a single one rewritten at each switch**: VPX re-saves its file itself, and the user sets both managed keys (kept in `vpxManual`) and free keys in it. A single file would mean setting aside, then restoring, each machine's own values at every switch, and the detection of hand edits would mistake the other machine's values for tweaks: a value set by hand on one machine would be overwritten or lost on the way back. With one file each, syncing a configuration never touches another one's file, and the memory of keys written stays tied to its own file (the "bascule aller-retour" (round-trip switch) test). The trade-off: a general setting changed later (through "Réglages de VPX", VPX settings) only applies to the active configuration.
- **Safeguard**: all the application code goes through the active configuration's file (`activeVpxIni`, `syncConfiguration` in [app/src/vpx-ini.ts](../app/src/vpx-ini.ts)); a test rejects any other access to `Locations.vpxIni`.

## Table orientation and framing

After the playfield is chosen, the wizard asks where the player stands: the flippers rotate the display until the text is upright, with the "Flippers · vous êtes ici" (Flippers · you are here) band on the player's side. It then asks for the diagonal, suggested from the EDID (the millimeter size from the detailed timing, otherwise the centimeters), and discarded if it does not match the image proportions, as happens with some TVs and with projectors. All of this is stored in `cabinet.json` (`playfield.flipperEdge`, `playfield.manualSizeCm` only if the diagonal differs from the EDID; `packages/core/src/layout.ts`). These questions can be redone on their own through system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Orientation et taille du playfield" (Playfield orientation and size) (`--assistant --playfield`), and with "Écrans et rôles" (Screens and roles) when the playfield moves to another screen.

Keys written to `VPinballX.ini` (`vpxLayoutSettings`, read from `ViewSetup.cpp` and `player.cpp` in VPX 10.8.1):

| Key | Value | Managed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Player] BGSet` | 1 (cabinet) if the image is taller than it is wide for the player, otherwise 0 | yes |
| `[Player] ScreenWidth`, `ScreenHeight` | real size in cm, the long side as the width whatever the orientation | yes |
| `[Player] CabinetAutofitMode` | in cabinet view, 1 ("Fit Table") or 2 ("Fit Screen"): the chosen mode is kept, any other value (absent, 0, `Manual`) becomes 1 | in game: the mode is free, manual is refused |

With automatic framing, VPX recomputes the table's whole cabinet view and derives the rotation from the shape of the window: flippers on the right of a landscape image, at the bottom of a portrait image, with no setting to change it.

#### Direction of tables in play

VPX draws in Windows coordinates: only the shape of the playfield window counts, never the orientation of the screen. What decides the direction (`vpxPlayedFlipperEdge`, `packages/core/src/vpx.ts`, read from `ViewSetup.cpp`, `player.cpp` and `pintable.cpp` of VPX 10.8.1, commit `af26b2d93`):

| Values read by VPX (`VPinballX.ini`, under the table's `.ini`) | View rotation | Flippers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `BGSet = 1`, `CabinetAutofitMode` 1 or 2 | `SetWindowAutofit` resets the table's rotation to 0 at every game, `GetRotation` adds 270° for a landscape window, 0° for a portrait window; `ViewCabRotation` has no effect | right (landscape), bottom (portrait) |
| `BGSet = 1`, manual framing (`CabinetAutofitMode` 0, or absent: VPX default) | that of the cabinet view set in the table, often 270° for Windows in landscape | unpredictable: sideways on Windows in portrait |
| `BGSet` 0 or 2 (desktop or FSS view) | that of the table's desktop view | bottom |

A rotation of r degrees puts the flippers at the edge whose `edgeRotation` is r (0 bottom, 90 left, 180 top, 270 right): the same convention as the menu rotation. Without `ScreenWidth`/`ScreenHeight`, VPX still fits the table, with its default size.

Hence the rule: in cabinet view, automatic framing is required, not suggested. Up to 0.1.3-essai.3, `CabinetAutofitMode` was only written when the key was empty: a `CabinetAutofitMode = 0` already there (old `VPinballX.ini`, setting changed in the VPX menu or in "Réglages" (Settings)) stayed, and every table went back to its own cabinet view, rotated for Windows in landscape. On a playfield in portrait in Windows, flippers at the bottom, the menu was upright and the game sideways.

- **Sync** (`requireAutofit`, `orientationFinding`, `packages/core/src/sync.ts`): a value other than 1 and 2 is replaced by 1, with the finding "cadrage automatique de VPX rétabli" (VPX automatic framing restored) when it was written; a cabinet already set up is corrected at the next start, without redoing the wizard. After writing, the direction is computed again from the file: if VPX will not draw the flippers on the player's side (Windows orientation to change, `BGSet` changed by hand, `CabinetAutofitMode` kept by the user in `vpxManual`), a warning says so, with the values at fault.
- **Settings**: the "Manuel" (Manual) choice, and the VPX default that is one, are no longer offered for `CabinetAutofitMode` in the general setting.
- **Doctor**: "Table de travers sur cette borne" (Table sideways on this cabinet, `tableOrientation`) when a table's `.ini`, through `BGSet` or `CabinetAutofitMode`, stops VPX drawing the flippers on the player's side; it then replaces "Cadrage manuel imposé par la table" (Manual view forced by the table). The repair removes these keys only; the file goes to the recycle bin if it holds nothing else. If `VPinballX.ini` itself is at fault, the sync says so.
- **Test**: `packages/core/src/test/orientation.test.ts` goes through the four Windows orientations, of a landscape screen and of a native portrait screen, the four flipper edges and seven starting `VPinballX.ini` files: the game goes the player's and the menu's way, without a warning, and a second sync changes nothing more; screen rotated outside the wizard or flip refused: right direction, or a warning.

For a player on the left or at the top, the wizard rotates the playfield screen itself, for the session only (see [Desktop orientation on exit](#desktop-orientation-on-exit)). At the "Où sont les flippers ?" (Where are the flippers?) step, it announces "Start : retourner l'écran pour VPX" (Start: flip the screen for VPX); Start switches the screen to a flipped orientation (`SessionOrientation.want`, then `setDisplayOrientation`, `packages/win-displays/src/orientation.ts`: `ChangeDisplaySettingsExW` tried as a dry run, applied without `CDS_UPDATEREGISTRY`, read back, and put back as it was if Windows did something else). The wizard runs the detection again, repositions its windows and recomputes the player's edge in the new image (left ↔ right, top ↔ bottom): the player always sees the text upright. Like Windows, it then asks whether to keep this orientation: without Start within 15 seconds, or with Exit, the screen returns to its original orientation, and Start then carries on without flipping (tables upside down). The choice of rotation is in `vpxOrientationFix` (`packages/core/src/vpx.ts`): a flip, which keeps the resolution and the layout of the other screens; a quarter turn only for a Windows portrait screen seen from the side. The sync, on the other hand, does not touch the orientation: it reports the problem and points to the wizard. With another cabinet frontend installed or running (PinUP Popper, PinballX, PinballY), the first Start flips nothing: it warns that the orientation is a Windows setting that the other frontend is subject to as well; Start flips anyway, Exit keeps the Windows orientation (see [Coexisting with another cabinet frontend](#coexisting-with-another-cabinet-frontend)).

The saved edge applies to the orientation Windows had at the time of the wizard, stored with the screen (`lastSeen.rotation`). If it later changes outside the wizard (Settings > Display, driver, waking from sleep), the image rotates under the player: `currentFlipperEdge` (`packages/core/src/layout.ts`) rotates the saved edge by the difference between the two orientations, and this current edge is the one used for framing, for the sync's "à l'envers" (upside down) warning, for rotating Cyber Pinball's windows on the playfield (`playfieldRotation`) and for the edge suggested when the wizard is run again. If no orientation was recorded at save time, the saved edge is kept as is.

A model saved before this step gets no framing key until the wizard has been run again.

#### Desktop orientation on exit

Report of 27 October 2026: after quitting Cyber Pinball, the desktop and Start menu on the playfield screen stayed upside down. The wizard rotated the screen with `CDS_UPDATEREGISTRY`: the Windows setting changed for good, desktop included, and nothing gave it back on exit. Neither the menu nor VPX touched the orientation; only the wizard did (`WINDOWS_CHANGES`, `packages/core/src/coexistence.ts`).

Windows now keeps its orientation, and Cyber Pinball only rotates the screen while it is open (`SessionOrientation`, `packages/core/src/sessionorientation.ts`; `app/src/display-orientation.ts`):

- **Playing orientation**: the wizard records in the model the Windows orientation in which it configured the playfield, when it had to change it (`playfield.windowsRotation`). Every start (menu, wizard, settings) gives it back to the screen, before opening the windows, through a dynamic change: nothing is written to the Windows setting, and restarting the PC always gives the desktop its orientation back.
- **Origin recorded before touching anything**: before the first rotation, each screen's original orientation (fingerprint, GDI name, origin, orientation given) is written to `<data>\orientation-ecrans.json`, which survives a crash. The file goes away once no screen is rotated any more.
- **Every exit gives it back**: "Quitter Cyber Pinball" (Quit Cyber Pinball), closing the last window, an update, the dedicated cabinet (exit code 77) go through `will-quit`, before the dedicated cabinet's own exit (`main.ts` imports `display-orientation.ts` first), then through `process.on('exit')` as a last resort (`app.exit`). A relaunch (menu ↔ wizard, language, configuration, Shell Launcher) hands over (`handOverOrientation`): the next process picks up the file, and the screen does not rotate in between.
- **Crash, forced stop**: the file stays; at the next launch, a screen still in the orientation Cyber Pinball gave it (or back to its origin after a restart) keeps its recorded origin; a screen the user has rotated in the meantime keeps its own, which becomes the origin. Shutdown, restart and sleep from the power module: since nothing is written to the Windows setting, the screen comes back in its own; on waking from sleep, if Windows has put its setting back (screens enumerated again), the session takes back the playing orientation (`check`, on `display-added`, `display-metrics-changed` and `resume`).
- **Desktop mode**: "Bureau Windows" (Windows desktop) gives the Windows orientation back (`suspend`), returning takes back the playing one (`resume`); after a quarter turn, the menu relaunches to reposition its windows.
- **Warning**: an orientation Windows refuses to give back is written to the log ("Orientation : … n'a pas repris son orientation d'origine (0°) : … Paramètres > Affichage > Orientation de l'écran la rétablit.", i.e. did not get its original orientation back; Settings > Display > Display orientation restores it) and to the file; the next start tries again and repeats it in a banner ("À la dernière fermeture de Cyber Pinball : …", when Cyber Pinball last closed).
- **Cabinets configured before 27/10/2026** (`legacyOrientation`): a model without `windowsRotation`, a recorded orientation (`lastSeen.rotation`) other than 0° and such that the wizard would have chosen it starting from 0° (`vpxOrientationFix`). If Windows has lost it (restart), the menu records the playing orientation and takes it back without asking. If Windows still keeps it, the menu offers: "Bureau Windows tourné par Cyber Pinball" (Windows desktop rotated by Cyber Pinball); Start gives 0° back to the Windows setting (the only write to that setting, `giveBack`) then puts the playing orientation back for the session, and the screen flickers twice; Exit keeps the desktop orientation as it is. Either way, the question does not come back. Guest mode: Start does nothing there (`message.orientation`).

Simulated screens (`CYBERPINBALL_DISPLAYS`, unattended run, off screen): the rotation goes to `<data>\ecrans-simules-orientation.json` (`windows`: the setting; `session`: the dynamic change, lost on restart), which `observedDisplays` applies to the screens; no real screen rotates. Evidence: `packages/core/src/test/sortie-orientation.test.ts` (every exit path on the four sets of `docs\ecrans`, with a simulated Windows that counts writes to its setting; recovery after a crash, Windows refusing, repair) and `.\docs\demo-orientation-sortie.ps1` (menu off screen: game and Quit, desktop mode, crash, old cabinet with the offer, old cabinet whose orientation Windows has lost). On the cabinet: manual tests 459 to 464.

#### Direction of tables, checked

A table could show upside down after every restart or session reconnection (seen on a PinCab in October 2026: 4K playfield lying flat, player at the left edge of the image, Windows flipped by the wizard): Windows had given its own setting back to the playfield screen. The menu, which follows the current orientation (`currentFlipperEdge`), stayed upright; VPX, which only looks at the shape of the window, drew the table upside down, and it had to be turned around in VPX's menu at every startup. The session now gives the game orientation back at startup, on wake and on reconnection (previous section); what follows checks it and shows it.

- **Cabinet health** (`orientationFacts`, `orientationVerdict`, `packages/core/src/gameorientation.ts`): under "Screens", after the recognised configuration, "Tables the right way for you, flippers …" with the Windows orientation, or, blocking, "Tables upside down: VPX will draw them with the flippers …, you stand …", with the cause: the game orientation Windows did not take back, or, with no orientation to take back, "run “Playfield orientation and size” again".
- **Log**: the sync's warnings also go to the console ("Synchronisation : …"), including "Tables will show upside down".
- **Fix made in VPX's menu**: to turn a table the right way, one had to switch to manual framing and rotate the view, which VPX saves in the table's `.ini` (`CabinetAutofitMode = 0`, `ViewCabMode`, `ViewCabRotation`). Cyber Pinball does not overwrite it. When the `.ini` gives the view and its rotation, its direction is known (`manualCabRotation`, `packages/core/src/vpx.ts`, after `ViewSetup::GetRotation`: in "Window" mode the rotation is added to 270° for a landscape window, to 0° for a portrait window; in "Legacy" or "Camera" it stands alone): the doctor does not report "Table de travers" (Table sideways) for a table the right way for the player, and says where VPX will draw the others. A fix made while Windows had lost the game orientation turns the table upside down once the orientation is taken back: the doctor reports it, and its repair removes `BGSet` and `CabinetAutofitMode` from the table's `.ini` (the remaining rotation is ignored by automatic framing).
- **Test**: `packages/core/src/test/gameorientation.test.ts` pins the invariant on a landscape screen and a native portrait screen, for the four Windows orientations and the four player edges: wizard, exit, simulated restart (Windows gives its setting back), session start, then reconnection (`check`); each time, VPX draws the game on the player's side, the menu too, with no warning, and nothing is written to the Windows setting. A cabinet from before 27/10/2026 back at 0° gets its direction back (`legacyOrientation`).
- **Off-screen demo**: `.\docs\demo-sens-playfield.ps1` (the cabinet's simulated screens: cabinet from before 27/10/2026 whose orientation Windows lost, two startups; cabinet configured since; flip refused in the wizard; "Santé de la borne" (Cabinet health) captures in `tmp\demo-sens-playfield`). On the cabinet: manual tests 465 and 466.

### Table framing, during a game

A table's framing is judged on the running table, not on a list of values. Table details screen > "Régler l'affichage en partie" (Adjust display in game) launches the table with `-PovEdit` instead of `-Play` (VPX 10.8.1, `AppCommands.cpp`): the same game, with VPX's menu open from the start on "Point Of View". The details screen's "Ajuster la vue" (Adjust the view) screen, which picked these values outside a game and then tried them out, was removed (24 October 2026), together with its `-TableIni` trial run.

With automatic framing, VPX recomputes the cabinet view after reading the table's overrides: the `[TableOverride] ViewCab*` keys only apply with manual framing. The "Point Of View" page therefore only offers what the framing reads again (VPX 10.8.1 code: `docs/recherche/vue-par-table.md`):

| Mode (`[Player] CabinetAutofitMode`) | Settings on the page | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| "Fit Table" (1) | the mode and `CabinetAutofitPos` (0 to 20 %) | position of the bottom flippers on the screen; higher up, the apron rises into the screen, at the expense of the top of the table |
| "Fit Screen" (2) | the mode only | the full length of the table, stretched or squeezed |
| manual (0) | offsets, scales, glass, rotation, then `ScreenPlayerX/Y/Z` | a hand-set view, which the doctor reports when a table forces it |

- **Save** (Start) > "Save as Table Override": in the table's `.ini`. Back on the details screen, it says "Affichage enregistré pour cette table" (Display saved for this table) if that file changed.
- **Outside a game**, these keys can no longer be changed for a table: in "Réglages de cette table" (This table's settings), they carry the "En partie" (In game) badge and only the table's own value can be removed (`isLiveVisual`, `packages/core/src/vpx-settings.ts`); the table's essentials no longer list them.
- **Doctor**: "Cadrage manuel imposé par la table" (Manual framing forced by the table) when the table's `.ini` (often shipped with it, set up for its author's screen) contains `CabinetAutofitMode = 0`; the repair removes the key, and the old file goes to the trash.

## Sound

VPX 10.8.1 has only two outputs: `[Player] SoundDeviceBG` (backglass: ROM music and voices, AltSound, PuP-Pack, table music) and `[Player] SoundDevice` (playfield: mechanical sounds, mixed according to `Sound3D`). It identifies them by the output's **Windows name** ("Haut-parleurs (USB Audio Device)", i.e. "Speakers (USB Audio Device)" on an English Windows), compared character for character; a name it cannot find silently sends it to the Windows default output. This name is localized, can be renamed, and is sometimes shared by two identical cards: as with screens, Cyber Pinball keeps its own fingerprint and rewrites the current name at every synchronization.

**Detection** (`probeAudioOutputs`, `packages/win-displays/src/audio.ts`): active outputs are read from `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\MMDevices\Audio\Render\{guid}`, read-only and through the Unicode API (`RegGetValueW`), because `reg.exe` mangles accented characters. For each one: the MMDevice identifier (`{0.0.0.00000000}.{guid}`), the name VPX expects ("DeviceDesc (interface)"), bus, form factor, channels and speaker mask set in Windows, ContainerId, and default output (the active output with the highest `Level:0`, cross-checked against Chromium on the development PC). An HDMI output is tied to its screen through the ContainerId of the PnP monitor (`ObservedDisplay.containerId`); the container shared by everything built into the PC (`{00000000-0000-0000-FFFF-FFFFFFFFFFFF}`) ties nothing. `npm run probe` shows all of this.

**The setup wizard's "Son" (Sound) step**, after the screens, or on its own (system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Son de la borne" (Cabinet sound), `--assistant --son`: only `SoundDevice`, `SoundDeviceBG` and `Sound3D` are written). Virtual outputs (Steam, Oculus: bus `ROOT`) and Bluetooth outputs are left out, unless they already had a role. Each output plays, on itself, a sound generated by an oscillator (no file), n beeps for output n, in a loop: `AudioContext.setSinkId` in a wizard window, the output being found by `enumerateDevices` under its Windows name (Chromium appends "(vid:pid)" to the name of USB outputs, which is ignored for the comparison). The player answers with the buttons:

| Button | Output played | Speaker test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Left flipper | backglass | playfield, near the player |
| Right flipper | playfield | playfield, backglass side |
| Start | both (SSF with a single card) | in the backglass |
| Exit | no role, next output | nothing heard |
| Backspace | previous output | previous pair |

The backglass screen's speaker is offered by default for the backglass, and a card set to 7.1 (failing that, the playfield screen's speaker) for the playfield; these outputs are played first, and the screen whose output is the speaker lights up. Running the wizard again starts from the saved outputs. If the playfield output reports 6 or 8 channels, each speaker pair (front, rear, side) beeps on the left, then at a higher pitch on the right (`ChannelMergerNode`, discrete channels in the order of the Windows mask, like SDL), and the player says where they hear it. The answers choose the mix (`playfieldModeFromTest`, `packages/core/src/audio.ts`): front in the backglass and another pair on the playfield gives SSF; front and another pair on the playfield gives surround, in the direction heard; otherwise stereo. Rear / side wiring that is swapped for SSF is flagged. The summary shows the chosen outputs and the `Sound3D` value that will be written.

Permissions: Chromium only gives output names, and only allows `setSinkId`, with the `media` or `speaker-selection` permission; the wizard grants these two to its windows (`setPermissionCheckHandler`) and allows playback without a user gesture (`autoplayPolicy`), since the buttons go through the main process. If the output is not found, or if Chromium opens fewer channels than reported, the screen says so and the player can still answer.

Model (`cabinet.json`, `audio` field): outputs stored by `endpointId`, with the `containerId` as a fallback (for a driver that recreates a screen's HDMI output) and the fingerprint of the screen whose speaker it is; `roles` (backglass, playfield, possibly the same output); `playfieldMode` (`stereo`, `surround`, `ssf`) and `lockbarPair` in surround. At each synchronization, outputs are found again by identifier, then by ContainerId if there is no ambiguity, never by name.

Keys written to `VPinballX.ini` (`vpxAudioSettings`), managed like the others (a value changed by hand is never silently overwritten):

| Key | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `[Player] SoundDeviceBG` | current name of the backglass output; empty if none (Windows default output) |
| `[Player] SoundDevice` | current name of the playfield output; empty if none |
| `[Player] Sound3D` | stereo: 0, or 1 on a 7.1 output (mode 0 cancels the gains there); surround: 3 (front on the lockbar side) or 2 (rear on the lockbar side); SSF: 5 |

A model without a "Son" (Sound) step (saved before that step existed, or with no output to offer) writes none of these keys, and the settings screen leaves them editable. Points to check, reported by the synchronization and the wizard: output not found (the key stays as it is, VPX will play on the default output), two active outputs with the same name (VPX takes the last one), a `Sound3D` too ambitious for the channels set in Windows (surround with fewer than 4 channels, SSF with fewer than 8: VPX falls back to stereo; set the output to 5.1 or 7.1 in the Sound control panel). Cyber Pinball never changes a Windows audio setting or the default output by itself. Volumes (`MusicVolume`, `SoundVolume`) remain player settings.

To be confirmed on a real cabinet (`docs/manual-tests.md`, scenarios 29 to 38): how `Sound3D` modes 2, 3 and 5 actually sound, the expected direction of the rear and side pairs in SSF (Pinscape guide: rear on the player's side), the channels Chromium actually opens on a 7.1 card, the ContainerId of an HDMI output after a port change, and the disappearance of a screen's HDMI output when the screen is in standby.

## VPX plugins: backglass, PuP and DMD

VPX 10.8.1 draws the backglass, PuP packs and the DMD itself, through its plugins ("plugins" mode): `ScreenRes.txt`, `B2STableSettings.xml` and `DmdDevice.ini` are read by nobody in that mode (`docs/recherche/generateurs-classiques.md`). Everything therefore goes through `VPinballX.ini`. Classic mode (VPX 10.8.0, COM components) and DOF will come later.

**Who draws on each window.** Several plugins can draw on the Backglass, ScoreView (DMD role) and Topper windows. For each frame, VPX tries their renderers in descending order of `Priority.<moteur>` and keeps the first one that draws (`player.cpp`, `OnAuxRendererChanged`). Without these keys, they are all 0 and the order depends on the order in which plugins load. A PuP pack only draws when it has a video to show: placed in front, it leaves the .directb2s backglass visible the rest of the time (`docs/recherche/pup-packs.md`, § 4.3).

Keys written (`vpxSettingsFor` and `vpxPluginDefaults` in `packages/core/src/vpx.ts`, `packages/core/src/dmd.ts`):

| Key | Value | Managed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Backglass] Priority.PUP`, `Priority.B2S` | 100, 50; if the backglass role is assigned | yes |
| `[ScoreView] Priority.PUP`, `Priority.B2S`, `Priority.ScoreView` | 100, 50, 10; if the DMD role is assigned. DMD-only screen: `Priority.B2S = 0` ([DMD screen](#dmd-screen-fulldmd-or-dmd-only)) | yes |
| `[Topper] Priority.PUP` | 100; if the topper role is assigned (B2S does not draw on the topper) | yes |
| `[Plugin.B2SLegacy] Enable` | 0 as long as B2S is enabled: both answer tables that request `B2S.Server`, and the last one loaded wins | yes |
| `[Plugin.B2S] ScoreViewDMDOverlay`, `ScoreViewDMDAutoPos` | 1 and 1 if the DMD role exists and is not a DMD only. The FullDMD of a .directb2s goes in front of ScoreView: without the overlay, only its frame would be visible. The DMD's position is searched for in the image | no, offered if the key is empty |
| `[Plugin.DMDUtil] Enable`, and `ZeDMD`, `PIN2DMD` or `Pixelcade` | 1 for the recognized real DMD; nothing without a real DMD | yes |
| `[Plugin.PinMAME] PinMAMEPath` | `<racine>\pinmame`, if it contains `roms` | no, offered if the key is empty |
| `[Plugin.Serum] SerumPath`, `[Plugin.VNI] VniPath` | `<racine>\pinmame\altcolor`, if it contains a `<rom>\<rom>.cRZ` or `.cROMc` (Serum), `.pal` or `.vni` (VNI) | no, offered if the key is empty |

Each table first looks for its ROMs and colorizations in its own folder (`pinmame\roms`, `serum`, `vni`, `pinmame\altcolor`); the shared folders only come after. The synchronization reports "B2S et B2SLegacy activés ensemble" (B2S and B2SLegacy enabled together) when both are enabled (B2SLegacy turned back on by hand, then kept).

**Real DMD.** `probeUsbDevices` (`packages/win-displays/src/usb.ts`) lists the USB devices present through the Windows configuration manager (`CM_Get_Device_ID_ListW`) and reads their name and serial port from the registry, read-only. No port is opened, whereas libdmdutil's detectors send a connection request to every port. Recognized identifiers (`HARDWARE_DMD_IDS`):

| DMD | VID:PID | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ZeDMD | `303A:1001` (ESP32-S3), `1A86:55D3` (CH343); `1A86:7523` (CH340) and `10C4:EA60` (CP210x), common converters flagged "à confirmer" (to be confirmed) | libzedmd's automatic detection filter (`ZeDMDComm.cpp`) |
| PIN2DMD | `0314:E457` | libdmdutil (`PIN2DMD.cpp`) |
| Pixelcade | `1B4F:0008` (IOIO-OTG board) | IOIO firmware; libdmdutil recognizes it by its "IOIO" reply |

DMDUtil finds the port itself: Cyber Pinball writes neither `ZeDMDDevice` nor `PixelcadeDevice`. When the DMD is unplugged, its keys are kept as they are. `npm run probe` shows the recognized DMDs (`npm run probe -- --usb`: all USB devices).

### Several roles on one screen: zones

A two-screen cabinet often puts the backglass and the DMD on the backglass monitor. A cabinet without a DMD screen embeds the DMD in the playfield image. A desktop PC puts everything on one screen: the table on one side, the backglass on the other. In the cabinet model, several roles can therefore point to the same screen, each in its own zone (`zones`), the playfield included. A zone is a rectangle expressed in fractions of the screen image (0 to 1): it survives a resolution change. It can keep an aspect ratio (`aspect`, width over height: 16/9, 4…): it is then resized without changing its proportions. Without a zone, the role fills the whole screen, as before. Code: [packages/core/src/zones.ts](../packages/core/src/zones.ts).

VPX 10.8.1 can do both, without any third-party tool (`src/renderer/Window.cpp`, `Renderer.cpp`):

| The role is… | Keys written | Effect in VPX |
| --- | --- | --- |
| alone on its screen | `<Préfixe>Output = 1`, `FullScreen = 1`, `WndX = WndY = 0`, `Width` and `Height` of the screen | borderless full-screen window |
| in a zone of a screen (playfield included) | `Output = 1` (not for the playfield), `Display`, `FullScreen = 0` ("Windowed"), `WndX`, `WndY`, `Width`, `Height` of the zone, in pixels relative to the screen | borderless window placed over the zone (all VPX windows are borderless) |
| in the playfield zone, on its screen | `Output = 2` ("Embedded in playfield"), `WndX`, `WndY`, `Width`, `Height` of the zone, relative to the playfield window | drawn inside the playfield image, on a black background, after the table: no window to keep in the foreground |

A role on the playfield screen but outside its zone (desktop PC) is a separate window. Table framing follows the playfield window, not the screen: cabinet view when the window is taller than it is wide, `ScreenWidth` and `ScreenHeight` set to the zone's actual size. "Garder" (Keep) on a size changed in VPX brings it back to the whole screen.

**Stacking order.** VPX has no setting for it: embedded displays are drawn backglass, then DMD, then topper (`Renderer.cpp`), and it is Windows that stacks two overlapping windows. Nothing prevents two zones from overlapping (a DMD placed over the bottom of the backglass, for example): this is reported for information, along with what will be in front. Only two roles assigned to the same screen with no zone for either of them give a warning: that is an oversight.

**No per-zone rotation.** VPX 10.8.1 only has rotation settings for the table view (`ViewDTRotation`, `ViewCabRotation`…) and for VR: its Backglass, ScoreView and Topper windows, and their embedded displays, are always drawn in the orientation of the Windows image.

**DMD window: FullDMD and DMD layered.** The DMD role's zone is the ScoreView window's zone. It has two layers: at the back, the FullDMD (the FullDMD image of the .directb2s, or screen 5 of a PuP-Pack, which the PUP plugin takes as the ScoreView root, falling back to screen 1: `PUPManager.cpp`); on top, the ROM's DMD, which the plugins place in the window themselves (B2S: `ScoreViewDMDOverlay` and `ScoreViewDMDAutoPos`, which looks for the DMD's place in the image; PuP: screen 1 placed over screen 5 by `CustomPos`). A zone therefore does not need to target the 128×32 DMD: it gives the FullDMD's place. What the screen shows, FullDMD or DMD only, is said once and for all ([DMD screen](#dmd-screen-fulldmd-or-dmd-only)). `splitBackglassDmd` splits a screen between the backglass at the top and a centered DMD strip at the bottom, at the requested proportions (4:1 for a DMD alone, 16:9 for a FullDMD), taking at most one third of the height.

- **Synchronization**: reports two roles overlapping on one screen (the case of two roles assigned to the same screen without a zone), an invalid zone in `cabinet.json` (the whole screen is then used), a role embedded over the whole playfield, and an embedded display that the player would see sideways or upside down: VPX does not rotate embedded displays.
- **Screen layout** ([app/src/arrange.ts](../app/src/arrange.ts)): a wizard step after the roles, opened on "Terminer" (Finish) (a Start keeps everything as it is), or on its own from the system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet configuration) > "Disposition des écrans" (Screen layout), or `--assistant --disposition`; at the screens step, "Placer librement…" (Place freely…) opens it on a role and returns to the wizard afterwards. The panel appears on the playfield screen; each screen shows its zones, the selected one with its corners. Rows: the zone (any role), its screen ("Aucun écran" (No screen) for a secondary role), the kept aspect ratio ("Libre" (Free), 16:9, 16:10, 4:3, 5:4, 21:9, 4:1, 9:16, 3:4), full screen (with a locked ratio: the largest zone at that ratio, centered), horizontal and vertical position, width, height, exact size, exact position, placement above, below, left or right of another role on the same screen (shrunk, ratio kept, if there is not enough room), alignment to an edge or to the center, snapping, and "Annuler les changements" (Undo changes). Position and size are free to the pixel across the whole screen; a zone never leaves the screen and stays at least 16 px on each side. Opened from the menu, the step starts on the "Zone" row; in the wizard, on "Terminer". The panel docks at the top or the bottom, opposite the selected zone when that zone is on its screen.
  - **With the buttons**: flippers to choose a row, Start to open it, flippers to adjust to the pixel (immediate preview; held down, they speed up to 64 px per step), Start to confirm, Exit to cancel.
  - **With the keyboard**: arrows to move the zone (row closed) or to adjust in both directions; digits then Enter for the exact size (width only when a ratio is locked) and for the exact position (X, then Y).
  - **With the mouse**: click a row, or a zone to select it; drag a zone to move it, even onto another screen (it moves there at once, at the same size if it fits, and becomes embedded if it lands in the playfield zone); drag a corner to resize it, the opposite corner staying in place. Each zone shows its corners, and each screen reminds you of the gesture. Dragged edges snap within 12 px to the screen's edges and middle and to the edges of the other zones (`snapRect`); the "Aimantation" (Snapping) row turns it off, or hold Alt during the gesture. The playfield cannot be moved to another screen with the mouse: the roles step decides that.
  - **Layout on its own**: saves the model and only rewrites the windows and the framing in `VPinballX.ini`; a role whose screen is merely unplugged keeps its screen.
- **Adjusting in VPX**: a windowed or embedded display moved or resized in the VPX interface (the window's display page, which frames the embedded display) is reported at startup like any key changed by hand. "Garder" (Keep) takes it back into the role's zone, and the key stays managed. A full-screen window that was retouched is left to the user.
- **PuP-Packs**: choosing the pack option compares the proportions of the zone, not those of the whole screen, and follows the type of the DMD screen (FullDMD or DMD only).
- **Screen wizard**: at the DMD step, the flippers offer the free screens (a wide screen twice: FullDMD on the whole screen, then DMD only as a centered 4:1 strip), then the bottom of the backglass screen (a 4:1 strip for a DMD alone, or 16:9 for a FullDMD with the DMD on top; the backglass keeps the top) and embedding in the playfield (4:1, at the edge opposite the flippers, over 40% of the width). A two-screen cabinet therefore always has a place for the DMD. The offered zone is drawn on the screen, dotted, at the exact place where VPX will show it. The saved layout is offered again by default, unchanged if it was retouched in VPX since then ("comme enregistré" (as saved)). Fine adjustment is done in VPX (see above).
- **Menu**: one window per physical screen, not per role. Roles that share a screen are drawn in the same window, each in its zone; an embedded DMD is drawn on the playfield without rotating, as in VPX. Two windows on the same screen would fight over the full-screen state that covers the taskbar (`coverDisplay`). The playfield page fits within its zone, sizes included (`cqmin`, relative to the zone).
- **Demo**: `.\docs\demo-zones.ps1`, off screen on `docs\ecrans\pc-dev.json` (`-Ecrans`). Layouts: `fronton` (DMD below the backglass), `incruste` (DMD in the playfield), `editeur` (layout on its own: DMD selected, 4:1 ratio, placed below the backglass, corner dragged with the mouse, dragged onto the playfield screen where it becomes embedded, 960 px wide and position 100, 50 with the keyboard, snapping off, saved), `bureau` (one screen: playfield in 9:16 on the left, backglass on the right, embedded DMD); plus `assistant`, for the development PC's screens. The keys written and the screenshots (`tmp\captures\zones`) are displayed; neither the real model nor the real `VPinballX.ini` is touched. The wizard's autopilot can drag a zone: `drag:<écran>:<rôle>:<move|nw|ne|sw|se>:<dx>:<dy>`.

### DMD screen: FullDMD or DMD only

VPX 10.8.1 has only one window for the DMD and the FullDMD (ScoreView). Yet the screen that shows it does not play the same part on every cabinet, and Cyber Pinball has you say it once and for all (`dmdKind` in the cabinet model: `fulldmd` or `dmd`). Code: [packages/core/src/dmdscreen.ts](../packages/core/src/dmdscreen.ts).

| | FullDMD | DMD only |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Screen | a whole wide (16:9) screen, often the speaker panel; or a 16:9 strip under the backglass | a 4:1 strip: real DMD screen, strip under the backglass, 16:9 screen behind the speaker panel's cutout, embedded in the playfield |
| Menu | the `(FullDMD)` media over the whole screen (`(DMD)` shrunk if missing) | the `(DMD)` media in the strip (`(FullDMD)` shrunk if missing), black around it |
| VPX | `[ScoreView]` full screen; `Priority.B2S = 50`: the `.directb2s` FullDMD image, the game DMD inset (`ScoreViewDMDOverlay`, `ScoreViewDMDAutoPos`) | window on the strip; `Priority.B2S = 0`: the game DMD through ScoreView, without the FullDMD image squeezed into the strip |
| PuP-Packs | option that puts its screen 5 (FullDMD) in the DMD window | option that puts its screen 1 (DMD) there, or nothing |

- **Where to say it**: in the wizard, "Écrans et rôles" (Screens and roles) step: a free wide screen is offered twice for the DMD, "FullDMD ?" (the whole screen) then "DMD en bande 4:1 au centre ?" (DMD as a 4:1 strip in the middle?); a screen that is already 4:1 only as "DMD ?"; the bottom of the backglass at 4:1 (DMD only) or 16:9 (FullDMD); embedding in the playfield is always a DMD only. Placed freely, the DMD takes the type of its zone's shape. Or, without redoing the wizard, system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Configuration de la borne" (Cabinet setup) > "Écran du DMD" (DMD screen): the current state, then "C'est un FullDMD" (It's a FullDMD) and "C'est un DMD seul" (It's a DMD only), each with its sketch. Start saves and relaunches the menu; its windows, `VPinballX.ini` and the PuP-Packs follow. The zone only changes if it does not have the shape of the choice: a FullDMD alone on its screen gets the whole screen; a DMD only, a centered 4:1 strip; under the backglass, the bottom strip at the new shape (`withDmdKind`). A strip that already has the right shape is kept as it is.
- **Screen layout**: the type is kept as long as the DMD zone does not change; dragged to another shape, the shape decides (`dmdKindAfterArrange`, boundary at 8:3). The zone is named after the type ("FullDMD" or "DMD").
- **Configurations from before this choice**: nothing is rewritten. Without `dmdKind`, the type is deduced as before from the zone's proportions (4:1 or 16:9, boundary at 8:3). Only one case is ambiguous: a DMD strip alone in the middle of a wide screen that shows nothing else (`dmdKindQuestion`), perhaps a FullDMD cut down by mistake ("Poste à 3 écrans 2", October 2026: a 4.78:1 strip in the middle of a 16:9 screen; the menu showed the FullDMD image shrunk into it). Synchronization reports it ("À vérifier", To check), the menu announces it at startup with a banner, and the "Écran du DMD" entry carries the red dot until it is answered. A type that contradicts the zone's shape (FullDMD in a strip, DMD only on the whole screen) is reported for information.
- **Background of screens without the playfield**: backglass, DMD and topper show their zones on pure black, never the ambient background nor the 9:16 playfield frame of the desktop menu (`#media-bg`, specific to the playfield; `layout-check.ts` rejects it on any other screen).
- **VPX 10.8.1 limit**: in the DMD window, VPX keeps the first renderer that draws. The PUP plugin never draws the table's DMD: for a table that draws its own, the doctor gives the window back to the DMD (`[ScoreView] Priority.PUP = 0`, `pupHidesDmd` rule), and the pack's screen 5 does not show there during play.
- **Demo**: `.\docs\demo-fulldmd.ps1`, off screen on `docs\ecrans\pc-dev.json`. Cases: `ambigu` (the development PC's configuration, banner, black around the strip, "Configuration de la borne" > "Écran du DMD"), `fulldmd`, `bande`, `assistant` (the answers offered for the DMD, nothing saved). The `[ScoreView]` keys written and the screenshots (`tmp\captures\fulldmd`) are displayed.
- **Tests**: [dmdscreen.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/dmdscreen.test.ts).

### FullDMD artwork in game

Reported on 26 October 2026 (*The Walking Dead*, 16:9 DMD screen): in the menu, the FullDMD screen shows the table's `(FullDMD)` media, artwork at the top and a frame at the bottom with a still DMD in it; in game, only a plain DMD stretched across the whole width was left. Code: [fulldmdframe.ts](../packages/core/src/fulldmdframe.ts), [fulldmdtable.ts](../packages/core/src/fulldmdtable.ts), [app/src/fulldmd.ts](../app/src/fulldmd.ts).

- **What shows in game**: VPX 10.8.1's ScoreView window, on the whole FullDMD screen. Three plugins can fill it, by priority: a PuP-Pack (screen 5, otherwise 1), the B2S plugin (FullDMD image of the `.directb2s`, `DMDImage`, with the DMD inset), then the ScoreView plugin, which draws the ROM, FlexDMD or UltraDMD DMD with its layouts (`plugins\scoreview\layouts\*.scv`: "Fit: Contain", "Glass-Dirty" glass). Neither dmdext nor Freezy: VPX's plugins do their job. On *The Walking Dead*: a `.directb2s` without a FullDMD image (only the backglass image), the PuP-Pack on its "No Topper BG and DMD seperate" option (screens 1 and 5 off): the ScoreView plugin drew alone, on the whole screen.
- **Where the artwork comes from**: the table's `(FullDMD)` media (VPinMediaDB, a 1920 × 1080 image or a video), which only the menu showed. The ScoreView plugin cannot draw a background image: the `- Image` visual of its layouts is rejected ("Not yet supported", `ScoreView.cpp`, commit af26b2d93), and its glass (`Glass`) only covers the DMD rectangle.
- **Options compared**: (a) Cyber Pinball keeps the artwork on the screen and places the DMD window in its frame: faithful to the menu, for every table (ROM, FlexDMD, UltraDMD), nothing to install, and the menu window already stays behind VPX during the game (in-game background); (b) the three-screen `.directb2s` or the PuP-Pack already dress the FullDMD: kept as is when that is the case, but many tables have neither, and the PUP plugin never draws the table's DMD (`pupHidesDmd`); (c) one `.scv` layout per table: impossible in 10.8.1, for lack of a background image. Chosen: (a), and (b) when the table already has it.
- **The frame** (`detectDmdFrame`): a rectangle whose four sides are clean edges, at least 40 % of the image wide, with a DMD's proportions (2.5:1 to 6.5:1); of a frame with several strokes (double neon, gold fillet), the inside is kept. A video is read at 10, 35, 60 and 85 % of its length, off screen, by the converter ([media-convert.ts](../app/src/media-convert.ts)): the frame is the one most of those images show. The result is kept per media (path, size, date) in `data\cache\fulldmd-cadres.json`. On the cabinet: a frame found for *The Walking Dead*, *Game of Thrones*, *2001*, *Terminator 2*, *Star Trek* and *Goonies*; none in the *Guardians of the Galaxy* video.
- **Before each game** (`prepareDressing`): when the DMD screen is a FullDMD (not embedded in the playfield), the table has a `(FullDMD)` media with a frame (its own or the cabinet's, not the `.directb2s` image), it draws a DMD (unknown script: yes), and neither the `.directb2s` (FullDMD image) nor a PuP-Pack (DMD window, non-zero `Priority.PUP`) already dresses it, the table's `.ini` gets `[ScoreView] ScoreViewFullScreen = 0`, `ScoreViewWndX`, `ScoreViewWndY`, `ScoreViewWidth` and `ScoreViewHeight` on the frame (the media is shown whole and centred in the zone, as in the menu). The placement written is remembered in the `.info` (`CyberPinball.fullDmd.window`): Cyber Pinball updates it (other media, other screen) or removes it when there is no artwork any more; the menu log records it ("Habillage FullDMD (…) : …"). Otherwise nothing changes: the DMD window keeps its whole zone.
- **During the game**: the menu window on the DMD screen keeps the artwork (`momentScreen`, `dressed`), still, or animated if "Media during the game" is set to "animated"; also when set to "off", since it is part of the display. The game's DMD sits in the frame, VPX's window on top.
- **Fine tuning**: in game, VPX menu > "Display Settings" > "ScoreView Display": position and size to the pixel, then "Save as Table Override". A placement that is no longer the one Cyber Pinball wrote is the user's: it is never touched again. The doctor shows it ("Écrans placés pour cette table", Screens placed for this table); "Revenir à la disposition générale" (Back to the general layout) removes it, and the frame found comes back at the next game.
- **Detection**: the doctor reports "Le DMD cacherait l'habillage FullDMD" (The DMD would hide the FullDMD artwork, `fullDmdHidden`, warning) when the DMD placement set for the table covers at least half of the artwork (`dressCoverage`; `ScoreViewFullScreen = 1`, for instance); the fix puts the DMD in the frame. During the game, the menu reads Windows' window stack again: a VPX window covering the artwork is logged ("Partie : le DMD de VPX recouvre … % de l'habillage FullDMD", `liveDressCoverage`).
- **Diagnosis of every table**: `npm run habillage` (`docs\diagnostic-habillage.ps1`: Electron on a hidden Windows desktop, nothing appears on screen) lists each table, the frame found, the DMD placement in game, and marks "[À CORRIGER]" (to fix) those whose DMD would cover the artwork. Nothing is changed in the tables.
- **Demo**: `.\docs\demo-moments.ps1 -Scenarios habillage`: a simulated game off screen, media "off"; the table's `.ini` and the in-game background of the DMD screen are checked.
- **Tests**: [fulldmdframe.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/fulldmdframe.test.ts) (frame, video, placement on the screen, table `.ini`, invariant on simulated screens, doctor) and the `fullDmdHidden` trap in [doctor-traps.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/doctor-traps.test.ts).

### Adjusting the display during a game

Every visual setting whose result has to be seen is made **during a game**, on the real picture, with VPX 10.8.1's own menu: the table's framing (above), the position and size of the backglass, DMD and topper (`DisplaySettingsPage`, read in the code at commit af26b2d93), and the look of the displays. A PuP-Pack, for example, often draws a frame on the backglass (bottom centre) where the DMD must land, and each pack has its own.

1. **Enter VPX's menu**: from the details screen, "Régler l'affichage en partie" (Adjust display in game) (the table starts with the menu open on framing, and the game does not count in the statistics); or, during any game, both MagnaSave buttons pressed together (Credit and Start on a cabinet without MagnaSave, F12 on a keyboard). Cyber Pinball writes this combination to `[Input] Mapping.InGameUI`, next to F12 (`inGameMenuButtons`, `packages/core/src/buttons.ts`), **even without recorded buttons**: it is then the only `[Input]` key it writes, with the encoders' default keys (`inGameMenuSetting`). Exit is never part of it: on its own, it quits the game.
2. **Adjust**: "Point Of View" for framing; Exit goes back to the menu's home page, then "Display Settings" > "Backglass Display", "ScoreView Display" (the DMD) or "Topper Display": position and size to the pixel, applied live; the window can also be dragged with the mouse.
3. **Save** (Start): "Save as Table Override" writes to the table's `.ini`, for that table only; "Save Globally" to `VPinballX.ini`, for every table.

Buttons in VPX's menu (`InputManager.cpp`), listed on the adjustment's loading screen ("Réglage de l'affichage" (Display adjustment), `displayKeys`):

| Button | In VPX's menu |
| --- | --- |
| Left, right MagnaSave | change line |
| Flippers | adjust the value |
| Start | save: for this table or for all |
| Credit | cancel the changes |
| Plunger | default value |
| Exit | previous page, then close the menu; with the menu closed, quit the game |

| The role is… | Adjustable live | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| on a zone of a screen ("Windowed" window) | position and size | the case of the DMD placed on the backglass |
| alone on its screen (full screen) | no | switching to windowed in this menu writes `FullScreen` straight to the general settings, applied at the next launch |
| embedded in the playfield | position and size | outlined in green while the page is open |

- **After the game**: a position saved for the table gives the doctor the finding "Écrans placés pour cette table" (Screens placed for this table) (information, with sizes and positions), and its fix "Revenir à la disposition générale" (Back to the general layout) removes those keys only (`tableWindowsFinding`, `packages/core/src/doctor.ts`). A position saved for every table is a managed key changed by hand: the menu asks at startup whether to keep it (see "Several roles on one screen: zones").
- **Outside a game**: in "Réglages de cette table" (This table's settings), visual settings (framing and camera, windows, `[DMD]` and `[Alpha]` display profiles, the DMD's place in the B2S picture, PuP screen margins) carry the "En partie" (In game) badge and can no longer be changed; only the table's own value can be removed. Cabinet measurements (screen width, lockbar) remain adjustable.
- **Who draws in the DMD window**: the PUP plugin never draws the table's DMD; it only reads its frames to recognize scenes (PuPCapture, `PUPManager::ProcessDmdFrame`). If it takes the ScoreView window (a pack with a screen 5, or a screen 1 not placed on the backglass), the score of the ROM, FlexDMD or UltraDMD does not show there. VPX reads the priority from the table's settings: for a table that draws its DMD (PinMAME ROM, `CreateObject("FlexDMD.FlexDMD")` or `UltraDMD.DMDObject`, DMD folder) and whose script does not write the score on the pack itself (`LabelNew`, `LabelInit`), the doctor writes `[ScoreView] Priority.PUP = 0` in the table's `.ini` ("Le PuP-Pack cacherait le DMD de la table", The PuP-Pack would hide the table's DMD, `pupHidesDmd`), and the menu does it by itself before the game. The pack keeps the backglass and the topper; a value already set for the table in the VPX menu is kept. Case found on *2001* (UltraDMD score, "DMD on Fulldmd" option), 26 October 2026.
- **Limits**: without MagnaSave, you cannot change line in VPX's menu (`UIUp`, `UIDown`); the stacking order of two overlapping VPX windows (DMD on the backglass) is left to Windows. To check on the cabinet: manual tests 86 to 89, 225 and 226.

## Game media

Each game can have its own presentation media: logo, playfield video, backglass, display, topper, loading video, music, launch sound, flyer and rules. The menu shows each one on the intended screen at the right moment. When a media item is missing, the menu shows a fallback drawn by Cyber Pinball in its place: never an empty screen, never the Windows desktop. Design: `docs/recherche/medias.md`, § 5.

A game's media go in its table's folder, in a `medias\` subfolder, and are named after the folder, `(Type) <nom du dossier>.ext`, as VPX 10.8.1 suggests: `medias\(Backglass) Terminator 2 (Williams 1991).mp4`. The `.vpx` name works too, for media specific to one version. The flyer and the rules can have several pages: `(GameHelp) <nom>.png`, then `(GameHelp) <nom> 2.png`, `… 3.png`. No per-screen setting: each media item's screen, proportions and rotation come from the cabinet model.

| Type (file) | In the menu | What it is for | Where and when it is shown | Formats | Without a file |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `(Wheel)` | "Logo" | Identifies the table in the menu list, on a transparent background. | Playfield: table list; attract mode | png, apng, webp, gif | the title as stylized text |
| `(Playfield)` | "Plateau" (Playfield) | The table in play, in the background behind the list. | Playfield, dimmed under the list; attract mode | mp4, webm, m4v, or an image | discreet teal smoked glass, no fake table visual |
| `(Backglass)` | "Fronton" (Backglass) | The game's backglass image or animation. | Backglass: selected table, loading, attract mode; during play if VPX leaves the screen free | mp4, webm, m4v, or an image | the lit image of the `.directb2s`, otherwise a card: title, manufacturer, year |
| `(DMD)` | "Afficheur" (Display) | Animation for the 4:1 display. | Narrow display (4:1), at the same moments as the backglass | same as the backglass | the "Afficheur large" media scaled down, otherwise the title in dots on a 128 × 32 grid |
| `(FullDMD)` | "Afficheur large" (Wide display) | 16:9 animation for a wide display. | Wide display (16:9), at the same moments | same as the backglass | the "Afficheur" media centered, otherwise the title in dots |
| `(Topper)` | "Topper" | The animated banner above the backglass. | Topper, at the same moments | same as the backglass | animated logo and Cyber Pinball brand, manufacturer and year |
| `(Loading)` | "Chargement" (Loading) | Video played while VPX loads the table. | Playfield, from the Start press until the game appears: full screen on a cabinet, in the 9:16 frame on a desktop | mp4, webm, m4v | backup loading screen: brand, title, manufacturer, year, progress bar |
| `(Audio)` | "Musique du jeu" (Game music) | The music of the selected table. | Backglass speakers, as long as the table is selected ("Musique des jeux" (Game music), on by default); in attract mode if attract-mode sound is set to reduced | mp3, ogg, m4a, flac, wav | the cabinet music, otherwise silence |
| `(AudioLaunch)` | "Son de lancement" (Launch sound) | A short sound at launch. | Backglass speakers, once, on Start | same as the music | nothing (or the loading video's sound) |
| `(GameInfo)` | "Prospectus" (Flyer) | One or more pages presenting the game. | Playfield, on request: details screen > "Prospectus", page by page | png, apng, webp, jpg, jpeg, gif, mp4 | a card generated from the `.info`: title, manufacturer, year, authors |
| `(GameHelp)` | "Règles" (Rules) | One or more rule cards. | Playfield, on request: details screen > "Règles", page by page | same as the flyer | the game controls |

`(Other)`, `(RealDMD)` and `(RealColorDMD)` (Popper, PinballX) have no screen in Cyber Pinball: they are read, kept and ignored. Popper's old formats (`flv`, `f4v`, `avi`, `mov`, `wmv`, `mpg`, `mpeg`) cannot be played: the media doctor reports them, and the import leaves them aside.

For each screen, the menu takes the first media found: `(Type) <nom du .vpx>` (the `.vpx` name), `(Type) <nom du dossier>` (the folder name), VPinFE's fixed names (`bg.png`, `wheel.png`, `table.mp4`…), the `.directb2s` image (backglass only), the **cabinet default** `<racine>\media\(Type).ext`, and finally the built-in fallback.

### Adding media

- **Details screen > "Médias du jeu" (Game media)** (the simplest way, with the buttons or the mouse): each media item with its role, the screen and the moment it appears, a preview, and what the menu shows today. You take a file copied into `<racine>\import` (from a USB stick), pick it on this PC or drag it onto the window; you replace, remove, set the playfield half-turn and the game's volume. Nothing is written before "Enregistrer" (Save), which shows the plan first. See the Manager: "Médias du jeu" section.
- **With the table**: an archive containing media laid out the PinUP Popper way (`POPMedia\…\Wheel\<jeu>.png`), the PinballX way or the VPinFE way is recognized when the table is added ("Ajouter une table" (Add a table)); the media are renamed `(Type) <dossier>` in `medias\`. A media pack on its own, without a `.vpx`, completes an installed table. See "Importing media".
- **From a PinUP Popper or PinballX installation**: system menu > "Cyber Pinball" > "Migration" > "Reprendre les médias" (Import media, from PinUP Popper / PinballX). Everything is copied; the original installation is not modified.
- **By hand**: copy the file into `medias\` under the name `(Type) <nom du dossier>.ext`. The menu shows it immediately on every screen, without restarting (see "Live changes"). The doctor (details screen > "Diagnostiquer et réparer" (Diagnose and repair)) reports a name that does not match the game, a file stored outside `medias\`, an unreadable format, proportions that do not fit the screen, and offers the rename.
- **Cabinet defaults**: a file `<racine>\media\(Type).ext`, with no game name, is used for every game that lacks that media type: a topper in your game room's colors, ambient music, a background video. It takes precedence over the built-in fallback.

- **Online, on demand**: "Chercher les médias" (Search for media) takes everything VPinMediaDB has for the game, with a plan to approve ("Media from VPinMediaDB"). For one specific media item, "Voir les propositions" (See suggestions) shows every version found, online and on the cabinet, and you choose from the previews ("Suggestions for each media item and deep search").

### Flyers and rules

Details screen > "Prospectus" (Flyer) or "Règles" (Rules) (the number of pages is written under the action): the page fills the playfield screen, rotated for the player, whole and undistorted. The flipper buttons go to the previous or next page, Start to the next one and then back to the details screen after the last page, Exit back to the details screen; with the mouse, the buttons under the page do the same. A video page (`mp4`) loops, muted. Without a file, the flyer is a sheet generated from the `.info` (a backglass-style card with the title, then manufacturer, year, authors, version, description), and the rules show the game's controls, each with a sentence saying how to add the real media file. Code: `sheetPages` ([packages/core/src/mediaview.ts](../packages/core/src/mediaview.ts)), the `pages` screen in `app/src/library.ts`, rendering by `renderPages` in `app/renderer/library.js`.


### In the menu windows

On the table menu, each menu window shows the media of the selected table (elsewhere, see "Outside the table menu: the default display"). The lookup follows the order given in § 5.3 of the research notes (`resolveMedia`, [packages/core/src/medias.ts](../packages/core/src/medias.ts)): `.vpx` name, folder name, VPinFE names, `.directb2s` image (backglass), cabinet defaults, then the built-in fallback.

| Screen | Media | Display |
| --- | --- | --- |
| playfield, behind the menu | `(Playfield)` | `cover`, darkened under the list; rotated so the media's flippers land on the cabinet's flipper edge (a portrait file has its flippers at the bottom, a landscape file on the right, as in Popper and VPinMediaDB) |
| menu list | `(Wheel)` | logo above the title of the selected table and in a fixed-size box in front of each title in the list (the title's initial when there is no logo); in the wheel, each table's medallion. Without a logo, the centre medallion writes the title (three lines at most) and the others carry the game's initial, large: shrunk to a neighbour's place, a name fell below the 20 px floor (13.6 px on a cabinet). Name and initial are never seen together: when a medallion leaves the centre, the name goes out in 60 ms, before being shrunk below 20 px, then the initial lights up (`fillWheelItem` and `placeWheelItem`, `app/renderer/frontend.js`; `.wheel-initial`, `frontend.css`) |
| backglass | `(Backglass)`, otherwise the lit image of the `.directb2s` | `contain` on black |
| DMD | `(FullDMD)` on a FullDMD screen, `(DMD)` on a DMD only ([DMD screen](#dmd-screen-fulldmd-or-dmd-only); without an answer, from the proportions of its zone), otherwise the other one | `contain` on black; around a strip, black |
| topper | `(Topper)` | `contain` on black |

- **Zones**: each role of a shared screen (backglass and DMD strip, DMD embedded in the playfield) has its own media in its zone.
- **Playback** (`app/renderer/media.js`): videos loop, muted, except the playfield background video when the "Son des vidéos du menu" (Menu video sound) setting asks for it (see "Menu sound"); 280 ms crossfade on table change; the media of the two neighboring tables on each side are preloaded, and a video you just left is kept ready for a while (eight items at most, four of them videos); the playfield background is paused under an opaque overlay (help, details screen, message); all videos are paused when the window is hidden. Fast navigation stacks nothing up: a layer still waiting for its first frame is dropped.
- **Fallback** when there is no media or on a playback error: the components of `app/renderer/fallback.js` (task T3) as soon as `frontend.html` has loaded them (`window.CyberFallback`), otherwise a minimal fallback: title, manufacturer and year on black, shrunk until they fit their zone, and nothing on the playfield background. The drawn topper shows the brand and the **game's title** (two lines at most), not the manufacturer and year, which are already on the backglass and in the menu.
- **Background during play**, the "Médias pendant la partie" (Media during play) setting on the "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract mode) screen (system menu; Start moves to the next choice, saved in `<données>\medias.json`): **frozen** by default (first frame, drawn once on a canvas, with no video decoder open during play), **animated** (the playfield and the screens VPX occupies stay frozen), or **off** (black, with a discreet title). See "Loading and play".
- **`cyber-media://` protocol** ([app/src/media-protocol.ts](../app/src/media-protocol.ts), rules in [packages/core/src/mediaview.ts](../packages/core/src/mediaview.ts)): the only path from the windows to the files (the CSP of `frontend.html` allows nothing else for images and videos). It only serves a media file (an extension from the catalog) placed directly in the `medias\` folder of a known table, in `<racine>\media\` or in the `.directb2s` image cache (`<données>\cache\medias\`), plus, one at a time, the files that "Médias du jeu" (Game media) previews before storing them (import folder, file picked on this PC, `allowPreview`); built-in fallbacks are drawn by the window, with no file. Subfolders, escaping with `..`, alternate data streams, symbolic links and any other extension are refused (403). It answers range requests (206), which video playback needs. The address changes with the file's date and size: a media file replaced under the same name is not served from the cache.
- **Live changes** ([packages/core/src/mediawatch.ts](../packages/core/src/mediawatch.ts)): a media file added, replaced or removed (any type: wheel logo, playfield, backglass, DMD, FullDMD, topper, loading video, music, launch sound, `.directb2s`) and a table added, renamed or deleted show up immediately on every screen, without restarting, whatever the path: "Médias du jeu" (Game media), "Chercher les médias" (Find media), suggestions, adding through "Récupérer" (Fetch), a file copied by hand. One single mechanism: the menu watches `<racine>\tables` and `<racine>\media` (recursive `fs.watch`, native on Windows); 400 ms after the last write (a copy produces a burst of them), it rescans the tables (`.vpx`, folder added or renamed) or forgets its resolved media (`MenuMedias.invalidate`; the `.directb2s` is read again while the previous image stays on screen, with no fallback in between), then sends its state to every window again. What VPX writes next to the tables during a game (`.ini`, logs) triggers nothing. In the wheel, a table already on screen keeps its place: only its content changes, the new logo replacing the old one (or the title) once loaded; the selection stays on the same table. Since file addresses change with their date and size, no screen keeps the old image: the `.directb2s` image cache is stamped the same way and the dimensions cache checks date and size, so there is nothing to repair at startup. A folder missing at startup (`<racine>\media`) is watched as soon as it exists (when the tables are rescanned); where watching is impossible (network drive), resolved media expire after 10 s.
- **`.directb2s` image**: extracted in the background, one table at a time, the first time the table comes up; the fallback shows in the meantime, then the image fades in to replace it.
- **Playback errors** (undecodable video, damaged image): reported by the window to the main process with the error code, written to the console and to the single log `<données>\logs\medias-erreurs.json` (format described in "The media doctor"), one entry per file; an entry disappears when the file changes. The doctor reports them.
- **Tests**: [mediaview.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/mediaview.test.ts) (addresses, allowed folders, rotation for each flipper edge, DMD or FullDMD, `.directb2s`, setting, error log, CSP and rendering hookups), [mediawatch.ts](../packages/core/src/test/mediawatch.test.ts) (folder watch, new file after a change of each type, `.directb2s` re-read without a fallback, the real renderer's wheel showing a logo added to a table already on screen, without moving).
- **Live changes, off-screen**: `.\docs\demo-roue-medias.ps1` runs the real menu on a sandbox and, while it runs, drops a logo and a backglass, replaces a logo under the same name, removes a logo and adds a table (autopilot steps `copy:` and `remove:`); each change is checked in the screens' report (`roue` and `couches` of `cyberEtat`), captures in `tmp\captures\roue-medias`.
- **Off-screen demo**: `npm run build` then `npx electron docs/demo-medias-menu.cjs` builds a sandbox in `tmp\medias-menu\racine` (eight tables, generated images and WebM videos, a `.directb2s`, a damaged video, a default topper), opens the menu windows without showing them (off-screen rendering) on three simulated cabinets (flippers on the left with a shared backglass and a topper, landscape with an embedded DMD, flippers on the right with a 4:1 DMD), and captures every screen: media present, missing, portrait, landscape, video, image, crossfade in progress, rapid burst, help, play frozen, animated, off. `tmp\medias-menu\rapport.json` lists what each layer displays, texts that overlap or overflow (none expected), the video pause while the window is hidden, and the playback errors reported.

### The table menu on a desktop, from 5:4 to 16:9 and wider

On a desktop (playfield zone wider than tall, `html.menu-desktop`), the table's media sits in a 9:16 frame on the left, full height; the wheel or the list, and the information, are on the right. Dimensions in reference pixels (`--u`, the zone height is 1080); the playfield frame never changes (x 64 → 627). With a band wheel (Médaillons, Bandeau, Minimaliste, Arche), the game's backglass is on the right, the wheel at the bottom, and the layout follows the screen width (`app/renderer/frontend.css`, `html.menu-desktop[data-menu-band]`):

- **16:9 and wider**: a band 1236 wide (`--b` = 1236 / 1080 `--u`), 34 from the right edge; a 16:9 backglass above it, aligned with the top of the playfield, the wheel overlapping its bottom; the help bar is aligned with the bottom of the playfield.
- **From 16:10 to 16:9**: the whole group shrinks to the room left on the right of the playfield (a band of `min(1236, W − 684)`, its left edge at 650). The alignments stay: top of the backglass on the playfield's top, help bar on its bottom; the wheel overlaps the backglass less.
- **Narrower than 16:10** (3:2, 4:3, 5:4): **compact layout**, two zones (Codex review, October 2026). On the right of the playfield, a column from 675 to 64 from the edge; the 16:9 backglass at the top, the whole band at the bottom, both as wide as the column; between them, the empty space stays dark (from 26 in 3:2 to 313 in 5:4). Before, the backglass and the band kept their 16:9 dimensions and covered almost all of the playfield frame; only a strip of the playfield stayed visible (16:10 overlapped too).
- **Texts of a shrunk band**: each font size has a floor (`max()`), larger than drawn when the band shrinks: 20 px from 16:10; in the compact layout, 26 for the title (23 on the Médaillons plate, so that it fits there with manufacturer · year), 23 for a long title, manufacturer · year and the counter, 22 for the help (20 px are only 14 pixels on a screen 768 high). Manufacturer · year stack above the centered counter, the title above them, without touching. In the compact layout, the title fits on one line ("…"), across the column for Bandeau and Minimaliste (the Arche and the Médaillons keep their drawn slot: widened, the title crossed the medallions sliding along the Arche's legs); the name in the center medallion, on two lines at most; the Médaillons counter never wraps. The medallions' name and initial have their floor too (`--floor-text`, computed outside the wheel), including while the name leaves or reaches the center.
- **Nothing moves** from one table to the next: all these dimensions depend only on the screen size. At 16:9 and wider, and for the title list, the rendering is the same as before, pixel for pixel.
- **Proof**: `.\docs\demo-noms-roue.ps1 -Seulement bureau` renders the four wheels and the list at 1080p, 1366 × 768, 16:10 (1280 × 800), 3:2 (1500 × 1000), 5:4 (1280 × 1024), 4:3 (1024 × 768) and 4K, at rest, mid-slide, with a long title and an unbreakable word in the center; the layout guard must report nothing (captures `bureau-1024x768-*` and the others in `tmp\captures\noms-roue`). [cadres-menu.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/cadres-menu.test.ts) checks that each menu frame belongs to its zone and that the guard sees two zones overlapping; [plancher-textes.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/plancher-textes.test.ts) redoes the computation of the wheel's floors.

### Outside the table menu: the default display

The cabinet screens (backglass, DMD or FullDMD display, topper, and the playfield background) only show a table's media while it is the subject. Outside the table menu, nothing of the last table on the wheel stays on screen or keeps playing: every screen switches to the **Cyber Pinball default display**, silently, and going back to the wheel gives the selected table its media and music back.

| Open screen | Cabinet screens | Sound |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Table menu, attract mode | media of the selected (or shown) table | its music, or the sound of its video |
| Loading, game | media of the table being played (see "Loading and game") | launch sound, then VPX |
