| A table's details screen and what opens from it ("Médias du jeu" (Game media) and its proposals, flyer, rules, "Fichiers" (Files), "Ce qu'il manque pour jouer" (What is missing to play), "Diagnostiquer et réparer" (Diagnose and repair), "Modifier les informations" (Edit details), that table's VPX settings, the file picker and on-screen keyboard opened from these screens) | media of **that** table: it is the subject, and you see what you are adjusting | its music |
| Failed game ("La partie n'a pas pu se dérouler" (The game could not run)) | media of the table that was played | its music |
| System menu and everything that opens from it (adding, catalogue, downloads, ROMs, settings, cabinet configuration, language, updates…), global VPX settings, controls help, messages, quit confirmation | default display | nothing |

- **Default display** (`defaultMediaView`, [packages/core/src/mediaview.ts](../packages/core/src/mediaview.ts)): each screen's built-in fallback with no game title, which draws the brand in the art direction (`app/renderer/fallback.js`, teal black background, signal green rules): backglass with the brand's cover ("CYBER" above "PINBALL" in signal green, light, wide and widely spaced Saira, the art direction's illustration on the right, see [Brand backglass and DMD](#brand-backglass-and-dmd)); DMD display with the stacked brand in its dot font; FullDMD with the stacked brand inside its lamp frame (live menu DMD: the brand, PRESS START and the clock, see [Menu DMD](#menu-dmd)); topper with the logo and the brand; playfield background in smoked glass. No table file is read, no video runs; the display follows its screen (DMD only or FullDMD). Dark from the first frame: never white at the switch.
- **Rule** (`screensTable`, [packages/core/src/menumoments.ts](../packages/core/src/menumoments.ts)): each menu phase states which table it is about, in an exhaustive table (a new phase does not compile until it is declared there). The system menu only keeps a table for a screen that declares it: a table's own screens (details, flyer, editing, doctor, removal) carry it, a described screen gives it when it opens (`openCustom(build, at, table)` in `app/src/library.ts`), an input or a notice keeps the one of the screen it opens from. Any new screen that says nothing gets the default display.
- **Trials**: the layer report (`cyberEtat()`) says "marque" (brand) for the default display, "repli" (fallback) for a table that lacks that media.
- **Tests**: [ecrans-par-defaut.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/ecrans-par-defaut.test.ts) (each phase, each screen, a renderer component for each, `frontend.ts` wiring). Demo: `.\docs\demo-moments.ps1 -Scenarios menus` (help, system menu, its settings, back to the wheel, details screen).

### Menu sound

The menu sound comes from a single window, the playfield one (`app/renderer/menu-audio.js`). The main process decides what to play at each moment (`menuSound`, [packages/core/src/menumoments.ts](../packages/core/src/menumoments.ts)); everything is set in **System menu > "Médias" (Media) > "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract mode)** (`app/src/menu-settings.ts`), saved in `<données>\medias.json` and applied immediately.

| Moment | You hear | Setting (default) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Navigation, the table's details screen, failed game | the selected game's music, `(Audio)` (otherwise the cabinet's, `<racine>\media\(Audio).*`), looping | "Musique des jeux" (Game music): **on** by default, so the wheel is never silent after installation (turn it off for a silent cabinet in a living room); "Volume de la musique" (Music volume) (60%) |
| Navigation, game with no music or music off | the sound of the playfield background video | "Son des vidéos du menu" (Menu video sound) (40%; 0%: muted) |
| Loading | the launch sound, `(AudioLaunch)`, once; without it, the sound of the loading video | "Volume du lancement" (Launch volume) (80%) |
| Play | nothing: everything fades out as soon as VPX's game window appears | — |
| Attract mode | as during navigation, for the table being shown | "Son en attente" (Attract mode sound): **off** by default, or reduced (30% of the volume) |

- **One sound at a time**: the game's music, otherwise the sound of the playfield video; never both. A screen that lets you listen to sounds (music suggestions, [Table sounds](#table-sounds)) silences the menu while it is open. The backglass, display (DMD or FullDMD) and topper videos are **always muted**, on purpose: several videos with sound at once would be a cacophony, and it is also PinUP Popper's usual setting. VPinMediaDB videos have no audio track anyway (`table.mp4` and `dmd.mp4` checked on the cabinet): a game's sound there is a separate file, its music (`audio`, about 650 games out of 1,150), stored as `(Audio)`. A game with no music whose playfield video has no sound therefore stays **silent in the menu**, whatever "Son des vidéos du menu" (Menu video sound) says, even if its display is animated (the case of *Guardians of the Galaxy* on the reference cabinet, October 26, 2026: no `(Audio)`, no sound in `(Playfield)` or `(FullDMD)`, and no music on VPinMediaDB). To hear it: give it music (details screen > "Médias du jeu" (Game media) > "Musique du jeu" (Game music): "Sons de la table" (Table sounds) to take one from its PuP-Pack, see [Table sounds](#table-sounds), or a `medias\(Audio) <dossier>.mp3` file), or add cabinet music `<racine>\media\(Audio).mp3`, played for every game that has none; "Musique des jeux" (Game music) must be "jouée" (on).
- **Per-game volume**: each volume is multiplied by the game's relative volume (details screen > "Médias du jeu" (Game media) > "Volume de ce jeu" (This game's volume), `CyberPinball.medias.volume` in the `.info`), without exceeding the maximum. A game set to 50% plays its music at 30% when the setting is at 60%.
- **Transitions**: on table change, the old music fades out (700 ms) and the new one only starts if the table stays selected for 350 ms: scrolling through the list plays nothing. A video's sound follows its crossfade. At launch, the music stops and the launch sound starts.
- **Output**: the backglass output in the cabinet model (the setup wizard's "Son" (Sound) step; it is also the output for VPX music, `SoundDeviceBG`), otherwise the playfield one, otherwise the Windows default output. It is found among Chromium's outputs by its Windows name (the "(vid:pid)" suffix of USB outputs is ignored), then music, launch sound and videos are sent to it (`setSinkId`). Output not found: the default output. For this, the menu windows have the `media` and `speaker-selection` permissions and playback without a user gesture (`autoplayPolicy`), like the wizard. **All of Cyber Pinball's sound** uses this output, not just the menu's: the Windows default output is not necessarily connected to the cabinet (on a PinCab, often an HDMI screen with no speakers; in October 2026, the wheel and the launch played on the cabinet, but not the sounds offered in "Médias du jeu" (Game media)). The sounds and videos of the library screens are sent to it as soon as they are created (`CyberMenuAudio.route`, `app/renderer/library.js`), and any audio or video element of the window that starts playing without going through it is sent there (`play` event, in the capture phase). So are the web pages of the "Récupérer" (Get) window (YouTube, previews), frame by frame (`audioSinkScript`, `packages/core/src/audiosink.ts`, run at each `did-frame-finish-load`): Electron has no per-window output. Its hidden views, and those of media conversion, stay muted.
- **Windows desktop** (windows minimized): the music goes silent, then resumes on return.
- **Off screen**, every application window is muted (`createWindow`, `app/src/offscreen.ts`): an unattended test makes no noise. After each step, the autopilot records what would have played (`CyberMenuAudio.status()`: file, volume, playback, output, video sound) and what each screen shows (`cyberEtat()`: phase, position, table shown in attract mode, and for each media layer the displayed item, video playing or paused, image, frozen image or fallback) in `son.txt`, next to the screenshots.
- **Tests**: [menumoments.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/menumoments.test.ts) (settings and hand-written values, volumes, sound for each moment, menu output, sound media resolved on disk, rendering hookups, muted windows off screen), [sortie-audio.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/sortie-audio.test.ts) (invariant: every rendering script that creates a sound routes it, every web page view is muted or routed; sound offered in "Médias du jeu" (Game media) on the cabinet output in the real rendering; web page script). Demo: `.\docs\demo-moments.ps1` (see "Unattended end-to-end tests").

### Interface sounds

Five sounds punctuate the menu, on top of the music: four pinball noises and a chime, never cutting or lowering it (`app/renderer/ui-sounds.js`). The registry, what triggers each sound and the rule deciding whether it plays are in [packages/core/src/uisounds.ts](../packages/core/src/uisounds.ts) (`UI_SOUNDS`, `uiSoundCue`).

| Sound | When | Source and licence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Solenoid click (`solenoid`) | a neighbouring table in the table menu (left and right buttons, or a click on the wheel) | WebAudio synthesis written for Cyber Pinball (filtered metallic strike, dull body of the coil, plunger return); Cyber Pinball's code licence, no recording |
| Coin drop (`coin`) | Start on "Jouer" (Play): launching a game (not the display adjustment) | WebAudio synthesis (bounces of a metal disc, inharmonic modes, drop into the cash box); same licence |
| Knocker (`knocker`) | back from a game in which a score entered the table's high-score list (`VPReg.stg`, see "High scores") | WebAudio synthesis (hammer on the wooden cabinet, cabinet resonance); same licence |
| Coil thump (`coil`) | cinematic launch: the moment VPX takes over (its game window holds the foreground, see [Cinematic launch](#cinematic-launch)) | WebAudio synthesis (a dry, dull 125 ms "thunk": a filtered noise contact, a muffled body falling from 145 to 95 Hz, a hint of metal around 650 Hz), as prescribed by Codex; same licence |
| Invitation chime (`invite`) | a friend invites you to a live game, in the menu as well as during a game (see "Live game" in [Friends](#friends-the-cabinet-network)) | WebAudio synthesis (three rising bell notes, A, C sharp, E, 110 ms apart, with their bell partials; 0.6 s, no reverb): the only musical sound of the registry, far from the tables' mechanical knocks, it can be recognised over VPX's music; same licence |

- **Settings**: **System menu > Médias (Media) > "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract mode)**: "Sons de l'interface" (Interface sounds; on by default; Start turns them off or back on, and plays the click) and "Volume des sons de l'interface" (Interface sound volume; 50% by default; choosing plays the coin). Saved in `<données>\medias.json` (`uiSounds`, `uiSoundsVolume`).
- **Never during a game**: the main process requests no sound during a game or in attract mode (`uiSoundCue`); the window, for its part, refuses any sound while the state says "game in progress", silences anything still ringing and closes its audio context: no stream is open on the output while VPX plays. The coin drops during loading, before VPX takes the screens. The one exception is the **handover** sound (`handover` in `UI_SOUNDS`: the coil thump alone): requested at the very moment VPX takes over, it starts at the beginning of the game and rings out there (125 ms), then the audio context closes; the window only accepts it for `coil`, even if another sound claimed to be a handover sound. The second exception is a **call** sound (`call` in `UI_SOUNDS`: the invitation chime alone): a friend's invitation awaits an answer, it rings once during the game; the window only accepts it for `invite`. Both follow the interface sounds setting and volume: turned off, they are silent too.
- **Output**: the menu sound's output (see [Menu sound](#menu-sound)), passed on by `menu-audio.js`; the audio context closes four seconds after the last sound.
- **Fast scrolling**: the same sound does not start again within 45 ms; each table passed clicks, without a machine-gun effect.
- **Licence**: no excerpt from a real pinball machine and no sound of uncertain rights; each sound is computed on the fly by WebAudio, with no file. An added recipe must be too, or come from a CC0 sound whose source is recorded in `UI_SOUNDS`.
- **Adding a sound**: its identifier in `UI_SOUND_IDS`, its entry (name, trigger, source, licence) in `UI_SOUNDS`, its recipe in `RECIPES` in `ui-sounds.js`, the `playUiSound('<id>')` call in `app/src/frontend.ts`, and its row in this table. The test rejects a sound with no recipe, no trigger, or missing from the manual.
- **High scores**: the knocker sounds back from a game when a score has entered the table's high-score list. High scores are read before launch and on return (`readTableRecords`, `newRecords`, [packages/core/src/highscores.ts](../packages/core/src/highscores.ts), the same source as the menu DMD) from what VPX 10.8.1 keeps for the table: `<dossier de la table>\user\VPReg.stg`, where VPW tables and most original tables save `HighScore1..N` and their initials. **Not covered**: PinMAME tables whose ROM keeps its own high scores (`nvram\<rom>.nv`, a raw image of the game's memory, to be read with each ROM's map); for them, the knocker does not sound. Without a reading before the game (missing or unreadable file, or several tables in the same file with no name to tell them apart), nothing is compared and nothing sounds: a high score is never assumed.
- **Off screen** the windows are muted; `CyberUiSounds.status()` reports what would have played (sound, volume) and whether the window is muted, recorded in `son.txt` by the autopilot (`interface` field). Demonstration: `.\docs\demo-moments.ps1 -Scenarios interface` (two clicks, the coin on Start, nothing during the simulated game, a `VPReg.stg` replaced during the game and the knocker on return, sounds back in the menu, off, on again, volume). **Listen** without launching the menu: `.\docs\rendu-sons-interface.ps1` writes the four sounds as WAV files to `tmp\sons-interface` (100% volume), with each one's peak and level, and fails if one clips.
- **Tests**: [uisounds.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/uisounds.test.ts) (nothing plays when off, at 0%, in attract mode or during a game, on both sides; recipe, trigger and manual row for each sound; knocker wired to the high scores before and after the game; each sub-screen of "Médias, son et attente" returns to its own row).

### Loading and play

What each screen shows from launch to the end of the game (`momentScreen`, [packages/core/src/menumoments.ts](../packages/core/src/menumoments.ts); rendering in `app/renderer/frontend.js`):

| Moment | Playfield | Other screens |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Loading (from Start until VPX's game window appears) | on a cabinet, the game's `(Loading)` video alone, full screen and rotated according to the flipper edge; on a desktop, the same video in the menu's 9:16 frame on the left, with the panel holding the title and the game's controls on its right; without it, the backup loading screen in its place (`fallback.js`: brand, title, manufacturer, year, progress bar) | the game's media, animated, until VPX's windows cover them |
| Play | first frame of the playfield background, under VPX | a screen VPX **does not occupy**: according to "Médias pendant la partie" (Media during play) (frozen by default, animated, off); a screen it **does occupy** (`.directb2s` backglass, PinMAME DMD, PuP): frozen, since it cannot be seen and must cost nothing |

- **Loading screen** (`loadingScreen`, `app/renderer/frontend.js`). **On a cabinet** (playfield zone taller than wide, `html.menu-cab`), the playfield suits the loading media: it fills the whole screen, alone, with no title, no "Chargement de la table…" (Loading table…) and no controls. Without media, the backup loading screen replaces it, full screen, without its label (`fallback.js`, option `label: false`). The one exception, the in-game display adjustment (details screen > "Régler l'affichage en partie" (Adjust the display during a game)) keeps its reminder, the buttons of VPX's menu (`loadingKeys.display`), as a banner over the media or under the fallback's progress bar. **On a desktop** (`html.menu-desktop`), the loading media (or its fallback) takes the place of the menu's media, in the 9:16 frame on the left (531 × 944 at x 80, y 92, for a short side of 1080); on the right, a single panel (`loadingCard`) uses the frame's measurements: same top, same height, same edge, 80 from the screen's edge, and a gap of 60 from edge to edge, the frame's own distance to the screen's edge. It holds, in the flow and with the rhythm approved by Codex, the title (64, or 48 for a long title; two lines at most), manufacturer · year, "Chargement de la table…" (Loading table…) and its signal-green progress bar, then, after a rule, the game's controls; whatever space is left stays below them. The controls (`fitLoadingCard`) take the most readable layout that fits without cutting anything off: two columns (at least 440 wide each) with no key split over two lines, otherwise one column, otherwise two columns with split keys, at a size of 24, 22 then 20 (the design system's floor); then a compact panel (32 margins, title at 48); only as a last resort, a size below the floor rather than a hidden control (a dozen joystick buttons in a 5:4 window). The brand stays in its place, top left. The layout is rebuilt if the window changes shape. Layout guard (`app/src/layout-check.ts`, `html.loading-phase`): on a cabinet, no interface text over the media; on a desktop, the panel does not cover the frame, edges included, and the fallback sits exactly in the frame. Tests: [chargement.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/chargement.test.ts) (the real renderer under linkedom, measurements from `frontend.css`). Demo: `.\docs\demo-chargement.ps1`.
- **Occupied screens**: as soon as the game window appears, then every 2 s, the Windows window stack is read again (`occupiedRoles`): a role is occupied when a window from another process covers at least half of its zone (the whole screen, or its share of a shared screen: DMD strip under the backglass, embedded DMD) above the Cyber Pinball window. The console says so: "Partie : écrans occupés par VPX : backglass, dmd." (Play: screens occupied by VPX: backglass, dmd.)
- **Focus**: none of this shows a window or brings one to the foreground; only the state sent to the menu windows changes, and they stay behind VPX's windows (screen guard).
- **Simulated game** (off-screen tests only): `CYBERPINBALL_PARTIE_SIMULEE=<rôles occupés>` ("backglass,dmd", or empty) replaces VPX with a fake game (`simulateVpx`, `app/src/launcher.ts`): loading, a 10 s game with the autopilot, return to the menu, without launching anything or grabbing a global shortcut. On screen, the variable is ignored.

### Cinematic launch

On Start on "Jouer" (Play), the launch plays like the opening of a film, on every screen of the cabinet: the game logo lights up in the middle of the playfield and flies up to the backglass, a spot of light crosses the DMD, the logo lands on the backglass and waits there; when VPX takes over, a coil thumps and everything fades. Setting "Lancement en cinéma" (Cinematic launch) in **System menu > Médias (Media) > "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract mode)**: on by default; Start turns it off (loading goes back to what it was: no logo, no pass over the DMD, no coil thump) or back on (the coil thump plays). Saved in `<données>\medias.json`, field `cinema`. The in-game display adjustment (game page > "Régler l'affichage en partie") has no cinematic launch, just as it has no coin.

| Time since Start | Playfield | DMD (live) | Backglass |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 – 240 ms | the logo appears in the middle, over the loading media; a short ripple leaves it | the table title, the ball at the end of the rail | its media |
| 240 – 340 ms | it pulls back a notch, like a ball drawn back by the plunger | the ball shakes by one dot | |
| 340 – 740 ms | it shoots up (towards the backglass) and leaves the screen | | |
| 660 – 900 ms | the loading media, alone | a 9-dot disc of light crosses the display from bottom to top, then a 40 ms flash | the backglass dims (800 – 1,080 ms) |
| 840 – 1,200 ms | | the title and the rolling ball (loading) | the logo enters from the bottom, barely overshoots, lands; a ripple as it lands (1,200 – 1,600 ms) |
| then | | | the logo waits, a halo breathes behind it |
| VPX takes over | VPX covers the screen | VPX takes the display | coil thump; logo and dimming fade in 240 ms |

- **VPX never waits for the animation.** The choreography starts on Start and lives in the menu windows that are already open: CSS animations (`frontend.css`, "Lancement en cinéma") and the live DMD's `lancement` animation. The main process only keeps its state (`LaunchCinema`, [packages/core/src/cinema.ts](../packages/core/src/cinema.ts)), set on Start and read by each window with its state (`viewFor`: `cinema` field), with no waiting and no timer: VPX starts at the same moment as with the setting off (after the loading screen, `CYBERPINBALL_LOADING_MS`, 2.5 s by default), which the console states for every game, with what took that time ("Lancement : VPX lancé 2731 ms après le Start (écran de chargement 2500 ms, préparation 224 ms ; lancement en cinéma joué)." — the preparation is the screens being re-read, the PuP-Pack and the FullDMD dressing; nothing else is left). If VPX takes over in the middle of the choreography, it stops there: whatever had not started yet never starts.
- **The handover is a real signal**, never a delay: the launcher ([app/src/launcher.ts](../app/src/launcher.ts), `onForeground`) reads the foreground window on every pass (250 ms); as soon as VPX's game window holds it, the coil thumps, once per launch (`LaunchCinema.handover`). If Windows never gives it the foreground, it does not thump. The logo and the dimming fade as soon as the game window appears ("game" state), usually on the same pass.
- **Screens in sync**: each window counts from the time of Start (`startedAt`); a window that sets up the choreography late picks it up at the right point. The timing is written once (`CINEMA_TIMELINE`, cinema.ts); the stylesheet and the DMD follow it, which the test checks.
- **Where**: on the playfield, the `#cinema` layer, turned like the menu (up is the backglass, from the flipper side); on a cabinet the logo only passes through (under a second), then the loading media stays alone, as the loading screen requires. On a desktop it starts from the media's 9:16 frame and leaves it through the top; the title and controls panel does not move. On the backglass, its zone (its own window, or its part of a shared screen).
- **Fallbacks**: without a logo (or an unreadable one), the wheel's medallion, dark with a chrome ring, with the title (three lines at most). Without a backglass, the logo still flies off and leaves the playfield: nothing waits for it elsewhere, loading stays the same. Classic or absent DMD: no pass. Interface sounds off: no coil thump, the rest plays.
- **Never a light screen**: no window is opened for the cinematic launch; everything happens in the menu windows, on their dark backgrounds, and the fade only touches the cinematic layer.
- **Design**: mock-up and timing reviewed by Codex, art director (26/10/2026): ignition with no bounce, a single small deceleration on landing, short thin ripples, backglass dimmed to 64 %, a calm halo while waiting; the DMD in its orange matrix (9-dot disc, core 14, 10-dot trail, a local flash at 15 only).
- **Demonstration**: `.\docs\demo-cinema.ps1` captures the key frames off screen, on a cabinet (playfield, DMD, backglass) and on a desktop, with and without a logo, without a backglass, at the handover; then runs a simulated game in the real menu, with and without the setting, and checks that VPX starts as soon as the loading screen and the preparation are over (nothing else, to within 150 ms), that each window shows its scene, and that the coil thumps at the handover. Tests: [cinema.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/cinema.test.ts) (the state returns no promise and sets no timer; `play()` launches VPX without waiting for anything from the choreography; the handover only comes from the Windows foreground; DMD animation; stylesheet timing; real rendering of the windows under linkedom).

### Attract mode

After a while with no button pressed on the table menu, the cabinet shows itself off (`AttractMode`, `pickAttractTable`, [packages/core/src/menumoments.ts](../packages/core/src/menumoments.ts)):

- **Delay**: "Mode attente" (Attract mode) (5 min by default; 1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 15 or 30 min; **never**). The countdown restarts with every button press and every click, and when you come back to the table menu: it never runs on the details screen, the help, a message, a settings screen or during a game.
- **Tables**: a table drawn at random every N seconds ("Table suivante en attente" (Next table in attract mode), 20 s by default), never the table selected in the menu nor one of the last ones shown (about a third of the tables), so that every table eventually comes up. Each screen shows that table's media; the playfield shows its background (less darkened than in the menu), its logo or, failing that, its title as a backup logo (`fallback.js`), manufacturer and year, and "Appuyez sur un bouton pour revenir au menu" (Press any button to return to the menu).
- **Exit**: the first button, whichever it is, or a click, returns to the menu as it was, with no other effect: the selection does not move, Start does not open the details screen, Exit does not open the system menu.
- **Sound**: "Son en attente" (Attract mode sound) is off by default; when set to reduced, the music of the table shown (or its video's sound) plays at 30% of its volume.

### Cabinet schedule

Like an arcade cabinet, the cabinet lives on its own when nobody is playing (attract mode, above), and a schedule rules its day (`ArcadeHours`, [packages/core/src/arcadehours.ts](../packages/core/src/arcadehours.ts); in the menu, [app/src/frontend.ts](../app/src/frontend.ts) and [app/src/arcade-hours.ts](../app/src/arcade-hours.ts)). System menu > "Médias" (Media) > "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract mode) > **"Horaire de la borne"** (Cabinet schedule); the settings go into `<data>\medias.json` (`hours` field). Mock-up reviewed by Codex: `tmp\maquette-attente` (the feature's worktree).

- **Cabinet schedule: off** by default. While off, its settings stay visible and editable ("Settings kept; no effect while the schedule is off").
- **Night**: "Night starts" (23:00) and "Night ends" (8:00), in whole hours; the night spans midnight. Equal start and end are refused in the list ("choose two different times"), rather than an invented 24-hour night. Local time; a clock change follows the wall clock.
- **Screens during night attract mode**: at night, in attract mode only, a black veil covers every window (media, zones, menu, prompt included): **black veil at 50, 70 (default) or 85%**, **black screens** (100%) or **screens unchanged**. It comes down in 4 s and lifts at once at the first button. Under black screens, once the veil is opaque, the videos stop and the table shown no longer changes. Attract mode is **silent at night**, whatever this choice. With attract mode set to "never", there is no veil (the settings screen line says so).
- **Automatic stop**: at a time (2:00 by default) or **never**; **stop mode**: **sleep** (default) or **shutdown**. At that time, if the cabinet is **free** (attract mode running, or in the menu with no button for the attract delay, 5 min at least; never during a game, a loading screen, the Windows desktop or another power action), the playfield shows a **30 s warning** above the veil: "Going to sleep in 22 s" (or "Shutting down in"), a draining gauge, "Press a button to keep the cabinet on". At zero, the stop goes through the power module (section "Shutting down, sleeping, restarting the cabinet"): the same plain black screen on every screen, settings saved before acting, `SetSuspendState` or `shutdown.exe`. The warning is repeated neither on the DMD nor by a sound (Codex review).
- **The slightest button gives control back**: during the warning, a button or a click removes it at once, with no other effect (like leaving attract mode); the cabinet stays on as long as it is used. A game in progress holds the stop back: it waits until the cabinet is free again, with a new full warning, until the **end of the night**; after that, it is no longer made and the stop is scheduled for the next night. Woken up within the stop window (between 2:00 and 8:00) then left alone, the cabinet goes back to sleep. A stop time outside the night is flagged in orange; it then applies for one hour only (otherwise a stop at exactly 8:00 would stop the cabinet all day).
- **Missed, not caught up**: with the cabinet already asleep or off during the stop window, the stop is missed (`onMissed` of the power module), never made on wake.
- **Waking up**: from sleep, the PC power button; or a cabinet button if Windows allows its device to wake the PC (Device Manager > the device > Power Management). The screen's "Réveil depuis la veille" (Waking from sleep) block says so, read from Windows (`MSPower_DeviceWakeEnable`, keyboard, HID and mouse classes), without writing anything there: "Devices allowed by Windows: Pinscape Controller, HID Keyboard Device", "No allowed device detected", or "Device list unavailable". A permission is not a guarantee: waking with a cabinet button has to be checked on the cabinet (the BIOS must also power USB in sleep). After a shutdown, only the PC power button turns the cabinet back on. Cyber Pinball never ticks that box itself and schedules no Windows wake-up (wake timer, scheduled task): to be decided with the user.
- **Tests**: never a real sleep or a real shutdown (`powerSimulated`: the stop is logged, "Alimentation simulée : …"). In tests only, `CYBERPINBALL_HORLOGE=2026-10-27T01:59:48` shifts the schedule's clock (the cabinet believes it is 1:59) and `CYBERPINBALL_REVEIL=Name 1|Name 2` ("-": unavailable) replaces reading Windows; outside tests, the clock is the PC's.
- **Proofs**: [arcadehours.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/arcadehours.test.ts) (night spanning midnight, stop window, warning, button that removes it, game until morning, sleep during the window, settings read again; never a real executor in a test; read-only wake-up; settings closed to guests; rendering of the veil and the warning). `.\docs\demo-attente.ps1`: night, black screens, sleep, button, shutdown and settings scenarios, offscreen, on the cabinet and on a two-screen desktop, in French and English (captures in `tmp\demo-attente`).

### Menu DMD

While you browse, Cyber Pinball draws the DMD display itself, like a machine in attract mode: a 128 × 32 dot grid that cycles through the selected table's title, the invitation to play, its high scores, its DMD media, the time and the credits. Setting "DMD du menu" (Menu DMD) in **System menu > "Médias" (Media) > "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract mode)** ( Start switches between the two, saved in `<data>\medias.json`, field `dmd`): **live** by default, or **classic** (the table's DMD media, else the generated DMD that scrolls its title, as before).

| Page | What it shows | Duration |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Title | the title in large (9 × 14 dots), manufacturer and year below in small; a title wider than the display (3-dot margins included) starts left-aligned, holds for 1 s, scrolls at 24 dots per second, holds 1 s on its end | 4.8 s, more if the title scrolls |
| PRESS START | in large, blinks twice (0.8 s on, 0.4 s off) | 2.4 s |
| High scores | one page per rank, 4 at most: "HIGH SCORE #1  MPT" in small, the score in large, digits grouped by three | 2.4 s each |
| DMD media | the table's DMD (or FullDMD) media, image or video, if there is one | 5 s |
| Clock | the time in very large (18 × 28 dots), one shade down, the colon beating the seconds | 3 s |
| Credits | "CREDITS n": the credits the table saved, 0 otherwise (the menu has no coin door) | 1.8 s |

- **Cycle** (`liveDmdPages`, `LiveDmdProgram`, [packages/core/src/livedmd.ts](../packages/core/src/livedmd.ts)): in the table's order, looping; a page with nothing to show (no high scores, no media) is skipped, never replaced by an empty page. About 28 s for a table with 4 high scores and a media. Selecting another table starts again from the title; so does the same table when its high scores or media change. Between two dot pages, the new one pushes the old one up (160 ms); two high scores replace each other without movement; to and from the media, a hard cut, which does not move the FullDMD artwork.
- **No table** (system menu, settings, help, messages: the default display): the stacked brand in its font (CYBER at shade 11, PINBALL at full brightness), PRESS START, the clock.
- **Loading**: the "chargement" animation ("LOADING" and a 4 × 2-dot ball rolling on a rail) until the game starts; with the cinematic launch, the "lancement" animation (the table title, the ball shaking at the plunger, the disc of light of the passing logo, then the same rolling ball; see [Cinematic launch](#cinematic-launch)). **Game**: nothing more is sent, VPX owns the display; the DMD screen goes back to its in-game background ("Loading and play"). Desktop mode also stops the display.
- **On the DMD screen** (`liveDmdArea`): DMD only, the dots fill the 4:1 strip. FullDMD: if the table's FullDMD media has a drawn DMD frame (searched as for the in-game artwork, once per media, kept in `<data>\cache\fulldmd-cadres.json`), the dots sit inside that frame, inset by one dot, where VPX will put its DMD during the game, and the media shows around them; the media page then shows the media alone. Without a media, or without a frame: the 4:1 strip across the full width, centred, black around it. Without a table, no strip: the fallback FullDMD shows the brand over its whole 160 × 90 grid. The frame of a video not searched yet is searched in the background: the strip shows first, the frame as soon as it is found. Under dots drawn on black, the zone's video is paused.
- **Rendering**: 128 × 32 grid, 16 shades (0: dot off, still visible; 14 for titles, scores and PRESS START; 11 for the clock, the credits, the manufacturer and the high score labels; 5 for the rail), in the chosen colour (signal green by default, see [Brand backglass and DMD](#brand-backglass-and-dmd)), a round hole per dot and a light glow (`CyberFallback.dotMatrix`, [app/renderer/fallback.js](../app/renderer/fallback.js)); an unlit dot stays a barely visible neutral grey, never a shade of the colour. "Saira Points" font, drawn after Saira ([packages/core/src/dmdfont.ts](../packages/core/src/dmdfont.ts)): small 5 × 7, large 9 × 14 (drawn dot by dot, no more Scale2x), clock from the large one doubled (18 × 28), brand 11 × 11; accents removed, unknown signs as "?". The display's words stay those of pinball machines (PRESS START, CREDITS, HIGH SCORE). Mock-ups and values reviewed by Codex, art director (sheet: `docs\recherche\maquettes\dmd-vivant\planche.ps1`, rendered off screen with the real engine).
- **How it works**: the program is computed without a browser (`livedmd.ts`); the main process runs it ([app/src/live-dmd.ts](../app/src/live-dmd.ts)) and only sends the DMD screen's window (channel `dmd`) the frames that change: a still page is sent once, what moves (scrolling, blinking, clock, push, animation) at 25 frames per second. The window draws them as dots over the DMD zone (`syncLiveDmd`, [app/renderer/frontend.js](../app/renderer/frontend.js)); a reloaded window receives the current frame straight away.
- **On-demand animations** (for "Lancement en cinéma" and later features): `playDmdAnimation(name, { label })` ([app/src/frontend.ts](../app/src/frontend.ts)) plays an animation over the cycle and returns `{ stop(), done }`; the cycle resumes on `stop()`, or at the end of an animation that has a duration. Animations are listed by name in `DMD_ANIMATIONS` (`livedmd.ts`): `chargement` and `lancement`, whose text `label` can replace.
- **High scores** ([packages/core/src/highscores.ts](../packages/core/src/highscores.ts), a source shared with the interface sounds): VPX 10.8.1 stores what a table saves in `<table folder>\user\VPReg.stg`, an OLE compound file (one storage per name chosen by the script, one UTF-16LE stream per value). VPW tables and most original tables keep `HighScore1..N`, `HighScore1Name..N` and `Credits` there (checked on the cabinet: Guardians of the Galaxy, Game of Thrones, The Goonies). Nothing is made up: without `VPReg.stg`, without a table of scores, or with several storages none of which carries the file name or the title, there is no high score page. The file is read again as soon as it changes (after a game). `newRecords(before, after)` gives the scores that entered the table, for the knocker. Not covered: PinMAME tables whose ROM keeps the high scores in `nvram\<rom>.nv` (it would take each ROM's memory map, the pinmame-nvram-maps project).
- **Hardware DMDs** (ZeDMD, PIN2DMD, Pixelcade): they do not receive the menu display yet; VPX alone drives them during a game, through the DMDUtil plugin. Studied on 26 October 2026, what is missing:
  1. A callable interface: the libraries shipped with VPX 10.8.1 (`plugins\dmdutil\zedmd64.dll`, `dmdutil64.dll`) only export C++ classes (`ZeDMD::Open`, `ZeDMD::RenderRgb888`, `DMDUtil::DMD`…), no C functions: it would take a native module built for Electron.
  2. Shared access: a serial port (ZeDMD, Pixelcade) or a libusb device (PIN2DMD) has a single owner. Held by the menu at launch, DMDUtil could not open it and the DMD would stay dark during the game; handing it over before every launch and taking it back on return leaves no room for error.
  3. Safe detection: it stays passive (USB identifiers, "Real DMD"); a CH340 or CP210x may be something other than a ZeDMD, and sending it a handshake would be risky.
  4. A test bench: ZeDMD's serial protocol (handshake, compression, firmware versions) is only described by libzedmd's code, and the reference cabinet has no hardware DMD.

  The safe route is libdmdutil's server (`dmdserver`): it alone holds the hardware, VPX (`[Plugin.DMDUtil] DMDServer`, `DMDServerAddr`, `DMDServerPort`) and the menu send it their frames over the local network. It would need installing (it is not shipped with VPX 10.8.1), starting with the menu, switching VPX to it, and writing a client for its protocol in `live-dmd.ts` (a "DMDStream" header then the frame, according to libdmdutil's code, to be checked on a real DMD). The frames are ready: `LiveDmd` already produces the 128 × 32-shade grid a hardware DMD displays.
- **Tests**: [livedmd.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/livedmd.test.ts) (font, DMD text, every page within its margins, fixed or scrolling title, order and durations, pushes, dot placement, animation, only changed frames sent, pause during a game), [highscores.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/highscores.test.ts) (VPReg.stg, several tables in one file, nothing made up, new high scores). Demonstration: `.\docs\demo-dmd-vivant.ps1` (DMD only on a 4:1 screen and as a strip on a 16:9 screen, dressed FullDMD, no media and no table, media page, loading; captures in `tmp\captures\dmd-vivant`).

### Brand backglass and DMD

Wherever Cyber Pinball writes its brand or draws a dot display, it follows the art direction ([docs/design-system](design-system/README.en.md)), with one rule for every screen (feedback of 27 October 2026: a backglass in big generic capitals, an orange DMD in an ordinary dot font). Mock-ups, font and colours reviewed by Codex, art director.

- **The brand** is written only by its components (`app/renderer/fallback.js`): on one line, "C Y B E R  P I N B A L L" (light Saira, widened to 125%, widely spaced, PINBALL in signal green) on the topper, the loading screen, the "in game" screen and the logo without a title; stacked, CYBER above PINBALL, on the backglass. Never as a game title.
- **Backglass without a game** (system menu, settings, help, messages): the design system's cover. The art direction's illustration (`app/renderer/fronton-marque.webp`, the cover's teal ball and flippers) on the right, faded to black under the stacked brand on the left; in 4:3 and in portrait, the brand moves up and the illustration fills the bottom, its top always faded; in a strip (3:1, 4:1), the brand takes half the height. Fixed light. A game's backglass without media keeps its card (title, rule, manufacturer and year).
- **Display font**: "Saira Points" ([packages/core/src/dmdfont.ts](../packages/core/src/dmdfont.ts)), drawn dot by dot after Saira, at the DMD's real resolution: rectangles with chamfered corners, C and G with horizontal terminals, R with a slanted leg, pointed A, open 4. Small 5 × 7 (running text); large 9 × 14 with two-dot strokes (titles, scores, PRESS START; a 12-letter title fits in 122 dots); clock: the large one doubled by nearest neighbour (18 × 28); brand 11 × 11 with one-dot strokes, 4 dots apart (PINBALL: 91 dots). The live DMD (main process) and the windows' fallback displays (`dmd` at 128 × 32, `fullDmd` at 160 × 90, which enlarges the large one ×3 or ×2 for a short title) write with the same glyphs: the windows read a copy, `app/renderer/police-points.js`, written by `node scripts/police-points.mjs` after `npm run build`; a test rejects an outdated copy. Press Start 2P, the former font of small texts, is no longer shipped.
- **The brand on a display**: stacked, 3 free rows at the top and bottom, 4 between the words. A display has only one colour: light carries the hierarchy the interface gives by colour, CYBER at shade 11, PINBALL at full brightness (15).
- **DMD colour** (System menu > "Médias" (Media) > "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract mode) > **"Couleur du DMD" (DMD colour)**, kept in `<data>\medias.json`, field `dmdColor`): the colour of the dots of the menu DMD and of the fallback displays, not of VPX's DMD during play (the table's own). **Signal green** `#8BE23A` by default (the design system's `dmd` token), or a preset: teal `#36C9B4`, light ink `#E6F0EC`, glacier blue `#5FB4CF`, soft amber `#F2B33D`, plasma orange `#FF8A1F` (the former colour); or **"Couleur libre…" (Custom colour…)**, typed in hexadecimal on the on-screen keyboard (`#8BE23A`, `8be23a` or `#8be`). A colour too dark (no component at 112 or more) is refused, with the reason; an unreadable value in `medias.json` falls back to signal green. The choice returns to its row; the DMD, which shows the brand in the system menu, changes at once (`setDmdColor`, [app/renderer/fallback.js](../app/renderer/fallback.js), applied with every state; the minimal fallback follows the `--dmd` token).
- **Unlit dot**: a barely visible neutral grey, the same for every colour, instead of a veil of the colour over the whole grid.
- **Proofs**: [marque-dmd.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/marque-dmd.test.ts) (font copy up to date and loaded before `fallback.js`; no display text drawn with a text font; brand written only by its components; same default colour in the core, `fallback.js`, `theme.css` and the design system; presets readable and taken from the art direction; custom colour and refused colour; setting read, kept and sent to the windows), [livedmd.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/livedmd.test.ts) (drawing of the three sizes, brand at its shades). `.\docs\demo-replis.ps1`: every fallback for six games and "no game" (the brand) at eight sizes, and the DMD in every preset, off screen, under the layout guard (captures in `tmp\captures\replis`); `.\docs\demo-dmd-vivant.ps1`: the real application's live DMD.

### Manager: "Médias du jeu"

Details screen > "Médias du jeu" (Game media): what the menu shows for this game, screen by screen, without handling any file (design: `docs/recherche/medias.md`, § 5). One card per media item: its label ("Logo", "Plateau" (Playfield), "Fronton" (Backglass), "Afficheur" (Display), "Afficheur large" (Wide display), "Topper", "Chargement" (Loading), "Musique du jeu" (Game music), "Son de lancement" (Launch sound), "Prospectus" (Flyer), "Règles" (Rules)) and its file name in parentheses, **where** it appears (screen and moment, in plain words, instead of Popper's screen names), its **role** in one sentence, a preview (the image, a frame from the video, or the description of the fallback), and the status: "Présent" (Present), or the fallback in use and which one ("Repli : image du .directb2s" (Fallback: .directb2s image), "Repli : défaut de la borne" (Fallback: cabinet default), "Repli : Afficheur large" (Fallback: Wide display), "Repli intégré" (Built-in fallback)), with dimensions and file size. A media item the cabinet does not show says so ("Cette borne n'a pas d'écran topper" (This cabinet has no topper screen), or a narrow display that shows the "Afficheur" rather than the "Afficheur large"). The files in `medias\` that the game does not use are listed with the reason (another game's name, unreadable format, type with no screen).

- **Actions**, with the buttons or the mouse: take a file from `<racine>\import` (subfolders included, only the formats the menu reads for this media type), pick it on this PC, or drag it onto the window (on the overview, the type is read from the name: `(Wheel) …`, VPinFE's `bg.png`, Popper's `Wheel` folder or PinballX's `Backglass Images`, otherwise it is the selected media item); replace; move to the trash; half-turn of the playfield and of the loading video (the preview is rotated the way the menu will show it, as seen by the player); relative game volume (0 to 200%); for the flyer and the rules, the list of pages, and for each page move up, move down, replace or remove.
- **Nothing is written before "Enregistrer" (Save)**, which first shows the plan, one line per media item. Files are stored as `(Type) <nom du dossier>[ N].ext` (a file from the import folder leaves it, a file picked on this PC or dragged in is copied); any other file of that type for this game (old file, another format that would take precedence, VPinFE name, unreadable format) goes to the trash; pages are renumbered in two passes, so that none overwrites another. Half-turn and volume go into the `.info` under `CyberPinball.medias` (`halfTurn`, `volume`), with its other fields kept. Leaving with unsaved changes asks "Quitter sans enregistrer ?" (Leave without saving?).
- Code: `packages/core/src/mediaedit.ts` (where each media item appears, draft, plan, renaming, `.info`), tests in `packages/core/src/test/mediaedit.test.ts`; screens in `app/src/library-medias.ts`, cards in `app/renderer/library.js` and `library.css`.

### Importing media

Other frontends organize media differently; the import recognizes them and renames them. Code: [packages/core/src/mediaimport.ts](../packages/core/src/mediaimport.ts), hooked into table adding ([tableimport.ts](../packages/core/src/tableimport.ts)) and into the takeover screen ([app/src/media-import.ts](../app/src/media-import.ts)); tests in [mediaimport.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/mediaimport.test.ts).

**What is recognized**, in an archive, a selection or the import folder:

| Layout | Example | Type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PinUP Popper | `POPMedia\Visual Pinball X\Wheel\<jeu>.png`, folders `PlayField`, `Backglass`, `DMD`, `Menu` (FullDMD), `Topper`, `Loading`, `Audio`, `AudioLaunch`, `GameInfo`, `GameHelp`, `Other2` | folder name; pages `<jeu>01.png`, `<jeu>02.mp4` in order |
| PinballX | `Media\Visual Pinball\Wheel Images\<jeu>.png`, `Table Videos`, `Table Images`, `Backglass Videos`, `DMD Videos`, `FullDMD Videos`, `Topper Videos`, `Launch Audio`, `Table Audio`, `Loading Videos`, `Real DMD…`; `Media\Flyer Images\Front`, `Back`, `Inside1`… (flyer pages, in that order) | folder name |
| VPinFE | `medias\bg.png`, `wheel.png`, `table.mp4`, `dmd.png` (FullDMD)… | fixed file name |
| Already named | `(Backglass) <n'importe quel nom>.mp4`, anywhere | the name |

A Popper folder with an ambiguous name (`DMD`, `Audio`, `Backglass`, `Topper`, `Loading`, `Menu`) is only recognized under `POPMedia` or next to an unambiguous folder (`Wheel`, `PlayField`, `GameHelp`…): alone in an archive, a `DMD` folder is more often a table's FlexDMD content. A DMD folder loaded by the script always stays a DMD folder.

**Assigning to a game**: in a single-table package, the media belong to that table. Otherwise, the file name decides (the game name for Popper and PinballX, `(Type) <nom>`, the table folder for VPinFE): name of a `.vpx` or of the folder, ROM, title (manufacturer and year break ties), game recognized by the VPS database (compared with the `VPSId` in the `.info` of installed tables). A media file named after a game that is neither in the import nor among the installed tables stays where it is ("Non importé" (Not imported)).

**Storage**: `(Type) <nom du dossier>.ext`, for all versions of the game. A single file per type, in the best format (video before image for screens, Visual Pinball X before Visual Pinball 9); the others are flagged "Doublon" (Duplicate). Flyers and rules keep all their pages (`(GameHelp) <nom>.png`, `(GameHelp) <nom> 2.png`…). A media file of the same type already in the table (including another extension or other pages) goes to the trash; the plan marks it "remplace l'existant" (replaces the existing one).

**Never stored, always flagged in the plan**: formats the menu does not read (`flv`, `f4v`, `avi`, `mov`, checked on Electron 44.5.1: convert them to mp4 or webm), a format not intended for the type (a video as a logo, for example), media for an emulator other than Visual Pinball (`POPMedia\Future Pinball\…`). In move mode (import folder), they stay in `import\`.

**"Reprendre les médias de PinUP Popper / PinballX" (Take over media from PinUP Popper / PinballX)** (system menu, Exit in the menu): offers the installations found in the usual locations (`C:\vPinball\PinUPSystem\POPMedia`, `C:\PinballX\Media`…) or a chosen folder (`PinUPSystem`, `POPMedia`, an emulator's or a type's folder, the PinballX folder or its `Media`). Each media file is matched to an installed table, never by guessing: a name that refers to several tables ("Fathom" for two versions stored in two folders) is not copied. The plan gives, table by table, the types received, their size, how the table was recognized and what is replaced; then the unreadable formats, the games without a table and the other emulators. Free space is checked beforehand. "Copier" (Copy) **copies**: the original installation is never modified. PuP-Packs (`PUPVideos`) and Popper's default media (`System`, `Default`) are not taken over.

**Unrecognized cases**: Popper's `MediaSearch` field (a media item shared by several tables, with a wildcard) and `MediaWildCard` (several files picked at random: only one is kept); media named differently from the game in Popper's or PinballX's database, when that name resembles neither the `.vpx`, the folder, the title nor a VPS game (they are listed as "Sans table" (No table)); PinballX's `Table Images Desktop` and `Table Videos Desktop` (desktop views); Popper's `Wheelbar` and themes; subfolders of a type folder, other than PinballX flyers.

Unattended demo, with the window never shown (screenshots in `tmp\captures\import-medias`): `npm run build`, then `npx electron docs/demo-import-medias.js`.

### The media doctor

The doctor ([doctor.ts](../packages/core/src/doctor.ts), details screen > "Diagnostiquer et réparer" (Diagnose and repair)) checks, without launching anything:

| Finding | When | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Format the menu does not read | `flv`, `f4v`, `avi`, `mov` (checked with `canPlayType` in Electron 44.5.1), `wmv`, `mpg`, `mpeg` (not among Chromium's formats); or a format not intended for the type (`(Wheel)` as a `jpg`, `(Loading)` as an image…) | trash if a readable version has the same name; otherwise, convert (HandBrake) |
| Display stored under the wrong type | `(DMD)` in 16:9, or `(FullDMD)` in 4:1: the menu picks DMD or FullDMD from the type of the DMD screen ("Écran du DMD"; without an answer, its proportions, boundary at 8:3) | rename to the other type, if the name is free |
| Misnamed media | `(Type) nom` whose name is neither that of a `.vpx` in the folder nor that of the folder (pages included: `Nom 2`, `Nom_02`); file with neither `(Type)` nor a VPinFE name | rename to `(Type) <dossier>` (the next free page for the flyer and the rules), unless `medias\` already shows that type |
| Media stored outside `medias\` | PinUP Popper layout (`POPMedia\…\Wheel\<jeu>.png`) or PinballX layout (`Media\Visual Pinball\Wheel Images\…`) in the table folder, or `(Type) nom` placed next to the table; the `pupvideos`, `pinmame` and `cache` folders and the script's DMD folder are ignored | store and rename as `medias\(Type) <dossier>.ext`; the emptied tree goes to the trash |
| Proportions differ from the screen | a gap of more than 15% (`MEDIA_RATIO_TOLERANCE`, measured as a ratio) from the role's zone in the cabinet model: cropped playfield (only the aspect ratio matters, since the menu rotates the media; an almost square file does not tell where the flippers are), black bars or a portrait media item on a landscape screen for the backglass, DMD and topper, FullDMD shown shrunk for lack of a DMD | — (information) |
| Very heavy media | image over 25 MB or over 7680 × 4320 pixels, video over 300 MB, sound over 50 MB (`MEDIA_SIZE_LIMITS`, `MEDIA_MAX_PIXELS`) | — (re-encode) |
| Duplicate media | several files of the same type for the same game (`.png` and `.webp`, `.vpx` name and folder name, `(Wheel)` and VPinFE's `wheel.png`): only one is shown; a version in the folder that has its own media is not a duplicate | trash the never-shown files of the same kind; an image next to a video of the same type is only flagged |
| Media the menu could not read | error recorded by the display in the error file, for a file unchanged since then | trash for a damaged or rejected file (decoding, format, image); only flagged for a disk error |

Every fix is safe: rename, move into `medias\`, or move to the trash (`trash\`); nothing is converted or deleted. "Tout réparer" (Repair all) chains the fixes without any two of them targeting the same file; anything that depends on a rename (the type of a misnamed display, for example) is checked at the next analysis.

**Playback error file**: `<racine>\data\logs\medias-erreurs.json`, written by the main process ([mediaerrors.ts](../packages/core/src/mediaerrors.ts), `recordMediaError`) when the display passes it an error from `<video>`, `<audio>` or `<img>`. Format, version 1:

```json
{
  "version": 1,
  "errors": [
    {
      "file": "D:\\CyberPinball\\tables\\T2 (Williams 1991)\\medias\\(Backglass) T2 (Williams 1991).mp4",
      "type": "Backglass",
      "role": "backglass",
      "kind": "decode",
      "message": "PIPELINE_ERROR_DECODE",
      "size": 48211763,
      "mtimeMs": 1792500000000.123,
      "first": "2026-10-21T20:14:03.512Z",
      "last": "2026-10-21T20:31:40.007Z",
      "count": 3
    }
  ]
}
```

`kind` comes from `MediaError.code` (2 `network`, 3 `decode`, 4 `unsupported`, anything else `other`) or is `image` for `<img>`; an interrupted playback (code 1, the table was changed) is not recorded. `type`, `role` and `message` (300 characters at most) are optional. An entry only applies to the file as it was (same size, same date): a replaced or re-encoded file is no longer flagged, and its old entries disappear at the next write. 200 entries at most, the most recent ones.

### Unattended proofs

`.\docs\demo-medias.ps1` replays the whole milestone off screen, on the hidden Windows desktop, with the real menu: a sandbox of three tables with generated media (`docs\demo-medias.cjs`): a complete one (all types, two flyer pages, a rule card, music, launch sound, loading video), a partial one (logo, playfield as an image, backglass taken from the `.directb2s`), one with no media at all and a very long title, plus a default topper in `<racine>\media`. On the reference cabinet (`docs\ecrans\borne.json`: 4K playfield lying flat, player at the left edge, backglass, 4:1 DMD, topper), then on the two-screen PC (`poste-deux-ecrans.json`: landscape playfield, backglass and DMD as a strip on the other screen), the same sequence captures every screen at every moment: navigation over the three tables, the flyer page by page, the rules, their fallbacks, "Médias du jeu" (Game media) and its pages, loading (video, then backup screen), a simulated game occupying the backglass (free screens animated, "en partie" (during play) fallbacks), return to the menu, attract mode and leaving it. The script fails if a screen is empty (uniform image, `vides.txt`), if a text overlaps another, runs off the screen or is clipped (`mise-en-page.txt`, a safeguard on every screen), or if a screen does not show the expected media or fallback (layers recorded in `son.txt`). One contact sheet per step, with all the screens side by side and the playfield turned upright as the player sees it, in `tmp\captures\medias\<écrans>\planches`. `-Ecrans borne` replays only one of them.

### Media from VPinMediaDB: "Chercher les médias"

[VPinMediaDB](https://github.com/superhac/vpinmediadb) (the `superhac/vpinmediadb` repository on GitHub) stores media for about 1,155 games, one folder per VPS identifier. Cyber Pinball downloads them **on request only**, never automatically (design: `docs/recherche/medias.md`, § 3.1, § 3.4 and § 5.5).

- **Disclaimer**: VPinMediaDB declares no license (`license: null` in the GitHub API, no LICENSE file and no terms of use, checked on October 21, 2026). Its media belong to their authors, and the game artwork to its manufacturers: **personal use, on your own cabinet only**, without redistributing them. Cyber Pinball ships none of them. The disclaimer is shown on first use (accepted once, date stored in `<données>\mediadb.json`) and at the top of every plan. The source is credited in the plan, on the media item's details ("Source : VPinMediaDB (github.com/superhac/vpinmediadb), téléchargé le …" (Source: VPinMediaDB (github.com/superhac/vpinmediadb), downloaded on ...), followed by the file's address) and here.
- **Two entry points**:
  - a table's details screen > "Médias du jeu" (Game media) > "Chercher les médias" (Search for media): everything VPinMediaDB has for that game;
  - system menu > "Médias" (Media) > "Trouver les médias manquants" (Find missing media): whatever each table is missing, one table per folder. From here, an existing media file is never replaced, and a table matched by its title alone is not served automatically.
- **Matching**: by the `VPSId` in the `.info`. Otherwise, by the table's name (title, initials, manufacturer, year) matched against the VPS database (section "VPS database"), which supplies the identifier. An identifier that is known but missing from VPinMediaDB only leads to the other editions of the same game (Pro, Premium, LE), to be checked on the previews. When nothing is certain, deep search takes over (next section). "Pas le bon jeu ?" (Not the right game?) is always offered: it shows the possible games with the reasons for each, and lets you search for another title with the keyboard.
- **What is taken**:

  | VPinMediaDB | Media | Notes |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `wheel.png` | Logo `(Wheel)` | 500 × 500, transparent |
  | `1k/table.mp4`, otherwise `table.png` in 1k or 4k | Playfield `(Playfield)` | video first; landscape, flippers on the right, and the menu rotates it |
  | `1k/bg.png` | Backglass `(Backglass)` | 1k only |
  | `1k/dmd.mp4`, otherwise `1k/dmd.png` | Wide display `(FullDMD)` | 16:9; scaled down on a 4:1 display |
  | `flyer.png` | Flyer `(GameInfo)` | page 1 |
  | `audio.mp3` | Game music `(Audio)` | |

  Not taken: `cab.png` (photo of the cabinet), `fss.png` (playfield and backglass for a single screen), `realdmd*.png` (physical DMD, which Cyber Pinball does not handle). VPinMediaDB never has a 4:1 display, a topper, a loading video, a launch sound or rules.
- **1k or 4k**: only the playfield image exists in 4k. The "Qualité téléchargée (VPinMediaDB)" (Download quality (VPinMediaDB)) setting is in system menu > "Médias" (Media) > "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract mode):
  - automatic: 4k if the playfield zone, in the active configuration, is larger than 2,560 pixels on a side;
  - or 1k, or 4k, forced.

  The plan states what was chosen, and why.
- **Plan to approve**: one card per media item.
  - Each card shows the preview (the 1k image of the same media item, received and verified, which is then reused for the download), the size (HEAD request, cached by MD5 in `downloads\vpinmdb.sizes.json`), what gets replaced, and the name the media file will be stored under.
  - Start on a card switches to the next variant: video, image, or nothing.
  - Selected automatically: missing media, and the new version of a file that already came from VPinMediaDB. A media file added by the user stays "Gardé" (Kept); replacing it has to be requested ("Remplace l'actuel" (Replaces the current one); the old one goes to the trash).
- **Download**: one file at a time, received as `downloads\vpinmediadb\<md5>.ext.part`. The size and MD5 from the index are checked, then the file is stored as `medias\(Type) <nom du dossier>.ext`, and the old file goes to `trash\<date> Médias <dossier> (VPinMediaDB)`. An interrupted download (connection lost, 30 s without data) or an invalid one (MD5, size, HTTP response) is deleted: nothing is left behind, and the other media carry on. Progress: "Plateau (2 sur 6) : 1,2 Mo sur 1,7 Mo…" (Playfield (2 of 6): 1.2 MB of 1.7 MB...).
- **Source in the `.info`**: `CyberPinball.medias.sources.<Type>` receives `{ origin: "VPinMediaDB", url, date, vpsId, key, md5, file }`. The forced 180° turn of a replaced playfield is removed: the new file follows the standard, which the menu rotates on its own. A later search says "À jour" (Up to date), or offers the new version if the MD5 in the index has changed.
- **Index**: `vpinmdb.json` (1.7 MB), downloaded on first use from `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/superhac/vpinmediadb/main/vpinmdb.json` and kept in `<racine>\downloads`.
  - It is rechecked at most every 12 hours, in the background; GitHub answers 304 (ETag) if it has not changed.
  - Without a network connection, the cache is used, and an invalid response never replaces it.
  - Only addresses from the `superhac/vpinmediadb` repository are accepted. The `bg_thumbs` entry, which is empty, is ignored.
- **Measured volumes** (October 21, 2026, HEAD requests):

  | | 1k | 4k |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | Terminator 2, all defaults (playfield and display videos) | 14 MB | 14 MB |
  | Terminator 2, images only | 9.8 MB | 20 MB |
  | Playfield image only (Terminator 2) | 3.5 MB | 13 MB |
  | Average of 12 games, all defaults | 11 MB | 17 MB |
  | Average of 12 games, playfield image | 3.2 MB | 13 MB |

  4k only changes the playfield image, which is about four times heavier. By default, the playfield video (1k) takes precedence over it, when it exists.
- **Code**: `packages/core/src/mediadb.ts`, tests in `packages/core/src/test/mediadb.test.ts` (fake index and files served by a local server, no network access); screens in `app/src/media-download.ts`. Real trial without a window: `npm run build`, then `node docs/essai-vpinmediadb.mjs --types Wheel` (sandbox `tmp\essai-vpinmediadb`). Demo: `.\docs\demo-vpinmediadb.ps1` (section "Unattended end-to-end tests"). Manual tests: [docs/manual-tests.md](manual-tests.md), scenarios 160 to 168.

### Suggestions for each media item and deep search

The automatic choice is not always enough: the table's name has been changed, shortened or translated ("GOTG_2.1.1.vpx", title "Gardiens de la galaxie"), or you prefer another version of the backglass. A table's details screen > "Médias du jeu" (Game media) > a media item > **"Voir les propositions" (See suggestions)** shows every version found for that media item, each with its preview, where it comes from, and why.

- **What is suggested**, in this order:
  - VPinMediaDB, for **each selected game**: the recognized game, its other editions (Pro, Premium, LE), and the close matches you add; each variant (video or image, 1k or 4k), with a file shared by two editions shown only once;
  - what the **table already has** without knowing it:
    - in the **PuP-Pack**: the file that `screens.pup` assigns to the backglass, topper or display screen; those that `triggers.pup` sets at startup (`D0`) or in attract mode (`E454`), and the music of its `MusicOnly` screens (the game-mode backgrounds are in-game scenes, left out); the `PupImages` frames with a backglass aspect ratio (`-4x3`, `-5x4`, `-16x9`); folders with telling names (`BGDefault`, `Topper`, `DMDAttract`...), except for a file whose screen `screens.pup` or `triggers.pup` already state; music files (over 500 KB);
    - the animations in the FlexDMD or UltraDMD folder that the script loads;
    - in **each `.directb2s` in the folder**: the lit image and the display, then the same images **"tout allumés" (all lit)**, with every visible bulb from `<Illumination>` laid over them the way the B2S server shows them when they light up (a piece of lit image is laid on as is, a glow takes the bulb's color). The compositing is done once, with no dependency (PNG reading and writing in `packages/core/src/png.ts`), and kept in `<données>\cache\medias` (`b2sLitImages`, `packages/core/src/b2simage.ts`); on Terminator 2 (8 flashers, 2,517 × 2,397 image), about 4 s the first time. A second `.directb2s` is another possible backglass, offered with its name ("que VPX ne charge pas" (which VPX does not load)); a copy of the same file under another name, which is common (the site's name next to the table's name), is read and shown only once;
    - in the **`.vpx`**: the screenshot the author stored in it; the flat playfield image (`IMAG` field, except Visual Light Mapper atlases); the rule card that the rules Markdown points to (`![…](nom)`), otherwise an image with a card-like name; the backglass and the logo from the VR room. Only the start of each image is read to get its name; the selected images are written once to `<données>\cache\medias` (`vpx-<clé>-…`), and read again if the `.vpx` changes;
  - the **community sites**, with the user's own account: **VPForums**, then **VPUniverse** as a last resort (see "Community site media" below);
  - the **VPS database thumbnails**: the playfield of each version of the table and the backglass of each `.directb2s` (from 352 × 626 to 1,000 × 750, "basse définition" (low definition)), often the only image of a game missing from VPinMediaDB: 2,608 games have a playfield one, 1,746 a backglass one;
  - the **VPS database music** (`soundFiles`): Dropbox share links to an mp3 or ogg, downloaded only when you choose the card, one at a time. The received file is checked by its signature (`isAudioFile`): a dead link returns an HTML page, and the card then says "Lien mort" (Dead link).
- **Choosing**: Start on a suggestion puts it in the draft, like a file from the import folder (a new page for the flyer and the rules). An online file is downloaded and verified (VPinMediaDB MD5) at the moment it is chosen; a file from the table is copied, and the original stays in place. The card says "Actuel" (Current) or "Choisi, à enregistrer" (Chosen, to be saved); the "Actuel" card of an online file shows the cabinet's file (the video itself, not the site's preview image). **Taking the current media again changes nothing**: Start on the "Actuel" card says so instead of putting it back in the draft, and a chosen file whose content is the current one's, byte for byte (size then MD5, `sameFileContent`), brings the draft back to today's state with "identique au média actuel" (identical to the current media): without this, "Enregistrer" (Save) rewrote the same file and the old one seemed to stay. Nothing is written before "Enregistrer" (Save), which shows the plan; the origin of a downloaded file (VPinMediaDB or VPS database, address, date, VPS identifier) goes into the `.info`, and disappears if the media file is later replaced by hand. A suggested music track plays as soon as its card is selected.
- **Gallery**: suggestions are shown as tiles, four per row on a desktop, three on the cabinet, the preview in the shape of the screen that shows the media (16:9 for the wide display and loading, 4:1 for the display, 4:3 for the backglass, square for the wheel, upright for the playfield and pages); each tile carries the name, the source, the state and the popularity, and under the gallery sits the detail of the chosen tile (where it comes from, what arrives on download, the game it belongs to). Start puts the suggestion in the draft **and stays on the suggestions**: the tile turns "Choisi, à enregistrer" (Chosen, to be saved), the message says under which name it will be stored.
- **One media item after another**: "Média suivant : …" (Next media: …) and "Média précédent : …" (Previous media: …) open the suggestions of the neighbouring media item among those the cabinet shows (the 4:1 Display is skipped on a cabinet that shows the Wide display), without going back to the overview; the detail says when the neighbour already has a pending choice. "Enregistrer" (Save) appears as soon as there is a change and opens the same plan as the overview.
- **Popularity**: the download count **VPUniverse** gives for each file page ("510 téléchargements", 510 downloads) is on the tile; "Trier : pertinence" (Sort: relevance) becomes "Trier : popularité" (Sort: popularity) (most downloaded first, then the usual order; stable sort, kept from one media item to the next during the visit). VPinMediaDB gives none (a GitHub repository without a counter), nor does the VPS database; VPForums is not read for it until it has a witness page of its results (`packages/core/test-pages`). The entry only appears when a suggestion has that count (`sortProposals`, `app/src/media-proposals.ts`).
- **"Autre jeu" (Other game)**: a suggestion from a VPS game that is not the table's own (same title, other authors, other year) carries this sign, and its detail names both. For The Matrix (VP_Cooks, JPSalas, 2023), VPinMediaDB only has the media of "The Matrix (Original 2026)", another table (VPW, March 2026): the year 2026 comes from the VPS database, not from a Cyber Pinball error. The "Chercher les médias" (Search for media) plan says so too (`otherGameRows`).
- **The choice holds, even when the list moves** (`packages/core/src/focus.ts`): while you look, a file page received from a site turns into its files, a download made in "Récupérer" (Get) lands at the top, a preview arrives. The cursor points at an item, not a rank: each action has an identity (its key, `mediaChoiceKey` for a suggestion, otherwise its label when unique), the cursor follows it from one view to the next of the same screen, and a click carries the identity of the clicked tile (ignored if it has gone). A file awaited from a site only enters the draft on arrival if that media item has not changed since the request (`pagesSignature`): another choice made in the meantime, here or on the media item's screen (import folder, this PC, dropped file, media removed), wins. A card screen without identities is reported once in the log. The invariant "the stored media is the one validated" is pinned by `packages/core/src/test/choix-media.test.ts`.
- **Display the cabinet does not show**: a cabinet whose DMD screen is a FullDMD shows the Afficheur large (FullDMD); the Afficheur (4:1 DMD) is only used when it is missing (and the other way round for a DMD only). Its suggestions say so first ("Pas montré sur votre borne", Not shown on your cabinet) and "Propositions : Afficheur large" (Suggestions: FullDMD) goes straight to those of the media the cabinet shows. Sites often have FullDMD videos and no 4:1 DMD: without this warning, you searched on the wrong screen.
- **"Chercher sur le web…" (Search the web...)**, when nothing fits: the media's free search, the same as "Chercher moi-même sur le web…" (Search the web myself...) on its screen (see "Free search" below), on the title typed in "Chercher un autre titre…" (Search another title...) if there is one. What you download there then stays offered first ("Téléchargé du web", Downloaded from the web) for that table only; a pack laid out the Popper way also serves the other screens.
- **"Autres jeux…" (Other games...)**: the possible games, most likely first, with their logo (or a thumbnail), what they have online and their reasons; Start adds a game to or removes it from the suggestions. Selected automatically: the first game, its other editions, and those almost as likely (four at most).
- **"Chercher un autre titre…" (Search for another title...)**: typing on the on-screen keyboard (or a real keyboard) an English title ("lord of the rings"), initials ("TMNT") or a ROM name ("t2_l8"); the first game found is added to the suggestions.
- **Deep search**: every clue the table gives about itself, checked one by one against the VPS database, with each lead keeping its reasons in plain words:
  - the **name written into the table** by its author (File > Table Info in the VPX editor: the `TableInfo` storage of the `.vpx`, read without loading the table): "GOTG_2.1.1.vpx" is called "Guardians Of The Galaxy" there; its **author and version** point to the exact recreation in VPS; an **IPDB number** quoted in its description points to the game;
  - the title on the details screen, the name of the `.vpx`, of the folder and of the `.directb2s`;
  - the **ROM**, the **PuP-Pack** and the **DMD folder** read from the script (the script is read through VPX if it never has been before);
  - the **initials** ("GOTG", "TMNT", "TAF", "T2") and **fuzzy words**, for a translated title ("gardiens", "galaxie");
  - several independent clues pointing to the same game push it up; file names, which copy one another, count as one.

  The automatic plan ("Chercher les médias" (Search for media)) and the system menu's "médias manquants" (missing media) use it when the name is not enough: a table recognized this way is shown for checking, never served automatically.
- **Code**: `packages/core/src/vps.ts` (initials, fuzzy words, other editions, free search), `vpxinfo.ts` (reading the `.vpx`), `mediasearch.ts` (clues and leads), `mediachoices.ts` (suggestions), `mediaedit.ts` (origin on save), tests in `packages/core/src/test/mediasearch.test.ts` and `b2slit.test.ts`; screens in `app/src/media-proposals.ts` and `app/src/media-search.ts`. Off-screen demo, with the real VPinMediaDB: `.\docs\demo-propositions.ps1` (screenshots in `tmp\demo-propositions\captures`). Manual tests: [docs/manual-tests.md](manual-tests.md), scenarios 182 to 187 and 206 to 211.

### Table sounds

Table details screen > "Médias du jeu" (Game media) > "Musique du jeu" (Game music) or "Son de lancement" (Launch sound) > **"Sons de la table" (Table sounds)**: the sounds the table already has, by folder, to make one of them its presentation music or its launch sound. A PuP-Pack often has dozens (music in the `Topper` or `Music` folder, callouts, intro videos) that VPinMediaDB does not have: *Guardians of the Galaxy* has 38 music tracks and 64 videos with sound in its pack, and no music on VPinMediaDB.

- **Folders**: each with what it holds ("38 sons" (38 sounds), "1 vidéo avec du son" (1 video with sound)); those with a music name (`Music`, `Songs`, `Topper`, `Attract`…) first, then `medias\`, then the others. A PuP-Pack folder is named after its pack ("PuP-Pack gotg_2020 › Topper"). A single folder: its files open directly.
- **Listening**: each file is a card; the selected card's sound plays, looping, and stops as soon as another card is selected or the screen is left (one sound at a time, as in the suggestions). Meanwhile the menu music goes silent, and resumes when leaving the screen (`previewsSound` in `app/src/library.ts`, read by `soundView` in `app/src/frontend.ts`): before, the table's music played over the music suggestions. Start takes it.
- **What is shown**: sound files (mp3, ogg, m4a, flac, wav), and MP4 videos (`.mp4`, `.m4v`, `.mov`) that have an audio track the menu can play (AAC, MP3, Opus or FLAC), with that track's duration. Not the backup copies and options of a pack (`Topper.orig`, `_backup`, `PuP-Pack_Options`…), nor the empty files of packs ("@blank.mp4", "30 Second Blank Mp3"), nor silent videos. A table stored in the shared tables folder: only its files in `medias\` (the rest of the folder belongs to the other tables).
- **From a video, only the sound**: its audio track is extracted into an `.m4a`, without re-encoding (the track is copied as is into a new file, `packages/core/src/mp4audio.ts`; there is no ffmpeg on the cabinet), kept in `<données>\cache\sons\`. The 2.6 s intro of the *Guardians of the Galaxy* pack (1 MB of video) gives a 46 KB sound. A fragmented video, or one whose sound is in a format Chromium cannot play (AC-3), is refused, with the reason.
- **Nothing is moved**: the choice goes into the "Médias du jeu" (Game media) draft; on save, the file is **copied** into `medias\` under the media's name (`(Audio) <table>.mp3`, `(AudioLaunch) <table>.m4a`), and the original stays in the pack, which still needs it.
- **Code**: `packages/core/src/tablesounds.ts` (inventory), `packages/core/src/mp4audio.ts` (an MP4's audio track, extraction), tests in `packages/core/src/test/tablesounds.test.ts` (fake MP4s: moov in front or behind, `co64`, uneven chunks, refusals); screen in `app/src/media-sounds.ts`, hooked into `app/src/library-medias.ts`. Off-screen demo with real files from the cabinet's pack: `.\docs\demo-sons-table.ps1` (muted window: playback is checked by position and decoded audio bytes; screenshots in `tmp\demo-sons-table\captures`). Manual tests: [docs/manual-tests.md](manual-tests.md), scenarios 364 and 365.

### Community site media

PinUP Popper is not on the cabinet: Cyber Pinball replaces it. Its media server can only be reached from Popper Setup, but the same authors post on the forums, with a free account. The suggestions for each media item search them, in the session of the "Récupérer" (Get) window (where you sign in yourself; Cyber Pinball never sees a password). Survey of 24/10/2026: `docs/recherche/sources-medias.md` § 6.

- **VPForums first**: 808 complete media packs, 4,153 logos, 1,829 videos. Its search is for members only: without a sign-in, a row says so and "Se connecter à VPForums…" (Sign in to VPForums...) opens its sign-in page.
- **VPUniverse as a last resort**: about 24,000 menu media (logos, playfield, backglass, DMD and topper videos, loading videos, cards, launch sounds, flyers), but **about 45 s of waiting before each file** with a free account, and a limited number of downloads. Its results show without an account; downloading needs one.
- **Search**: when a media's suggestions open, each site's results pages for each title (the title of each selected game without its subtitle, "terminator 2", or what was typed in "Chercher un autre titre…" (Search for another title...)), read in a hidden view, three at most (`SITE_SEARCH_PAGES`, 25 entries per page on VPUniverse).
