VPUniverse searches only the categories of the open media (`siteSearchNodes`: "Topper Videos" and "Topper Images" for the topper): with all categories mixed, Game of Thrones' 16 wheels filled the first page and its toppers fell to the next ones (8 toppers in their categories, 4 on the mixed first page). VPForums does not filter by category: one search serves every media. The page script (`SITE_SEARCH_PROBES`) takes as title the entry's link that has text: signed in, VPUniverse puts an "unread" dot before each entry not yet seen, a link to the same entry without text, which the script took for the title (on 24/10/2026, the 25 Game of Thrones results came out untitled and counted as "other games"). What the script returns is checked (`checkSiteSearch`): an untitled entry is dropped and logged to the console; if most are, the site's line says "page de résultats illisible" (unreadable results page) instead of counting the entries elsewhere. The scripts run in the tests on a real saved results page (`packages/core/test-pages/`), as a visitor gets it and as a member sees it. Each site's line says how many of its results serve the open media and where the others go ("25 résultats (aucun pour ce média ; les autres : Logo 16, Topper 4, Afficheur large 2 ; …)", 25 results (none for this media; the others: Wheel 16, Topper 4, FullDMD 2; …), a complete pack counted separately, `siteHitSummary`): a bare total, next to a screen with no suggestion, looked like a failure. Results are kept for half an hour; nothing is searched in the background. Each result keeps its title, category, author, date, download count and the thumbnail posted by the author (a still image, even for a video).
- **Matching**: the title of each result, stripped of the media words ("Topper Video", "Animated Wheel"...), is checked against the whole VPS database; it only counts if it points to a selected game (a "Terminator 3" result is not taken for Terminator 2). The media type comes from the category ("Topper Videos": topper); for VPForums' mixed videos, from the title; a complete pack counts for every media item.
- **Queue**: Start on a card puts it in its site's queue, one file at a time per site, each site on its own. The download happens in a hidden view, like the direct download of "Récupérer" (Get); **VPUniverse's timer runs in the page**, as for a member who clicks: the page asks the server for the wait, counts down, then starts the file, and the card shows the seconds left ("Attente du site · 31 s" (Site wait · 31 s)). Cyber Pinball waits and shortens nothing. Start on a queued card removes it; "Arrêter la file" (Stop the queue) stops everything. A download cap stops that site's queue; a page that asks for something (several files to choose from, terms, a check) opens in the visible window, and the file downloaded there goes back to the media item.
- **Receiving**: the file is stored in `<téléchargements>\sites\<site>-<entry>\fichiers`, the archive unpacked; a pack laid out the Popper or PinballX way gives each file's type by its folder. Received files are recorded in `<téléchargements>\sites\recus.json` and suggested on later visits, for this media item as for a pack's other screens ("Reçu de VPForums" (Received from VPForums)). The file awaited by the last card chosen for a media item goes into the draft by itself; another choice made in the meantime is never replaced. Nothing is written to `medias\` before "Enregistrer" (Save); the source (site, entry address) goes into the `.info`.
- **Code**: `packages/core/src/mediasites.ts` (categories, addresses, results page scripts, matching, count per media, web search, received files), `sitesessions.ts` (timer, `WAIT_PROBES`), `app/src/media-sites.ts` (search, thumbnails, queue, received files), `app/src/acquire-window.ts` (hidden view, download recipient), `app/src/media-proposals.ts` (cards); tests in `packages/core/src/test/mediasites.test.ts`. Off-screen demo: `.\docs\demo-medias-sites.ps1` (one results page from each real site, as a visitor, then fake entries for sign-in, queue, timer and pack, the display the cabinet does not show, a fake web search and taking the current media again; captures in `tmp\demo-medias-sites\captures`). The test bench calls the garbage collector during every wait: a hidden view nothing holds any more would vanish at the whim of a GC, and closing it afterwards threw "Object has been destroyed"; `acquire-window.ts` therefore keeps each hidden view until it is closed (`hiddenViews`, `closeHidden`, invariant test in `offscreen.test.ts`). Manual tests: [docs/manual-tests.md](manual-tests.md), scenarios 241 to 246, 250 and 251.

### Free search: "Chercher moi-même sur le web…" (Search the web myself...)

When the connected sources do not have what you need (game music for Guardians of the Galaxy, when the suggestions only have toppers), you search yourself in the built-in browser, and Cyber Pinball sees what you download arrive. Findings and choices: `docs/recherche/sources-medias.md` § 7.

- **Entry**: on each media item's screen (table sheet > "Médias du jeu" (Game media) > a media item), right after "Voir les propositions" (See suggestions), **"Chercher moi-même sur le web…" (Search the web myself...)** opens the "Récupérer" (Get) window without waiting for the suggestions search. Its detail gives the search text ("Built-in browser, free search: “Guardians of the Galaxy table music vpinball”"), "Resumes the page where you left off" once the window is closed, or "Downloading: 1.2 MB of 3.4 MB…" during a download.
- **Bar**: the window opens on a pre-filled DuckDuckGo search (table title without manufacturer or year, screen words in English, "vpinball": `webSearchQuery`, `packages/core/src/mediasites.ts`). Under the usual line, a search line: the **Web**, **Images**, **Videos** (DuckDuckGo) and **YouTube** engines, the field and **"Chercher" (Search)** (`webEngineUrl`, `packages/core/src/webmedia.ts`). The field keeps the text of the last results page, even on the pages after it; switching engine runs the same text again. While you type in the field, keys are not cabinet buttons; Esc always closes. The "Retour à la fiche" (Back to the entry) button becomes "Retour à la recherche" (Back to the search). Closing (Esc, Exit, Alt+F4) keeps the page and its history; the window reopened for the same media item resumes where you left off, with the search text. The status line is a how-to ("the file is checked, then offered for “Musique du jeu”"), not a warning.
- **Video pages**: every page loaded, or changed without reloading (YouTube), is read by a probe (`VIDEO_PAGE_PROBE`): a YouTube video (watch, shorts, youtu.be), Vimeo, Dailymotion, or a `<video>` element on another page. One more line describes it ("YouTube video: “…” · 2:47"). When the page plays an ordinary video file (http(s) address), **"Télécharger cette vidéo" (Download this video)** downloads it like a link and it comes back to the media item, sound included (for music, it is extracted). YouTube, Vimeo and Dailymotion play a segmented stream (`blob:`): the line offers **"Récupérer le son" (Get the sound)** and **"Récupérer la vidéo" (Get the video)** through yt-dlp (see below), the move expected for this media item lit first (the sound for music and the launch sound, the video for a screen). The title is read from the live page: YouTube does not update its meta tags from one video to the next.
- **yt-dlp** (the user's choice of 26/10/2026, `docs/recherche/sources-medias.md` § 7.7): the tool that gets the video or the sound of a YouTube, Vimeo, Dailymotion… page, **alone, without ffmpeg**. It does not ship with Cyber Pinball: the **first** request downloads it from the official repository's GitHub releases (`yt-dlp.exe`, about 18 MB, the bar's line says so before the click), checks it against **two SHA-256 hashes** that must agree (the one GitHub's API gives for the file, and its line in the same release's `SHA2-256SUMS`; a file that differs is deleted, never run) and stores it in `<racine>\tools\yt-dlp` with `version.json` (`packages/core/src/ytdlp.ts`, `app/src/ytdlp-tool.ts`). A new release is looked for at most once a week, on use, and replaces the old one with the same checks; with no network, the installed tool is used as is. yt-dlp never updates itself (`--no-update`). Without ffmpeg, only single-file formats: **the sound alone as M4A** (accepted as is by the music, can be trimmed), otherwise the best sound (WebM Opus, converted to WAV); **the video alone as MP4** H.264 up to 1080p (silent, which is enough for a menu screen). A single file, never a playlist (`--no-playlist`), no user configuration read (`--ignore-config`). For YouTube, yt-dlp needs a JavaScript engine: Cyber Pinball's executable stands in for it (`--js-runtimes node:<executable>`, `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, Node 24; yt-dlp needs Node 22 at least). The retrieval has its line in the bar, like a download (installation, then percentage, amount, speed), and "Annuler" (Cancel), or Start then Start on the cabinet, stops yt-dlp and what it started, keeping nothing. The file received goes through the same check and the same "Received" screen as a download. A failure gives its reason ("private video", "YouTube asks to prove you are not a robot…", "network unreachable"…). yt-dlp is only offered for a search started from a media item. YouTube's terms only allow downloading where the service permits it (§ 7.5 of the research): what you get is your own call.
- **Detection**: every file downloaded in the window comes back to it (`will-download`, the page's recipient); during the search, **Windows Downloads** is watched too (`watchDownloads`) for what you get in your own browser ("Ouvrir dans mon navigateur", Open in my browser): only media and archives that arrive after opening count (`isMediaDownload`), copied (the original stays where it is), a file still being written (`.crdownload`, `.part`) ignored. Watching stops when you leave the table's media, at most two hours later.
- **Check** (`judgeWebFile`, `packages/core/src/webmedia.ts`): each file, archive unpacked, is judged for the media item from its **content** (first bytes, `sniffMedia`), not only its name: **accepted** as is (a WebM video named `.mp4` is stored as `.webm`), **to convert**, or **refused** with its reason ("web page saved under a file name: the site sent back a page instead of the file", "an image, whereas “Musique du jeu” needs: mp3, ogg, m4a, flac, wav", ".mkv video the menu does not play; converting it would need an external tool (ffmpeg)", "unknown format (.txt)"). A file of a pack laid out for another screen goes to it. Everything is noted in `<téléchargements>\sites\web.json` (`files`, `convert`, `refused`, `windows`); what is of no use is not kept.
- **Conversions with no external tool** (`app/src/media-convert.ts`): Chromium's engine decodes, in a hidden page (a view with no window, in a separate session whose every address is served by the application, with no network): an image (JPEG for the logo, BMP, AVIF, ICO) is redrawn as **PNG**; a sound the menu does not play (Opus, AAC, WebM) or the **sound of a video** (MP4, WebM, MKV, MOV) is decoded, trimmed and written as 16-bit 44.1 kHz **WAV** (`wavFile`). An MP4 or MOV video can also give its sound track **as is**, as `.m4a`, with no re-encoding ("Take its sound as is (.m4a) and assign", `extractMp4Sound`, the same means as "Sons de la table" (Table sounds)). A video in a format the menu does not play is not converted.
- **"Reçu pour « … »" (Received for “…”) screen**: it opens by itself behind the window as soon as the file arrives, if the table's media are still open. It shows the file with its preview (a sound plays when its card is selected), its state ("Accepted as is", "To convert to PNG", "Video sound, to extract to WAV"), **what will be written** ("Stored as “(Audio) Guardians of the Galaxy (Stern 2017).wav”") and what goes to the trash, then the refused files and those that go to other screens. Actions: **"Affecter à « … » et enregistrer" (Assign to “…” and save)** (or "Convert to PNG, then assign"), "Put in the draft" (writes nothing, with the other changes), "Back to the search" (resumes the page), "Keep for later" (stays in the suggestions). Several useful files: one screen shows them all, you choose. Nothing usable: "Received: nothing usable for “…”", with the reasons.
- **Trimming a sound** (game music, launch sound): "Trim, then assign…" shows two cards, **the excerpt** (it plays when the card is selected, on "Assign this excerpt") and **the waveform** of the whole sound (`waveformPng`), the kept part in signal green on a lit band, the rest in dimmed teal, the fade-out darker. Actions: "Start later / earlier", "End later / earlier", "Step: 1 s / 5 s / 15 s", "Fade-out: yes / no" (2 s), "The whole sound". Offered first: the first ten seconds for the launch sound; the whole sound, up to three minutes, for the music (with a fade if it is cut). The excerpt is redone in the background on each change (the decoded sound stays in the hidden page).
- **Immediate assignment**: "Assign … and save" writes **this media item only** at once (`planSingleMedia`, `packages/core/src/mediaedit.ts`, same plan and same trash as "Enregistrer" (Save)): the file copied into `medias\` under its name, the old one to the trash, the origin (host and address, or "Windows Downloads") in the `.info`; the table is read again for the menu. The draft's other changes stay to be saved; this media item's, replaced, is dropped. It is the only write that skips the plan screen: the "Received" screen has shown what would be written.
- **Code**: `packages/core/src/webmedia.ts` (engines, `sniffMedia`, `judgeWebFile`, WAV, trimming, waveform, video page probe), `app/src/media-web.ts` (entry, "Received" and trim screens), `app/src/media-convert.ts` (conversions), `app/src/media-sites.ts` (reception, check, Windows Downloads), `app/src/acquire-window.ts` and `app/renderer/acquire.*` (search bar, video page); tests in `packages/core/src/test/webmedia.test.ts` and `ytdlp.test.ts` (including the probe on its sample pages `youtube-video.html` and `video-directe.html`, as a visitor and as a member, detection with a fake "Récupérer" (Get) window and a fake Downloads folder, assigning a single media item). Off-screen demo: `.\docs\demo-recherche-libre.ps1` (fake sites only; captures in `tmp\demo-recherche-libre\captures`). Manual tests: [docs/manual-tests.md](manual-tests.md), scenarios 366 to 373 and 380 to 383.

### Recording a game for the wheel

Requested on 29/10/2026: a table's presentation video on the wheel (the playfield full screen, on a cabinet) is first of all the table in real conditions. Cyber Pinball films it while you play, with nothing to download or install: Chromium's screen capture (DXGI or Windows Graphics Capture) and the MediaRecorder encoder (MP4, H.264, AAC sound), inside the application's Electron.

- **Entry**: table sheet > **"Jouer et enregistrer"** (Play and record) (door `sheet.record`, closed in guest mode). The preparation screen says what will be filmed and starts it: "Lancer et enregistrer" (Launch and record). Its settings, kept for next time in `<data>\enregistrement.json`: **screens filmed** (always the playfield; the backglass and the display as you choose, when the cabinet has them), **start** (0, 5, 10, 20, 30 or 60 s after the table appears; 10 by default), **length** (15, 30, 45, 60 or 90 s; 30 by default), **game sound** with the playfield video (yes by default).
- **Why no gesture during the game** (options weighed): 1. a button combination in game, read by Cyber Pinball while VPX has the foreground: every cabinet button already belongs to VPX (both MagnaSaves open its menu, Start launches a ball, Exit quits), one more gesture would also trigger the VPX action; 2. a keyboard shortcut (Ctrl+Shift+…): useless on a cabinet without a keyboard; 3. **recording armed from the sheet, automatic start and end**: chosen, no button is hijacked, nothing can fire by mistake during a real game, and the player knows in advance when it starts and when it stops.
- **The two events** (`advanceRecording`, [packages/core/src/gamerecording.ts](../packages/core/src/gamerecording.ts)): **`recording-start`**, when the VPX game window has held the foreground (the launcher's `onForeground`) for the chosen delay, to skip the VPX fade-in; **`recording-end`**, at the first of: the length reached (clean end: 0.4 s fade to black, the wheel loop restarts without a jolt; a fade-in too), the table left (VPX closed: the last half second, held back before being written, is dropped, so what comes before the closing is never in the video), a capture failure. An end under 5 s keeps nothing. The log says "Enregistrement (…) : événement de début." and "… : événement de fin (duration, game-end or failed).".
- **Witness**: a discreet pill in the corner of the playfield, top right for the player (far from the flippers and the ball), turned like the menu (`indicatorBounds`): green ring and "Enregistrement dans 7 s" (Recording in 7 s) during the delay, a slowly beating red dot, "REC" and "0:12 / 0:30" while recording, then "✓ Enregistré · 0:30" (Recorded) for three seconds (or, in amber, "Trop court : pas gardé", Too short: not kept). Transparent window, no focus nor click, above VPX, **excluded from capture** (`setContentProtection`, WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE since Windows 10 2004): the video never contains it. It is the only Cyber Pinball window left on top during a game (exception bounded by `pupvisible.test.ts` and `buttons.test.ts`: one corner of the playfield, never the focus, removed three seconds after the end). Page `app/renderer/recording.html`, reviewed by Codex (a single red accent, a low-amplitude beat, still when Windows reduces animations).
- **Capture** (`app/src/game-recording.ts`, `app/renderer/recorder.js`, a window never shown): one capture per filmed screen, at the size just needed (a 4K playfield is captured at 1920 × 1080); each role takes **the zone where VPX draws it** there (backglass and DMD sharing a screen: each its band). The playfield is **always rendered in portrait, flippers at the bottom** (`uprightRotation` from the edge where VPX draws the flippers, `orientationFacts`), 1080 × 1920 at most: that is the standard the wheel knows how to turn (`mediaFlipperEdge`), and it shows it exactly as the player saw it, in every screen orientation; a desktop playfield (landscape, flippers already at the bottom) is cropped in the centre to 9:16. Backglass and DMD keep their orientation, scaled to 1280 wide at most. The sound is Windows' (output loopback), on the playfield video only.
- **Back from the game**: the **"Enregistrement de la partie"** (Game recording) screen previews each video, what it would replace, its size and length, then: **"Utiliser pour la roue"** (Use for the wheel) first opens the plan (`planMediaChanges`: the video stored in `medias\` under the game's name, the old media to Cyber Pinball's recycle bin, the old one's forced half-turn removed); nothing is written before **"Confirmer"** (Confirm), then the wheel shows the new video. **"Garder de côté"** (Keep aside) (and Exit) changes nothing on the wheel: the file waits in `<data>\enregistrements\<.vpx name>\(Playfield) YYYY-MM-DD HH-MM-SS.mp4` and comes first among that media's proposals ("Médias du jeu" > the media > "Voir les propositions", source "Enregistré en partie sur cette borne", Recorded in game on this cabinet). **"Rejouer et enregistrer"** (Play and record again) starts over (the first stays aside). **"Jeter l'enregistrement"** (Discard the recording) sends it to the recycle bin.
- **Simulated game** (off-screen tests): no screen is captured; the recorder draws a fake table the way VPX would draw it (red band at the top, green flippers at the bottom), the rest of the path is the real one. Unattended, the recorded game lasts the delay and the length, plus 3 s; `CYBERPINBALL_PARTIE_MS` shortens it (end by leaving the table).
- **Proof**: [enregistrement-partie.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/enregistrement-partie.test.ts) (events, delay, length, leaving, failure, witness, fade, settings, playfield orientation in the four orientations round trip with the wheel, desktop crop, shared zones, files, proposals); demo `.\docs\demo-enregistrement-partie.ps1` (end at the length and used for the wheel, table left and kept aside, lying playfield with backglass and DMD: each video read back as the wheel reads it, orientation and sound checked); mock-up `docs\recherche\maquettes\enregistrement-partie.ps1`. Manual tests 567 to 572 (the real capture, on the cabinet).

## Launching a game

`app/src/launcher.ts` launches the table the way a frontend does: `VPinballX_BGFX64.exe -Ini <VPinballX.ini> -Play <table.vpx>`, then watches the process's windows every 250 ms until it ends. The game starts when the "Visual Pinball Player" window appears (it takes the foreground back if needed, `onPlaying`); VPX has taken over when Windows says that window holds the foreground (`onForeground`, read on every pass: the cinematic launch's coil thump). Closing that window shuts VPX down cleanly (exit code 0). Ctrl+Shift+K stops a stuck game (polite close, then forced after 5 s).

**No screen ever shows the Windows desktop.** The menu windows are no longer hidden during a game: they stay behind VPX's windows, without taking focus from it. The playfield keeps the loading screen (the game's loading video, or a fallback screen; on a desktop, the controls panel) until the game window appears, then each screen switches to its **in-game background**: the game's media, frozen by default, visible only on a screen VPX does not use (topper without a window, backglass without a `.directb2s`, no DMD); see "Loading and play". For this, each window receives a `game` state (table, media, playback of each role), rendered by `renderGame` (`app/renderer/frontend.js`). On return, the menu takes the foreground back as before.

A single rule covers every moment, the **screen guard** (`app/src/screen-guard.ts`, started by `main.ts` before any other window, except the waiting screen for another running frontend):

- **one black cover per screen**, present from startup to shutdown, which follows screens being added, removed and rotated; it never takes focus (`focusable: false`) and only appears once painted black. It covers screens without a Cyber Pinball window: a screen with no role, and screens other than the main one with `--reglages`;
- **gap-free relaunch** (`relaunchCovered`, the only way to relaunch the application: menu → wizard, wizard → menu, settings → wizard): the new process opens its covers and windows on top of the old one's, which only closes 400 ms after the new one has reported its windows painted (marker file `CYBERPINBALL_RELEVE`, created by `paintedOnScreen` in `app/src/offscreen.ts`; 15 s wait at most): being visible to Windows is not enough, since a window is transparent until its first frame;
- **never a light screen**: when a window appears, Windows 11 paints it in a light color before showing its page, even when already painted (`ready-to-show`) and despite `backgroundColor`. Two distinct flashes, measured on the real screens on October 24, 2026 (a probe reading the screen every 8 ms): the light theme's system backdrop (#F3F3F3, "Mica"), as long as the window has received no frame, always for the process's first window and for one to three seconds when the menu starts, on all four screens; and a pale blue fill (#D3E3FD) of about 25 ms each time a window appears already full screen (shown, or restored from the taskbar). `createWindow` therefore creates every window without a system backdrop (`backgroundMaterial: 'none'`): before its first frame, it lets what it covers show through (the black cover). And `coverDisplay` only makes a window full screen once it is shown: not yet shown, it takes the size of its screen and goes full screen when it appears; `minimizeCovered` ("Bureau Windows", Windows desktop) leaves full screen before minimizing it. Tried without enough effect: DWM transitions disabled, Electron's dark theme (gray #202020), appearing transparent then opaque (`setOpacity`: while transparent, the window does not present its frames to Windows, and the flash comes back when it turns opaque);
- **taskbars hidden during a game**: when VPX takes the foreground, before its game window goes full screen, Windows briefly brings the taskbar back in front of the screens it does not occupy (seen on the DMD screen). They come back at the end of the game, when the application closes, and at the next startup if the application stopped in the middle of a game (marker `barres-des-taches-masquees` in the data folder): only the ones Cyber Pinball hid, a taskbar already hidden by another frontend stays hidden;
- **taskbars always behind the menu** (taskbar guard, `packages/core/src/taskbarguard.ts`): Explorer only puts a screen's taskbar behind a full-screen window when that window takes the foreground, and brings it back in front as soon as another window takes it or the taskbar is shown again. At startup, each menu window takes the foreground as it appears; after a game, only the playfield takes it back, and the DMD and backglass never have it: their taskbar, shown again, stayed in front (reported on 29/10/2026, at times on the DMD, sometimes on the playfield). Now each taskbar is put under the menu window of its screen before being shown again (`placeWindowBelow`, without activating anything), then the taskbar guard runs again when coming back from a game, from the desktop, at every screen or work area change, every time a menu window takes the foreground back, and at every screen guard check: a taskbar in front of a menu window is put back behind (« Barre des tâches (Shell_SecondaryTrayWnd) devant « Cyber Pinball — DMD » sur l’écran « DMD » (retour d’une partie) : remise derrière. »). Never while another program has the foreground (VPX in game, Start menu). A taskbar that Windows brings back in front five times within ten seconds is hidden as during a game, with the same marker, until desktop mode or until the application closes. Nothing is set in Windows: only the window order changes, as Explorer does itself;
- **screens log** (`<data>\logs\ecrans.txt`, 512 KB at most, oldest entries removed): at every switch (menu startup, game start, VPX game window shown, back from a game, Windows desktop and back, screen change, taskbar put back behind), the Windows foreground window, for each cabinet screen the Cyber Pinball window that covers it and everything above it (« BARRE DES TÂCHES DEVANT », taskbar in front; « DÉCOUVERT », uncovered), and the rectangle of each taskbar, shown or hidden, permanently on top or not. "Santé de la borne" (Cabinet health), "Écrans" (Screens) group, says how many times the guard put a taskbar back behind since startup, and flags as to check a taskbar it had to hide. Unattended demonstration: `.\docs\demo-barre-dessus.ps1` (simulated Windows, no window opened);
- **console report**: twice a second, the Windows window stack is read again (`screenCover`, `packages/core/src/screencover.ts`); a screen that stays for more than a second without a Cyber Pinball window covering it, or under a taskbar, produces "Écran « Topper » : aucune fenêtre de Cyber Pinball ne le couvre, le bureau Windows se voit." (Screen "Topper": no Cyber Pinball window covers it, the Windows desktop shows through.), then "de nouveau couvert" (covered again). The endurance test counts these reports per launch ("écrans découverts" (uncovered screens)). "Bureau Windows" (Windows desktop) in the system menu suspends the rule until you return;
- **desktop mode**, the reverse rule (`app/src/desktop-mode.ts`, system menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Bureau Windows" (Windows desktop) or Ctrl+Shift+D): every screen of the PC becomes usable again, however many there are. Every application window leaves its screen, whatever it is (`BrowserWindow.getAllWindows`): the menu windows are minimized, the black covers hidden (`hideCovered`, taken out of full screen first so they do not come back pale blue), and taskbars a game may have left hidden come back. Reported on 26 October 2026: often only one screen came back, because only the menu windows were minimized and the covers, which have no taskbar button, stayed full screen on every screen. On the desktop, twice a second, a Cyber Pinball window back on a screen (cover of a screen plugged in meanwhile, reopened window) is put away again ("Mode bureau : « … » était revenue sur son écran, de nouveau rangée." (Desktop mode: "…" was back on its screen, put away again.)), and the guard reports a screen still covered, even partly ("Mode bureau : écran « Topper » encore couvert par « … »." (Desktop mode: screen "Topper" still covered by "…".), `desktopBlockers`). Ctrl+Shift+D becomes a global shortcut for the duration of desktop mode (released on return; if another program already holds it, the console says so and the taskbar remains the way back). On return, each screen first gets its cover back (a screen plugged in during desktop mode gets its own), then the windows put away come back in front of it, and the playfield takes the foreground again.

Tests pin down the rule: no window is hidden with `hide()` (except the covers, through `hideCovered`, in desktop mode only), the application only relaunches through `relaunchCovered`, and the guard starts before the other windows (`packages/core/src/test/screencover.test.ts`); every window is created without a system backdrop, and full screen and minimizing only go through `cover-display.ts` (`packages/core/src/test/offscreen.test.ts`); in desktop mode, with the simulated screens of `docs\ecrans` (1 to 4 screens), no Cyber Pinball window covers anything any more, then every screen is covered again on return, its cover put back before its window (`packages/core/src/test/mode-bureau.test.ts`). Off-screen proof of the switch and of the menu: `docs\demo-mode-bureau.ps1`.

**No editor or audit window.** With `-Play`, VPX 10.8.1 does not create its editor (`PlayTableCommand`, `src/core/AppCommands.cpp`): no table audit (the "Table audit" box only exists in the editor, when opening a table with errors), no script editor (a script error goes to the log and to an in-game notification), no progress window, debugger or table picker. Checked on the development PC with three tables, including Terminator 2 1.25, whose audit reports 18 errors: only the "Visual Pinball Player", "Visual Pinball Backglass" and "Visual Pinball Score View" windows (class `SDL_app`) are shown; "Visual Pinball Topper" is created hidden when the topper is not assigned; nothing more on exit.

Two VPinballX.ini settings still make VPX ask a question in the middle of loading; Cyber Pinball manages them (written at every sync, with manual changes detected and never silently overwritten, like the other managed keys):

| Setting | Value written | Otherwise |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Player] SecurityLevel` | `0`: all ActiveX objects in the script allowed (VPX default) | from 1 to 3, "This table is attempting to create an object of type …" (Yes / No) for each object, even `Scripting.Dictionary`; at 4, all refused and the script stops |
| `[PlayerVR] AskToTurnOn` | `2`: VR off (VPX default) | at 0, "Connection to VR headset failed / Retry connection ?" (Yes / No) or "VR mode activated but OpenXR initialization failed."; the Windows build of VPX includes OpenXR |

Every other visible VPX window is classified (`classifyVpxWindow`, `packages/core/src/vpxwindows.ts`):

- **dialog box** (`#32770`): the game stops and its text becomes the on-screen message, without the buttons. The ActiveX prompt and the VR headset prompt are recognized: the message names the setting to restore. The text is read with `WM_GETTEXT` (`windowTexts`): it is the only way to read an edit box in another process, where VPX puts the audit report and script errors;
- **editor window**, if another version of VPX opened one: the editor (class `VPinball`), the script editor (`CVFrame`), "Preparing Table" and the debugger are hidden (closing them would quit VPX or ask a question), the table audit ("Dialog" box whose text starts with "Table audit:" or "This table contains error(s)") is closed; the game goes on, and the game window takes the foreground back;
- **unknown window**: left on screen.

The last two cases leave a one-sentence report (`LaunchResult.notices`): written to the console at the end of the game, added to the message of a failed game and to the endurance test summary ("autres fenêtres de VPX" (other VPX windows)).

`node scripts/sonde-fenetres.mjs --exe … --ini … --table …` (after `npm run build`) lists every window of a game, hidden ones included, along with what the launcher would do with them; `--commande Edit` opens the table in the editor to check that the editor and the audit are recognized on real windows. Always on copies of the table and of VPinballX.ini.

## Coexisting with another cabinet frontend

Cyber Pinball installs on a cabinet that already has PinUP Popper, PinballX or PinballY without changing anything in their installation. Code: [packages/core/src/coexistence.ts](../packages/core/src/coexistence.ts) (known frontends, taskbars, list of the Windows settings it changes) and [app/src/frontend-gate.ts](../app/src/frontend-gate.ts) (waiting screen); the invariant is pinned down by [coexistence.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/coexistence.test.ts).

**What stays separate.**

- Everything lives in the Cyber Pinball root folder: its own copy of VPX (`emulators\vpx\`), its tables, ROMs and media.
- VPX is launched with `-Ini <root>\config\VPinballX.ini`: the other frontend's `VPinballX.ini` is neither read nor written. Only the log `%APPDATA%\VPinballX\10.8\vpinball.log` is shared if the other frontend also uses VPX 10.8: each game rewrites it.
- VPX plugin mode: no component is registered in Windows (VPinMAME, B2S and PinUP Player stay the other frontend's); `B2STableSettings.xml`, `DmdDevice.ini`, the DOF configuration and `PUPDatabase.db` are not touched.
- The other frontend's files are never moved: "Reprendre les médias de PinUP Popper / PinballX" (Import media from PinUP Popper / PinballX) copies them, and a table picked outside `import\` is copied.
- ROMs and NVRAM are stored separately: high scores and game settings are not shared.

**What is shared: a few Windows settings.** Their full list is `WINDOWS_CHANGES`. The test rejects a declared call made outside its file, and any call that would change something else (`FORBIDDEN_WINDOWS_CALLS`: registry outside the installer, default audio output, the application starting itself at sign-in, system parameters, processes killed by name).

| Setting | When | What Cyber Pinball does for the other frontend |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Orientation of the playfield screen, for the session only, given back on exit | wizard, "Où sont les flippers ?" (Where are the flippers?) step, only if VPX cannot put the flippers on the player's side; given again at every startup | Another frontend installed or running: the first Start warns ("Retourner l'écran dans Windows ?" (Flip the screen in Windows?): its menu and tables would be upside down too). Start flips anyway, then asks for confirmation within 15 s as usual; Exit keeps the Windows orientation, tables upside down in Cyber Pinball. |
| Taskbars | during a game | Only the ones Cyber Pinball hid come back (`taskbarsToRestore`): a taskbar already hidden by the other frontend stays hidden. The `barres-des-taches-masquees` marker keeps the HWND of each one. |
| Ctrl+Shift+K shortcut | during a game | Released at the end of the game. |
| Ctrl+Shift+D shortcut | in desktop mode | Released on return to the menu. |
| Start at sign-in | installer option, unchecked by default | Leave it unchecked if the other frontend starts with Windows: the two would fight over the screens. |
| "Programs and Features" entry | installation (`HKCU`) | Removed on uninstall. |

**Another frontend already running.** The two frontends would fight over the screens, the DMD display and the cabinet buttons. At startup, before covering any screen (`otherFrontendsGate`, called by `main.ts` before the screen guard), Cyber Pinball looks for the processes of the known frontends (`OTHER_FRONTENDS`: `PinUpMenu.exe` and `PinUpDisplay.exe` for PinUP Popper, `PinballX.exe`, `PinballY.exe`; `PinUpPlayer.exe`, the PuP-Pack player, is not one of them). If it finds one, only the main screen shows "PinUP Popper est ouvert" (PinUP Popper is running): Cyber Pinball starts on its own as soon as it is closed (checked every second); Start or "Continuer quand même" (Continue anyway) starts right away; Exit or "Quitter Cyber Pinball" (Quit Cyber Pinball) quits. Nothing is closed or changed in the other frontend. Before each game, the menu checks again: a frontend opened in the meantime gives "PinUP Popper est ouvert" (Start: play anyway · Exit: back to the menu). A frontend accepted once is no longer reported until Cyber Pinball closes, menu ↔ wizard relaunches included (`CYBERPINBALL_AUTRES_MENUS_ACCEPTES`, passed on to the new process).

**Installed frontends** (`installedFrontends`, for the orientation warning): the executable, or the `POPMedia` or `Media` folder, in the default locations (`vPinball\PinUPSystem`, `PinUPSystem`, `PinballX`, `PinballY`…, on C:, D: and E:).

**Unattended test**: `.\docs\demo-autre-menu.ps1` (offscreen) runs the waiting screen then the menu starting when Popper "closes", the message before a game then the simulated game, and the wizard warning (orientation kept, then simulated flip). Offscreen, only simulated frontends count: `CYBERPINBALL_AUTRES_MENUS` (running, executable names separated by commas; with `CYBERPINBALL_AUTRES_MENUS_FICHIER`, closed when that file disappears) and `CYBERPINBALL_AUTRES_MENUS_INSTALLES` (installed). On a real cabinet with PinUP Popper: manual tests 231 to 235.

## Dedicated cabinet

An option, off by default: until it is turned on, Cyber Pinball writes nothing to Windows. When on, it makes Cyber Pinball the Windows *shell* for the cabinet account, instead of Explorer: at sign-in it opens instead of the desktop and the taskbar, and the desktop never shows again, not even during the 1 to 2 s of startup (see [Known limitations of the prototype](#known-limitations-of-the-prototype)). Other accounts, the administrator included, keep Explorer. Entry: System menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Borne dédiée" (Dedicated cabinet).

**Chosen means: Shell Launcher**, the Windows feature made for kiosks (optional features `Client-DeviceLockdown` and `Client-EmbeddedShellLauncher`, WMI class `WESL_UserSetting`): one shell per account (SID), and an action when the application exits, depending on its exit code (0 restart it, 1 restart the PC, 2 shut it down, 3 do nothing). It only exists in the Enterprise, Education and IoT Enterprise editions (Windows 11 IoT Enterprise LTSC 2024 included), not in Pro or Home: the screen says so ("Not available"). Ruled out: the `Shell` value under `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows NT\CurrentVersion\Winlogon`, per account and without elevation, but described by no current Microsoft documentation, with no restart if Cyber Pinball closes or crashes (black screen), and without the startup programs that Explorer launches.

Code: [packages/core/src/dedicated.ts](../packages/core/src/dedicated.ts) (elevated script, simulated Windows, judging the results), [app/src/dedicated-menu.ts](../app/src/dedicated-menu.ts) (screen), [app/src/dedicated-system.ts](../app/src/dedicated-system.ts) (executor: the real one, and the simulated one of offscreen runs and sandboxes), [app/src/dedicated-session.ts](../app/src/dedicated-session.ts) (session where Cyber Pinball is the shell). Tests: [dedicated.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/dedicated.test.ts) runs the logic of the real script in PowerShell on a simulated system layer (no call to Windows) and compares it with the simulator; [dedicated-menu.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/dedicated-menu.test.ts) walks through the screen and pins the invariant: **no write to Windows starts anywhere but the "Turn on" and "Turn off" actions**. Offscreen demonstration: `.\docs\demo-borne-dediee.ps1` (captures in `tmp\demo-borne-dediee`).

### Turning on

1. First create another administrator account, with a password: it is the way back in, as a rescue. Without it, the screen refuses to turn on ("Blocking").
2. Sign in with the cabinet account, with Cyber Pinball installed (`<root>\app\CyberPinball.exe`; from the sources, turning on is refused).
3. System menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Borne dédiée" (Dedicated cabinet): the screen says what changes and checks the Windows edition and the rescue account.
4. "Turn on for this account": Windows asks for permission (UAC; an administrator's password if the cabinet account is a standard account). A single script, `<data>\borne-dediee\activer.ps1`, reads the state of Windows, enables the optional features, makes Explorer the shell of other accounts if it was not, gives this account the command `"<root>\app\CyberPinball.exe" --borne-dediee`, starts Shell Launcher, then reads the state again; the screen checks every point. If turning on fails midway, the script puts the previous state back by itself.
5. Restart the PC: the session opens on Cyber Pinball. If Windows asks for a restart to enable the features, the screen says so; after the restart, "Finish turning on".

Kept in `<data>\borne-dediee`: `etat.json` (the previous state of Windows, reference for turning off) and `desactiver.ps1` (the rescue script).

### Going back

System menu > "Borne" (Cabinet) > "Borne dédiée" (Dedicated cabinet) > "Turn off and bring back the desktop" (Windows permission). The script puts every point back in its previous state, reads it again, then disables the optional features if they were disabled; the screen compares point by point and marks each one "Checked". A difference stays on screen ("Blocking"), and the previous state is kept to try again. The desktop comes back at the next sign-in; the features are fully removed at the next restart.

| Point | Turn on | Turn off |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Startup of the cabinet account (`SetCustomShell`) | Cyber Pinball, restarted on any exit except code 77 | the previous setting, or none |
| Shell Launcher running (`SetEnabled`) | yes | the previous value |
| Startup of other accounts (`SetDefaultShell`) | `explorer.exe`, if it was not | the previous value |
| `Shell` value under `HKLM\…\Winlogon` (rewritten by `SetEnabled`) | not written by Cyber Pinball | the previous value, checked |
| Optional features | enabled | disabled, if they were |

### Rescue

- **On the cabinet**: Ctrl+Alt+Del, then "Task Manager"; File > Run new task: `explorer.exe` (a file window) or any other program. Or Ctrl+Alt+Del, "Sign out", then sign in with the administrator account, which keeps the desktop. Shell Launcher does not block Ctrl+Alt+Del (the Windows keyboard filter would, but Cyber Pinball does not enable it).
- **From the administrator account, without Cyber Pinball**: in PowerShell started as administrator, the rescue script puts the previous state back, like "Turn off", and prints its result; the next time the "Borne dédiée" screen opens, Cyber Pinball reads it, checks it and forgets the activation.

```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "D:\CyberPinball\data\borne-dediee\desactiver.ps1"
```

- **Cyber Pinball keeps restarting** (it crashes at startup, Shell Launcher restarts it): Ctrl+Alt+Del, "Sign out", administrator account, the script above, then restart; the cause is written to `<data>\logs\erreurs.txt`.
- **As a last resort**, without Cyber Pinball's files, in PowerShell as administrator (`Borne`: the name of the cabinet account):

```powershell
$sid = (New-Object System.Security.Principal.NTAccount('Borne')).Translate([System.Security.Principal.SecurityIdentifier]).Value
$sl = [wmiclass]'\\localhost\root\standardcimv2\embedded:WESL_UserSetting'
$sl.RemoveCustomShell($sid)
$sl.SetEnabled($false)
```

### What the dedicated cabinet changes elsewhere

- **Restarts** (menu ↔ wizard, language, cabinet setup): Shell Launcher only watches the process it started. That process writes down its arguments (`<data>\borne-dediee\relance.json`, valid for one minute), closes, and Shell Launcher starts it again; the new one picks them up. In between, the screen is black for a moment (without Explorer, there is no desktop to see).
- **Closing or crashing**: Shell Launcher restarts Cyber Pinball. "Quit Cyber Pinball" becomes "Restart Cyber Pinball".
- **Update**: Cyber Pinball closes with code 77, for which Shell Launcher does nothing; the installer (or `maj-legere.js`) restarts it, as usual. That Cyber Pinball is no longer watched by Shell Launcher until the next sign-in: if it closes, it restarts itself, but a crash is no longer caught. Restarting the PC after an update brings the watch back.
- **"Windows desktop" becomes "Open Windows Explorer"**: without Explorer there is neither desktop nor taskbar. The entry opens an Explorer window (under Shell Launcher, `explorer.exe` started on its own only opens a file window: it only becomes the shell, with desktop and taskbar, when the Winlogon `Shell` value is its own; behaviour reported by third-party sources, to be confirmed on the cabinet), then frees every screen like desktop mode; Ctrl+Shift+D or Alt+Tab brings back the menu.
- **Taskbars**: without Explorer there are none; hiding them during a game (screen guard) finds nothing to hide, and still only restores what it hid.
- **Wizard at first startup**: it does not offer the dedicated cabinet (off by default, Windows permission needed). On a dedicated cabinet, the wizard runs like the menu, and quitting it restarts Cyber Pinball.
- **Installer "at Windows startup" shortcut**: no effect on a dedicated cabinet (the Startup folder is launched by Explorer).

## Unattended end-to-end tests

**Rule, for every test and every development session: off-screen by default.** An unattended test must never open a window on the machine's screens, steal its focus or depend on the screens plugged in: the machine is used for work while the tests run in the background.

- **Off-screen** (`app/src/offscreen.ts`, rules in `packages/core/src/offscreen.ts`): automatic as soon as `CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT`, `CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE` or `CYBERPINBALL_STRESS` is set, or forced with `CYBERPINBALL_HORS_ECRAN=1`. Every window is created through `createWindow` and rendered in memory (Electron off-screen rendering, `webPreferences.offscreen`) at the size of its screen, with a scale factor of 1: never shown, never in the foreground, absent from the taskbar, sound muted. `coverDisplay` only sizes them (simulated rotation included); `show`, `focus`, full screen, minimizing and taking back the foreground have no effect. The rendering is that of a real window: captures (`capturePage`), the layout guard, replayed keys and clicks (`click:<selector>`, `sendInputEvent`) all work the same way.
- **Simulated screens**: `CYBERPINBALL_DISPLAYS=<file.json>` replaces screen detection everywhere (setup wizard, menu, settings, `sync`, button reset: `observedDisplays`, `app/src/displays.ts`), in the same shape as `npm run probe -- --json` (`displays` field), EDID included; a malformed file is rejected, naming the faulty screen and field. Screen sets versioned in `docs\ecrans\`: `pc-dev` (the four screens of the development PC, those of the cabinet model in `D:\CyberPinball`), `borne` (4K playfield lying flat, 1080p backglass, 4:1 DMD, topper), `poste-deux-ecrans` (the current machine: main screen and two screens), `un-ecran`. With simulated screens, orientation is never applied to a real screen.
- **Watch**: during an off-screen test, any application window that is visible or in the foreground (`isVisible`, `isFocused`, native windows of the process, dialog boxes included) is reported in the console ("Hors écran : …" (Off-screen: …)) and in `<captures>\hors-ecran.txt` ("Aucune fenêtre visible ni au premier plan" (No window visible or in the foreground) otherwise). The demo scripts fail if a window appeared; `npm test` replays the wizard off-screen on the simulated cabinet and checks this.
- **Application log**: everything it writes to its console (a table's copy, the host's settings, the group records, an invitation, a VPX launch…) also goes to `<racine>\data\logs\journal.txt`, one dated line per message, `[avertissement]` (warning) or `[erreur]` (error) when it is not plain information; beyond 2 MB it becomes `journal-ancien.txt` and a new one starts. What happened on a cabinet during a test can be read afterwards (`app/src/journal.ts`, `packages/core/src/journal.ts`, tests in `journal.test.ts`).
- **Uncaught exception**: on screen, on the cabinet, it is written to `<racine>\data\logs\erreurs.txt` (date, kind, stack; the oldest entries go beyond 512 KB) and to the console, and the application carries on: never Electron's modal box, which would freeze the menu until OK is clicked, often hidden under its full-screen windows. An unhandled promise rejection is logged the same way (`app/src/uncaught.ts`, `packages/core/src/errorlog.ts`, tests in `errorlog.test.ts`). Off-screen, it stops the test as failed, with the error in the console ("Hors écran : exception non rattrapée, essai arrêté." (Off-screen: uncaught exception, test stopped.)), instead of Electron's modal box ("A JavaScript error occurred in the main process"): on the hidden desktop nobody would close it and the main process would stay frozen, timers and safeguards included (`app/src/offscreen.ts`, invariant test in `offscreen.test.ts`).
- **Hidden desktop, mandatory for demos** (`docs\hors-ecran.ps1`): each `docs\demo-*.ps1`, `docs\demos-hors-ecran.ps1` and the launcher's demo actions relaunch themselves entirely (PowerShell, node, Electron, tools) on a separate Windows desktop, `CyberPinballEssais` (`Enter-BureauCache`, `CreateDesktop` then `CreateProcess` with that desktop), which the user never sees; their output comes back to the original console. Even if a window were shown by mistake, nothing would appear on the machine's screens. The application applies the same rule to any unattended test, whatever launched it (`npx electron app` by hand, another script, another session): when off-screen and outside the hidden desktop, it relaunches itself there with the same arguments (`relaunchOnHiddenDesktop`, `app/src/offscreen.ts`; `packages/win-displays/src/desktop.ts`), copies back its output and returns its exit code; `npm test` checks this. **Only known gap: a new console.** Windows 11 hands it to Terminal, which opens on the user's desktop and takes the focus, even from the hidden desktop: the scripts run their tools in their own console (`Start-Process … -NoNewWindow`, never `start`), and the application runs them with `windowsHide` (invariant tests in `offscreen.test.ts`). `.\docs\preuve-bureau-cache.ps1 -Etape notepad|console|electron` checks this: it launches a Notepad, inherited consoles or a full-screen Electron window on the hidden desktop, confirms they are visible there, and fails if anything appears or takes the foreground on the user's desktop.
- **Reserved for the machine's screens**: a VPX game ("Jouer" (Play), "Régler l'affichage en partie" (Adjust display in game)), the endurance test and captures of the whole desktop are refused off-screen with the message "… nécessite les écrans du poste" (… requires the machine's screens), and only run with `CYBERPINBALL_A_L_ECRAN=1` (real windows, full screen, focus taken): `docs\demo-barre-taches.ps1`, `docs\demo-fenetres.ps1`, the launcher's "Endurance" (Endurance) action. The "Choisir des fichiers" (Choose files) dialog box is also refused off-screen. Reading a table's script (`VPinballX -ExtractVBS`) is still allowed: VPX then opens no visible window.
- **Fake VPX installation, never a junction to the real emulators**: a demo whose menu must see VPX installed ("Jouer" (Play) offered, game simulated by `CYBERPINBALL_PARTIE_SIMULEE` or refused off-screen) calls `New-FauxVpx <root>` (`docs\hors-ecran.ps1`): the manifest's version, an empty `VPinballX64.exe` and `current` as a real folder with its marker. On 29/10/2026, a disk tidy-up (Robocopy /MOVE, which follows junctions) emptied `D:\CyberPinball\emulators` through the demos' `tmp\demo-*\root\emulators` junctions. Only those that really launch VPX, on screen, keep one, removed at the end (`Remove-JonctionsSortantes`): `demo-fenetres.ps1`, `demo-barre-taches.ps1 -Partie`, `demo-ecrans-couverts.ps1` (games and endurance) and `demo-tables.ps1` on screen, for the duration of the game. [jonctions.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/jonctions.test.ts) refuses any other junction in `docs\*.ps1` (the `JONCTIONS_PERMISES` list, each exception with its reason and its number of junctions).
- **Demos**: each `docs\demo-*.ps1` loads `docs\hors-ecran.ps1` and runs off-screen on `docs\ecrans\pc-dev.json` (`-Ecrans borne`, for example, for another cabinet). `.\docs\demos-hors-ecran.ps1` (the launcher's "demos" action) replays them all in a row, with the output of each in `tmp\demos\`, and fails if any of them fails. The scripts in `docs\` are JavaScript that `tsc` does not read: [demonstrations.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/demonstrations.test.ts) reads them with the TypeScript compiler and checks, at every `npm test`, that the application modules they load exist, that the names they take from them are still exported, that their calls pass every required argument and member (simulated hosts included, nested objects too) and that the translated texts they name (action labels, screen titles) still exist in the application. Born on 29/10/2026: the friend's library menu now expected `downloads`, the simulated host of `demo-sortie-de-liste.cjs` did not pass it, and the demo crashed without anyone noticing. A path through screens that has changed only shows when the demo is replayed. A test by hand:

  ```powershell
  $env:CYBERPINBALL_DISPLAYS = "$PWD\docs\ecrans\borne.json"
  $env:CYBERPINBALL_HOME = "$PWD\tmp\essai\home"; $env:CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE = "$PWD\tmp\essai\captures"
  $env:CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT = 'SansBoutons,Digit1'
  npx electron app --assistant
  ```

**Layout guard** (`app/src/layout-check.ts`): at each key replayed by the autopilot (setup wizard, table menu, VPX settings), every window is inspected in its actual rendering. It reports two texts or controls that overlap (a layer with an opaque background hiding another one, like the help over the menu, does not count), anything that goes outside the window, and text clipped by a container that hides its overflow, itself included. An intended cut ("…", lines limited by `-webkit-line-clamp`) only excuses the element that makes it: the lines it keeps must not be clipped by any other container (a title limited to two lines in a zone one line high lost its first line without being reported). A scrolling list must carry no fade (`mask-image`: the line at the edge would look disabled), and the selected entry of a list (action, row, card) must be whole within it: neither clipped nor hidden by what stays pinned at the top of the list, except in a list the user moved away from it themselves with the wheel (`data-user-scroll`, set by `scroll-keeper.js` until the next change of selection). A list that scrolls without a user gesture (wheel, pointer, keyboard in the page) or a render from `scroll-keeper.js` is reported ("défilement sans geste de l'utilisateur" (scrolling without a user gesture), also in the page's console); the autopilot, which brings a row into view before clicking it, declares that as a gesture. Proofs: `defilement.test.ts` (progress ticks and change of selection in the real renderer of the menu and of the VPX settings, no `scrollIntoView` or `scrollTop` set outside `scroll-keeper.js`) and `.\docs\demo-defilement-progression.ps1` (real wheel, ticks, flipper, another screen, forced scrolling reported; captures in `tmp\demo-defilement-progression`). A test checks that the injected scripts compile (`packages/core/src/test/layout-check.test.ts`): a syntax error there made the guard silent. The height used for a text is that of its font, in the right direction for a rotated view. **20 px floor** (design system): a text or an icon smaller than 20 px at the scale of a 1,080 px short side, as it is seen, is reported ("texte plus petit que 20 px" (text smaller than 20 px)): the font size is multiplied by the scale of every `transform` above the text, and an animation that loops counts at its smallest step. The names in the wheel's small medallions, shrunk by `scale(--s)`, went down to 13.6 px without being reported: the guard only measured icons, and the stylesheet test only read sizes in `cqmin` (Codex review, October 2026). A font size in fixed px does not follow the screen: the screens' stylesheets have none (only the developer's sheet, `fallback-demo.css`, keeps some); the "Récupérer" banner had fifteen, from 12 to 16 px. What a `clip-path: inset()` cuts off (the wheel brings its medallions in through its edges) does not count as outside the window. **Menu zones**: each frame of the table menu states its zone (`data-frame`: "plateau" for the media's 9:16 frame, "menu" for the backglass, the wheel, the information and the help bar); two different zones that overlap are reported ("cadres qui se recouvrent", frames overlapping), as is a frame that leaves the playfield zone. Two frames of the same zone may overlap (the wheel overlaps the backglass), and a frame that covers the whole zone is a background (the playfield media on a cabinet). At 4:3, the backglass and the wheel covered the playfield frame without anything saying so: no text overlapped there (Codex review, October 2026; see "The table menu on a desktop, from 5:4 to 16:9 and wider"). A deliberate last resort below the floor is declared with `data-below-floor` (the loading card's controls at their smallest size); the cinema launch, which carries the medallion off the screen, is set apart, as are the fallbacks (`.cp-fb`, `fallback.js`): media drawn in place of an image or a video of the game, their texts follow the size of their zone like a media's (the loading fallback in the desktop's 9:16 frame). Proofs: [plancher-textes.test.ts](../packages/core/src/test/plancher-textes.test.ts) (wheel geometry, gliding included; no size in fixed px, nor written below 20 px, in the stylesheets, the menu's `--u`, `--w` and `--b` units included; reference cases in a real off-screen Chromium) and `.\docs\demo-noms-roue.ps1` (the four wheels and the list, without logos, on a cabinet and on a desktop, at rest, mid-glide, long title and unbreakable word in the centre; captures in `tmp\captures\noms-roue`). Problems go to the console ("Mise en page : …" (Layout: …)) and to `<captures>\mise-en-page.txt` ("Aucun problème de mise en page." (No layout problems.) otherwise); `docs\demo-boutons.ps1` fails if there are any. `.\docs\demo-mise-en-page.ps1` replays every state of the wizard on every screen of the simulated cabinet (1920 × 1080 and 1366 × 768 with `pc-dev`): the "Boutons" (Buttons) step in landscape and on a playfield rotated left and right, screens, orientation and flip, diagonal, sound with two fake outputs (one of them 7.1), summary and saving; it fails at the first problem found.

The wizard screen is built as a flow (`app/renderer/screen.*`): header, content centered in the remaining space, footer (message, step, help) and flipper strip, with no absolute positioning; content that is too tall pushes the footer down instead of sliding under it. The summary panel, which does not scroll, shrinks its body by one or two steps as long as it overflows. The button list switches to two columns when the screen is taller than it is wide.

**Sandbox guard** (`packages/core/src/sandbox.ts`, `app/src/sandbox-guard.ts`): as soon as `CYBERPINBALL_HOME` is set together with `CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT`, `CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE` or `CYBERPINBALL_STRESS`, the application and `sync` refuse any file write outside `CYBERPINBALL_HOME`, `CYBERPINBALL_ROOT`, the project's `tmp\` folder and the Windows temporary folder; the offending path is written to the console and the operation fails. It came out of two incidents on October 20, 2026 where a test touched the real root (an ini path copied from a real cabinet model, an installer parameter that was ignored). A deliberate test on the real root is done without `CYBERPINBALL_HOME`.

- **Setup wizard**: `CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT` replays a sequence of keys, `CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE` saves a capture of each screen after each key. The `SansBoutons` pseudo-code acts as if nobody had touched anything during the "Boutons" step (current keys kept, without waiting 20 s). Each key is pressed then released (button capture counts on release); `Maintenir3:KeyA` holds it for 3 s, `CtrlQ` sends Ctrl+Q. These keys go straight to key handling; `Clavier:KeyA`, on the other hand, goes along the path of a real key (`webContents.sendInputEvent` on the primary screen, `before-input-event`, Chromium, the page) and reports a key whose press or release did not arrive; `Joy3` presses button 3 of an SDL virtual joystick. Each capture leaves a line in the console ("Assistant : relevé game, credit sur Digit5 : gardé", or refused, or skipped).

  ```bash
  CYBERPINBALL_HOME=tmp/home CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT=SansBoutons,ShiftLeft,Digit1,Digit1,Digit1,Digit1,ShiftRight,Digit1,Escape,Digit1 CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE=tmp/captures npx electron app --assistant
  ```

  When unattended, screen orientation is simulated (the geometry changes in `ecrans-simules-orientation.json`, no screen rotates). The `Attendre16` pseudo-code lets 16 seconds pass without a key, to see the countdown expire. Proposing, canceling, letting expire, then confirming the flip:

  ```bash
  CYBERPINBALL_HOME=tmp/flip/home CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT=SansBoutons,Digit1,ShiftRight,Digit1,Escape,Digit1,Escape,ShiftRight,Digit1,Attendre16,ShiftLeft,Digit1,Digit1,Digit1,Escape,Escape,Escape,Digit1 CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE=tmp/flip/captures npx electron app --assistant
  ```

  The "Son" (Sound) step really plays its beeps (short ones: each key arrives 400 ms after the previous one) and writes a report of each sound to the console (output found, channels opened). `CYBERPINBALL_AUDIO_OUTPUTS=<file.json>` replaces the detected outputs with a list in the format of the `audio` field of `npm run probe -- --json`, for example to add a fake 7.1 card and capture the speaker test (Electron cannot find it: no sound, message on screen). With four screens, a fake backglass HDMI output and a fake 7.1 card:

  ```bash
  CYBERPINBALL_AUDIO_OUTPUTS=tmp/son/sorties-simulees.json CYBERPINBALL_HOME=tmp/son/home CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT=SansBoutons,ShiftRight,ShiftRight,ShiftRight,Digit1,Digit1,Digit1,Digit1,Escape,Escape,ShiftLeft,ShiftRight,Escape,Escape,Digit1,ShiftLeft,ShiftRight,Digit1 CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE=tmp/son/captures npx electron app --assistant
  ```

- **Exits and single steps**: `.\docs\demo-sorties.ps1` builds a cabinet model through the full wizard (simulated screens, fake audio outputs) in `tmp\demo-sorties`, then leaves each wizard mode without saving, each one with a different gesture (key held 3 s during button capture at first startup, Ctrl+Q, Esc or Exit held 2 s, button clicked with the mouse, Exit at the first question; "Placer librement…" (Place freely…) on the DMD in `sortie-ecrans`), and checks that `cabinet.json`, `VPinballX.ini` and `vpx-owned.json` have not changed and that the question announced what would be lost; finally it saves each step on its own (screens, playfield, sound, layout) and checks that no key or field outside the step has changed. `-Scenarios sortie-son,etape-son` replays only some of them. Captures in `tmp\captures\sorties`.
- **Cabinet buttons**: `.\docs\demo-boutons.ps1 -Run` copies the real cabinet model and `VPinballX.ini` into a sandbox (`tmp\demo-boutons`), then replays the "Boutons" step on its own with an encoder set to other keys (duplicate and Windows key refused, Exit redone with Backspace, validation refused without a test, Credit redone by clicking its row, test then saving) and shows the resulting `[Input]`; with SDL's virtual joystick (1d), then keyboard and joystick mixed (1e); the interface buttons on their own (1f: J, L, a joystick button, U, cheat sheet on I; game buttons and `VPinballX.ini` unchanged, checked); the full wizard with no answer for 21 s (default keys kept); the full wizard with "Touches par défaut" (Default keys) and "Recommencer" (Start over) clicked, the interface buttons offered on the game ones and confirmed, then driven with the new keys; the menu's cheat sheet, then the reset to default keys from the system menu with the mouse; the menu driven by the interface buttons of 1f (4b: the game flipper does nothing there). Wizard autopilot pseudo-code: `click:<selector>` clicks in the window of the first screen. Captures in `tmp\captures\boutons`.

- **Launcher endurance** (milestone 2 criterion, **requires the machine's screens**: `CYBERPINBALL_A_L_ECRAN=1`, set by the launcher action): `CYBERPINBALL_STRESS="N,S"` launches the table chosen by `CYBERPINBALL_TABLE` N times, stops each game after S seconds and checks that the menu takes back the foreground. The report is displayed and written to `data\logs\stress-*.json`; these games do not count in the statistics. The launcher's "Endurance" action.

  ```bash
  CYBERPINBALL_A_L_ECRAN=1 CYBERPINBALL_STRESS=50,10 CYBERPINBALL_TABLE=1.25 npx electron app
  ```

- **VPX windows** (**requires the machine's screens**: `$env:CYBERPINBALL_A_L_ECRAN = '1'`): `.\docs\demo-fenetres.ps1 -Run` copies Terminator 2 1.25 (failing audit), the real cabinet model and `VPinballX.ini` into a sandbox (`tmp\demo-fenetres`), then plays three games through the endurance test: INI as is (Cyber Pinball writes `SecurityLevel = 0` and `AskToTurnOn = 2`, game successful, no other window), then `SecurityLevel = 1` and `AskToTurnOn = 0` set by hand (kept, game stopped on the VPX question with the setting to restore). At the end it checks that the real `VPinballX.ini` has not changed.

- **Taskbar** (**requires the machine's screens**: `$env:CYBERPINBALL_A_L_ECRAN = '1'`): `.\docs\demo-barre-taches.ps1 -Etiquette apres` launches the menu, the settings and the wizard in a sandbox (`tmp\demo-barre-taches`) and captures the whole desktop meanwhile (`capturePage` does not see the taskbar); `bords.txt` gives the brightness of the first and last row of each screen. The `bureau` scenario picks "Bureau Windows" (Windows desktop) in the system menu, then reopens the playfield as if through its taskbar button. `-Scenarios` replays only some of them; `-Partie` plays a game of Terminator 2 with the real VPX and checks the return to the menu. Captures in `tmp\captures\barre-taches`.
- **Taskbar behind the menu** (no screen, no window): `.\docs\demo-barre-dessus.ps1` replays on a simulated Windows (4K playfield, backglass, DMD; `packages\core\src\test\simulated-explorer.ts`) the return from a game before the fix (the backglass and DMD keep their taskbar in front), ten games with today's return, the old return caught by the taskbar guard alone, a changed work area, VPX in the foreground (the guard touches nothing) and an Explorer that brings its taskbars back in front at once (they end up hidden). `-Scenarios` replays only some of them. Screens logs in `tmp\demo-barre-dessus\<scénario>\ecrans.txt`.

- **Covered screens**: `.\docs\demo-ecrans-couverts.ps1 -Etiquette apres` continuously captures the whole desktop during a game of Terminator 2 (real VPX, copy in `tmp\demo-ecrans-couverts`), the menu → wizard and wizard → menu relaunch, the settings alone and an endurance test (`-Lancements`, 10 by default). Each capture is compared, screen by screen, with a capture of the desktop taken just before: a pixel identical to the desktop is a visible desktop pixel (`couverture.txt`: share of the screen and of its bottom strip, the taskbar's). The report separates startup (before the first windows), what follows (everything must be covered) and shutdown; captures taken while another instance of Cyber Pinball is running are ignored, and the script waits for it to finish before each scenario. Each screen is also measured in light pixels (`b` in `couverture.txt`, "ÉCRAN BLANC" in the report), and a fast watch (`docs\guet-ecrans-clairs.cjs`, one row of pixels per screen, about a dozen readings per second) catches flashes too short for the screenshots (`clair.txt`: screen, duration, color, and what followed; "puis sombre" (then dark) is a flash, "puis autre chose" (then something else) is usually the desktop coming back at shutdown). `-Scenarios partie,endurance` replays only some of them. Captures in `tmp\captures\ecrans-couverts`.

- **Menu rotation**: `CYBERPINBALL_FLIPPERS=bottom|left|top|right` forces the flipper edge without modifying `cabinet.json` (left: 90°, right: 270°, top: 180°). With `--reglages`, the screen alone only rotates if this variable is set. The autopilot's `drag` step: the file drop frame.

  ```bash
  CYBERPINBALL_FLIPPERS=left CYBERPINBALL_HOME=tmp/home CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT=Digit1,Escape,Escape CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE=tmp/captures npx electron app
  ```

- **Adding tables with the VPS database**: `.\docs\demo-vps.ps1 -Run` fills the import folder of a sandbox (`tmp\demo-vps`) with empty files named after real tables (certain games, uncertain ones, a missing one), copies the VPS database into the cache, then replays the "Quel jeu est-ce ?" (Which game is it?) screen, the plan and the installation; captures in `tmp\captures\vps`, written `.info` files shown in the terminal.

- **VPX plugins**: `.\docs\demo-plugins.ps1 -Run` copies the real cabinet model and `VPinballX.ini` into a sandbox (`tmp\demo-plugins`), creates a shared `pinmame` folder there and simulates a ZeDMD (`CYBERPINBALL_USB_DEVICES=<file.json>`, in the format of the `usb` field of `npm run probe -- --json`). It syncs, turns B2SLegacy back on by hand, syncs again (change and finding reported), restores Cyber Pinball's values, then goes through the settings screen; captures in `tmp\captures\plugins`.
- **PuP-Packs**: `.\docs\demo-pup.ps1 -Run` builds dummy packs (options in `PuP-Pack_Options`, one `.bat` per option, one of them unreadable, macOS leftovers, an extra folder) and two dummy tables whose script is pre-recorded in the cache (VPX is never run on these files), then replays choosing the option, the plan, the installation and the check, and the media doctor on a table with a badly organized pack ("Tout réparer" (Repair all), then the option). A second sandbox replays adding on a simulated two-screen cabinet: the option is picked automatically. Captures in `tmp\captures\pup` and `tmp\captures\pup-deux-ecrans`. `.\docs\demo-pup-options.ps1` shows, off screen, the "PuP-Pack" screen of a details screen in all its states on the *Guardians of the Galaxy* test pack (desktop option in place and recommended option, list browsed down to the bottom, option applied and read back, file not found, no clear choice), in landscape, turned, portrait, French and English; the report fails if the selected entry leaves the view or if a list is cut off. Captures in `tmp\demo-pup-options\captures`.

- **ROM library**: `.\docs\demo-roms.ps1 -Run` prepares a sandbox (`tmp\demo-roms`) of nine dummy tables with their script pre-recorded in the cache, with their ROMs copied as before the library existed (the real `t2_l8` from the test table, three identical copies and another, older version; other ROMs of realistic size), an unused ROM in the library and a missing ROM in the import folder. It replays the global view, the organization plan (choosing the version to keep, then back to the most recent one), the organization and its report, sending an unused ROM to the recycle bin, then adding the missing ROM; captures in `tmp\captures\roms`, space taken by the ROMs measured before and after in the terminal. With `-Terminator2`, the real case from a cabinet: *Terminator 2* damaged by an old import (the four chips of the real `t2_l8` at its root, next to its `.ini`, `altsound.log` and `t2_l8_0.vgm`), another table that plays the same ROM, and in the import folder `t2_l8.zip` made by `Compress-Archive`, its chips in a `t2_l8` subfolder. Two sandboxes: doctor first (one-step repair, then the same zip added, recognised, "même contenu que la ROM de la bibliothèque" (same content as the ROM in the library)) and adding first (ROM recognised, rebuilt flat, linked in both tables, then the check moves the leftover chips to the recycle bin); captures in `tmp\captures\roms-t2-medecin` and `roms-t2-import`, content of the rebuilt zip and links checked in the terminal.

- **Project page screenshots**: `.\docs\demo-captures.ps1` rebuilds the images in `docs\images\en` and `docs\images\fr` (README.md and README.fr.md). Made-up tables (no third-party artwork, no real game name): the playfield, backglass and topper of the three main games come from `docs\demo-captures-medias` (original illustrations generated once with Codex), and the wheel logos (a medallion with a metal bezel, the backglass art and a title that overflows it) and other media are drawn by `docs\demo-captures.cjs`, the simulated `borne` cabinet, the cabinet model from `demo-medias.cjs modele`, and a next version announced by a local site whose feed is signed with a test key (`CYBERPINBALL_MAJ_CLE`). For each language (`CYBERPINBALL_LANGUE`): table menu, game page, system menu and an available update; the wizard's "Screens and roles" step; VPX settings (VPinballX.ini read from a `subst` drive, for a short path). Then everything is assembled as WebP (`demo-captures.cjs --planche`): the playfield lying flat is turned upright, the whole cabinet is laid out screen by screen. The layout guard and the off-screen watch must stay silent. `-Langues en`, `-Etapes menu, images` redo only part of it.
- **Menu moments**: `.\docs\demo-moments.ps1` builds a sandbox of tables with generated media (`docs\demo-moments.cjs`: images, WebM videos some of which have a sound track, music and launch sounds in WAV, a `.directb2s`, a game volume at 50%), then replays the real menu off-screen, on the hidden desktop, with the real cabinet model (backglass output: the machine's first active output). Scenarios `navigation` (each game's music, relative volume, sound of the playfield video, "Médias, son et attente" (Media, sound and attract mode) screen), `silence` (game music off and videos muted: nothing plays) `partie` (three simulated games, `CYBERPINBALL_PARTIE_SIMULEE=backglass`: loading video and launch sound, fallback loading screen with a very long title, portrait loading video with sound; during play, occupied backglass frozen, topper animated, silence; return to the menu and its music) `pivotee` (the same game, menu rotated with `CYBERPINBALL_FLIPPERS=left` and DMD as a strip under the backglass, then attract mode rotated) and `attente` (attract mode after 5 s, one table every 3 s at reduced volume; a flipper, Start then Exit leave it without changing the selection or opening anything). The sound and media layers expected on each screen at each step are compared with the `son.txt` record. Windows muted; the layout guard must stay silent; captures in `tmp\captures\moments\<scénario>`. The script fails otherwise. Before that, the layout guard runs its reference cases.
- **Loading screen**: `.\docs\demo-chargement.ps1` takes the tables of `docs\demo-moments.cjs`, then `docs\demo-chargement.cjs` sends the loading state of each case to the real playfield page (its preload, the media protocol), off screen on the hidden desktop: cabinet upright and rotated (90°, 270°), desktop at 1080p, 1366 × 768, 1280 × 720, 1280 × 1024, 1024 × 768, 2560 × 1080 and 4K; landscape or portrait loading video, no media (fallback, very long title), many controls with long names (joystick buttons), display adjustment. Captures and the guard's record in `tmp\captures\chargement`; the script fails on the slightest layout problem.
- **Game media, the whole milestone**: `.\docs\demo-medias.ps1` (typical cabinet, then a two-screen machine; navigation, flyers and rules, "Médias du jeu" (Game media), loading, play, attract mode; empty screens, layout and fallbacks checked): see "Game media", "Unattended proofs". Also in `.\docs\demos-hors-ecran.ps1`.
- **Media of one game**: `npm run build`, then `npx electron docs\demo-medias-fiche.cjs`. Entirely off-screen (windows never shown, `offscreen` rendering): generates one sandbox per screen in `tmp\demo-medias-fiche` (images, WebM videos, sound, `.directb2s`, cabinet default, import folder), then goes through the details screen, the overview, the playfield and its half-turn, the rules pages (moving, adding), replacing the logo and the backglass from the import folder, the display and its fallback, a drop on the overview, the volume, the plan and saving, a table without media (backglass taken from the `.directb2s`), dropping a file organized Popper-style, and "Quitter sans enregistrer ?" (Quit without saving?). Three screens: landscape 1920 × 1080, the same lying flat (menu rotated by a quarter turn) and portrait 1080 × 1920. Captures and layout check in `tmp\demo-medias-fiche\captures`, resulting files in `resultat.txt`.
- **Media from VPinMediaDB**: `.\docs\demo-vpinmediadb.ps1` (Internet connection required: it is the real repository). On the hidden desktop, entirely off-screen (`docs\demo-vpinmediadb.cjs`, windows never shown): sandbox `tmp\demo-vpinmediadb` of three tables (Terminator 2 with its `VPSId` and a user logo, Medieval Madness without a `VPSId`, an unknown table), in landscape and in portrait. Walkthrough: first-use warning, plan with previews, logo replaced, playfield as a video or as a 4k image, progress, media details with its source, then the system menu's "Médias manquants" (Missing media) (details of a table, download, second search with nothing to do). Captures, the `resultat.txt` record (files organized, recycle bin, `.info` sources) and the layout guard in `tmp\demo-vpinmediadb\captures`. Thumbnails of H.264 videos stay black in these captures: their frames are decoded by the graphics card and do not appear in an off-screen rendering; on screen, they are displayed.
- **Adjusting the display during a game**: it has no unattended demonstration, since everything happens inside VPX (`-PovEdit`); manual tests 86 to 90.
- **VPX settings**: `CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT` and `CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE` also work with `--reglages`, with `click:<selector>` (`click:.back`: the back button). The launcher's "demo-reglages" action: copies the real VPinballX.ini into a sandbox, changes one general setting and two settings of a table, keeps the captures in `tmp\demo-reglages`.

- **Installer**: in a sandbox (`CYBERPINBALL_HOME` set), the installer puts `root.json` and the shortcuts (`Menu Démarrer`, `Bureau`, `Démarrage`) in that folder and writes nothing to the registry. In silent mode, `/D=` is mandatory there (failing that, only the root remembered in that sandbox is accepted): never a fallback to the real root. The uninstaller reads this choice back from `installation.ini`. Installation, first launch (`--user-data-dir` also keeps Chromium's data in `tmp\`), then uninstallation:

  ```powershell
  $env:CYBERPINBALL_HOME = "$PWD\tmp\essai\home"
  Start-Process release\CyberPinball-Setup-complet.exe -ArgumentList '/S', "/D=$PWD\tmp\essai\racine" -Wait
  $env:CYBERPINBALL_AUTOPILOT = 'wait:500'; $env:CYBERPINBALL_CAPTURE = "$PWD\tmp\essai\captures"
  Start-Process tmp\essai\racine\app\CyberPinball.exe -ArgumentList "--user-data-dir=$PWD\tmp\essai\electron" -Wait
  Start-Process 'tmp\essai\racine\app\Désinstaller Cyber Pinball.exe' -ArgumentList '/S'
  ```

## Known limitations of the prototype

- Application startup: between its launch and its first black covers (about 1 to 2 s), the desktop stays visible; no window can exist any earlier. Except on a [dedicated cabinet](#dedicated-cabinet) (Windows Enterprise, Education or IoT Enterprise): Cyber Pinball replaces Explorer there, so there is no desktop left to see. If the application is force-stopped in the middle of a game, it leaves the taskbars hidden until its next startup (or until Explorer restarts). Before its first frame, a window is transparent (no Windows system backdrop): if nothing from Cyber Pinball is underneath, the desktop shows through until then; this is the case of the black cover at startup, hence the desktop staying visible a little longer. A screen that is plugged back in or wakes up while the menu is running (monitors put to sleep by the Windows lock screen, renumbered on wake-up) gets its black cover again, but the menu windows do not follow this change yet: the screen guard reports the uncovered or badly covered screen in the console, and restarting Cyber Pinball puts everything back in place.
- VPX 10.8.1 keys (`Player`, `Backglass`, `ScoreView`, `Topper` windows, `[Plugin.<id>] Enable`, priorities and settings of the B2S, PUP and DMDUtil plugins). The format of screen names and the plugins to enable change with the VPX version: they are described per version in the manifest (`adapterSettings`).
- VPX log for diagnostics: `%APPDATA%\VPinballX\10.8\vpinball.log` ("The selected display … is not available" lines).
- Executable and installers unsigned as long as no certificate is provided (configuration ready, see [Code signing](#code-signing)): SmartScreen warns when the installer is launched.
- Updates on request only (system menu, see [Updating from the cabinet](#updating-from-the-cabinet)): no background check. During installation (about a minute), the Windows desktop and the installer's progress page are visible on the main screen. Rolling back to the previous version when the installer fails has not yet been triggered in a test.
- VPX locates screens in logical coordinates: with Windows scaling other than 100%, the position written may not match. The wizard reports it.
- Model stored as JSON. The spec calls for SQLite.
- Cabinet configurations: per-table settings (including the framing set during a game, even though it depends on the playfield screen) are shared by all configurations. A general VPX setting changed after a configuration was created only applies to the active configuration. The selection screen, configuration management and the banner have not been captured yet (waiting for the off-screen test mode, so as not to occupy the machine's screens).
- VPX 10.8.1 plugin mode only: no `ScreenRes.txt`, `DmdDevice.ini` or `B2STableSettings.xml` yet for classic mode, no DOF.
- Zones: the stacking order of two overlapping zones and the rotation of a zone cannot be set (VPX has no setting for them). The actual rendering in VPX still has to be checked on a cabinet (manual tests 101 to 106, 144 and 145), in particular the windowed playfield of a desktop PC.
- Buttons: keyboard keys and joystick buttons (Pinscape, encoder in gamepad mode; see "Cabinet buttons"). A gamepad's hats and axes do not serve as cabinet buttons; an encoder button that sends a key combination is not supported.
- Sound: videos and menu music still play on the Windows default output, as do COM components outside plugins (standalone VPinMAME, B2S, PinUP Player). Nothing checks yet that the default output is the backglass one.
